# RT roadmap research: the evidence

*Written 2026-09-25. This is the evidence behind `docs/RT-ROADMAP.md`, sections 9 and 11: what the top engines ship, what research has found since, how RageV's code compares topic by topic, and what the verification found when it re-checked every claim. The roadmap holds the decisions; this file holds the reasons and the sources. Nothing here is a decision on its own: where this file and the roadmap disagree, the roadmap and the owner's rulings (D0 to D30) win.*

## How this was made

Three phases, each run once per area (A to G), on 2026-09-25:

1. **The survey.** For each area, what the top engines ship (Unreal 5.5 to 5.8 with Lumen and MegaLights, Snowdrop, id Tech 8, Anvil, Frostbite and SEED, Unity HDRP, RTXDI and NRD, Cyberpunk 2077, Alan Wake 2 and others), with sources; the techniques behind them; the research frontier; and a recommended baseline.
2. **The comparison.** Every topic set against RageV's code, with file and line citations, and a verdict: RageV behind, missing, equal, better, or not comparable. It also proposed ideas that could beat both RageV and the shipped baseline, a target design, roadmap changes and questions for the owner.
3. **The verification.** Every claim the comparison rested on (code facts, industry facts, every "RageV is better" verdict and every "could beat both" idea) re-checked against the working tree and the primary sources, and marked confirmed, partly true, plausible (sound, but only a measurement can show it), unsupported, or refuted, with the correction.

The verification read the primary talks the survey could not fetch: MegaLights (SIGGRAPH 2025), Lumen (SIGGRAPH 2022), Snowdrop (GDC 2024) and Hillaire's sky and atmosphere paper (EGSR 2020), whose text is kept in `build/rt_research/sources/`, and it fetched and read the Battlefield V and id Tech 8 PDFs directly. Where the survey said a figure came from a secondary summary, the verification's verdict supersedes it.

The raw output of all three phases is in `build/rt_research/` (`phase1_*.json`, `phase2_*.json`, `phase3_items_*.json`, `phase3.json`, and their digests), and the owner's answers to the 19 questions are in `build/rt_research/owner_answers.md`. `build/` is not in git, so this file carries everything the roadmap depends on. Sources are summarised in the phases' own words; nothing is reproduced at length.

**How to read the verdicts.** In the comparison tables, *behind* means the top engines ship something better, *missing* means RageV has nothing for it, *equal* means the same method or one of the same standing, *not comparable* means there is no shipped equivalent or it is a different tier, and *better (claimed)* marks the fourteen topics the comparison thought RageV led; the *verified* column gives what each became after the verification. None survived in full.

**Gate words.** The comparison's gates quoted below use "within grain", "near zero" and "display level" before the roadmap defined them; they mean what the roadmap's "Words this document uses" says (each tied to `parked_stats.py`'s parked noise floor on the same build). Where a quoted gate compares one isolated signal with the 16-ray reference's whole-frame numbers (494 speckles, 8.83 edge jitter), the roadmap's item compares it with that signal's own many-sample reference instead. Step labels in the quoted text follow the roadmap's current labels (RT2-23's 23a to 23e; its former 23f and 23g are now the items RT2-23f and RT2-23g).

## Gaps the research could not close

These hold for every area; each area's own list follows its section.

- **The web search budget ran out.** The session's 200 searches, shared by all seven survey agents, were used up while areas D, E and F were still being researched, so those finished from direct fetches of known URLs, and area G started with none left, so its sources are the pages and PDFs the other agents had already reached. Coverage of shipped-title details in D to G is thinner than in A to C, and several details rest on one source each.
- **Doom: The Dark Ages, Graphics Programming Conference 2025.** The visibility-buffer talk's link returned 404 and the path-tracing talk's PDF exceeded the fetch limit, so id Tech 8's visibility-buffer costs and its path tracer's handling of transparency and particles are unknown. The id Tech 8 global-illumination talk (Sousa) was read.
- **Assassin's Creed Shadows, SIGGRAPH 2025.** The PDF could not be loaded, so its bounce-light and reflection numbers stay unsupported (C-C16), and the roadmap uses none of them.
- **Login-gated talks.** Alan Wake 2's GDC 2024 path-tracing talk and Cyberpunk 2077's GDC 2024 talk need a login; their light sampling, denoiser order and cache settings are from secondary coverage only.
- **No numbers exist for the owner's machine.** Every published cost is from a console or a desktop RTX 20 to 50 card, usually at 1080p with an upscaler. Nothing was measured on an RTX 5070 Ti Laptop at 2560x1600; every conversion is an extrapolation, and every cost gate in the roadmap is measured on the owner's machine instead.
- **Unverified points the roadmap depends on.** Moreau's light-BVH timings name no GPU (A); whether current Unreal already puts the far field in a separate structure (E-BB3); whether NVIDIA's laptop drivers expose shader execution reordering and opacity micromaps for inline ray queries on Vulkan (E); the NRD licence's terms for non-NVIDIA hardware, which must be read before NRD ships outside a measured arm (D). Each item that rests on one of these says so and measures it before it relies on it.

## Contents

- A. Direct light, many lights, shadows
- B. Reflections
- C. Bounce light (GI), radiance caches, the bake
- D. Denoising, temporal reconstruction, anti-aliasing, upscaling, post
- E. Ray-tracing infrastructure and frame architecture
- F. Materials, G-buffer, composition
- G. Glass, water, fog, particles
- Totals

Each area has the same parts: in short; what the top engines ship; the techniques; the frontier; the survey's recommended baseline and candidates; RageV against them, topic by topic; the candidates that could beat both, with where the roadmap tests each; the verification of every claim; the design changes the verification required; its new findings; the comparison's corrections to the survey; and the questions left open.

## A. Direct light, many lights, shadows

**In short.** RageV's direct light has MegaLights' shape (a few lights picked per pixel, one ray per distinct light, the analytic light times that ray, its own accumulator). It is behind in how it picks (cost grows with the lights near a pixel), in area lights and glowing meshes, and in cleanup (no spatial filter). Neither "better" claim survived: measured change is RTXDI's shipped gradient. The verified verdicts, with the candidates tied to their items, are in the roadmap's section 11.

**Counts.** Comparison topics: 13 behind, 6 equal, 3 not comparable, 2 better (claimed), 1 missing. Verification of 22 claims: 12 confirmed, 5 partly true, 3 plausible, 2 refuted.

**The survey, in its own summary.** The top engines now handle direct light from many lights in the same basic way. Each pixel gets a fixed budget of light samples chosen at random, each kept sample gets one shadow ray, and a dedicated denoiser cleans the result. The cost depends on the number of pixels, not the number of lights. There are two production versions of this. NVIDIA's RTXDI (ReSTIR DI) picks candidate lights cheaply: from power-weighted presampled tiles at the camera's own surfaces, and from a world-space grid (ReGIR) at ray hits. It resamples them, tests visibility, and then reuses the kept samples across about 20 frames and a few screen neighbours. Each reused sample is shaded again from the current view. It shipped in Cyberpunk 2077 Overdrive and Alan Wake 2, both with DLSS Ray Reconstruction as the denoiser. Epic's MegaLights (experimental in UE 5.5, beta in 5.7, production-ready in 5.8, June 2026) does not reuse samples between pixels or frames. It draws about 4 samples per quarter-resolution pixel, steered by last frame's list of visible lights for each 8x8 tile. It traces screen-space rays first, then hardware rays. It shades the samples at full resolution and runs its own temporal denoiser (at most 12 frames, with variance clipping) plus a spatial filter that only runs where variance is high. On PS5 at 1080p with 941 area lights it costs 5.5 ms, of which 4.1 ms is opaque surfaces. Epic Japan's own deck shows leftover artefacts when TSR is turned off. Glowing meshes are not lights in UE: Lumen GI carries them, and Epic warns that small, bright ones are noisy. Cyberpunk reaches them only through BRDF-sampled rays. Only RTXDI treats emissive triangles as lights in their own right. Area lights are shaded with an analytic formula, and a ray to a random point on the light supplies the shadow. Spheres and tubes use a single representative point with an energy correction; rectangles use LTC. Unity HDRP is the only shipped engine found that uses Heitz's ratio estimator for ray-traced area shadows. Shadow denoisers that run once per light do not scale to many lights (NRD SIGMA is 0.40 ms per run at 1440p on an RTX 4080). Virtual shadow maps are the raster answer, with a fixed cost per light. Research since 2023 has improved ReSTIR in several ways: less correlation between pixels (MCMC mutations), better reuse weights (Pan 2024), handling of motion and uncovered areas (reservoir splatting), and better candidates (a neural visibility cache). None of these has shipped. No source found shows a measured, shipped technique that beats RTXDI or MegaLights on quality per millisecond. Every shipped system relies on TSR or DLSS RR at the end. So the owner's rule that the direct light must converge with anti-aliasing off is something RageV has to prove itself; no shipped engine demonstrates it.

**The comparison, in its own summary.** Where RageV stands: its direct light already has the shape Epic ships in MegaLights. It picks a few lights per pixel by what each is worth, traces one ray per distinct light, shades each pick with the analytic light times that ray's answer, and denoises diffuse without albedo in its own accumulator. The MegaLights talk confirms Epic shades the same way ("pre-integrated irradiance x sample weight"). RageV is better in three places. Measured change is an exact test for real change, where MegaLights only clips by neighbourhood variance. Its selection score is linear, so it does not depend on scene brightness; MegaLights weights by log luminance. And it has an every-light reference arm that no engine publishes. Where they are better: RageV scores every light in the pixel's cell (22 in the garage, up to 190 on the bridge) and every light in the scene at ray hits. At hits, lamps are points with hard shadows. Both light grids are built on the CPU for each view. Emitters are bounding rectangles and the direct pass never samples them. There are no rectangle lights and no soft sun. Picks use white noise. There is no spatial filter, so young pixels and the 4-frame specular history lean on TAA. MegaLights guides its picks by last frame's visible lights per 8x8 tile, uses blue noise, a 5x5 variance clip and a spatial pass driven by variance. Its own talk admits it still leans on TSR, so no shipped engine shows how to meet rule 3. What could beat both: keep only visibility in the history and recompute the unshadowed light every frame at the current view (no specular smear, no trail from a moving light's pool). One scale-free light BVH as the candidate source for hits and for hidden candidates. Spatial-only pairwise-MIS pooling, which RT-24 already proved on reflections. Tube points sampled where the highlight comes from. Roadmap changes: RT2-10 stops being a menu: analytic area light times one sampled ray, with the score equal to the shaded term. RT2-11 builds emitter clusters from triangles and counts each fixture once by MIS. RT2-12 splits: a world-state light BVH serves every hit (the bridge-shaped grid goes, ReGIR is dropped to a fallback), and a new RT2-12b adds guided primary sampling whose cost stops growing with light count. RT2-24 first builds the MegaLights-style denoiser as the baseline, then the visibility-ratio split, which is adopted only if it wins its gates. The owner makes three calls: live direct light for baked lamps, which copy draws a lamp's floor reflection, and a downsampled sampling tier.

**The verification, in its own summary.** I tested 22 claims: 12 confirmed, 5 partly true, 2 refuted, 3 plausible. The code claims (A-C1, 2, 7-10, 13) hold, with small widenings: the score mismatch also covers sized spheres, and the TAA-jitter freeze also covers the K light picks, not only the soft-shadow point. The MegaLights claims (A-C3, 4, 11, 12) and Unity's SN/UN (A-C16) check out against their sources. Moreau's numbers are close but slightly off (update range 0.18-0.89 ms), and his slides state no GPU. Both 'RageV is better' verdicts are refuted. For measured change, RTXDI's SDK ships the same method (3x3 strata, same light sample, diffuse and specular confidence) and Quake II RTX shipped A-SVGF. The every-light arm checks light selection, not the true image, and UE keeps a non-stochastic path and a path tracer to compare against. BB1 is only partly true: its R_d and R_s are biased ratio estimators, its exact U conflicts with BB2's light BVH, and its history cannot converge while picks are frozen without jitter. BB2, BB3 and BB4 are plausible but need measurement gates. BB2 also overstates the bridge's cell load: 150 is the busiest cluster, not every cell.

### A.1 What the top engines ship

- **Unreal Engine 5.5-5.8 MegaLights.** Stochastic direct lighting for local, area and (from 5.7) directional lights. Candidates come from the clustered light grid. Weighted reservoir sampling picks lights, weighting each by the log of its BRDF-weighted luminance. Picks are steered by the previous frame's visible-light list per 8x8 screen tile, read with a random bilinear pick among the 4 nearest tiles; lights that were hidden keep at most about 20% of the weight so they can still be found. Default is 4 samples per downsampled pixel at quarter resolution (a checkerboard half-resolution mode arrived in 5.7), with spatio-temporal blue noise. Tracing goes screen-space first, then hardware RT against simplified proxies near the camera and a merged far-field structure, with VSM as an option. Samples are shaded at full resolution. The denoiser: material demodulated, temporal history of at most 12 frames with a 5x5 YCoCg variance clip, a sparse spatial pass only where variance is high, a sample-weight clamp (MaxShadingWeight 20), then TSR. 5.8 no longer traces several rays to a light when the pixel is not in its penumbra, and culls lights early by power and falloff. Glowing meshes are lit by Lumen GI, not by MegaLights.
  Numbers: PS5, 1080p, 941 area lights, about 1 sample per full-res pixel: sampling 0.70, screen trace 0.47, HW RT 1.35, sample shading 0.55, visible-light list 0.05, denoise 0.96 ms (opaque total about 4.1 ms); with translucency and volumetric fog, 5.51 ms total. RTX 3080, 900 shadowed point lights (resolution not stated): 134 ms with MegaLights off, 13 ms on. 5.7: shading-sample vectorisation saves 0.1-0.2 ms on consoles. 5.8: the single ray outside penumbrae saves 0.3-1 ms on consoles; power culling gives about 20% fewer light samples in the Firefly scene.
  Status: Engine feature: experimental 5.5 (2024), beta 5.7 (Nov 2025), production-ready 5.8 (Jun 2026). The only shipped title found is Arm's Neural Dawn (mobile, UE 5.6.1, 2026). No AAA title shipping MegaLights was found.
  Sources: <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/MegaLights_Stochastic_Direct_Lighting_2025.pdf>; <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/megalights-in-unreal-engine?lang=en-US>; <https://github.com/Far-Beyond-Pulsar/Helio/issues/48>; <https://www.docswell.com/s/EpicGamesJapan/Z27EW1-DayDays26-MegaLights>; <https://tomlooman.com/unreal-engine-5-7-performance-highlights/>; <https://tomlooman.com/unreal-engine-5-8-performance-highlights/>; <https://www.unrealengine.com/news/unreal-engine-5-8-is-now-available>; <https://newsroom.arm.com/news/announcing-neural-dawn>; <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/lumen-global-illumination-and-reflections-in-unreal-engine?lang=en-US>
- **Unreal Engine 5 Virtual Shadow Maps (+ SMRT) and analytic area lights.** Raster shadow maps at 16k x 16k virtual resolution, split into 128x128 pages. Pages are allocated only where the depth buffer needs them and are cached between frames. Soft shadows come from Shadow Map Ray Tracing: several rays per pixel march the shadow map towards the light, which gives contact hardening. Sphere and tube light specular uses a representative point with a GGX energy correction (Karis 2013; for a line it is applied once, in the one dimension the lobe widens). Rect lights use LTC tables.
  Numbers: SMRT defaults to 8 rays per pixel at Epic scalability. Each local light has a fixed cost. Docs say many small local lights are still a work in progress. No ms published in the docs.
  Status: Shipped (Fortnite and UE5 titles).
  Sources: <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/virtual-shadow-maps-in-unreal-engine>; <https://cdn2.unrealengine.com/Resources/files/2013SiggraphPresentationsNotes-26915738.pdf>
- **Cyberpunk 2077 RT Overdrive (REDengine, with NVIDIA).** RTXDI ReSTIR DI over the game's analytic (primitive) lights, with uniform initial candidate sampling. Scenes hold up to about 250 lights, limited by the streamer. The unshadowed term matches the native raster lighting, and every light casts RT shadows. Glowing meshes and the sky are reached only by BRDF-sampled path rays. The two are blended by a cheap roughness heuristic: ReSTIR DI weighted by roughness squared, BRDF sampling by one minus that. The team warns that the ratio of initial candidates to lights decides quality (1/5 vs 1/25), that light indices must be translated between frames, and that disocclusion quality needs a spatial boost (8 samples on disocclusion, 2 after). ReSTIR GI was added in 2.1. Denoising: NRD at first, DLSS Ray Reconstruction later.
  Numbers: No ms published in the ReSTIR course talk.
  Status: Shipped (Apr 2023 preview; 2.1 in Dec 2023).
  Sources: <https://intro-to-restir.cwyman.org/presentations/2023ReSTIR_Course_Cyberpunk_2077_Integration.pdf>; <https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/on-demand/session/gdc24-gdc1002/>; <https://wccftech.com/cyberpunk-2077-2-1-patch-path-tracing-improvements/>
- **Alan Wake 2 (Northlight).** With RT on High, the path tracer replaces raster shadows. Its direct lighting uses ReSTIR DI (RTXDI) with some lights weighted up for art direction, plus OMM, SER and DLSS Ray Reconstruction. Lower RT settings keep raster shadow maps. The GDC 2024 talk's details are login-gated and were not read.
  Numbers: SER cut the path-tracing pass from 16.8 to 10.2 ms (GPU not stated in the Khronos write-up). No direct-light ms published.
  Status: Shipped (Oct 2023). Most details here come from secondary sources.
  Sources: <https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/on-demand/session/gdc24-gdc1003/>; <https://www.remedygames.com/article/how-northlight-makes-alan-wake-2-shine>; <https://www.digitaltrends.com/computing/gdc-2024-in-review/>; <https://www.khronos.org/blog/boosting-ray-tracing-performance-with-shader-execution-reordering-introducing-vk-ext-ray-tracing-invocation-reorder>
- **Indiana Jones and the Great Circle (id Tech 7, Vulkan).** Path-tracing mode: a raygen TraceMain pass (up to 2 bounces, with any-hit shaders for alpha), a SHaRC radiance cache updated with up to 4 bounces, a separate SunTracing pass, and forward+ shading that traces shadow rays with ray queries inside fragment shaders. Alpha-tested shadow rays use 4-state OMMs; indirect rays force 2-state OMM. Digital Foundry (secondary) lists RT sun shadows plus path-traced indirect light. The local-light sampling algorithm is not published.
  Numbers: OMM: TraceMain 7.90 to 3.58 ms (-55%), SharcUpdate -55%, SunTracing -14%, at 1080p internal (DLSS RR Performance to 4K). SER: -24% TraceMain on an RTX 5080; active threads per warp 38% to 70%.
  Status: Shipped (Dec 2024).
  Sources: <https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/path-tracing-optimizations-in-indiana-jones-opacity-micromaps-and-compaction-of-dynamic-blass/>; <https://forums.developer.nvidia.com/t/path-tracing-optimization-in-indiana-jones-shader-execution-reordering-and-live-state-reductions/333330>; <https://www.resetera.com/threads/df-indiana-jones-and-the-great-circle-pc-the-full-ray-tracing-deep-dive.1061721/>
- **Doom: The Dark Ages (id Tech 8).** The base mode uses RT GI; ray hits are lit through a camera-centred cascaded world-space light grid (16^3 cells per cascade, OBB-vs-cell culling in one async compute dispatch), with the same lighting loop as raster. Contact shadows come from a hemisphere ray-march for every light. Raster characters use art-driven light rigs that the RT world skips. A June 2025 path-tracing update adds SHaRC, SER and DLSS RR, but its light sampling is not published. Base-mode direct shadows are not described in the talk.
  Numbers: Light grid build about 0.2 ms on consoles, about 0.1 ms on PC (RTX 4080); limits of 4k lights retail / 8k dev, 30k decals. Whole GI 1.7-2.1 ms across Series S (900p) to PC (4K). PT: DLSS 4 figures only.
  Status: Shipped (May 2025; PT update Jun 2025).
  Sources: <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/SOUSA_SIGGRAPH_2025_Final.pdf>; <https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/geforce/news/doom-the-dark-ages-path-tracing-dlss-ray-reconstruction-update/>; <https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/how-id-software-used-neural-rendering-and-path-tracing-in-doom-the-dark-ages>
- **Metro Exodus Enhanced Edition (4A).** RT GI hits are lit by up to 256 analytic lights looked up in a world-space cluster grid. Each light's shadow map gives occlusion at secondary hits. Glowing surfaces contribute when rays hit them, so their soft area shadows appear in GI. Primary direct light is still hybrid, with shadow maps.
  Numbers: Not published.
  Status: Shipped (2021).
  Sources: <https://www.4a-games.com.mt/4a-dna/in-depth-technical-dive-into-metro-exodus-pc-enhanced-edition>
- **Unity HDRP.** Area lights shaded with LTC. RT shadows per light type (sun angular diameter, point/spot radius, rect lights), with a per-pixel sample count and a spatio-temporal denoiser. For rect lights in deferred Lit, the ray-traced area shadow accumulates shadowed and unshadowed sample sums (SN, UN) whose ratio multiplies the analytic lighting, which is Heitz 2018's ratio estimator. Forward or other shaders use an approximation. Ray hits use a camera-centred RT light cluster.
  Numbers: Not published; the docs say cost grows linearly with sample count.
  Status: Shipped engine feature.
  Sources: <https://docs.unity3d.com/Packages/com.unity.render-pipelines.high-definition@17.2/manual/Ray-Traced-Shadows.html>; <https://github.com/Unity-Technologies/Graphics/blob/master/Packages/com.unity.render-pipelines.high-definition/Runtime/RenderPipeline/Raytracing/Shaders/Shadows/RaytracingShadow.raytrace>; <https://docs.unity3d.com/Packages/com.unity.render-pipelines.high-definition@17.2/manual/Ray-Tracing-Light-Cluster.html>
- **AMD FidelityFX (Hybrid Shadows, Shadow Denoiser, Capsaicin GI-1.0).** Hybrid Shadows: a classifier runs a blocker search on the sun's cascades, rejects surely-lit and surely-shadowed pixels, shortens ray intervals, and traces 1 ray per pixel only in uncertain 8x4 tiles. The Shadow Denoiser handles a single light: 8x4 bitmask input, 17x17 moments, disocclusion mask, 3 edge-avoiding a-trous passes. GI-1.0 samples many lights through world-space hash-grid reservoirs.
  Numbers: No ms published on GPUOpen pages.
  Status: Public SDK and samples.
  Sources: <https://gpuopen.com/fidelityfx-hybrid-shadows/>; <https://gpuopen.com/manuals/fidelityfx_sdk/techniques/classifier/>; <https://gpuopen.com/manuals/fidelityfx_sdk/techniques/denoiser/>; <https://arxiv.org/html/2310.19855>
- **NVIDIA RTX stack (RTXDI 3.1, NRD, RTXPT, DLSS RR, SER, OMM).** RTXDI covers ReSTIR DI, ReGIR, ReSTIR GI and ReSTIR PT. NRD provides ReLAX (recommended for RTXDI signals), ReBLUR, and the per-light SIGMA shadow denoiser. RTXPT uses NEE-AT, feedback-guided per-tile light sampling (tiles jittered to avoid DLSS RR blockiness). DLSS RR replaces the denoiser in shipped PT titles.
  Numbers: NRD on RTX 4080, 1440p native: SIGMA_SHADOW 0.40 ms, SIGMA_SHADOW_TRANSLUCENCY 0.45 ms, REBLUR diff+spec 2.55 ms, RELAX diff+spec 3.25 ms. RTXDI blog: San Miguel with 11k emissive triangles in under 8 ms (GPU and resolution not stated).
  Status: Public SDK; shipped in CP2077 and AW2.
  Sources: <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/RTXDI>; <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD/blob/master/README.md>; <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/RTXPT>; <https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/lighting-scenes-with-millions-of-lights-using-rtx-direct-illumination/>
- **HypeHype (SIGGRAPH 2025 Advances; mobile).** Stochastic tile-based lighting. Big tiles hold 16-slot stratified reservoirs built by A-Chao weighted reservoir sampling over all lights. 16x16-pixel small tiles resample 1-4 unique lights from interleaved neighbouring big tiles. A deferred shadow pass evaluates one shadow term per 2x2 quad from a shadow-map atlas. The talk says TAA hides the quad-resolution silhouette errors and the 1-bit temporal dither.
  Numbers: Reservoir storage 32-128 KB at 1080p; shadow terms 0.5-2 MB. No ms extracted.
  Status: Shipped (mobile). Included as the counter-example of a design that leans on TAA.
  Sources: <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/s2025_stb_lighting_v1.1_notes.pdf>
- **Snowdrop (Avatar: Frontiers of Pandora), Frostbite/SEED.** Snowdrop ray traces GI and reflections with per-pixel detail. Its direct-light shadowing method is not stated in the sources found. No public Frostbite/SEED system for many-light RT direct lighting was found; GIBS is a GI technique.
  Numbers: Not published.
  Status: Shipped (Avatar 2023); these details were not found.
  Sources: <https://www.massive.se/blog/games-technology/snowdrops-ray-tracing-shines-a-light-on-pandora/>; <https://media.gdcvault.com/gdc2024/Slides/GDC+slide+presentations/Kuenlib_Quentin_Raytracing_In_Snowdrop.pdf>

### A.2 The techniques behind them

- **ReSTIR DI (spatiotemporal reservoir resampling, Bitterli et al. 2020)** (shipped; shipped in Cyberpunk 2077 RT Overdrive, Alan Wake 2 (PT mode), RTXDI SDK; confidence high). Per pixel, draw M cheap candidate lights, or points on lights, from a simple source distribution. Weighted reservoir sampling keeps one sample in proportion to a target function (unshadowed contribution with the BRDF). Visibility reuse: trace one shadow ray for the kept sample and zero it if occluded, before reuse. Temporal reuse merges the reprojected previous reservoir, with its history count capped at about 20x. Spatial reuse merges k neighbours' reservoirs, each re-weighted by the current pixel's target, and with MIS or bias correction. Final shading re-evaluates the kept sample with the current BRDF and view, plus one shadow ray.
  Fixes: Makes the per-pixel cost independent of light count, and finds the lights that matter, including after occlusion. Because the reused thing is a light sample re-shaded each frame, not a radiance value, specular highlights do not smear across views. Cost: Titan RTX, 1920x1080: Amusement Park with 3.4M dynamic emissive triangles in under 50 ms per frame, at most 8 rays per pixel. Other scenes on an RTX 2080 Ti. Equal-error 6-60x faster (unbiased) or 35-65x faster (biased) than prior methods, including Moreau 2019's light BVH. Per-pass ms on current GPUs: not published. Fails: Correlated blotches and boiling from reusing too much. The biased variant loses energy (darkening). A temporal lag when lights move or switch. Noise in newly uncovered areas. Fireflies from rare high-weight samples. It ignores visibility when choosing candidates, so lights behind walls waste samples. It still needs a denoiser.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: partly. The raw output is about 1 sample per pixel and needs a denoiser (paper: ReLAX-class spatio-temporal filtering). Shipped titles pair it with DLSS RR. Nothing in the algorithm needs TAA, but no shipped instance runs without an upscaler or denoiser. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Excellent: compute passes plus rayQuery shadow rays; no any-hit or raygen needed. SER is not available to ray queries, so shadow-ray divergence cannot be reordered.
  Needs: G-buffer with previous-frame surface data and motion vectors; a light buffer with stable or translated indices across frames; a random-number sequence decorrelated between neighbours (blue noise or permutations).
  Sources: <https://research.nvidia.com/publication/2020-07_spatiotemporal-reservoir-resampling-real-time-ray-tracing-dynamic-direct>; <https://cs.dartmouth.edu/~wjarosz/publications/bitterli20spatiotemporal.html>; <https://intro-to-restir.cwyman.org/>
- **RTXDI production pipeline (presampling, local light PDF, ReGIR, bias-correction modes, boiling filter)** (public_sdk; shipped in Cyberpunk 2077 RT Overdrive, Alan Wake 2; confidence high). A prepare pass writes every light into one buffer: analytic lights, emissive triangles (average emissive over each triangle), infinite lights and the environment. Local lights get a power-proportional PDF texture (power-of-two, full mip chain, Z-curve layout). A presampling pass draws lights by descending the mips and stores them in RIS tiles, so the initial candidates are a cheap, cache-coherent read from one random tile. Library defaults: 8 local + 1 infinite + 1 environment + 1 BRDF candidate, initial visibility on. Temporal: maxHistoryLength 20, permutation sampling on, depth 0.1 and normal 0.5 thresholds. Boiling filter on at strength 0.2. Spatial: 1 neighbour (8 when history is invalid), 32 px radius, material-similarity test. Final visibility reused if at most 4 frames old and within 16 px. Bias-correction modes (Off, Basic, Pairwise, Raytraced) trade shader cost against extra shadow rays; RayTraced tests each spatial neighbour with a shadow ray. ReGIR fills world-grid or onion cells from the presampled tiles for secondary hits.
  Fixes: Constant-cost candidate generation over millions of lights, glowing triangles as real lights, temporal stability (permutations decorrelate history; the boiling filter removes outlier reservoirs), and quality in newly uncovered areas. Cost: Not published per pass in the SDK docs. NVIDIA blog: San Miguel, 10M triangles, 11k emissive triangles, sun and sky, in under 8 ms (GPU and resolution not stated). Fails: The same as ReSTIR. Power-only presampling ignores distance, so candidate quality drops when the light count greatly exceeds the candidate count (the Cyberpunk team's 1/25 vs 1/5 ratio warning). Light indices must be translated between frames. Pairwise MIS is needed for glossy surfaces (added in 1.3).
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: partly. The SDK expects NRD ReLAX (its docs say ReLAX suits RTXDI signals), and shipped games use DLSS RR. There is no TAA dependency in the resampling itself. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Good: the shader library is portable HLSL/GLSL-style code over application callbacks; visibility can come from ray queries in compute (medium confidence that the sample has an inline mode).
  Needs: Persistent light buffer; PDF texture with mips; previous-frame light buffer plus a remapping table; G-buffer history.
  Sources: <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/RTXDI/blob/main/Doc/Integration.md>; <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/NVIDIA-RTX/RTXDI-Library/main/Source/ReSTIRDI.cpp>; <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/NVIDIA-RTX/RTXDI-Library/main/Include/Rtxdi/DI/ReSTIRDIParameters.h>; <https://github.com/NVIDIAGameWorks/RTXDI/blob/main/ChangeLog.md>; <https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/lighting-scenes-with-millions-of-lights-using-rtx-direct-illumination/>
- **ReGIR (grid-based reservoirs, Boksansky, Jukarainen, Wyman 2021)** (public_sdk; shipped in RTXDI SDK, RTXPT; confidence high). Each frame, every world-grid cell gets many light slots (default 16^3 cells, 512 slots per cell). Each slot is filled by RIS with 8 candidates, weighted by the light's intensity at the cell (distance clamped to the cell border). Only recently used cells are refreshed. A shading point picks a cell, jittered by up to half a cell (a random trilinear filter), then resamples a few slots with its own BRDF.
  Fixes: Good light candidates at arbitrary points (ray hits, GI, reflections) where screen-space reuse does not exist, at a cost independent of the light count. Cost: RTX 3090, 1920x1080: about 1.5 ms per frame in total, of which about 0.3 ms builds the grid; the rest depends on how many rays read the grid. Fails: Slightly biased (discrete cells, limited slots). Ignores walls, so light leaks across rooms in the candidates. The cell-size trade-off (small cells = better quality but less range) is exactly a world-scale tuning problem. On primary hits it is worse than screen-space ReSTIR unless there are few lights.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: partly. The chapter assumes a denoiser smooths the residual noise and bias. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Excellent: pure compute.
  Needs: A camera-centred or hashed world grid; a light buffer.
  Sources: <https://boksajak.github.io/files/RTG2_ReGIRGridBasedReservoirs.pdf>
- **MegaLights (visibility-guided stochastic direct lighting, no reservoir reuse)** (shipped; shipped in Unreal Engine 5.8 (production), Neural Dawn (Arm, mobile); confidence medium). Candidates come from the clustered light grid, with fast rejection. Weighted reservoir sampling picks a fixed number of samples per downsampled pixel (default 4 at quarter resolution; 2 as a scalability option), weighted by the log of BRDF-weighted luminance. Picks are guided by last frame's per-tile visible-light list, looked up bilinearly and at random; hidden lights are capped at about 20% of the weight and directional lights at about 50% of the local weight. Visibility: screen trace first, then HW RT against proxies near the camera and a merged far field, with compaction between stages; VSM as an option. Samples are shaded at full resolution. The denoiser demodulates albedo and EnvBRDF, keeps temporal history (max 12 frames, 5x5 YCoCg variance clip, distance-based rejection), and runs a sparse rotated spatial pass only where variance is high. It skips denoising where most of the energy was sampled. Output then goes through TSR.
  Fixes: Hundreds of shadowed area lights at near-constant cost, soft area shadows, and robustness to occluded lights (guiding) without the correlation artefacts of reservoir reuse. Cost: PS5, 1080p, 941 area lights, about 1 spp: opaque about 4.1 ms (sampling 0.70, screen trace 0.47, HW RT 1.35, shading 0.55, list 0.05, denoise 0.96); 5.51 ms including translucency and fog. RTX 3080, 900 shadowed point lights (resolution not stated): 13 ms vs 134 ms for classic deferred shadowed lights. A rough scaling to 2560x1600 on an RTX 5070 Ti Laptop is an extrapolation, not a measurement. Fails: From docs and talk: blur or ghosting as lighting complexity grows; noise from tiny bright lights and heavily overlapping ranges; brief noise or smear on fast-moving or flickering lights; a cold start with no guiding history (falls back to even sampling for 1-2 frames); weak penumbra quality if hit/miss ratios are used; tile-border seams (hence the random bilinear lookup); self-shadowing and leaking from proxy-geometry mismatch; noisy interiors when directional lights are on. Temporal blur when the denoiser runs below display resolution is listed as an open problem.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: partly. It has its own temporal and spatial denoiser, but Epic Japan's deck shows leftover artefacts with TSR off and recommends TSR plus the temporal filter. Chinese source-analysis coverage says the same. There is no published measurement of it with TSR disabled. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Good: compute plus inline rays. The two-structure far field (a separate simplified TLAS) is relevant to the kilometre-scale bridge.
  Needs: Clustered light grid, HZB for screen traces, RT proxies, a per-tile visible-light list with history, and STBN.
  Sources: <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/MegaLights_Stochastic_Direct_Lighting_2025.pdf>; <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/megalights-in-unreal-engine?lang=en-US>; <https://github.com/Far-Beyond-Pulsar/Helio/issues/48>; <https://www.docswell.com/s/EpicGamesJapan/Z27EW1-DayDays26-MegaLights>; <https://tomlooman.com/unreal-engine-5-8-performance-highlights/>
- **Light BVH / light tree sampling (Conty-Estevez & Kulla 2018; Moreau, Pharr, Clarberg 2019; Real-Time Stochastic Lightcuts 2020; Tokuyoshi et al. 2024)** (public_sdk; shipped in Arnold and Blender Cycles (offline), Falcor, UE path tracer (offline mode); confidence high). A hierarchy over all emitters, whose nodes bound position, power and orientation (a normal cone). Each shading point descends the tree with one random number, choosing each child by its predicted contribution (power x orientation bound / distance^2), which is logarithmic in the light count. Moreau uses two levels: rebuild the top level and refit the bottom levels per frame. Stochastic lightcuts share cuts between neighbouring pixels. Tokuyoshi 2024 scores nodes with accurate spherical-Gaussian lighting, which is better for glossy surfaces.
  Fixes: Distance- and orientation-aware candidate selection at any point, with no screen-space history, and scale-free: no cell size to tune for world scale. Cost: Moreau (RTX 2080 Ti; resolution not stated): Bistro, 26k emissive triangles: BVH update 0.85/0.18 ms (top rebuild / refit), sampling 2.6 ms, total 12.0 ms. Emerald Square, 85k: update 0.89/0.35 ms, sampling 2.2 ms. Full rebuild about 90-300 ms. Stochastic lightcuts (RTX 2080, 1080p, 24.8k emissive triangles, 4 samples per pixel): 11.5 ms for sampling plus shadow rays. Tokuyoshi 2024: 1.4x faster than Tokuyoshi 2022 on an RX 7900 XTX. Fails: Ignores visibility (lights behind walls get samples). Loose bounds for big merged meshes or wide cones. Build and refit cost for moving lights. Warp divergence in traversal. Alone it is noisier than ReSTIR at 1 spp.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: partly. Moreau's results use SVGF with 5 accumulated frames. The sampler itself is history-free. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Good: compute traversal, independent of RT cores.
  Needs: GPU build or refit, and per-node orientation bounds.
  Sources: <https://doi.org/10.1145/3233305>; <https://fpsunflower.github.io/ckulla/data/many-lights-hpg2018.pdf>; <https://www.highperformancegraphics.org/wp-content/uploads/2019/session3/dynamic_many-light_sampling.pdf>; <https://fileadmin.cs.lth.se/graphics/research/papers/2019/dyn_manylight/>; <https://dqlin.xyz/pubs/2020-i3d-SLC/>; <https://doi.org/10.1145/3680528.3687647>
- **World-space light grids for ray hits (Doom TDA cascaded light grid, HDRP RT light cluster, Metro cluster grid)** (shipped; shipped in Doom: The Dark Ages, Doom Eternal RT, Unity HDRP, Metro Exodus EE; confidence high). Camera-centred cascades of world-space cells (Doom: 16^3 per cascade), each listing the lights whose volume overlaps it. Doom builds them in a GPU compute pass with coarse and fine OBB-vs-AABB culling and ordered bit arrays. Ray hits loop over the lights in their cell.
  Fixes: Lights ray hits outside the frustum, where screen tiles and froxels do not exist. Cost: Doom: about 0.2 ms on consoles, about 0.1 ms on PC (RTX 4080), up to 4k lights, about 30 MB with decals. Fails: Cost grows linearly with the lights per cell; far cascades hold more lights; no sampling, so it is an every-light loop.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no. Deterministic light loop; no noise. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Excellent.
  Needs: A GPU culling pass per frame.
  Sources: <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/SOUSA_SIGGRAPH_2025_Final.pdf>; <https://docs.unity3d.com/Packages/com.unity.render-pipelines.high-definition@17.2/manual/Ray-Tracing-Light-Cluster.html>; <https://www.4a-games.com.mt/4a-dna/in-depth-technical-dive-into-metro-exodus-pc-enhanced-edition>
- **Stochastic tile-based lighting / tiled reservoir sampling (HypeHype 2025; Tokuyoshi 2021)** (shipped; shipped in HypeHype; confidence medium). Per screen tile, stratified reservoirs of unique lights are built once and shared by all pixels in the tile. Pixels resample 1-4 of them. Shadows are evaluated per 2x2 quad in a separate pass.
  Fixes: Bounded lights per pixel at very low cost on weak GPUs. Cost: No ms extracted. Storage 32-128 KB of reservoirs and 0.5-2 MB of shadow terms at 1080p. Fails: Visible tile patterns (fixed by interleaving); quad-resolution shadow errors at silhouettes; bias from 8-bit shadow terms.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: yes. The talk relies on TAA to hide the quad-shadow silhouette errors and the dither noise. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Fits, but it is designed for raster pixel shaders.
  Needs: Per-tile render targets.
  Sources: <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/s2025_stb_lighting_v1.1_notes.pdf>; <https://yusuketokuyoshi.com/>
- **Stochastic light culling (Tokuyoshi & Harada 2016)** (paper; shipped in not shipped; confidence medium). Gives each light a finite range and uses Russian roulette beyond it: a light's influence is kept with some probability and reweighted, so hard cut-offs disappear without bias.
  Fixes: Removes the dark rings and bias from hard range windows and fixed fade distances, independent of scale. Cost: Not published here (paper targets VPL-scale counts). Fails: Adds noise far from lights; needs a denoiser.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: partly. Adds variance that must be accumulated. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Excellent (a change to the light loop).
  Needs: None beyond the light loop.
  Sources: <https://jcgt.org/published/0005/01/02/>
- **Representative-point area lights with energy normalisation (Karis 2013)** (shipped; shipped in Unreal Engine 4/5; confidence high). Specular from a sphere or tube light is evaluated at the single point on the light nearest the reflection ray. The GGX lobe's widening is compensated by a normalisation ratio (alpha/alpha')^2 for spheres, applied once in the one widened dimension for lines/tubes. Diffuse uses a point or closest-point approximation.
  Fixes: Cheap, noise-free sized-light highlights with roughly correct energy for bulbs and tube lights. Cost: A few ALU ops per light; no ms published. Fails: Approximate: energy error for long tubes at low roughness and at grazing angles; no shadowing; not exact for the diffuse part.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no. Deterministic. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Excellent.
  Needs: None.
  Sources: <https://cdn2.unrealengine.com/Resources/files/2013SiggraphPresentationsNotes-26915738.pdf>
- **Linearly Transformed Cosines (Heitz et al. 2016; linear lights Heitz & Hill 2017)** (shipped; shipped in Unreal Engine rect lights, Unity HDRP area lights; confidence high). GGX times cosine is approximated by a linearly transformed clamped cosine, fitted into small tables indexed by roughness and view angle. Its integral over a polygon, line, disk or sphere has a closed form, which gives the exact-shape unshadowed diffuse and specular from an area light.
  Fixes: Noise-free, energy-correct unshadowed area lighting. This is the analytic term a ratio estimator needs. Cost: Two texture fetches plus polygon clipping or integration per light; no ms published. Fails: The fitted-table error is small; no visibility; cost is per light, so evaluating it for every light breaks a fixed per-pixel budget.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no. Deterministic. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Excellent.
  Needs: LTC lookup tables.
  Sources: <https://eheitzresearch.wordpress.com/415-2/>; <https://learnopengl.com/Guest-Articles/2022/Area-Lights>
- **BRDF / projected-solid-angle importance sampling of linear and polygonal lights (Peters 2021)** (paper; shipped in not shipped; confidence medium). Samples points on a line or polygon light in proportion to projected solid angle or to the LTC-transformed BRDF, using a fast iterative inversion (2 iterations), and combines the two with MIS.
  Fixes: Low-variance, unbiased area-light shading with soft shadows from very few rays. For line lights, low variance outside penumbrae at 2 samples per pixel. Cost: Not published here; designed for real-time use. Fails: Noise remains in penumbrae; more ALU per sample than uniform area sampling.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: partly. Penumbra noise needs a denoiser. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Excellent: the sampled point feeds a rayQuery shadow ray.
  Needs: LTC tables.
  Sources: <https://momentsingraphics.de/HPG2021.html>
- **Ratio estimator: analytic unshadowed lighting x denoised stochastic shadow (Heitz, Hill, McGuire 2018)** (shipped; shipped in Unity HDRP (RT rect-light shadows, deferred Lit); confidence medium). Shadowed light = U_analytic x (sum of sampled shadowed contributions / sum of sampled unshadowed contributions). Only the ratio's numerator and denominator images carry noise; they are denoised, and the analytic LTC term keeps texture and normal-map detail sharp. HDRP's RT area shadows accumulate exactly these two sums.
  Fixes: Noise and blur appear only in shadowed regions, and the denoiser cannot smear shading detail. This is the design RT2-24 plans. Cost: Two accumulation channels plus one denoise per light or light group; analytic U costs O(lights) per pixel. No ms published. Fails: Undefined or unstable where the unshadowed sum is near zero. Biased once denoised (consistent only as the filter radius shrinks). Many lights either need per-light buffers or one combined ratio, which is wrong where different lights cast different shadows. The analytic sum over all lights conflicts with a fixed-cost sampler.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no. Its own shadow denoiser; no TAA dependency in the method. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Excellent.
  Needs: An analytic U per light (LTC or representative point), an SN/UN buffer, and a shadow denoiser.
  Sources: <https://eheitzresearch.wordpress.com/705-2/>; <https://research.nvidia.com/publication/2018-05_combining-analytic-direct-illumination-and-stochastic-shadows>; <https://github.com/Unity-Technologies/Graphics/blob/master/Packages/com.unity.render-pipelines.high-definition/Runtime/RenderPipeline/Raytracing/Shaders/Shadows/RaytracingShadow.raytrace>
- **Ray-traced shadows with a dedicated shadow denoiser (NRD SIGMA; AMD FidelityFX Shadow Denoiser)** (public_sdk; shipped in NRD in many RT titles, FidelityFX samples; confidence high). 1 ray per pixel per light, jittered over the light's extent with blue noise. SIGMA takes distance-to-occluder plus the light's angular size to estimate the penumbra, which sets the blur radius, and stabilises over time (optional). AMD packs 8x4 visibility bits, uses local moments and a disocclusion mask, then 3 a-trous passes.
  Fixes: Soft, contact-hardening area shadows with sharp contacts. The penumbra-driven radius stops blur from crossing hard edges, which is the T5 failure RT2-24 records. Cost: SIGMA_SHADOW 0.40 ms (0.45 ms with translucency) per run, RTX 4080, 1440p native. AMD: not published. Fails: Runs once per light, so it does not scale to many lights (NRD suggests per-light runs or a combined translucency-style pass). Blue-noise shimmer if the pattern animates too long. AMD's denoiser is single-light.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no. Both have their own temporal stage; SIGMA's history can be disabled. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Excellent: compute plus rayQuery.
  Needs: Hit distance per ray; the light's angular size; blue noise.
  Sources: <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD/blob/master/README.md>; <https://gpuopen.com/manuals/fidelityfx_sdk/techniques/denoiser/>; <https://gpuopen.com/fidelityfx-denoiser/>
- **Hybrid shadows (AMD FidelityFX): shadow maps classify, rays only where uncertain** (public_sdk; shipped in not shipped; confidence medium). A blocker search in the cascades marks pixels that are surely lit or surely shadowed. Rays (1 per pixel) are launched only for uncertain 8x4 tiles, with shortened intervals, then denoised.
  Fixes: Cuts sun-shadow ray counts while keeping RT contact hardness. Cost: Not published. Fails: Directional light only; needs a full shadow-map pipeline; misclassification leaks.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no. Uses the FidelityFX denoiser. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Fits, but needs raster shadow maps, which runs against RageV's RT-first direction.
  Needs: Cascaded shadow maps.
  Sources: <https://gpuopen.com/fidelityfx-hybrid-shadows/>; <https://gpuopen.com/manuals/fidelityfx_sdk/techniques/classifier/>
- **Virtual shadow maps + SMRT (UE5); ReSTIR-sampled shadow maps (Zhang et al. 2025)** (shipped; shipped in UE5 titles (Fortnite); confidence high). VSM: a sparse 16k virtual shadow map per light, with cached pages; SMRT marches several rays per pixel through the map for soft, contact-hardening shadows. The EG 2025 paper instead picks, with ReSTIR, which lights get full-resolution maps and gives the rest imperfect shadow maps.
  Fixes: A raster route to many shadowed lights. Cost: VSM: fixed per-light cost; SMRT 8 rays by default; no ms published in the docs. The EG 2025 timings were not retrieved. Fails: Cache invalidation from WPO/skinned geometry, projective aliasing, a fixed cost per local light, many-small-light cost still a work in progress.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: partly. SMRT noise is usually resolved by TSR. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Not applicable (raster). Relevant only to the raster fallback.
  Needs: Virtual texturing of shadow pages.
  Sources: <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/virtual-shadow-maps-in-unreal-engine>; <https://graphics.cs.utah.edu/research/projects/restir-shadow-maps/>
- **Emissive triangles as sampleable lights (and single-counting fixtures)** (public_sdk; shipped in RTXDI (Cyberpunk and AW2 via RTXDI; Cyberpunk used BRDF sampling for emissives); confidence medium). RTXDI's prepare-lights pass turns every emissive triangle into a light with its true area and average emissive radiance (from the texture), in the same buffer and PDF as the analytic lights. UE instead leaves glowing meshes to Lumen's final gather. Cyberpunk reaches them only with BRDF-sampled rays, blended by the roughness heuristic.
  Fixes: Glowing geometry lights its surroundings with NEE-quality noise instead of random hits. Cost: Part of RTXDI's light preparation; not published separately. Fails: Double counting when an analytic light and its lens mesh both carry the light (no engine documents a luminaire link publicly). Animated emissive textures need re-averaging. Huge triangle counts need presampling or a hierarchy.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: partly. Same as the sampler it feeds. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Excellent.
  Needs: Per-triangle emissive power; a mapping from lights to instances.
  Sources: <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/RTXDI/blob/main/Doc/Integration.md>; <https://intro-to-restir.cwyman.org/presentations/2023ReSTIR_Course_Cyberpunk_2077_Integration.pdf>; <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/lumen-global-illumination-and-reflections-in-unreal-engine?lang=en-US>
- **Fast-motion and firefly controls for stochastic direct light** (shipped; shipped in RTXDI titles, UE 5.8; confidence medium). RTXDI: history cap of 20, permutation sampling (randomised reprojection that decorrelates history), boiling filter 0.2 (drops reservoirs far above their neighbourhood), 8-neighbour disocclusion boost, and final-visibility reuse limited by age (4) and distance (16 px). MegaLights: re-shades samples every frame; guided lists read bilinearly and at random with a hidden-light floor; a variance clip in YCoCg; temporal max 12 frames; a sample-weight clamp of 20; spatial filter only where variance is high. NRD ReLAX/ReBLUR: fast-history clamp and anti-lag.
  Fixes: Speckle when swinging the camera fast, boiling, trails behind moving lights, and noise in newly uncovered areas. Cost: Included in the passes above. Fails: Clamps bias energy and brightness; a short history raises noise; permutations can shimmer.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: partly. In shipped titles the last step is TSR or DLSS RR. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Excellent.
  Needs: Motion vectors, depth and normal history.
  Sources: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/NVIDIA-RTX/RTXDI-Library/main/Source/ReSTIRDI.cpp>; <https://github.com/Far-Beyond-Pulsar/Helio/issues/48>; <https://www.docswell.com/s/EpicGamesJapan/Z27EW1-DayDays26-MegaLights>
- **Shader Execution Reordering and Opacity Micromaps (performance enablers)** (shipped; shipped in Alan Wake 2, Indiana Jones, Doom: The Dark Ages PT; confidence high). SER regroups threads by hit or material. In Vulkan (VK_EXT_ray_tracing_invocation_reorder) reorderThreadEXT is available only in ray generation shaders. OMM encodes sub-triangle opacity in the BLAS so alpha-tested geometry resolves without an any-hit shader, and it has an execution mode for ray-query traversal.
  Fixes: SER: divergence in hit shading. OMM: alpha-tested shadow rays without any-hit, which fits a ray-query-only engine. Cost: SER: AW2 16.8 to 10.2 ms; Indiana Jones -24% TraceMain (RTX 5080). OMM: Indiana Jones TraceMain -55% (7.90 to 3.58 ms at 1080p), SunTracing -14%. Fails: SER is unavailable to inline ray queries in fragment or compute shaders, so a ray-query-only design forfeits it. OMM needs baking per alpha texture; unknown-state micro-triangles still need a shader test.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no. Performance only. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: OMM: yes, via the ray-query execution mode. SER: no (raygen only).
  Needs: VK_EXT_opacity_micromap; RTX 40/50-class hardware for OMM acceleration.
  Sources: <https://www.khronos.org/blog/boosting-ray-tracing-performance-with-shader-execution-reordering-introducing-vk-ext-ray-tracing-invocation-reorder>; <https://docs.vulkan.org/refpages/latest/refpages/source/VK_EXT_opacity_micromap.html>; <https://github.com/KhronosGroup/Vulkan-Docs/issues/2810>; <https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/path-tracing-optimizations-in-indiana-jones-opacity-micromaps-and-compaction-of-dynamic-blass/>
- **DLSS Ray Reconstruction (neural denoise + upscale)** (shipped; shipped in Cyberpunk 2077, Alan Wake 2, Indiana Jones, Doom: The Dark Ages; confidence high). One network replaces the hand-written denoisers and the upscaler, taking raw noisy RT signals and guide buffers.
  Fixes: Noise from about 1-sample-per-pixel direct and indirect light in shipped PT titles. Cost: Not published per pass; Doom PT figures are DLSS 4 frame-generation totals only. Fails: Vendor-locked; it is an upscaler and AA stage, which rule 3 forbids depending on.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: yes. It is the temporal upscaler. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Signal-agnostic, but it violates rule 3 as a dependency.
  Needs: NVIDIA RTX.
  Sources: <https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/geforce/news/doom-the-dark-ages-path-tracing-dlss-ray-reconstruction-update/>

### A.3 The frontier (published, not yet shipped)

- **Neural Visibility Cache for real-time light sampling** (Bokšanský & Meister 2025 (arXiv 2506.05930), 2025; evidence moderate). An online-trained hash-grid MLP predicts light visibility and feeds the weighted reservoir, giving lower error than screen-space ReSTIR at a similar cost, unbiased.
  Evidence: RX 7900 XT, 1920x1080. Kitchen, 32 lights: FLIP 0.298 vs ReSTIR 0.361, total 3.95 vs 3.61 ms. Sponza, 32 lights: FLIP 0.103 vs 0.195, 4.69 vs 3.95 ms. Clustered version: Subway, 59k lights, 7.37 ms; ZeroDay, 4.8k lights, 6.18 ms. About 16 frames to learn from scratch; error rises on teleports or abrupt changes. Could beat: ReSTIR DI and ReGIR candidate quality where many lights sit behind walls. Cost: About +10-20% time vs ReSTIR at 32 lights in the paper; needs fused-MLP inference on the GPU. Risk: Training lag under fast motion (the owner's swing test); only 32 lights per network unless clustered; a large engineering lift.
  Sources: <https://arxiv.org/abs/2506.05930>; <https://arxiv.org/html/2506.05930>
- **Neural Importance Sampling of Many Lights** (Figueiredo et al. 2025, 2025; evidence weak). A learned per-region distribution over light-tree clusters (residual on top of the tree's own distribution, trained online by a KL gradient) beats ATS, SLCRT, ReSTIR and VARL at equal time and equal samples.
  Evidence: SIGGRAPH 2025. The comparisons are at progressive sample counts up to 128 spp. No real-time ms found. Could beat: Light-BVH or ReGIR initial candidates. Cost: Not published for real-time budgets. Risk: An offline/progressive setting; training cost per frame unknown.
  Sources: <https://arxiv.org/abs/2505.11729>; <https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3721238.3730754>
- **Hierarchical light sampling with accurate SG lighting** (Tokuyoshi, Ikeda, Kulkarni, Harada, SIGGRAPH Asia 2024, 2024; evidence moderate). Light-tree node importance from accurate spherical-Gaussian lighting improves glossy many-light sampling.
  Evidence: 1.4x faster than Tokuyoshi 2022 on an RX 7900 XTX (paper); code released. Could beat: Power- or distance-only candidate scores (RTXDI presampling, ReGIR) on glossy surfaces such as the wet floor and chrome. Cost: Tree build plus SG evaluation per node visited; absolute ms not captured. Risk: Still ignores visibility.
  Sources: <https://doi.org/10.1145/3680528.3687647>; <https://yusuketokuyoshi.com/>; <https://github.com/yusuketokuyoshi/VSGL>
- **Decorrelating ReSTIR samplers via MCMC mutations** (Sawhney et al. 2024, 2024; evidence moderate). One MCMC mutation per reservoir per frame, using the same target function, removes correlation blotches and duplicate-sample impoverishment without bias.
  Evidence: TOG 2024 (NVIDIA): visual and error comparisons on glossy scenes with hard-to-sample light. Per-pass ms not captured. Could beat: RTXDI's permutation sampling and boiling filter as the anti-correlation mechanism. Cost: One extra target evaluation and, for visibility, possibly one ray per reservoir. Risk: Extra shadow rays; the benefit is mostly on glossy surfaces.
  Sources: <https://arxiv.org/abs/2211.00166>; <https://research.nvidia.com/labs/rtr/publication/sawhney2022decorrelating/>
- **Novel MIS weight for spatiotemporal resampling** (Pan et al. 2024, 2024; evidence weak). A resampling MIS weight that converges as the share of reused (non-canonical) samples grows. It cuts the energy loss of biased ReSTIR DI and the artefacts from heavy temporal reuse, at no extra cost.
  Evidence: Eurographics 2024 paper; no independent reproduction found. Could beat: RTXDI's Basic/Pairwise weights in terms of darkening. Cost: No extra overhead (claimed). Risk: Unverified outside the paper.
  Sources: <https://diglib.eg.org/items/df9d727e-13a1-4d48-9275-57da7fb87f7f>
- **Reservoir splatting and multi-layer reservoir splatting** (Liu, Lin, Kettunen, Wyman, Ramamoorthi 2025; Hong et al. 2026, 2025; evidence weak). Forward-reprojects last frame's primary hits into this frame (scatter instead of gather), so temporal reuse survives motion exactly. The 2026 multi-layer version keeps several depth layers so uncovered pixels still have history.
  Evidence: SIGGRAPH 2025 (code on Falcor 8) and SIGGRAPH 2026. Abstracts claim much less disocclusion noise for a small incremental cost; no ms captured. Demonstrated on ReSTIR PT and motion blur. Could beat: Gather-based temporal reuse (RTXDI) during the owner-speed swing and the dolly. Cost: Atomic splat pass plus extra layers; not published in what was read. Risk: Designed for path reuse; the benefit for DI alone is unmeasured.
  Sources: <https://github.com/Jebbly/Reservoir-Splatting>; <https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3721238.3730646>; <https://research.nvidia.com/labs/rtr/publication/hong2026multilayer/>
- **Area ReSTIR (anti-aliasing and depth of field inside the reservoir; not area lights)** (Zhang, Lin, Kettunen, Yuksel, Wyman 2024, 2024; evidence moderate). Reservoirs integrate over each pixel's film and lens area with sub-pixel-tracking temporal reuse, so anti-aliasing comes from the lighting estimator itself rather than from TAA.
  Evidence: SIGGRAPH 2024; code for the direct-lighting part on Falcor 7.0. Could beat: The need for TAA to resolve sub-pixel lighting detail, which bears on rule 3. Cost: Higher than pinhole ReSTIR; not captured. Risk: Complexity; ms unknown.
  Sources: <https://graphics.cs.utah.edu/research/projects/area-restir/>; <https://github.com/guiqi134/Area-ReSTIR/blob/master/README.md>
- **RIS combined with projected-solid-angle (LTC) sampling for many area lights** (Shah, KT, Narayanan 2023, 2023; evidence weak). Reformulating RIS so its candidates are sampled by projected solid angle (LTC) gives better convergence at equal time with thousands of area lights.
  Evidence: SIGGRAPH Asia 2023 Technical Communications, Vulkan code. MAPE on Zero-Day (10k), Bistro Interior (2k), Bistro Exterior (30k); no ms captured; no spatiotemporal reuse. Could beat: Uniform point-on-light candidates for the tubes in ReSTIR. Cost: Not captured. Risk: Unclear gain once spatiotemporal reuse is added.
  Sources: <http://ishaanshah.xyz/risltc/>; <https://github.com/ishaanshah/risltc>
- **Efficient Visibility Reuse for Real-time ReSTIR** (Tokuyoshi 2024, 2024; evidence weak). A cheaper way to reuse visibility in ReSTIR (AMD). Content not read; the PDF was too large to fetch.
  Evidence: SIGGRAPH 2024 talk; details not retrieved. Could beat: RTXDI's final-visibility reuse and RayTraced bias correction. Cost: Unknown. Risk: Unknown.
  Sources: <https://gpuopen.com/download/Efficient_Visibility_Reuse_for_Real-time_ReSTIR.pdf>; <https://doi.org/10.1145/3641233.3664320>

### A.4 The survey's recommended baseline and candidates

The survey's view before the comparison looked at RageV's code. The roadmap's items took these as their starting baselines where the comparison and verification agreed.

- **Baseline: A fixed per-pixel budget of stochastic light samples from ONE persistent GPU light buffer that every pass shares. It holds analytic lights plus emissive triangles, and each kept sample gets exactly one shadow ray.** Why: Both production systems (RTXDI in CP2077/AW2 and UE 5.8 MegaLights) make the cost independent of light count this way. RageV's per-pixel scoring of every light in the cell (22 per pixel in the garage, 67-190 on the bridge) is the pre-2020 shape. Shipped by: Unreal Engine 5.8 MegaLights, Cyberpunk 2077 RT Overdrive (RTXDI), Alan Wake 2 (RTXDI). Measured by: DirectTrace ms at 1k/5k/10k lights in the many-light scene; the curve must stay flat within the palindrome spread. Converged per-pixel diff against the every-light reference on the garage and bridge, showing no structure beyond grain.
- **Baseline: O(1)-per-candidate generation: power-proportional presampled tiles for primary surfaces (RTXDI local light PDF plus RIS tiles). For ray hits in reflections, GI, water and glass: a GPU-built, camera-centred world structure (ReGIR cells, or a cascaded light grid as in Doom TDA, whose cell size grows with distance), never a fixed world box.** Why: This is RTXDI's shipped split (screen-space ReSTIR for primary, ReGIR for secondary), and Doom's cascades are the shipped general form for ray hits. It removes RageV's CPU-sorted fixed 8x4x32 grid, which is tuned to one scene. Shipped by: RTXDI, Doom: The Dark Ages, Unity HDRP (RT light cluster). Measured by: Build ms on the GPU at 10k lights (Doom: about 0.1 ms on an RTX 4080). Noise at ray hits vs every-light shading; the bridge at km scale with no dark rings.
- **Baseline: Temporal and spatial reuse of light SAMPLES, not radiance, re-shaded at the current view: ReSTIR DI with a history cap of about 20, permutation sampling, a boiling filter, a disocclusion boost, and pairwise MIS. Neighbours are tested for depth, normal and material similarity.** Why: This is what the shipped path-traced titles use. Re-shading reused samples keeps the specular highlight tied to the current view, which is the root of RageV's four-frame specular smear (PairMemory 4). Shipped by: Cyberpunk 2077 RT Overdrive, Alan Wake 2. Measured by: Owner-speed swing speckle count and white speckles; emitter_lag.py (floor beside the moving light below 19.5%); the dolly's moving error; the pole and tube grain scores. Each is measured against the 16-ray reference.
- **Baseline: Area lights shaded by their true shape in every pass: an analytic unshadowed term (representative point with energy normalisation per Karis for spheres and tubes, or LTC for lines and rectangles), times visibility from a ray aimed at a sampled point ON the light, which gives soft shadows. The same formula is used at ray hits, never a point there.** Why: UE (representative point, LTC rect lights), HDRP (LTC) and MegaLights (area lights with soft RT shadows) all ship it. RageV's ray hits currently treat a 3.07 m tube as a point with a hard shadow. Shipped by: Unreal Engine 5, Unity HDRP, UE MegaLights. Measured by: A single tube over a glossy plane at 3 roughnesses vs brute-force tube sampling (per-pixel diff images). truth_test.py region scores on the garage chrome and car.
- **Baseline: A dedicated direct-light denoiser with its own history: demodulated diffuse and specular, variance-clipped temporal history with a short cap (MegaLights: at most 12 frames, 5x5 YCoCg clip), and a spatial pass only where variance is high, with a penumbra-aware radius (SIGMA-style: occluder distance and light size). It must converge with --aa=none.** Why: Every shipped system has this stage. Rule 3 forbids the TSR/DLSS RR crutch that UE and the path-traced games still lean on, so RageV must meet this bar itself. Shipped by: UE 5.8 MegaLights, NRD ReLAX/SIGMA. Measured by: Frame-to-frame variance of the direct buffer with --aa=none; the T5 shadow-edge test re-baselined on the current build; A,B,B,A cost.
- **Baseline: Ray-traced shadows as the primary shadow path for every light. Alpha-tested occluders are resolved in ray queries via OMM, not any-hit. Raster VSM or hybrid shadows only as the raster fallback.** Why: MegaLights defaults to RT visibility and CP2077/AW2 trace every light's shadow. OMM works with ray queries, which matches RageV having no any-hit stage. Shipped by: UE MegaLights, Cyberpunk 2077, Indiana Jones (OMM). Measured by: Shadow-ray ms with and without OMM on cutout foliage or cloth; correctness vs an alpha-tested reference.
- **Candidate: A ratio-estimator split on top of the sampled light: an exact analytic unshadowed term (LTC or representative point) times a denoised shadowed/unshadowed ratio, restricted to the few lights the sampler chose, so it stays fixed-cost.** Why better: Noise and blur appear only in shadows, while texture, normal-map and highlight detail stay sharp. It also directly answers RT2-24's trail and hard-edge problems. MegaLights instead denoises shaded light. Evidence: HDRP ships it for RT rect-light shadows; Heitz 2018 paper. No shipped many-light form found. Risk: The analytic sum over all lights is O(N) and fights the fixed budget. Ratio instability at low unshadowed light. Mixing lights with different shadows into one ratio. Measured by: Against the ReSTIR/MegaLights-style baseline: emitter_lag.py, the T5 moving shadow-edge error, per-pixel diff vs the every-light reference, and the cost with 22 and 190 candidate lights per pixel. Report bias where the unshadowed sum is near zero.
- **Candidate: Visibility-guided candidates inside ReSTIR: MegaLights' per-tile visible-light list (random bilinear lookup, with a hidden-light floor of about 20%) used as the initial candidate distribution for ReSTIR.** Why better: ReSTIR's candidates ignore walls. MegaLights shows that guiding by last frame's visibility is cheap (visible-list pass 0.05 ms on PS5 at 1080p) and robust. Combining the two could cut the noise that RT2-12's risk note warns about for rooms whose light ranges cross walls. Evidence: MegaLights talk and docs; no paper combining them was found. Risk: Guiding lag when lights move from hidden to visible; tile-border seams. Measured by: Noise and variance in a many-light scene with rooms whose ranges cross walls, vs plain RTXDI candidates at equal ms. A cold-start test (teleport) for lag.
- **Candidate: Light BVH (two-level, with orientation cones) as the candidate source instead of ReGIR for the kilometre-scale bridge.** Why better: Scale-free, with no cell size to tune (rule 2), and distance- and orientation-aware at any point. Evidence: Moreau 2019; Conty-Estevez & Kulla 2018; RTXDI's own paper beats a light BVH at equal error only when reuse is added. Risk: Traversal divergence; refit cost with many moving lights; still visibility-blind. Measured by: RT2-12's A/B: 1k/5k/10k lights, sampling plus build ms, noise vs every-light reference on Headland/Glitter/garage. Moreau's reference: 2.0-2.6 ms sampling, 0.2-0.9 ms update on an RTX 2080 Ti.
- **Candidate: LTC-proportional or projected-solid-angle point sampling on tubes (Peters 2021) for the shadow-ray target.** Why better: Lower variance per shadow ray for the 3.07 m tubes than uniform points on the segment, so fewer rays are needed for smooth penumbrae. Evidence: Peters HPG 2021 (low variance outside penumbrae at 2 spp). Risk: ALU cost per sample; the gain inside penumbrae is limited. Measured by: Tube-over-glossy-plane penumbra variance at equal rays; the garage tube grain score.
- **Candidate: MCMC mutation decorrelation (Sawhney 2024) and the Pan 2024 MIS weight as drop-in upgrades to the reuse arm.** Why better: Fewer correlation blotches and less darkening than the permutation plus boiling-filter heuristics. Evidence: Papers only. Risk: Extra rays per mutation; unverified in production. Measured by: The swing speckle count and parked-floor level difference vs the 16-ray reference; A,B,B,A cost.
- **Candidate: Neural visibility cache feeding the reservoir.** Why better: 20-45% lower FLIP than ReSTIR at similar ms in the paper. Evidence: arXiv 2025, one GPU (RX 7900 XT, 1080p). Risk: Training lag under fast motion; engineering size; clustering needed beyond 32 lights. Measured by: The same metrics as the baseline, plus teleport and swing recovery (16-frame training lag).

### A.5 RageV against them, topic by topic

- **Per-pixel candidate cost with many lights.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: DirectTrace walks every light whose range box touches the pixel's cluster cell: a 16-byte cull test, ScoreDirect (reads the 96-byte GpuLight) and K PCG draws per candidate, so selection cost is linear in overlapping lights (22/px garage, 67-190 bridge). Trace-only hit passes walk every light in the scene. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/direct_trace.rvshader:1186-1208`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/direct_trace.rvshader:507-593`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:2808`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:2937-2946`
  Top engines: MegaLights evaluates every light on a per-tile visible-light list plus only a stochastic subset (every N-th) of the froxel cell, and more of the cell on a history miss; its talk says sampling (0.70 ms) still costs more than shading because it scales with light count. RTXDI draws a fixed number of candidates from power-presampled tiles or ReGIR cells. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/megalights_2025.txt:514-541`; `build/rt_research/sources/megalights_2025.txt:1589-1596`; <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/RTXDI/blob/main/Doc/Integration.md>
  Why: Both shipped systems cap per-pixel candidate work; RageV's grows with every overlapping light and is unbounded at hits.
- **Selection score (target function).** Comparison: equal. Verified: equal. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: Linear luminance of unshadowed diffuse plus a GGX peak, scale-invariant (multiplying every light by a constant changes nothing). For sized and tube lights, however, the score uses the unwidened lobe with no radius or length renormalisation while DirectTerm shades with both (the comment puts the length error at 5-19x on the wet floor), so tubes are over-picked on glossy surfaces (audit 2: live, first to fix). Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/direct_trace.rvshader:586-591`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/direct_trace.rvshader:396-414`
  Top engines: MegaLights weights each light by Log2(Luminance(BRDF))+1, a perceptual weighting chosen partly to stop strong occluded lights dominating. RTXDI uses the unshadowed BRDF x light target. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/megalights_2025.txt:439-467`
  Why: RageV's linear, term-shaped score is the variance-optimal shape and obeys rule 2 (the log form depends on absolute brightness). But the tube mismatch is a live noise defect, and MegaLights needs its log weighting only because it has guiding to lean on for visibility; RageV has neither guiding nor a matched score.
- **Samples, rays per pixel, duplicate rays.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: K=8 reservoirs at Quality, full resolution; one ray per distinct lamp, duplicates share a ray: about 3.5 shadow rays per pixel in the garage (5.0 M at 1600x900, RT-1). Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/direct_trace.rvshader:1265-1299`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/RenderSettings.h:239`, `docs/RT-SERIES-2-FINDINGS.md:831`
  Top engines: MegaLights: about 1 spp (4 picks per 2x2 block, sampling at half resolution, stochastic bilinear upsample), skips duplicate rays and rays whose penumbra is below the pixel footprint. The talk admits the downsampled sampling does not converge to the full-resolution result and softens shadows. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/megalights_2025.txt:555-588`; `build/rt_research/sources/megalights_2025.txt:689-713`
  Why: Same deduplication idea, but MegaLights gets usable quality from about a third of RageV's rays, thanks to guiding. Its downsampling carries a convergence cost that RageV's gates would see.
- **Visibility guiding of light picks.** Comparison: missing. Verified: missing. Confidence: high.
  RageV: None. The score has no visibility term, so lamps behind walls receive picks and rays. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/direct_trace.rvshader:507-593`
  Top engines: MegaLights: a sorted visible-light list per 8x8 tile built from last frame's unoccluded samples, read after reprojection by a stochastic bilinear pick among 4 tiles. Hidden lights get a separate reservoir clamped to 20% of the weight sum (relaxed when visible lights are dim, 50% on reprojection failure). Large area lights also carry a 2x2 bitmask of their visible parts. The list build costs 0.05 ms on PS5. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/megalights_2025.txt:290-409`; `build/rt_research/sources/megalights_2025.txt:635-667`; `build/rt_research/sources/megalights_2025.txt:1537-1557`
  Why: This is the mechanism that lets MegaLights cap candidates without losing the important lights, and it is exactly RT2-12's recorded risk (rooms whose ranges cross walls).
- **Random numbers for picks and source points.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: Lamp picks use white-noise PCG hashes, one per candidate per reservoir. Soft-shadow points use interleaved gradient noise plus per-light R2, which is good. Every draw is frozen unless TAA's jitter is non-zero. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/direct_trace.rvshader:1177-1201`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/ray_shadow_trace.glsl:24-26`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/ray_shadow_trace.glsl:348-362`
  Top engines: MegaLights: spatio-temporal blue noise, with sample warping and dithered sampling so that one blue-noise value drives all N picks without losing its properties. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/megalights_2025.txt:439-513`
  Why: White-noise picks read as salt-and-pepper noise, which RageV itself recorded for shadow points. And the freeze without jitter is a TAA dependency by construction (RT2-7).
- **Shading form: analytic unshadowed light x sampled visibility.** Comparison: equal. Verified: equal. Confidence: high.
  RageV: DirectTerm evaluates the analytic unshadowed diffuse and representative-point specular for each kept lamp, multiplied by the 0/1 answer of one ray to a sampled point on the source. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/direct_trace.rvshader:329-473`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/direct_trace.rvshader:489-500`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/direct_trace.rvshader:1276-1297`
  Top engines: MegaLights shades 'pre-integrated irradiance x IES x light function x sample weight', explicitly separating shadowing from shading (Heitz 2018 cited as the known approximation) for lower noise and parity with non-stochastic lights. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/megalights_2025.txt:733-763`
  Why: The same estimator shape. Phase 1 implied MegaLights evaluates the light at the sampled point; the primary source shows it does not.
- **Area-light model (sphere, tube, rectangle, disk, sun).** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: Only spheres and tubes. Diffuse, cone and range come from the light's centre. Tube specular uses a representative point with radius renormalisation and RT-24's length renormalisation. One 0/1 ray scales the whole term, highlight included, so a partial occluder re-rolls a fully visible highlight. No rectangle or disk types; the sun is hard. At hits every lamp is a point. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/direct_trace.rvshader:346-360`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/direct_trace.rvshader:371-414`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/ray_shadow_trace.glsl:343-346`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/Light.h:56-61`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:2975-3096`
  Top engines: UE uses a representative point with Karis energy normalisation for spheres and tubes, and LTC for rectangles. HDRP uses LTC. MegaLights adds rect and textured lights, IES profiles, light functions and rect barn-door culling, with soft directional light. Sources: <https://cdn2.unrealengine.com/Resources/files/2013SiggraphPresentationsNotes-26915738.pdf>; `build/rt_research/sources/megalights_2025.txt:1235-1323`
  Why: Shipped engines shade areas as areas in every path; RageV has a centre-based diffuse, no rectangles or soft sun, and points at hits.
- **Point on the source for the shadow ray.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: Uniform over a disc perpendicular to L (not solid-angle sampling of the sphere) and uniform along the tube's length, shifted by IGN + R2. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/ray_shadow_trace.glsl:340-384`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/ray_shadow_trace.glsl:392-404`
  Top engines: MegaLights warps 2D STBN into each light's area-preserving sampling function and down-weights parts marked hidden in the per-tile 2x2 bitmask. Peters 2021 samples lines and polygons by projected solid angle (paper). Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/megalights_2025.txt:668-688`; <https://momentsingraphics.de/HPG2021.html>
  Why: Visibility-aware point sampling and blue-noise warping; RageV's uniform points waste rays on hidden and irrelevant parts of long tubes.
- **Directional light.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: Every directional light is shaded at every pixel outside K, with a hard ray to 1e4 m. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/direct_trace.rvshader:854-868`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/ray_shadow_trace.glsl:343-346`
