  Top engines: MegaLights puts the directional light in the sampler, capped at 50% of the local-light weight (relaxed when local lights are dim), with VSM as a fallback used mostly for the sun. A dedicated RT sun pass is the 'common solution' the talk names. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/megalights_2025.txt:589-634`; `build/rt_research/sources/megalights_2025.txt:1165-1209`
  Why: A dedicated pass is a legitimate choice (equal), but RageV's sun has no angular size, so shadows are knife-hard at any distance.
- **Lighting at ray hits (reflections, GI, water, glass).** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: Each lamp is a point, Lambert-shaded, with a hard ray to its centre. RT-11 keeps one light per hit by weighted reservoir over the walked list. In the trace-only passes the cluster and world-grid lookups are compiled out, so every hit walks every light. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:2808-2850`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:2851-2941`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:3098-3130`
  Top engines: RTXDI gives secondary hits a fixed number of candidates from ReGIR cells. Doom: The Dark Ages uses a camera-centred cascaded world light grid (about 0.1 ms on an RTX 4080). HDRP and Metro use world light clusters. Sources: <https://boksajak.github.io/files/RTG2_ReGIRGridBasedReservoirs.pdf>; <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/SOUSA_SIGGRAPH_2025_Final.pdf>
  Why: Shipped engines bound hit-lighting cost by locality and treat sized lights as sized; RageV's hits are O(all lights) and point-lit.
- **Light grid build.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: The 16x9x24 view cluster grid and the fixed 8x4x32 world grid (shaped for the bridge) are both rebuilt with a CPU sort in every BeginScene, that is, for each view every frame. Code: `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/LightGrid.h:155-161`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/Renderer3D.cpp:4297`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/Renderer3D.cpp:4323`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/LightGrid.cpp:195-215`
  Top engines: MegaLights: a two-pass GPU build (a coarse grid, one thread group per cell, then the main froxel grid), packed lists, HZB-culled occluded cells; 0.6 ms down to under 0.2 ms for thousands of lights. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/megalights_2025.txt:1253-1294`
  Why: GPU-built and general; RageV's world grid is tuned to one scene (rule 2) and the CPU cost grows with lights x views.
- **Emissive geometry as lights.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: Reflections and GI aim next-event rays at every listed emitter (RT-24 removed the 16 cap). Each emitter is its mesh's bounding-box rectangle, so merged meshes radiate over empty space. Reflections pick by a linear score walk, GI uniformly. Membership needs strength > 1. DirectTrace never samples emitters. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_trace.rvshader:398-436`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/rtgi_trace.rvshader:383-386`, `RageV/src/RageV/Scene/Scene.cpp:2224-2240`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/Renderer3D.cpp:3114-3135`
  Top engines: RTXDI turns every emissive triangle into a light in the same buffer and PDF as the analytic lights. UE leaves glowing meshes to Lumen, and the MegaLights talk calls that workaround quality-limited because BRDF sampling misses small emitters. Cyberpunk reaches emissives only through BRDF rays. Sources: <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/RTXDI/blob/main/Doc/Integration.md>; `build/rt_research/sources/megalights_2025.txt:116-139`
  Why: Better than UE's Lumen-only approach, worse than RTXDI's triangle lights: bounding rectangles, linear picks, and no direct-pass sampling.
- **Textured emitters (importance within an emissive map).** Comparison: not comparable. Verified: not comparable. Confidence: low.
  RageV: A per-emitter luminance CDF over the emissive map plus an affine uv map, searched in 12 binary steps. It engages only on flat, untransformed planes. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_trace.rvshader:447-475`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/Renderer3D.cpp:3156-3183`, `docs/TEXEL-EMITTERS.md`
  Top engines: RTXDI averages emission per triangle, with no importance inside a triangle. MegaLights supports textured rect lights as analytic lights. Sources: <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/RTXDI/blob/main/Doc/Integration.md>; `build/rt_research/sources/megalights_2025.txt:1240-1252`
  Why: RageV's texel tables are finer than anything shipped but work on a narrow class of meshes; no engine ships the same feature.
- **Counting a fixture once (analytic light plus glowing lens).** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: low.
  RageV: A paired fixture counts twice. On glossy surfaces the analytic highlight adds to the traced lens image, because the lobe ray keeps the struck lens's glow and weighs it only against the aimed-emitter sample, never against DirectTrace's highlight. On diffuse surfaces the live direct light adds to the lens emission stored in the bake. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/direct_trace.rvshader:371-414`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_trace.rvshader:568-589`, `docs/RT-SERIES-2-FINDINGS.md:864-885`
  Top engines: No engine documents a luminaire link. The MegaLights talk recommends shaping lights with light functions and skipping fixture geometry with a per-light ray end bias; artists had to tag fixtures in the BVH. Cyberpunk blends ReSTIR DI and BRDF sampling by roughness squared. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/megalights_2025.txt:1642-1659`; <https://intro-to-restir.cwyman.org/presentations/2023ReSTIR_Course_Cyberpunk_2077_Integration.pdf>
  Why: Shipped titles at least avoid the double count, by an authoring rule or a heuristic blend; RageV counts it twice. Nobody ships an exact rule.
- **History invalidation (detecting real change).** Comparison: better (claimed). Verified: A-OB1, equal: RTXDI's SDK ships the same gradient and Quake II RTX shipped A-SVGF. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: Measured change re-lights one pixel per 3x3 block with last frame's picks and rays; any difference is real change and shortens that pixel's memory. It is an exact temporal gradient. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/direct_trace.rvshader:1055-1130`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/direct_trace.rvshader:1398-1419`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_accumulate.rvshader:1795-1801`
  Top engines: MegaLights clips history by a 5x5 YCoCg variance box and cuts history weight by the history's distance from the box. RTXDI and NRD rely on history caps and anti-lag heuristics. A-SVGF gradients (Quake II RTX) are the research analogue of RageV's approach. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/megalights_2025.txt:794-860`
  Why: An exact change test instead of a statistical guess. It is not sufficient alone: RT-22's 19.5% trail survives it.
- **Neighbourhood clamp and demodulation.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: A 3x3 RGB box of mean +/- 3 spreads (BoundWidth 3). Diffuse is stored without albedo; specular is stored complete, so Fresnel and roughness texture sit inside the history. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_accumulate.rvshader:443-463`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_accumulate.rvshader:1809-1825`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/direct_trace.rvshader:301-305`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/Renderer3D.h:350`
  Top engines: MegaLights: 5x5 variance clipping in YCoCg, packed in groupshared memory; albedo and EnvBRDF factored out; pre-exposed R11G11B10 lighting with stochastic quantisation. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/megalights_2025.txt:764-860`
  Why: A larger, colour-decorrelated clip, and specular demodulation keeps material detail out of the filter.
- **Spatial filter; young and disoccluded pixels.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: No spatial filter (YoungRadius 0); young texels show the raw K-sample estimate, and a code comment says TAA carries the rest. A blur stopped only at G-buffer edges was measured to smear hard shadow edges (T5), so it was switched off. Code: `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/Renderer3D.cpp:7402-7419`
  Top engines: MegaLights runs one sparse, per-pixel-rotated kernel with SVGF edge stops, only where relative variance is high; for 4 frames after disocclusion it takes more samples and accumulates in tonemapped space. It relies on TSR to fill the sparse kernel's holes. NRD SIGMA sizes shadow blur from occluder distance and light size. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/megalights_2025.txt:861-901`; <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD/blob/master/README.md>
  Why: Both shipped filters handle young pixels spatially. RageV has nothing there, and its only attempt lacked a penumbra-sized radius.
- **View-dependent specular in the history.** Comparison: equal. Verified: equal. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: The specular half is stored as shaded radiance, reprojected by the surface, with a 4-frame memory. It smears on the chrome poles and wet floor at speed and needs TAA to converge. Code: `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/Renderer3D.cpp:7412-7414`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_accumulate.rvshader:1809-1860`
  Top engines: MegaLights also reprojects shaded specular and admits highlight ghosting is hard to reproject. Its 'shading confidence' skips denoising where 80% of the pixel's energy was sampled and passes the noise to TSR. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/megalights_2025.txt:946-968`
  Why: Both lean on the final temporal pass for the highlight; neither meets rule 3.
- **Sample reuse (ReSTIR).** Comparison: equal. Verified: equal. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: No reuse for the primary direct light (RT-10 measured its stages further from the truth; section 7, item 5). RT-24's reflection lamp pool is spatial GRIS reuse: neighbours' lamp points are re-weighted from the receiver with pairwise MIS (the canonical weight is (1/k) sum (p_c/k)/(p_j+p_c/k)), and one shadow ray is traced for the kept point. There are no similarity heuristics, and the result is clamped at 64 per channel. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_resolve.rvshader:255-378`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_resolve.rvshader:456`, `docs/RT-SERIES-2.md:2045`
  Top engines: RTXDI ships spatiotemporal reuse (history 20, pairwise MIS, boiling filter) in CP2077 and AW2. MegaLights rejects reuse for its constant cost and for the correlation that hurts denoisers, but ships a reweighted neighbour-ray reuse as a scale-down option. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/megalights_2025.txt:190-289`; `build/rt_research/sources/megalights_2025.txt:714-732`; <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/NVIDIA-RTX/RTXDI-Library/main/Source/ReSTIRDI.cpp>
  Why: RageV's pool is the same unbiased math as RTXDI's Pairwise mode, and its no-temporal-reuse stance matches MegaLights. The 64 clamp is an absolute-brightness bias (rule 2; area B).
- **Alpha-tested occluders in shadow rays.** Comparison: equal. Verified: equal. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: An inline test inside the rayQueryProceed loop, only for instances flagged masked: one fetch of base alpha at mip 0. Every other instance stays opaque and costs nothing. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/ray_shadow_trace.glsl:97-203`
  Top engines: MegaLights traces inline first, then retraces masked hits with any-hit evaluation (its materials are graphs), or falls back to VSM for foliage. Indiana Jones uses OMMs (-55% TraceMain). Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/megalights_2025.txt:1138-1209`; <https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/path-tracing-optimizations-in-indiana-jones-opacity-micromaps-and-compaction-of-dynamic-blass/>
  Why: RageV has no material graphs, so the 'fixed-function' test MegaLights calls ideal fits it. Neither uses OMM (RT2-38, area E).
- **Ray-tracing representation, self-shadowing, contact detail.** Comparison: not comparable. Verified: not comparable. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: Shadow rays trace the same meshes the raster draws, offset 2-10 mm along the normal-mapped normal. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/ray_shadow_trace.glsl:232-256`
  Top engines: MegaLights traces proxy meshes near the camera (150 m) and a merged far-field TLAS, and needs HZB screen traces first to avoid proxy self-shadowing and to recover contact detail. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/megalights_2025.txt:970-1137`
  Why: There is no proxy mismatch at RageV's current scale; Nanite-scale content forces UE's design. RT2-38 and RT2-41 own the far-field question.
- **Range, cutoffs and far lights.** Comparison: equal. Verified: equal. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: Windowed inverse-square falloff with authored range (UE's shape). The Hybrid reject distance is packed as 13-bit eighths of a metre (caps about 1 km). WR-17 clamps ranges to 300 m at Performance, and the lit loop fades shadow rays over 300-600 m at Quality. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/direct_trace.rvshader:346-359`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/Renderer3D.cpp:104-122`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/Renderer3D.cpp:4007-4025`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/RenderSettings.h:239-241`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:1329-1360`
  Top engines: MegaLights keeps range, cone angles and barn doors as content optimisations and drops samples below an exposure-relative threshold. Tokuyoshi 2016 stochastic light culling (paper). Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/megalights_2025.txt:514-554`; `build/rt_research/sources/megalights_2025.txt:1670-1686`
  Why: The falloff is the industry's. The metre-based caps and fades are rule-2 defects of RageV, but only Performance's cutoff touches the RT direct pass.
- **Baked direct light with moving-object loss rays.** Comparison: not comparable. Verified: not comparable. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: Full and Hybrid lamps' far direct light is baked. Static pixels trace one extra moving-only ray per flagged lamp, outside K; the flag costs lights x non-static meshes on the CPU per view. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/direct_trace.rvshader:1304-1396`, `RageV/src/RageV/Scene/Scene.cpp:3052-3102`
  Top engines: No RT-first engine bakes direct light in its RT mode; MegaLights replaces all direct shadowing and shading. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/megalights_2025.txt:1539-1545`
  Why: A RageV-specific consequence of the bake (D4); nothing shipped to compare against.
- **Reference arm and instruments.** Comparison: better (claimed). Verified: A-OB2, not comparable: it checks light selection, not the true picture; Unreal keeps a non-stochastic path and a path tracer. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: An every-light arm (K=0) that matches the old loop to 0.034 levels, --direct-signal=off, ray and lights-per-pixel counters, and the exact re-light. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/direct_trace.rvshader:1139-1165`, `docs/RT-SERIES-2-FINDINGS.md:797`
  Top engines: Not published by any engine. Sources: none given
  Why: An in-engine exact reference makes every sampling change measurable against the truth, which no shipped system documents.
- **Cost.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: DirectTrace 3.18 ms plus accumulate 0.43 ms at 1600x900 in the garage; about 10.3 ms at native by pixel-count scaling (inferred). Code: `docs/RT-SERIES-2.md:93`, `docs/RT-SERIES-2.md:1978`
  Top engines: MegaLights, PS5, 1080p, 941 area lights, 20-80 lights per pixel, about 1 spp: opaque 4.08 ms (sampling 0.70, screen trace 0.47, HW RT 1.35, shading 0.55, list 0.05, denoise 0.96); 5.51 ms with translucency and fog. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/megalights_2025.txt:1537-1596`
  Why: MegaLights lights 20-80 lights per pixel, with its denoiser, for roughly what RageV spends at 22. The laptop-to-PS5 ratio is unmeasured.

### A.6 Candidates that could beat both

- **A-BB1: Visibility-only history: recompute the unshadowed diffuse and specular every frame at the current view over the pixel's candidate set, and accumulate only the visibility ratios R_d and R_s (the K picks' visibility weighted by their diffuse and specular terms). Output = U_d x R_d + U_s x R_s.**
  Why better than both: MegaLights and RTXDI denoise merged shaded light, so a view-dependent highlight and a moving light's unshadowed pool of light live in a lagging history. MegaLights passes that residue to TSR; the RTXDI titles pass it to DLSS RR. Here the history holds only bounded [0,1] visibility, view-independent for the diffuse half. The highlight and the light pool are exact every frame, and nothing is handed to TAA. Evidence: Heitz, Hill and McGuire 2018 (ratio estimator). Unity HDRP ships it for RT rect-light shadows. The MegaLights talk separates shadowing from shading at shading time (megalights_2025.txt:733-763) and credits per-light ratio denoisers with blurring only penumbrae (902-945). RageV measured that the RT-22 trail sits in the direct history (RT-SERIES-2.md:1311). Derivation: once the score equals the unshadowed term, the mean visibility of picks drawn in proportion to that term is an unbiased per-frame estimate of sum(fV)/sum(f) in luminance. Risk: MegaLights reports that factoring out one stochastically chosen important light failed (megalights_2025.txt:925-938); that failure transfers wherever U is itself an estimate (cells above the candidate cap, such as the bridge). Colour bias where differently coloured lights are shadowed differently. Denoising the ratio is biased (consistent only as the radius shrinks). U needs the full term for every candidate. Cost: About today's sweep once the score is the term (the rgb term replaces the luminance score). History lanes shrink from 2 x RGB to 2 scalars plus moments.
  The comparison's gate: Against RT2-24's baseline merged arm at equal ms: emitter_lag.py floor beside the moving light below both 19.5% and the baseline; spin_measure.py owner swing with the direct signal isolated (--capture-signals): pole edge-jitter at or below 8.83 and speckle at or below 494 (16-ray reference); under --aa=none, frame 400 vs 200 within parked_stats.py's noise floor on the garage and Headland; converged diff against the every-light reference shows no structure beyond grain; T5's moving shadow-edge error no worse than a re-measured baseline.
  Verification: **partly true.** (1) R_d and R_s weighted by f_d and f_s over picks drawn by f_d+f_s are ratio estimators (biased per frame), not the unbiased estimate the claim cites. (2) 'U exact every frame' needs an enumerated candidate set. The target design's U includes a stochastic hidden-candidate estimate, and with BB2's light BVH there is no enumerated set, so U carries noise with no history, and that noise goes to TAA or stays on screen. (3) A moving light changes R through its unshadowed weights even when no visibility changes, so a lag remains in R. (4) Picks and soft points are frozen without jitter (direct_trace.rvshader:1178; ray_shadow_trace.glsl:24-26), so the R history cannot converge under --aa=none until RT2-7 decouples the seeds. Keep it as a plausible arm behind a measurement gate; the talk's own failure (A-C4) does not apply directly, because U is summed over the whole set rather than one light.
  In the roadmap: RT2-24 step 2 (diffuse ratio only; the lamps' shine goes to RT2-23g).
- **A-BB2: One world-state light BVH (two levels, nodes with power, bounds and a normal cone) as the only candidate source in RT mode: every ray hit, transparent surfaces, and the hidden-light candidates behind MegaLights-style guiding at primary pixels.**
  Why better than both: MegaLights' hidden candidates are a stride over the froxel cell, whose cost grows with the cell (talk 1589-1596). RTXDI's ReGIR needs a cell size (metres, or camera-relative view state) and ignores orientation. A light BVH is importance-sampled at O(log N) with no size parameter (rule 2), one structure serves both editor views (the ray world is world state), and it handles the bridge's 600 m lamps where every grid cell holds 150 lights. Evidence: Conty-Estevez & Kulla 2018; Moreau, Pharr & Clarberg 2019 (Bistro, 26k emissive triangles: update 0.18/0.85 ms, sampling 2.6 ms on an RTX 2080 Ti); Tokuyoshi 2024 SG node scoring for glossy surfaces. UE's path tracer, Cycles and Arnold ship light trees offline. MegaLights' guiding list costs 0.05 ms on PS5. Risk: Traversal divergence. Moreau's sampling ms may not fit 4.1 MP at native. Still blind to visibility (guiding covers primary pixels only). MegaLights rejected hierarchies for build cost and merging artefacts (talk 177-186). Cost: About 16 node evaluations per sample at 190 lights and about 28 at 10k, against today's 190-light walk per bridge hit. The CPU build changes only where lights change.
  The comparison's gate: Many-light scene (RT2-2d) at 1k/5k/10k lights: DirectTrace and hit-lighting ms grow by no more than the palindrome spread; per hit, RV_COUNT_HIT stays at or below 2*ceil(log2 N) + a constant. At equal ms, the converged-diff grain against the every-light reference is at or below both baseline arms (the froxel stride at primary pixels; the world grid of RT2-6 arm 10 at hits). The bridge with every lamp live shows no dark ring past 1 km. Build and refit take at most 0.5 ms of CPU at 10k lights with 10% moving.
  Verification: **plausible.** 'Every grid cell holds 150 lights' is wrong. FINDINGS:4005 says the busiest cluster holds 150, with 57.6 lights on average at hits and 118 per fragment. I could not confirm that UE's path tracer uses a light tree; it is documented with a light grid, so drop it from the citation. It conflicts with BB1: a BVH gives no enumerated set for an exact U. RTXDI's own paper beats a light BVH at equal error only once reuse is added. The cost at 4.1 MP on the laptop is unmeasured.
  In the roadmap: RT2-12, gated against the camera-centred cascaded light grid Doom: The Dark Ages ships (the rule-1 baseline arm; corrected after the roadmap's second critic, because RT2-6 arm 10's 8x4x32 grid is RageV's scene-shaped interim, not Doom's form).
- **A-BB3: Spatial-only pairwise-MIS pooling of light samples for the primary direct light (RT-24's PooledLamp generalised), with no temporal reuse.**
  Why better than both: It sits between MegaLights (no reuse, so more noise per ray) and RTXDI (temporal reuse, so correlation and lag). Spatial-only GRIS reuse, re-weighted from the receiving pixel, stays unbiased and adds candidates without the temporal correlation the MegaLights talk blames on ReSTIR. Evidence: RT-24 on reflections (RT-SERIES-2.md:862-870): tube grain 10.57 to 9.07 and car grain 7.01 to 5.84 at +2.1 ms with 8 neighbours at 1600x900, and the parked floor 3.2-3.5 levels closer to the 16-ray reference. Bitterli 2022 and Lin 2022 (GRIS). MegaLights ships a reweighted neighbour-ray reuse as its scale-down option (talk 714-732). Risk: Correlated blotches reaching the denoiser (MegaLights' objection); +1-2 ms; one more shadow ray per pixel if pooling runs after the K picks. Cost: +1.0 ms (4 neighbours) to +2.1 ms (8) at 1600x900 on reflections; scale by pixel count at native before tuning.
  The comparison's gate: At equal ms against the unpooled RT2-12b base: the grain score and speckle count (spin_measure.py, speckle_count.py) lower; the parked converged diff unchanged (unbiased); A,B,B,A cost; the pooled output passes through no absolute clamp.
  Verification: **plausible.** The only evidence is reflections' aimed lamp samples on the garage, not primary direct light. Unbiasedness needs each neighbour's exact pick pdf (its score over its own candidate total), so the guided list and hidden clamp must be recomputable at the receiving pixel. Spatial reuse still correlates neighbours. The primary cost is multiplied by K. Today's sea neighbour merge (direct_trace.rvshader:990-1020) is the biased M-weighted combine; this arm should replace it.
  In the roadmap: RT2-12b, its later arm.
- **A-BB4: Highlight-aware points on area lights: projected-solid-angle (LTC-proportional) sampling of tubes and rectangles (Peters 2021), with one-sample MIS between a diffuse-proportional point and a specular-lobe-proportional point.**
  Why better than both: MegaLights samples the light's area uniformly, down-weighting hidden quadrants through a 2x2 bitmask. RageV samples uniformly along the tube and lets one 0/1 scale the whole highlight, so a pole near one end of a 3.07 m tube re-rolls a fully visible highlight every frame (direct-05, note 3). Sampling the point the highlight actually comes from gets the highlight's visibility right with the same one ray. Evidence: Peters, HPG 2021 (low variance outside penumbrae at 2 spp); Heitz & Hill 2017 (linear lights); RT2-10's recorded symptom. Risk: ALU per sample; each of the two strategies needs its own pdf; little gain on rough surfaces. Cost: A few dozen ALU ops per kept light; no extra rays.
  The comparison's gate: A tube over a glossy plane with a pole near one end, at roughness 0.1/0.3/0.6: at equal rays, the frame-to-frame variance over the highlight region is lower than uniform sampling's; the diff against --reference-render is within grain; the swing's tube-patch grain score is lower.
  Verification: **plausible.** 'Re-rolls every frame' is true only under TAA jitter; without it the point is frozen, which gives a fixed bias rather than flicker. Peters 2021 (linear lights) and Heitz & Hill 2017 are papers, not shipped, so this needs the reference-render gate.
  In the roadmap: RT2-10, its later arm.

- **A-C1** (confirmed). Claim: ScoreDirect's non-water branch scores sized and tube lights with the unwidened lobe, with no radius or length renormalisation, while DirectTerm shades with both, so the pick and the shade disagree for tubes on glossy surfaces
  Evidence: direct_trace.rvshader:507-593 (ScoreDirect): the non-water branch uses alpha = Roughness^2 with no widening and no specScale for radius or length. For a tube, H points at NearestOnLamp, not at the closest point on the capsule. DirectTerm (365-414) builds H toward the closest point on the capsule, widens alpha by 0.5*angular, applies (alpha/widened)^2, and applies the RT-24 length factor alpha/(alpha+0.25*subtended). The score is called at 924, 994 and 1193, and nothing overrides it later.
  Correction: The mismatch is wider than tubes. Sized spheres (Direction.w > 0, no length) are also scored with H toward the centre and an unwidened peak, while DirectTerm uses the representative point and the (alpha/alpha_w)^2 scale. The score also leaves out G and the Fresnel. On glossy surfaces the unwidened peak overstates the shaded peak by about (alpha_w/alpha)^2 or more.
- **A-C2** (confirmed). Claim: The trace-only passes (reflection, GI, water) compile out the cluster and world-grid lookups, so every hit walks every light, and each lamp at a hit is a point with a hard ray to its centre
  Evidence: pbr_fragment.glsl:2857-2940: the cluster and world-grid lookup sits inside #ifndef RV_TRACE_ONLY. Under RV_TRACE_ONLY the #else (2941-2944) loops i < LightCount. RV_TRACE_ONLY is defined by reflection_trace:46, rtgi_trace:40, water_trace:43 and water_choose:32, among others. The point-light shadow ray goes to the centre (3070-3074 TraceShadowFrom(hitPosition, hitNormal, L, dist)), and so does RT-11's kept-light ray (3149-3150). There is no radius, length or soft point.
  Correction: This needs one qualification. Under RV_LIGHT_CULL, most lights are rejected by the 16-byte record before the 80-byte read, but the walk still visits every index. RT-11's hitOneLight keeps one light by reservoir, but only after walking all of them. Hit specular (RV_HIT_SPECULAR, 3127-3136 and 3155-3160) is also point-light GGX with no widening.
- **A-C3** (confirmed). Claim: MegaLights shades with pre-integrated (analytic) irradiance x accumulated sample visibility weight, separating shadowing from shading
  Evidence: megalights_2025.txt:733-763. Visible sample weights are accumulated per light and multiplied by pre-integrated irradiance x IES x light function. The talk says this assumes shadowing can be separated from shading, citing Heitz 2018, and that it keeps compatibility with the non-stochastic path.
  Correction: The separation happens only at shading time. The merged, shaded result is then denoised as light (764-900), not as visibility.
- **A-C4** (confirmed). Claim: MegaLights tried factoring out the most important light per pixel (a ratio-style denoiser for merged lighting), and it failed because light selection is stochastic
  Evidence: megalights_2025.txt:925-938. Epic tried factoring out the most important light per pixel. It did not work well because light selection is stochastic.
  Correction: The talk gives two more reasons. Factoring out the BRDF makes the filters run on incorrect values, and the most-important light changes discontinuously between pixels, which leaves artefacts where values are blended. The attempt was a workaround for denoising at render resolution before upscaling.
- **A-C5** (partly true). Claim: A light BVH can sample at real-time cost: Moreau 2019 reports 2.0-2.6 ms of sampling and 0.18-0.85 ms of update on an RTX 2080 Ti (resolution not stated); this has to fit 4.1 MP on the laptop
  Evidence: The Moreau HPG 2019 slides, extracted from the PDF. Bistro Exterior (20k static + 6k dynamic emissive triangles), 2-level BVH: update 0.85/0.18 ms, sampling 2.6 ms, total 12.0 ms. Emerald Square (19k + 66k), 2-level: update 0.89/0.35 ms, sampling 2.2 ms, total 12.6 ms. The 1-level arms sample in 2.0-2.4 ms. The slides name no GPU, resolution or spp for the table. The NVIDIA abstract gives 16.1 ms per frame and less than 1 ms of update.
  Correction: The update range is 0.18-0.89 ms, not 0.18-0.85, and the 2-level sampling is 2.2-2.6 ms. The RTX 2080 Ti is not stated in the slides; it may be in the paper, which I could not fetch (over the size limit). 'Real-time cost' at RageV's 4.1 MP on a laptop is an assumption until measured.
- **A-C6** (partly true). Claim: With the selection score equal to the unshadowed term in luminance, the per-frame mean visibility of the K picks is an unbiased estimate of sum(fV)/sum(f); the colour bias is limited to lights of different hue and visibility
  Evidence: The derivation holds. In the primary path (direct_trace.rvshader:1166-1210), each of the K reservoirs runs exact weighted reservoir sampling over the whole cell list, so each pick is drawn in proportion to its score, independently and with replacement. With score = luminance of the unshadowed term f, E[V of a pick] = sum(fV)/sum(f). That is unbiased per frame over the draw distribution.
  Correction: There are three limits. (1) The claim holds only for one combined luminance ratio over an enumerated candidate set. BB1's separate R_d and R_s weight the picks by f_d and f_s while the picks are drawn by f_d+f_s, which makes them self-normalised ratio estimators: consistent, but biased at finite K. Bias also comes from the diffuse/specular split, not only from lights of different hue. (2) The premise that the score equals the term is not true today (A-C1). (3) Without TAA jitter the salt is 0 (direct_trace.rvshader:1178), so every frame repeats the same picks. The per-frame expectation is over draws that never change per pixel, and temporal accumulation cannot converge it.
- **A-C7** (confirmed). Claim: The direct light's specular half is stored as shaded radiance, surface-reprojected, with memory min(frames, 4) and a 3x3 RGB mean +/- spread clamp; diffuse has no spatial pass (YoungRadius 0)
  Evidence: Renderer3D.cpp:7402-7419: DirectSignal sets YoungRadius 0, PairMemory 4, and History as an on/off bool. reflection_accumulate.rvshader:443-463 (Neighbourhood2 is a 3x3 RGB mean and sd). 1820-1845: held2 is clamped to mean2 +/- sd2*width, and frames2 = min(frames, Tuning.w), where Tuning.w = PairMemory (Renderer3D.cpp:7255). The longer-memory branch does not apply because 4 is not greater than History.y. direct_trace.rvshader:1422 writes specular as shaded radiance.
- **A-C8** (confirmed). Claim: Shadow rays return only 0/1 with no occluder distance, so a penumbra-sized radius needs a change to the shadow query
  Evidence: ray_shadow_trace.glsl:232-256: TraceShadowFromMasked returns 1.0 or 0.0 from the committed intersection type, with TerminateOnFirstHit. No hit t is read or returned. The soft and tube variants wrap it.
- **A-C9** (confirmed). Claim: The soft-shadow point is uniform on a disc perpendicular to L and the tube point uniform along its length, both frozen unless TAA's jitter is non-zero
  Evidence: ray_shadow_trace.glsl:340-384: the point is uniform on a disc about L, from an IGN shift plus R2. It moves with the frame only if RV_TRACE_ANIMATED, which is defined at 24-26 as Jitter != 0. The tube point (392-404) is uniform along the length and takes the same gate. DirectTrace overrides the macro only under RV_DIRECT_RELIGHT (direct_trace.rvshader:46-62, 636), where it is set from u_Direct.Animated, which is itself the jitter test (Renderer3D.cpp:8053, 8163, 8333).
  Correction: The same TAA gate also freezes DirectTrace's K light picks and the sea's neighbour rotation (direct_trace.rvshader:944, 1178: salt = 0 unless Animated).
- **A-C10** (confirmed). Claim: Emitters are bounding-box rectangles with strength > 1; DirectTrace binds no emitter table; GI picks emitters uniformly
  Evidence: Scene.cpp:2221-2250: membership requires strength > 1, and the emitter is a rectangle from the mesh's local AABB (the two largest half-extents, the thin axis as the normal). rtgi_trace.rvshader:364-386 picks int(GiRandom*emitters), which is uniform. direct_trace.rvshader has no u_Emitters or Emitter reference (grep is empty).
  Correction: Texel-emitter aiming tables exist inside a chosen emitter, but the choice of emitter is still uniform.
- **A-C11** (confirmed). Claim: MegaLights evaluates the whole visible list plus only every N-th light of the grid cell, and its sampling cost still scales with light count
  Evidence: megalights_2025.txt:514-541: MegaLights checks all lights on the visible list and every N-th light of the grid cell, and increases N on a history miss. 1589-1596: 'sampling cost scales with the number of lights', which is why opaque sampling costs more than shading at half resolution.
- **A-C12** (confirmed). Claim: MegaLights' sparse spatial kernel and 'shading confidence' rely on TSR to finish the image
  Evidence: megalights_2025.txt:863-896: a sparse kernel 'rotated per pixel, TSR will smooth this out', with 'some noise can be handled by TSR'. 946-968: shading confidence says to 'pass some noise to TSR'. 1781-1786 names denoiser-plus-TSR as an open challenge.
- **A-C13** (confirmed). Claim: RT-24's PooledLamp uses the canonical pairwise-MIS weights and clamps the pooled result at 64 per channel (an absolute-brightness bias)
  Evidence: reflection_resolve.rvshader:306-331: the canonical sample's weight is m_c = (1/k) sum_j (p_c/k)/(p_j + p_c/k). 353: each neighbour's weight is m_j = (p_j/k)/(p_j + p_c/k). Together these are the standard pairwise-MIS weights and sum to 1. 456: min(PooledLamp(...), vec3(64.0)) is an absolute radiance clamp per channel, applied before the tint.
- **A-C14** (partly true). Claim: WorldLightGrid is a fixed 8x4x32 box, and both grids are rebuilt with a CPU sort in every BeginScene
  Evidence: LightGrid.h:155-161: kCellsX/Y/Z = 8/4/32 are compile-time constants, with the axes shaped to the bridge. LightGrid.cpp:264-300: the box is the union of the lights' position +/- range, recomputed on every Build. Renderer3D.cpp:4297 (Grid.Build) and 4323 (WorldGrid.Build) are inside BeginScene (from 3861). Each Build does one std::sort by brightness (LightGrid.cpp:207, 362).
  Correction: The cell counts and axis assignment are fixed, but the box is not: it follows the lights' reach every frame. The CPU sort is one sort per grid per BeginScene, which means per view.
- **A-C15** (partly true). Claim: MegaLights' 12-frame history cap and MaxShadingWeight 20 (phase 1) are not in the SIGGRAPH 2025 talk; they come only from Epic Games Japan's deck
  Evidence: The SIGGRAPH 2025 talk text has no 12-frame cap and no MaxShadingWeight (grep). The Epic Games Japan deck (docswell) shows r.MegaLights.Temporal.MaxFramesAccumulated = 12 (slides 95-96), r.MegaLights.MaxShadingWeight = 20 (slide 94) and r.MegaLights.NumSamplesPerPixel = 4 (slide 56).
  Correction: These are UE console-variable defaults that the deck reports, so they are engine facts and not the deck's own. 'Only' from the deck is unproven, because UE's source would also carry them. The values stand.
- **A-C16** (confirmed). Claim: Unity HDRP accumulates shadowed and unshadowed sums (SN/UN) as a ratio estimator for RT rect-light shadows
  Evidence: Unity Graphics master, RaytracingShadow.compute (RaytracingAreaShadowPrepass): it writes UnL = Luminance(Un)/numSamples, where Un is the clamped unoccluded analytic radiance. RaytracingShadow.raytrace (RayGenAreaShadowSingle) accumulates float2(visibility*sample.x + prev.x, sample.y + prev.y), which is the shadowed and unshadowed sums.
  Correction: Only rectangle and area lights use it, and phase 1 notes that forward shading uses an approximation. The final SN/UN division was not seen in the files fetched.
- **A-OB1** (refuted). Claim: RageV is BETTER than the top engines at: History invalidation (detecting real change). Ours: Measured change re-lights one pixel per 3x3 block with last frame's picks and rays; any difference is real change and shortens that pixel's memory. It is an exact temporal gradient. Theirs: MegaLights clips history by a 5x5 YCoCg variance box and cuts history weight by the history's distance from the box. RTXDI and NRD rely on history caps and anti-lag heuristics. A-SVGF gradients (Quake II RTX) are the research analogue of RageV's approach. Why: An exact change test instead of a statistical guess. It is not sufficient alone: RT-22's 19.5% trail survives it.
  Evidence: The RageV half is true. direct_trace.rvshader:1055-1130 and 1398-1419 re-light one pixel per 3x3 block with last frame's picks and rolls. reflection_accumulate.rvshader:1795-1801 shortens memory to 1/measured. But RTXDI's own documentation (NVIDIAGameWorks/RTXDI doc/Confidence.md; ChangeLog: 'computing denoiser confidence signals from ReSTIR') computes temporal gradients as 'the difference between the shading results of the same surface using the same light sample on two consecutive frames', with the image split into 3x3 strata and one pixel per stratum, separately for diffuse and specular, and fed to the denoiser as history confidence. Quake II RTX shipped A-SVGF gradients in 2019, so they are shipped, not only research.
  Correction: This is at parity with the shipped RTXDI SDK (same 3x3-stratum, same-sample gradient) and with Q2RTX's A-SVGF, not better. RTXDI also filters the gradient spatially (a-trous) and temporally before converting it to confidence; RageV reads the raw block value. The MegaLights half of the comparison is accurate (5x5 YCoCg clip, weight cut by distance from the box, talk 830-855).
- **A-OB2** (refuted). Claim: RageV is BETTER than the top engines at: Reference arm and instruments. Ours: An every-light arm (K=0) that matches the old loop to 0.034 levels, --direct-signal=off, ray and lights-per-pixel counters, and the exact re-light. Theirs: Not published by any engine. Why: An in-engine exact reference makes every sampling change measurable against the truth, which no shipped system documents.
  Evidence: The RageV half is true. direct_trace.rvshader:1135-1165 shades every lamp when K==0 or cellCount<=K, and EngineConfig.cpp:426 has direct-signal. FINDINGS:797 records 0.034 levels. But UE keeps the non-stochastic deferred lights as MegaLights' comparison target (the talk, 748-757: stochastic lights 'look exactly the same as the non-stochastic ones'), MegaLights compared against a 64-bit reference (825-828), and UE and Falcor/RTXDI ship in-engine path-tracing references.
  Correction: The every-light arm is exact only in light selection. It traces the same single soft-shadow point per light (frozen without jitter) and uses the same representative-point, point-diffuse shading, so it measures sampling error, not truth. It is a good instrument, but not one the top engines lack.
- **A-BB1** (partly true). Claim: Could beat both RageV and the shipped baseline: Visibility-only history: recompute the unshadowed diffuse and specular every frame at the current view over the pixel's candidate set, and accumulate only the visibility ratios R_d and R_s (the K picks' visibility weighted by their diffuse and specular terms). Output = U_d x R_d + U_s x R_s.. Why: MegaLights and RTXDI denoise merged shaded light, so a view-dependent highlight and a moving light's unshadowed pool of light live in a lagging history. MegaLights passes that residue to TSR; the RTXDI titles pass it to DLSS RR. Here the history holds only bounded [0,1] visibility, view-independent for the diffuse half. The highlight and the light pool are exact every frame, and nothing is handed to TAA.
  Evidence: The sources check out: Unity HDRP's SN/UN (A-C16), MegaLights 733-763 and 925-945, RT-SERIES-2.md:1311 ('Most of RT-22's remaining trail sits in this history'). The reasoning has gaps, checked against the code and maths (A-C6).
  Correction: (1) R_d and R_s weighted by f_d and f_s over picks drawn by f_d+f_s are ratio estimators (biased per frame), not the unbiased estimate the claim cites. (2) 'U exact every frame' needs an enumerated candidate set. The target design's U includes a stochastic hidden-candidate estimate, and with BB2's light BVH there is no enumerated set, so U carries noise with no history, and that noise goes to TAA or stays on screen. (3) A moving light changes R through its unshadowed weights even when no visibility changes, so a lag remains in R. (4) Picks and soft points are frozen without jitter (direct_trace.rvshader:1178; ray_shadow_trace.glsl:24-26), so the R history cannot converge under --aa=none until RT2-7 decouples the seeds. Keep it as a plausible arm behind a measurement gate; the talk's own failure (A-C4) does not apply directly, because U is summed over the whole set rather than one light.
- **A-BB2** (plausible). Claim: Could beat both RageV and the shipped baseline: One world-state light BVH (two levels, nodes with power, bounds and a normal cone) as the only candidate source in RT mode: every ray hit, transparent surfaces, and the hidden-light candidates behind MegaLights-style guiding at primary pixels.. Why: MegaLights' hidden candidates are a stride over the froxel cell, whose cost grows with the cell (talk 1589-1596). RTXDI's ReGIR needs a cell size (metres, or camera-relative view state) and ignores orientation. A light BVH is importance-sampled at O(log N) with no size parameter (rule 2), one structure serves both editor views (the ray world is world state), and it handles the bridge's 600 m lamps where every grid cell holds 150 lights.
  Evidence: Moreau's numbers are verified (A-C5: 26k emissive triangles in Bistro, update 0.85/0.18 ms, sampling 2.6 ms; no GPU in the slides). MegaLights' guiding list costs 0.05 ms and 0.1 ms (talk table around 1575), and sampling scales with light count (1589-1596). Cycles and Imageworks' Arnold (Conty-Estevez & Kulla 2018) ship light trees offline.
  Correction: 'Every grid cell holds 150 lights' is wrong. FINDINGS:4005 says the busiest cluster holds 150, with 57.6 lights on average at hits and 118 per fragment. I could not confirm that UE's path tracer uses a light tree; it is documented with a light grid, so drop it from the citation. It conflicts with BB1: a BVH gives no enumerated set for an exact U. RTXDI's own paper beats a light BVH at equal error only once reuse is added. The cost at 4.1 MP on the laptop is unmeasured.
- **A-BB3** (plausible). Claim: Could beat both RageV and the shipped baseline: Spatial-only pairwise-MIS pooling of light samples for the primary direct light (RT-24's PooledLamp generalised), with no temporal reuse.. Why: It sits between MegaLights (no reuse, so more noise per ray) and RTXDI (temporal reuse, so correlation and lag). Spatial-only GRIS reuse, re-weighted from the receiving pixel, stays unbiased and adds candidates without the temporal correlation the MegaLights talk blames on ReSTIR.
  Evidence: RT-SERIES-2.md:862-870 matches the numbers: tube 10.57->9.07, car 7.01->5.84, +2.1 ms at 8 neighbours at 1600x900, parked floor 3.2-3.5 levels closer. The pairwise-MIS weights are correct (A-C13). Talk 714-732: MegaLights reweights neighbour rays as a scale-down option and says it softens contact shadows.
  Correction: The only evidence is reflections' aimed lamp samples on the garage, not primary direct light. Unbiasedness needs each neighbour's exact pick pdf (its score over its own candidate total), so the guided list and hidden clamp must be recomputable at the receiving pixel. Spatial reuse still correlates neighbours. The primary cost is multiplied by K. Today's sea neighbour merge (direct_trace.rvshader:990-1020) is the biased M-weighted combine; this arm should replace it.
- **A-BB4** (plausible). Claim: Could beat both RageV and the shipped baseline: Highlight-aware points on area lights: projected-solid-angle (LTC-proportional) sampling of tubes and rectangles (Peters 2021), with one-sample MIS between a diffuse-proportional point and a specular-lobe-proportional point.. Why: MegaLights samples the light's area uniformly, down-weighting hidden quadrants through a 2x2 bitmask. RageV samples uniformly along the tube and lets one 0/1 scale the whole highlight, so a pole near one end of a 3.07 m tube re-rolls a fully visible highlight every frame (direct-05, note 3). Sampling the point the highlight actually comes from gets the highlight's visibility right with the same one ray.
  Evidence: RageV half confirmed: ray_shadow_trace.glsl:392-404 draws a uniform point along the tube, and one 0/1 multiplies both d and s (direct_trace.rvshader:1275-1281). MegaLights half confirmed: talk 635-690, area-preserving sampling with a 2x2 visibility bitmask that down-weights hidden parts. FINDINGS direct-05 records the symptom.
  Correction: 'Re-rolls every frame' is true only under TAA jitter; without it the point is frozen, which gives a fixed bias rather than flicker. Peters 2021 (linear lights) and Heitz & Hill 2017 are papers, not shipped, so this needs the reference-render gate.

### A.8 Design changes the verification required

Each was applied to the roadmap's items on 2026-09-25 (section 9 lists them).

- **Direct-light reconstruction: visibility apart from shading.** Drop 'measured change beats the top engines'. It is RTXDI's shipped 3x3-stratum confidence, so step 1's baseline should copy RTXDI's gradient to a-trous to temporal to confidence pipeline, not the raw block value. Step 2 must say that R_d and R_s are ratio estimators (biased, consistent), and that U has an exact part and a stochastic part: the hidden-candidate estimate needs its own accumulation or noise gate. It must also depend explicitly on RT2-7 decoupling the pick and soft-point seeds from TAA jitter, because the R history cannot converge under --aa=none otherwise. Add a gate that a moving light with static occluders still lags in R. Rests on: A-OB1, A-BB1, A-C6.
- **Candidate structure: one world-state light BVH for RT mode.** Correct the citations: update 0.18-0.89 ms, 2-level sampling 2.2-2.6 ms, total 12.0-12.6 ms, and the GPU and resolution are not in the slides. Remove 'every cell holds 150' (that is the busiest cluster) and the UE path tracer light-tree claim. Say the world grid's box adapts to the lights' reach, with fixed 8x4x32 resolution and bridge-shaped axes. Note the conflict with reconstruction step 2's exact U when the BVH is the candidate source. Keep the cost gate as the deciding measurement. Rests on: A-C5, A-BB2, A-C14.
- **Light model and one light library.** The every-light arm (K=0) is not a truth reference: it keeps one frozen soft point per light and point diffuse. Gates that call it 'the every-light reference' must use --reference-render (RT2-2) as truth and keep K=0 only for sampling error. Include sized spheres in the ScoreDirect fix (A-C1 correction), not only tubes. Rests on: A-OB2.
- **Primary direct sampling: visibility-guided, with bounded candidate work.** The 'converged diff against the every-light reference' gate measures only sampling error. Add a reference-render gate. The spatial pairwise-MIS arm needs each pixel's exact pick pdf, meaning the candidate total including the clamped hidden fraction, stored for neighbours (as RT-24's o_LampSample.w does), and it should replace the biased M-weighted sea merge. Its evidence is reflections on the garage only. Rests on: A-OB2, A-BB3, A-C6.
- **Shadows, the sun and far-light thinning.** No structural change. Record that the soft and tube points are frozen without jitter today (a fixed bias rather than flicker), so the highlight-aware sampling arm must be judged under --aa=none after RT2-7's seed change. Rests on: A-BB4.

### A.9 New findings from the verification

- **A-N1.** DirectTrace's K light picks are also frozen per pixel when jitter is zero (salt = 0 unless u_Direct.Animated, direct_trace.rvshader:1171-1180 and 944). Under --aa=none, both the light choice and the soft-shadow point are a fixed per-pixel pattern that no accumulator can converge. This affects every 'converge without TAA' gate and BB1.
- **A-N2.** RTXDI (NVIDIAGameWorks/RTXDI doc/Confidence.md) ships the same temporal-gradient method as RageV's measured change: 3x3 strata, the same light sample on two frames, diffuse and specular separately. It filters the gradient before use; RageV does not.
- **A-N3.** ScoreDirect's sized-sphere case (no tube) aims H at the light's centre, not the representative point, and uses an unwidened lobe. The score/shade mismatch covers every sized light, not only tubes.
- **A-N4.** Hit specular in trace-only passes (pbr_fragment.glsl:3127-3136 and 3155-3160) is point-light GGX with no radius or length widening, so lamps seen in glossy hit surfaces give pinpoint highlights.
- **A-N5.** The sea's neighbour reservoir merge (direct_trace.rvshader:990-1020) uses the M-weighted (Talbot) combine without MIS, which is biased where neighbours' candidate sets differ.
- **A-N6.** Moreau 2019's table does not name its GPU. The NVIDIA abstract gives 16.1 ms per frame in total and less than 1 ms of hierarchy update.

### A.10 The comparison's corrections to the survey

- Phase 1 implied MegaLights evaluates each light at its sampled point and denoises that. The talk (megalights_2025.txt:733-763) says shading is 'pre-integrated irradiance x IES x light function x sample weight': analytic unshadowed light times accumulated visibility, the same estimator shape as RageV's DirectTerm x ray. Corrected.
- Phase 1 listed the ratio estimator as a candidate to beat the baseline without mentioning MegaLights' negative result. The talk (925-938) reports that factoring out the most important light per pixel failed because light selection is stochastic. Added.
- Phase 1 said the cost 'depends on the number of pixels, not the number of lights'. For MegaLights' sampling stage that is overstated: the talk (1589-1596) says sampling costs more than shading because it scales with light count. It evaluates the full visible list plus only every N-th grid light, and more of the grid on a history miss (514-541). Corrected.
- Phase 1's 'at most 12 frames' of history and 'MaxShadingWeight 20' are not in the SIGGRAPH 2025 talk. The talk only says history weight is reduced by confidence from the distance to the neighbourhood bounds (842-856), with disocclusion handling for the first 4 frames (897-901). These two numbers remain unverified (Epic Japan deck only).
- Confirmed from the talk: 4 samples per 2x2 block with sampling at half resolution (555-584, 1589-1590); the 8x8 visible-light list with stochastic bilinear lookup (306-338); the hidden-light cap of 20% of the weight sum, relaxed when visible lights are dim and raised to 50% on reprojection failure (355-409); the directional cap of 50% (589-634); Log2(Luminance(BRDF))+1 weights (442); the 5x5 YCoCg variance clip (802-834); the 80% shading-confidence pass-through to TSR (946-968); the 150 m near-field proxies with a separate far-field TLAS (1000-1029); the PS5 per-stage timings, totalling 4.08 ms opaque and 5.51 ms overall (1537-1557).
- Added from the talk, missing in phase 1: 20 to 80 lights per pixel in the PS5 demo (1563); a 2x2 per-light bitmask of visible light parts (635-667); 'don't trace duplicated rays', including penumbrae below the pixel footprint, is already in the 2025 talk rather than new in 5.8 (689-713); reweighted neighbour-ray reuse as a scale-down option (714-732); the GPU two-pass light grid build with HZB culling, 0.6 to under 0.2 ms (1253-1294); fixtures skipped by a per-light ray end bias, with artists tagging fixture meshes in the BVH (1649-1659).
- The talk states directly that downsampled sampling 'doesn't converge on the same result as full screen sampling and can make shadows somewhat softer' (581-583), and that the sparse spatial kernel relies on TSR to fill its holes (865, 891). Both strengthen phase 1's secondhand note on MegaLights' dependence on TSR.
- Phase 1's 'checkerboard half-resolution mode arrived in 5.7': the 2025 talk already describes checkerboard and 4-rooks downsampled patterns (557-560); only the release version of the mode is unconfirmed.
- Phase 1's RTX 3080 figure (900 shadowed point lights, 134 ms vs 13 ms) does not appear in the talk and remains a secondary-source number.

### A.11 Questions left open

As the survey recorded them. Where the verification later read a primary talk the survey could not fetch, the verification's verdict above supersedes the line here.

- The MegaLights SIGGRAPH 2025 slides (14 MB) exceeded the fetch limit. Per-stage PS5 timings, the 20% hidden-light cap, the 80% shading-confidence skip, and the proxy/far-field split come from a secondary GitHub summary whose table is internally consistent. The 4-samples default, 12-frame history, MaxShadingWeight 20 and the TSR dependency come from Epic Games Japan's deck. Both should be confirmed against the slides.
- No published measurement of MegaLights or RTXDI with TSR/TAA/DLSS RR fully disabled was found. Whether either converges without an upscaler is unproven.
- The Alan Wake 2 GDC 2024 path-tracing talk is login-gated. Its light sampling (candidate counts, emissive handling, denoiser before DLSS RR) was not verified; AW2 details here are secondary.
- Indiana Jones and Doom: The Dark Ages path-tracing modes do not publish their direct-light sampling algorithm (ReSTIR DI, light tree or other). Doom's base-mode direct shadows are not described in the GI talk.
- RTXDI publishes no per-pass ms on current GPUs; the only NVIDIA figure (San Miguel under 8 ms) lacks GPU and resolution. The presampling tile size and count defaults were not confirmed in the library source.
- Moreau 2019's timing resolution is not stated in the slides; HDRP ray-traced shadow costs are not published; AMD Hybrid Shadows and the Shadow Denoiser have no public ms.
- Tokuyoshi's 2024 'Efficient Visibility Reuse for Real-time ReSTIR' and the SIGGRAPH 2026 'LightOpt' paper could not be read (size limit / 403).
- Whether UE 5.8 MegaLights treats emissive meshes as lights was not found; the docs found point to Lumen for emissives.
- Snowdrop (Avatar) and Frostbite/SEED direct-light RT shadowing details were not found in primary sources.
- All translations of published numbers to 2560x1600 on an RTX 5070 Ti Laptop are extrapolations. The laptop's RT throughput relative to PS5 or an RTX 4080 has not been measured in RageV.
- Claims the verification could not settle without a measurement or a source: A-BB2, A-BB3, A-BB4 (each is gated in the roadmap item named above).

## B. Reflections

**In short.** RageV aims at every lamp inside a reflection, which puts it ahead of Lumen 2022 and Snowdrop there and behind the RTXDI-class path tracers. It is behind on cost (every glossy pixel traced at full rate), on hit shading and on the temporal design (about twenty speed-keyed rules where NRD has one confidence blend). Only NRD's design meets the owner's rule 3. The verified verdicts, with the candidates tied to their items, are in the roadmap's section 11.

**Counts.** Comparison topics: 21 behind, 5 equal, 2 missing, 2 better (claimed), 2 not comparable. Verification of 26 claims: 17 confirmed, 5 partly true, 2 refuted, 2 plausible.

**The survey, in its own summary.** Shipped ray-traced reflections (2018-2026) mostly follow one pattern. The engine traces one GGX-VNDF ray per pixel, or per quad on glossy tiles, but only below a roughness cutoff: Lumen's default is 0.4; AMD traces below 0.2 and falls back to diffuse probes above 0.6. Rougher surfaces take their reflection from the diffuse GI or a radiance cache. Hits are shaded as cheaply as possible: screen-space reuse first, then a surface or radiance cache. Full hit lighting is only a quality mode, and Epic's docs say it "greatly increases GPU cost". A dedicated specular denoiser that keeps its own history then cleans the result. The reference denoiser is NVIDIA NRD ReBLUR. It is open source and runs on Vulkan. It costs 2.55 ms for diffuse plus specular at native 1440p on an RTX 4080; the specular part alone is not published. It works as follows:; The input is divided by the specular albedo before denoising and multiplied back after.; A prepass takes the smallest in-lobe hit distance among neighbours, not an average, to track the reflection.; Two history candidates are fetched with occlusion-aware Catmull-Rom sampling: one follows the surface's motion, the other the virtual image, placed from the hit distance and the signed curvature. They are blended by confidence: a normal test sized to the lobe, a roughness test, parallax against hit distance, a second normal test one frame further back, and material ID.; History length is capped by parallax and by a roughness curve.; A 6-frame fast history bounds the 30-frame main history.; Young pixels (their first 3 frames) are rebuilt from neighbours, a firefly clamp relative to the neighbourhood runs, and a final stabilisation pass clamps by luminance. Lumen in UE 5.4 used a lighter chain: 5-sample BRDF reweighting within 8 px, 32-frame temporal accumulation, a bilateral filter last, and a clamp on ray brightness at 100 after exposure. UE 5.5 replaced it with a new denoiser whose internals are not published. Neural denoisers (DLSS Ray Reconstruction, FSR Ray Regeneration) lead on quality, but RR takes over the upscaler and FSR's needs RDNA4 on DX12, so neither can be the baseline under rule 3. No published, measured technique from 2023-2026 beats NRD-class filtering on stability per ms for mirror and glossy surfaces. The measured gains are in the inputs and in hit shading:; bounded VNDF and spherical-cap VNDF sampling;; geometric specular AA;; texture LOD at hits from ray cones;; temporal gradients as a history-confidence signal;; primary surface replacement (PSR) for mirrors;; screen reuse plus radiance caches, which add about 1 ms at 1080p on an RX 7900 XTX in AMD's 2023 method. ReSTIR does not help low-roughness reflections; RTXDI's own ReSTIR GI docs say so. For RageV's symptoms (speckle on chrome and the wet floor in fast swings, wrong history at depth edges, grain against ghosting), the shipped answers are:; tracking by the smallest hit distance, with misses flagged;; occlusion-aware history sampling;; a fast-history clamp;; rebuilding young pixels plus a relative firefly clamp;; removing noise before the denoiser: noise from hit shading, aliasing from normal maps, and aliasing of textures at hits.

**The comparison, in its own summary.** Where RageV is ahead. Reflection rays now aim at every lamp. One pass picks a lamp by weighted reservoir sampling, and power-heuristic MIS weighs the aimed sample against the lobe ray. The resolve also pools neighbours' lamp points the way ReSTIR does. Lumen 2022 lists explicit emissive sampling as future work, and Snowdrop clamps emissive hits instead. RageV's measured change re-lights last frame's rays to detect real change. Lumen, Snowdrop and NRD do not produce such a signal. Its half-float stochastic rounding and object-locked histories match the best shipped practice. Where the top engines are ahead: cost, hit shading and the temporal design.; **Cost.** RageV traces every glossy pixel at full resolution from a full-screen fragment pass. It then runs a 24-64-tap resolve, the 8-neighbour lamp pool and three 49-tap blurs on every pixel. That is about 12.7 ms at 1600x900, roughly 36 ms at native. Lumen and Snowdrop classify tiles and trace rough surfaces less or not at all. They shade hits from the previous frame's image or from a cache. Snowdrop's whole reflection pass costs 2.1 ms at 4K on an RTX 4080.; **Hit shading.** RageV keeps one random light per hit, so even a mirror is grainy. Textures at hits are read at mip 0. The default sampler is not VNDF and piles rejected draws onto the mirror direction.; **Temporal design.** The accumulator uses about twenty memory rules that depend on camera speed. NRD's design has no such rules: it blends two history candidates by confidence, tracks by the nearest in-lobe hit, caps one counter by parallax against the lobe, and adds a fast history, a history fix and a stabilisation pass. What could beat both:; Rays that stop in stored light once the lobe's footprint is wider than a cache cell. This gives one estimator at every roughness, with no seam and no distances in metres.; A ray rate set by the lobe footprint instead of tuned tile weights.; One specular signal. The lamp's NEE is shared with direct light and MIS-weighted against the reflection ray, so each fixture is counted once and the lamp pool is paid for once. Roadmap changes:; **RT2-0** finishes by tracing the remaining speckle and edge jitter to their cause with one-switch arms. Both are now close to the 16-ray reference.; **RT2-23** is re-cut into steps: inputs, temporal, spatial and stabilisation, hit shading, and the one specular signal. Its ray-allocation step (23d) moves into RT2-27.; **RT2-27** becomes footprint-based tiers with a proposed budget of 4 ms at native resolution.; An NRD reference arm is the owner's call.

**The verification, in its own summary.** I tested 24 claims. Every code claim holds except B-C9 (the budget pass is tile-resolution and the measured-change passes run at 1/3 resolution) and B-C11 (the audit did find the three values; section 10 dropped them). The industry claims hold, with small wording fixes: Snowdrop's glyph is certainly ¼, and the TSR pass-through is conditional. The verdict and beats-both claims hold up worst. OB2 and BB4 are refuted: NRD's docs prescribe RageV's measured-change method and its sample implements it, and BB4's evidence depends on TAA. OB1 is partly true: RageV beats Lumen 2022 and Snowdrop at lamps in reflections, but not RTXDI path tracers, and its MIS works against rectangle proxies. BB1 is partly true: Snowdrop is not discontinuous in roughness, SHaRC's rule is tested at hits, and the bake field is irradiance-only. BB3 is partly true: the direction is sound but it is RTXDI/NRD practice, the MegaLights wording is overstated, and fixtures need a single representation. BB2 and BB5 are plausible but need measurement gates. Design components C2, C4, C5, C6 and C7 need the changes listed, plus the C0 bookkeeping fix.

### B.1 What the top engines ship

- **Unreal Engine 5 - Lumen Reflections (5.4 defaults; 5.5+ new denoiser).** Screen trace first, then SW (SDF) or HW ray tracing; hits lit from the Lumen Surface Cache by default or 'Hit Lighting' (material + shadow rays at hit) as a quality mode. Rays only where roughness < Max Roughness To Trace (default 0.4); rougher pixels reuse the Screen Probe Gather (diffuse final gather). 5.4 cvars: DownsampleFactor 1 (full-res, 1 ray/pixel; 2 = one ray per quad), ScreenSpaceReconstruction (BRDF reweighting) 5 samples, kernel 8 px, Temporal.MaxFramesAccumulated 32, Temporal.DistanceThreshold 0.03, Temporal.MaxRayDirections 16, BilateralFilter on as last step, MaxRayIntensity 100 (pre-exposed per-ray firefly clamp). UE 5.5 release notes: 'new Lumen reflection denoiser' (sharper, faster, less noise/ghosting) - internals not published. 5.6: new ray weighting for stability; basic SER for hit lighting. Temporal reprojection via reflection hit depth: from memory of the 2022 talk, not verified in text.
  Numbers: Console budgets target 1080p internal + TSR to 4K (Epic=30 fps, High=60 fps). Disabling Lumen reflections saves ~1 ms on Xbox Series S (Epic doc example). Hit lighting is 'expensive for games'; a secondary source claims ~5x slower than surface cache on PS5 (Matrix Awakens), unverified. 5.6: 32-bit output saves 0.02 ms at 900p on console.
  Status: Shipped (many UE5 titles); HW hit lighting is mostly a PC/high-end mode.
  Sources: <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/lumen-performance-guide-for-unreal-engine>; <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/lumen-technical-details-in-unreal-engine>; <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2022/SIGGRAPH2022-Advances-Lumen-Wright%20et%20al.pdf>; <https://indxzero.github.io/ue544cvarwiki/articles/r.lumen.reflections.downsamplefactor/>; <https://indxzero.github.io/ue544cvarwiki/articles/r.lumen.reflections.temporal.maxframesaccumulated/>; <https://indxzero.github.io/ue544cvarwiki/articles/r.lumen.reflections.temporal.distancethreshold/>; <https://indxzero.github.io/ue544cvarwiki/articles/r.lumen.reflections.screenspacereconstruction.numsamples/>; <https://indxzero.github.io/ue544cvarwiki/articles/r.lumen.reflections.screenspacereconstruction.kernelradius/>; <https://indxzero.github.io/ue544cvarwiki/articles/r.lumen.reflections.bilateralfilter/>; <https://indxzero.github.io/ue544cvarwiki/articles/r.lumen.reflections.maxrayintensity/>; <https://dlss.download.nvidia.com/uebinarypackages/Documentation/UE5+Raytracing+Guideline+v5.4.pdf>; <https://tomlooman.com/unreal-engine-5-5-performance-highlights/>; <https://tomlooman.com/unreal-engine-5-6-performance-highlights/>
- **NVIDIA NRD (ReBLUR / ReLAX specular).** Library denoiser for demodulated specular radiance + hit distance. ReBLUR: prepass (spatial, min in-lobe hitT for tracking), temporal accumulation with surface-motion and virtual-motion candidates (Catmull-Rom with occlusion-aware bilinear fallback), confidence-weighted blend, parallax/roughness-limited history, 6-frame fast history, history fix (3 frames, 5x5 stride 14), anti-firefly, blur/post-blur with radius shrinking with history, temporal stabilization with luma box + antilag. ReLAX: SVGF-style A-trous with variance, better for clean/high-rpp or RTXDI input. Optional inputs: history confidence, material ID, disocclusion threshold mix.
  Numbers: RTX 4080, 1440p native, default settings: REBLUR_DIFFUSE_SPECULAR 2.55 ms (3.40 ms SH), RELAX_DIFFUSE_SPECULAR 3.25 ms (4.80 ms SH); anti-firefly +0-2% (ReBLUR), +7-10% (ReLAX). Specular-only cost not published.
  Status: Shipped (NVIDIA-sponsored RT titles, e.g. Cyberpunk 2077 RT/PT before Ray Reconstruction, Portal with RTX; secondary knowledge). Public SDK, Vulkan via NRI.
  Sources: <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD>; <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD/blob/master/Include/NRDSettings.h>; <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD/blob/master/Shaders/REBLUR_TemporalAccumulation.cs.hlsl>; <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD/blob/master/Shaders/REBLUR_TemporalStabilization.cs.hlsl>; <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD/blob/master/Shaders/REBLUR_Common_SpatialFilter.hlsli>; <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD/blob/master/Shaders/REBLUR_Config.hlsli>
- **NVIDIA DLSS Ray Reconstruction.** Neural joint denoiser + upscaler taking noisy RT color plus guides (diffuse/specular albedo, normals, roughness, depth, MVs) and either specular motion vectors or specular hit distance + view/proj matrices. When on it completely overrides DLSS Super Resolution (it is the temporal AA/upscale pass).
  Numbers: No official ms table published. Reviews (secondary) report DLSS 4.5 RR roughly the same cost as 4.0 on RTX 4090/5090 at 4K.
  Status: Shipped (Cyberpunk 2077 2.0+, Alan Wake 2, Indiana Jones, Doom: The Dark Ages PT update). Vulkan sample exists.
  Sources: <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/Streamline/blob/main/docs/ProgrammingGuideDLSS_RR.md>; <https://github.com/nvpro-samples/vk_denoise_dlssrr>; <https://www.techpowerup.com/review/nvidia-dlss-4-5-ray-reconstruction/6.html>
- **AMD FidelityFX Hybrid Reflections + Reflection Denoiser (+ GI-1.1 glossy).** FFX Classifier builds RT and denoise tile lists by roughness (global and RT roughness thresholds), samples per quad (variable rate: full for mirror, down to quarter for glossy), temporal-variance-guided tracing and per-pixel SSR hit feedback (hybridSpawnRate/hitCounterHistory). SSSR hierarchical depth trace first, HW RT for failed rays, reshading re-projected pixels. Denoiser: reprojection pass (surface vs hit-point reprojection picked by normal similarity, roughness match, radiance vs local neighbourhood variance; 3x3 search on disocclusion; 8x8 radiance mip), spatial variance-guided edge-aware filter (15 samples in 7x7), temporal pass with outlier removal and 9x9 Gaussian statistics clip. GI-1.1 (SA 2023): GGX VNDF, screen probes for rough specular, RT at half res for roughness < r_rt, hit radiance from previous frame's lit screen if visible else hash-grid cache/area light, A-trous upscaling denoiser, dual-source temporal (surface + hit point, blend by brightness difference), cleanup pass (firefly by counting brighter neighbours).
  Numbers: GI-1.1 glossy paper, RX 7900 XTX at 1080p, r_rt=0.2, diffuse fallback 0.6: RT reflection 0.18-0.28 ms (half-res), denoise+upscale 0.46-0.96 ms, specular/diffuse interpolation 0.31-0.37 ms; reflection overhead ~1 ms over GI-1.0 (3.1-3.7 ms). Hybrid Reflections sample: no published ms.
  Status: Public SDK / sample; GI-1.1 is research + SDK. Not confirmed shipped in a named game.
  Sources: <https://gpuopen.com/manuals/fidelityfx_sdk/samples/hybrid-reflections/>; <https://gpuopen.com/fidelityfx-hybrid-reflections/>; <https://gpuopen.com/manuals/fidelityfx_sdk/fidelityfx_sdk-page_techniques_denoiser/>; <https://github.com/GPUOpen-Effects/FidelityFX-Denoiser/blob/master/ffx-reflection-dnsr/ffx_denoiser_reflections_reproject.h>; <https://gpuopen.com/manuals/fidelityfx_sdk/fidelityfx_sdk-struct_ffxclassifierreflectiondispatchdescription/>; <https://gpuopen.com/download/SA2023_RealTimeReflection.pdf>; <https://gpuopen.com/learn/gi-1-1-glossy-reflection-rendering/>
- **AMD FSR Ray Regeneration.** ML denoiser decoupled from upscaling; denoises direct/indirect diffuse and specular, AO, specular occlusion; inputs include 2.5D MVs, oct normals+roughness, specular/diffuse albedo, hit distances.
  Numbers: RX 9070 XT at 1080p: 1 signal 2.63 ms, 2 signals 3.05 ms, 4 signals 4.09 ms; 267-394 MB VRAM at 1080p.
  Status: Shipped SDK (2025); requires RX 9000 (RDNA4), DX12 SM6.6, Windows 11 - not usable on an RTX GPU or Vulkan.
  Sources: <https://gpuopen.com/manuals/fsr_sdk/techniques/denoising/>
- **Frostbite - Battlefield V RT reflections (EA DICE, 2018).** Variable Rate Tracing per tile (ray budget by normalized importance, more rays on water/grazing angles), ray binning by direction/screen offset, screen-space hybrid (hierarchical SS trace first, RT on miss), defrag of hits, per-cell light lists at hit, then SSSR-style BRDF ratio spatial filter (up to ~13-16 taps), temporal filter, and an 'image filter' post blur sized by a LUT of {angle, roughness} to kernel width/height.
  Numbers: Pass costs from the slides: VRT 0.37, generate 0.19, binning 0.15, SS hybrid 0.36, intersect/material 1.98, defrag 0.08, lighting 0.46, spatial 1.45, temporal 0.24, image filter 1.00 = 6.29 ms total. GPU/resolution not stated in slide text (2018, Turing era).
  Status: Shipped (Battlefield V, 2018).
  Sources: <https://developer.download.nvidia.com/video/gputechconf/gtc/2019/presentation/s91023-it-just-works-ray-traced-reflections-in-battlefield-v.pdf>; <https://gdcvault.com/play/1026282/It-Just-Works-Ray-Traced>
- **Frostbite SSSR (2015) / EA SEED PICA PICA (2018).** SSSR: GGX importance-sampled rays at half resolution; each pixel reuses neighbours' rays with BRDF/pdf reweighting (ratio estimator), temporal filter reprojecting with the reflected hit, luminance-weighted resolve for firefly robustness. PICA PICA: DXR reflections at reduced resolution with SSSR-style spatial reconstruction, temporal accumulation with variance clipping, bilateral cleanup, 'reactive multi-scale mean estimator'; GDC 2018 talk introduced dual-source (surface + hit-point) reprojection.
  Numbers: Not extracted (60 MB chapter PDF, slides not fetched).
  Status: SSSR shipped (Frostbite titles, e.g. Mirror's Edge Catalyst); PICA PICA is a demo.
  Sources: <https://www.ea.com/frostbite/news/stochastic-screen-space-reflections>; <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2015/>; <https://www.ea.com/seed/news/hybrid-rendering-for-real-time-ray-tracing>
- **Cyberpunk 2077 RT: Overdrive (REDengine + NVIDIA).** Path tracing: ReSTIR DI (RTXDI) for direct light, multi-bounce diffuse and specular paths, ReSTIR GI added in 2.1, NRD denoising and later DLSS RR. Emissive/sky MIS is a cheap heuristic: BRDF-sampled paths weighted by (1 - roughness^2), ReSTIR DI by roughness^2 (a 'poor man's MIS'). ReSTIR described as a sophisticated pre-blur that lets the denoiser use shorter history and smaller radius.
  Numbers: Up to ~250 lights per typical scene; candidate/light ratio matters (1/25 vs 1/5). No reflection ms published in the course slides.
  Status: Shipped (2023+).
  Sources: <https://intro-to-restir.cwyman.org/presentations/2023ReSTIR_Course_Cyberpunk_2077_Integration.pdf>; <https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/on-demand/session/gdc24-gdc1002/>
- **Metro Exodus Enhanced Edition (4A Engine).** RT reflections for glossy/mirror surfaces where fine detail matters, complementing SSR; hits use the same low-LOD material system as the RTGI pipeline but the full PBR model for analytic lights (deferred lighting of hit positions); rough specular is reconstructed from the RTGI spherical harmonics instead of dedicated rays.
  Numbers: No reflection timings published.
  Status: Shipped (2021).
  Sources: <https://www.4a-games.com.mt/4a-dna/in-depth-technical-dive-into-metro-exodus-pc-enhanced-edition>
- **Insomniac (Spider-Man Remastered / Miles Morales / Ratchet & Clank).** Secondary sources only: RT reflections at reduced (quarter) resolution in 60 fps modes with heavy reuse of past-frame data assuming a mostly static world; simplified geometry and lighting in the reflection world. PC ports: Medium = half-res, High = full-res reflections.
  Numbers: None published by Insomniac found.
  Status: Shipped (PS5 2020+, PC 2022+).
  Sources: <https://www.resetera.com/threads/df-inside-ratchet-and-clank-rift-apart-on-ps5-the-insomniac-technology-breakdown.440950/>; <https://gamingbolt.com/marvels-spider-man-miles-morales-update-improves-ray-traced-reflections-in-performance-rt-mode>
- **Unity HDRP ray-traced reflections.** Built on the SSR override. Modes: Ray Tracing or Mixed (SSR ray march, then RT). Performance mode: half res (one ray per 4 px) or full res (1 rpp) + spatio-temporal denoiser (radius, anti-flicker). Quality mode: N samples, multiple bounces. Minimum smoothness / fade, max ray length, last-bounce fallback to probes/sky, 'Clamp Value' on the pre-exposed value against fireflies.
  Numbers: No ms published in docs.
  Status: Shipped engine feature.
  Sources: <https://docs.unity3d.com/Packages/com.unity.render-pipelines.high-definition@17.0/manual/reference-screen-space-reflection.html>
- **Assassin's Creed Shadows (Anvil).** Dynamic RT GI for diffuse and specular; screen-space ray marching first, then RT; RT reflections on PS5 Pro (Quality/Balanced), with half-width, full-height resolution reported (secondary).
  Numbers: Not extracted (slides 14 MB).
  Status: Shipped (2025).
  Sources: <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/Advances%202025%20-%20Raytracing%20the%20world%20of%20Assassin's%20Creed%20Shadows.pdf>; <https://blog.playstation.com/2025/04/11/assassins-creed-shadows-ubisoft-deep-dives-into-ps5-pro-updates/>
- **Alan Wake 2 (Northlight), Indiana Jones and the Great Circle / Doom: The Dark Ages (id Tech), Avatar: Frontiers of Pandora (Snowdrop).** All ship RT reflections (AW2 opaque + transparent; Doom TDA RT reflections on PC; Avatar RT reflections on all platforms) and PT modes with DLSS RR on PC. Reflection denoiser and hit-shading details were not found in readable primary sources.
  Numbers: AW2 PS5 Pro quality mode 2176x1224 internal + PSSR (secondary). No reflection ms found.
  Status: Shipped.
  Sources: <https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/on-demand/session/gdc24-gdc1003/>; <https://www.remedygames.com/article/how-northlight-makes-alan-wake-2-shine>; <https://media.gdcvault.com/gdc2024/Slides/GDC+slide+presentations/Kuenlib_Quentin_Raytracing_In_Snowdrop.pdf>; <https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/geforce/news/doom-the-dark-ages-path-tracing-dlss-ray-reconstruction-update/>
- **Bevy Solari (indie data point, 2026).** Specular GI via 0-3 bounce path tracing with bounded VNDF; true mirror BRDF (delta pdf handled separately for sampling vs MIS); PSR through mirror bounces for smooth metals only; ReSTIR GI skipped for smooth metals; world-cache termination when the path cone footprint exceeds the cell size; DLSS RR for denoising. Author saw no benefit from specular motion vectors with RR.
  Numbers: None for specular.
  Status: Shipped in an open-source engine release.
  Sources: <https://jms55.github.io/posts/2026-04-12-solari-bevy-0-19/>

### B.2 The techniques behind them

- **VNDF importance sampling variants (Heitz 2018; spherical caps 2023; bounded VNDF 2023)** (shipped; shipped in Bevy Solari (bounded VNDF), NRD sample (VNDF v3); confidence high). Sample the GGX distribution of visible normals, then reflect. Spherical caps: sample a cap of reflected directions and take the halfway vector (simpler). Bounded VNDF: shrink the sampled cap so directions that would reflect below the horizon are never generated, with an analytic pdf.
  Fixes: Wasted/rejected rays and variance at roughness where the lobe crosses the horizon; NRD requires a sampler that never casts rays into the surface ('VNDF v3') so hitT stays in-lobe. Cost: Spherical caps: up to 39% faster sampling routine than Heitz 2018 (paper benchmark, ALU only). Bounded VNDF: comparable ALU, variance reduction largest for rough, low-anisotropy surfaces; no frame ms published. Fails: The pdf used by the ratio estimator/resolve must be the pdf of the sampler actually used; a mismatch biases the reuse weights (RageV RT2-23 notes the resolve writes the old sampler's density).
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no. Pure sampling improvement. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Trivial; shader-only.
  Needs: Anisotropic alpha, view vector in tangent space.
  Sources: <https://jcgt.org/published/0007/04/01/>; <https://arxiv.org/abs/2306.05044>; <https://gpuopen.com/download/Bounded_VNDF_Sampling_for_Smith-GGX_Reflections.pdf>; <https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3651291>
- **Roughness cutoff for traced reflections; rough specular from the GI/radiance cache** (shipped; shipped in UE5 Lumen, Metro Exodus EE, AMD GI-1.1 (SDK); confidence high). Trace dedicated reflection rays only below a max roughness; above it, evaluate specular from the diffuse final gather / screen probes / SH (Lumen reuses Screen Probe Gather above 0.4; AMD GI-1.1 traces below 0.2, uses 8x8 screen probes up to 0.6 and diffuse above; Metro uses RTGI SH).
