  Fixes: Cost and noise on rough surfaces where ray divergence is highest and detail is lowest. Cost: Saves the trace + denoise on rough pixels; AMD: whole reflection overhead ~1 ms at 1080p on RX 7900 XTX with this split. Fails: Brightness/sharpness seam at the cutoff if the two estimators disagree (RageV: probe ~1/3 brighter than the trace); probe angular resolution too low for glossy.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no.  Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Good; classification is a compute pass.
  Needs: A GI representation that stores directional radiance (screen probes, SH, radiance cache) consistent with the traced result.
  Sources: <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/unreal-engine/lumen-performance-guide-for-unreal-engine>; <https://gpuopen.com/download/SA2023_RealTimeReflection.pdf>; <https://www.4a-games.com.mt/4a-dna/in-depth-technical-dive-into-metro-exodus-pc-enhanced-edition>
- **Tile classification and variable-rate / variance-guided tracing** (shipped; shipped in Battlefield V, AMD FidelityFX SDK; confidence high). Per tile, classify by roughness (mirror, glossy, rough, none) and allocate rays per quad (full rate for mirrors, quarter for glossy); optionally raise the rate where the temporal variance of the previous frame is above a threshold (AMD) or by a normalized importance map (BFV: more rays on water and grazing angles).
  Fixes: Spends rays where the reflection is sharp or unconverged; skips non-reflective tiles for the denoiser too. Cost: BFV VRT pass 0.37 ms (GPU/res not stated). AMD classifier: no ms published. Fails: Budget read at the wrong screen location for moving content (RageV defect noted in RT2-23); fixed thresholds (vrtVarianceThreshold) are tuned values.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: partly. Reduced-rate tiles rely on the denoiser's spatial/temporal reconstruction, not TAA, if the denoiser has its own history. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Good; indirect dispatch over a tile list.
  Sources: <https://developer.download.nvidia.com/video/gputechconf/gtc/2019/presentation/s91023-it-just-works-ray-traced-reflections-in-battlefield-v.pdf>; <https://gpuopen.com/manuals/fidelityfx_sdk/fidelityfx_sdk-struct_ffxclassifierreflectiondispatchdescription/>
- **Screen trace first, RT on miss (hybrid) and screen-space reuse of hit radiance** (shipped; shipped in UE5 Lumen, Battlefield V, Unity HDRP (Mixed), Assassin's Creed Shadows; confidence high). March the depth pyramid along the reflection ray; accept on-screen hits and shade them from the already-lit frame (Lumen, BFV, AMD, HDRP Mixed, AC Shadows). AMD GI-1.1 additionally looks up the previous frame's lit pixel when an RT hit is visible on screen, falling back to a hash-grid cache.
  Fixes: Cheaper, full-quality (all lights, GI, multi-bounce) hit shading for on-screen content; removes hit-shading noise for those rays. Cost: BFV SS hybrid pass 0.36 ms; AMD RT reflection pass 0.18-0.28 ms at half 1080p on RX 7900 XTX including screen reuse. Fails: Screen-space hits can pick up occluded/thin-geometry mismatches (depth thickness), view-dependent previous-frame radiance is reused for another view direction, disocclusions change the source.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no.  Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Good: compute pass before the ray query; RT only for failed rays (compaction/defrag helps occupancy).
  Sources: <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/lumen-technical-details-in-unreal-engine>; <https://developer.download.nvidia.com/video/gputechconf/gtc/2019/presentation/s91023-it-just-works-ray-traced-reflections-in-battlefield-v.pdf>; <https://gpuopen.com/download/SA2023_RealTimeReflection.pdf>
- **Hit shading: hit lighting vs surface/radiance cache (Lumen Surface Cache, SHaRC, NRC, AMD hash grid)** (shipped; shipped in UE5 Lumen, Metro Exodus EE, Battlefield V, Half-Life 2 RTX (NRC); confidence medium). Either evaluate the material and lights (with shadow rays) at the hit, or read a world-space cache. SHaRC rule: use the cache only when the lobe's cone footprint at the hit (2*len*sqrt(0.5*a^2/(1-a^2))) exceeds the voxel size, otherwise keep tracing. Metro/BFV: deferred lighting of hit G-buffer with analytic lights (per-cell light lists).
  Fixes: Hit-shading noise (stochastic NEE at the hit makes even a roughness-0 mirror noisy) and hit cost/divergence. Cost: Lumen hit lighting 'greatly increases GPU cost' (Epic, no ms); secondary claim ~5x surface cache on PS5 (weak). BFV per-cell light-list lighting 0.46 ms. SHaRC memory 160 MiB (256 MiB with directional) for 2^22 cells, no ms published. NRC ~2.6 ms at 1080p (paper); HL2 RTX reports +15% performance from fewer bounces. Fails: Caches are blurry/biased for mirrors, can leak, and are view-independent unless directional; hit lighting is noisy if lights are sampled stochastically with 1 sample.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no.  Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Good for caches and deferred hit shading (write hit record, shade in compute). SER needs the RT pipeline (hit objects), not inline queries (medium confidence), so with inline queries coherence comes from ray binning/sorting (BFV) or deferred shading.
  Sources: <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/hardware-ray-tracing-in-unreal-engine>; <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/SHARC>; <https://arxiv.org/abs/2106.12372>; <https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/geforce/news/rtx-remix-half-life-2-rtx-demo-launching-march-18/>; <https://www.4a-games.com.mt/4a-dna/in-depth-technical-dive-into-metro-exodus-pc-enhanced-edition>
- **Albedo demodulation (split-sum) before denoising** (shipped; shipped in NRD integrations, AMD GI-1.1; confidence high). Denoise specularRadiance*BRDF/envBRDF (pre-integrated specular albedo) and multiply back by envBRDF after; AMD GI-1.1 denoises incoming radiance and applies a GGX directional-albedo LUT.
  Fixes: Keeps material/texture detail and Fresnel out of the filtered signal so the blur and history clamp act on lighting only. Cost: Negligible (LUT fetch). Fails: Mismatch between the LUT and the actual BRDF/sampler; roughness-varying textures still need geometry-aware weights.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no.  Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Trivial.
  Sources: <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD>; <https://gpuopen.com/download/SA2023_RealTimeReflection.pdf>
- **Spatial ratio estimator / BRDF reweighting (ray reuse)** (shipped; shipped in Frostbite (SSSR, Battlefield V), UE5 Lumen 5.4; confidence high). Each pixel re-evaluates neighbours' ray results with its own BRDF divided by the neighbour ray's pdf, normalized by the sum of weights (Stachowiak 2015). Lumen 5.4: 5 samples, 8 px kernel, roughness scale 1; BFV up to ~13-16 taps.
  Fixes: Turns 1 ray/pixel into several effective samples per pixel, keeps specular elongation. Cost: BFV spatial filter 1.45 ms (largest filter cost); Lumen not published. Fails: Needs the true pdf of the sampler; bright rare samples leak to neighbours (fireflies spread into halos); fixed disc radius blurs mirrors. Needs geometry weights at depth edges.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no.  Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Good; compute pass.
  Sources: <https://www.ea.com/frostbite/news/stochastic-screen-space-reflections>; <https://indxzero.github.io/ue544cvarwiki/articles/r.lumen.reflections.screenspacereconstruction.numsamples/>; <https://developer.download.nvidia.com/video/gputechconf/gtc/2019/presentation/s91023-it-just-works-ray-traced-reflections-in-battlefield-v.pdf>
- **Hit-distance prepass: minimum in-lobe hit distance for tracking (NRD)** (shipped; shipped in NRD ReBLUR; confidence high). A spatial prepass (default radius 50 px for specular, limited to a lobe fraction 0.3) stochastically accepts neighbours by a geometry/normal/material/hit-distance weight and keeps the MINIMUM non-zero hitT as 'hitDistForTracking', stored separately from the averaged hitT used for filtering. Misses/zero hitT are excluded. hitT must be in-lobe, not divided by pdf, exclude the primary distance; with NEE use the indirect hitT for both direct and indirect specular.
  Fixes: A depth average mixes foreground and background reflectors (and sky), placing the virtual image where nothing is: history fetched from the wrong place at reflected depth edges. Cost: Part of REBLUR (2.55 ms total, 1440p, RTX 4080); not published separately. Fails: Min biases toward the nearest reflector on edges (preferred: less ghosting); needs a valid sample within the footprint (3x3 hit distance reconstruction at <1 rpp).
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no.  Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Good.
  Sources: <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD/blob/master/Shaders/REBLUR_Common_SpatialFilter.hlsli>; <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD/blob/master/Shaders/REBLUR_Config.hlsli>; <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD>
- **Virtual-motion (hit-point) reprojection with signed curvature** (shipped; shipped in NRD ReBLUR/ReLAX, AMD FFX Reflection Denoiser; confidence high). Place a virtual point along the view ray behind the surface at the (tracking) hit distance, corrected by local curvature (mirror equation; NRD GetXvirtual, curvature estimated from neighbour pixels by line-plane intersection along the motion direction, signed, with negative curvature clamped to limit acceleration), then project with the previous view-projection. FFX: extend the view ray by surface depth + ray length and reproject.
  Fixes: Reflections slide over the surface under camera motion; surface motion alone smears them. Cost: Inside REBLUR TA; not separately published. Fails: Wrong for rough lobes (motion is between surface and virtual), for moving reflected objects (camera-only), and on high curvature (thin chrome poles) where the image lies near the focal point; normal-map curvature gives nonsense.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no.  Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Good; needs previous matrices and a geometric normal/curvature lane.
  Sources: <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD/blob/master/Shaders/REBLUR_TemporalAccumulation.cs.hlsl>; <https://github.com/GPUOpen-Effects/FidelityFX-Denoiser/blob/master/ffx-reflection-dnsr/ffx_denoiser_reflections_reproject.h>
- **Two history candidates blended by confidence (dual-source reprojection)** (shipped; shipped in NRD, AMD FFX denoiser, SEED PICA PICA (demo); confidence high). NRD: virtualHistoryAmount blends surface-motion and virtual-motion history by comparing the accumulation each would support; virtual confidence = lobe-sized normal test (widened with history), roughness test, parallax test (distance between candidate UVs vs hit-distance-to-lobe-spread in pixels), a prev-prev normal test along the virtual motion, material ID; jitter-friendly (weights relaxed when travel < 1 px). Stachowiak 2018 / AMD GI-1.1: blend by brightness difference to the current neighbourhood, then clamp. FFX: pick by normal similarity, roughness match, radiance vs neighbourhood variance.
  Fixes: Neither candidate is right everywhere (glossy lobes, curved surfaces, depth edges); picking one pops. Cost: Inside the temporal pass (REBLUR 2.55 ms total; FFX/AMD 0.46-0.96 ms denoise+upscale at 1080p RX 7900 XTX). Fails: Colour-similarity blends accept a wrong history that happens to match in colour and reject a right one under lighting change; NRD confidence stack has many tuned constants.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no.  Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Good.
  Sources: <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD/blob/master/Shaders/REBLUR_TemporalAccumulation.cs.hlsl>; <https://gpuopen.com/download/SA2023_RealTimeReflection.pdf>; <https://github.com/GPUOpen-Effects/FidelityFX-Denoiser/blob/master/ffx-reflection-dnsr/ffx_denoiser_reflections_reproject.h>
- **History length limited by parallax and roughness (single counter)** (shipped; shipped in NRD ReBLUR; confidence high). NRD keeps one accumulation counter per candidate (6 bits, max 63; default max 30). Surface-history confidence compares the parallax angle (pixels moved * pixel size / distance) with the lobe half-angle (scaled by NoV); max frames = maxAccumulatedFrameNum * confidence. A roughness curve (GetSpecMagicCurve) shortens history for low roughness, with an optional responsive-accumulation floor. External confidence: historyLength *= lerp(confidence, 1, 1/(1+historyLength)).
  Fixes: Ghosting on mirrors/glossy while keeping long history where the reflection cannot have moved within its own blur. Cost: Part of TA pass. Fails: Frame-rate compensation constant (REBLUR_FRAME_RATE_COMPENSATION = frameRateScale * 0.75) ties the rule to a reference frame rate - conflicts with the owner's rule 2 unless expressed in time.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no.  Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Good.
  Sources: <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD/blob/master/Shaders/REBLUR_TemporalAccumulation.cs.hlsl>; <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD/blob/master/Include/NRDSettings.h>; <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD>
- **Occlusion-aware Catmull-Rom history sampling with bilinear fallback** (shipped; shipped in NRD, AMD FFX denoiser; confidence high). Gather the 2x2 (or 4x4) previous viewZ/normals/material; weight each tap by a plane-distance test (disocclusionThreshold 0.01 of viewZ, slope-scaled) and material match; use Catmull-Rom when all taps are valid, otherwise re-weighted bilinear. FFX: on low confidence, 3x3 search for the best-matching neighbour, then weighted 2x2.
  Fixes: History fetched across a depth edge (pole against background) and progressive blur of mirror history from repeated bilinear resampling. Cost: Small; CatRom can be disabled in NRD performance mode. Fails: Thin geometry needs relaxed thresholds (NRD disocclusionThresholdAlternate 0.05 via strand material ID).
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no.  Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Good.
  Sources: <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD/blob/master/Shaders/REBLUR_Config.hlsli>; <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD/blob/master/Include/NRDSettings.h>; <https://github.com/GPUOpen-Effects/FidelityFX-Denoiser/blob/master/ffx-reflection-dnsr/ffx_denoiser_reflections_reproject.h>
- **Fast-history clamp / anti-lag** (shipped; shipped in NRD ReBLUR/ReLAX; confidence high). A second, short luminance history (NRD maxFastAccumulatedFrameNum 6 vs main 30) clamps the long history to fast mean +/- 2 sigma (fastHistoryClampingSigmaScale 2, radius 2 px); REBLUR TS antilag compares history to neighbourhood stats (luminanceSigmaScale 2, sensitivity 3) and resets speed. ReLAX: accelerationAmount 0.3, reset on spatial/temporal sigma.
  Fixes: The grain vs ghosting trade-off: long history for low grain, bounded by a short history that cannot outlive a change. Cost: Small (extra luma channel). Fails: Luma-only clamp misses chroma changes; sigma scales are convention constants.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no.  Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Good.
  Sources: <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD/blob/master/Include/NRDSettings.h>; <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD/blob/master/Shaders/REBLUR_TemporalStabilization.cs.hlsl>
- **History fix for young/disoccluded pixels** (shipped; shipped in NRD ReBLUR/ReLAX; confidence high). For the first historyFixFrameNum (3) frames after a reset, rebuild the pixel from a sparse 5x5 kernel with a large base stride (14 px) that shrinks as history grows, with geometry/normal weights; in REBLUR TS young pixels' luma is also clamped to M1*(1.2 + 1/(1+n)).
  Fixes: Raw 1-spp grain and white specks at disocclusions (pole silhouettes during fast swings) that would otherwise be left to TAA. Cost: Runs only on young pixels; not separately published. Fails: Blurry for a few frames at disocclusions; stride constant is tuned.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no.  Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Good.
  Sources: <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD/blob/master/Include/NRDSettings.h>; <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD/blob/master/Shaders/REBLUR_TemporalStabilization.cs.hlsl>
- **Firefly suppression that is not scene-tuned** (shipped; shipped in NRD, AMD GI-1.1, UE5 Lumen (absolute clamp), Unity HDRP (absolute clamp); confidence high). Relative forms: NRD anti-firefly clamps the centre against neighbourhood statistics (radius 4, sigma scale 2; fireflySuppressorMinRelativeScale 2; hitT clamped to history*(1.2+1/(n+1))); AMD cleanup marks a sample as a firefly by counting brighter vs darker neighbours (after Mara 2017) and replaces it with a 3x3 bilateral of unmarked neighbours; source-side: floor lobe-selection probabilities (~0.05, energy increase under 20-30x). Absolute forms, for contrast: Lumen MaxRayIntensity 100 (pre-exposed), HDRP Clamp Value.
  Fixes: White specks from rare bright samples (lamp hits on rough floor, NEE with tiny pdf). Cost: NRD anti-firefly +0-2% of ReBLUR; AMD cleanup part of 0.46-0.96 ms. Fails: Relative clamps remove real small highlights (glints) if applied to converged pixels; absolute clamps are brightness-dependent (break the owner's rule 2) and bias bright scenes.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no.  Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Good.
  Sources: <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD/blob/master/Shaders/REBLUR_Config.hlsli>; <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD>; <https://gpuopen.com/download/SA2023_RealTimeReflection.pdf>; <https://indxzero.github.io/ue544cvarwiki/articles/r.lumen.reflections.maxrayintensity/>
- **Spatial blur sized by lobe footprint and hit distance, shrinking with history (ReBLUR) or variance-guided A-trous (ReLAX/SVGF)** (shipped; shipped in NRD, AMD FFX, Battlefield V; confidence high). ReBLUR: radius from roughness, normalized hitT and frustum size, scaled from maxBlurRadius 30 to minBlurRadius 1 as history grows; per-tap plane distance (planeDistanceSensitivity 0.02 of distance), lobe-angle normal weight (lobeAngleFraction 0.15), roughness weight (0.15), hit-distance weight, material ID. ReLAX: A-trous iterations (5) with luminance edge stopping on propagated variance. FFX: 15 samples in 7x7, variance guided. BFV: image filter kernel from a {angle, roughness} LUT.
  Fixes: Residual noise without blurring mirrors; variance stops the blur where the texel is already converged. Cost: BFV image filter 1.00 ms; NRD part of 2.55 ms. Fails: Fixed uncertainty gates cause per-texel on/off blur (speckle); radius in metres or hard ramps are scale-dependent.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no.  Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Good; compute with shared memory.
  Sources: <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD/blob/master/Include/NRDSettings.h>; <https://gpuopen.com/manuals/fidelityfx_sdk/fidelityfx_sdk-page_techniques_denoiser/>; <https://developer.download.nvidia.com/video/gputechconf/gtc/2019/presentation/s91023-it-just-works-ray-traced-reflections-in-battlefield-v.pdf>
- **Temporal gradients / history confidence input (A-SVGF)** (shipped; shipped in Quake II RTX (A-SVGF); confidence medium). Re-shade a sparse subset of last frame's samples with this frame's scene and lights (same random numbers), reconstruct the temporal gradient, and set the per-pixel history weight from it. NRD accepts such a signal as IN_SPEC_CONFIDENCE ('highly recommended' to fight lag).
  Fixes: Detects that the reflected content changed (moving car, light switched) under any motion, with no scene thresholds; addresses lag and ghosting directly instead of shortening history everywhere. Cost: Not extracted; spends part of the ray budget on gradient samples. Fails: Needs deterministic re-evaluation of the previous sample (same seeds, previous TLAS or approximation); sparse gradients need reconstruction.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no.  Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Fits (ray queries in compute); needs previous-frame geometry/light data.
  Sources: <https://cg.ivd.kit.edu/publications/2018/adaptive_temporal_filtering/adaptive_temporal_filtering.pdf>; <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD>
- **Primary Surface Replacement (PSR) for mirrors** (shipped; shipped in Cyberpunk 2077 RT Overdrive (secondary), Portal with RTX (secondary), Bevy Solari; confidence medium). Follow mirror bounces to the first non-mirror surface and write its (virtual) position, normal (mirrored), viewZ, motion vector and material into the primary guide buffers, so denoiser and AA treat the reflected surface as primary with exact motion.
  Fixes: Smear/jitter of mirror reflections under motion; gives the AA pass real motion vectors for reflected content. Cost: Extra bounce tracing on mirror pixels; no ms published. Fails: Exact only for flat mirrors; on curved mirrors NRD recommends 2x-10x larger disocclusion thresholds; limited to near-zero roughness.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: partly. Improves the AA pass's reprojection rather than depending on it to hide noise. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Good; inline query loop through mirror bounces.
  Sources: <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD>; <https://jms55.github.io/posts/2026-04-12-solari-bevy-0-19/>
- **DLSS Ray Reconstruction (neural denoiser + upscaler)** (shipped; shipped in Cyberpunk 2077, Alan Wake 2, Indiana Jones and the Great Circle, Doom: The Dark Ages; confidence high). Replaces hand-tuned denoisers and the DLSS upscaler with one network fed noisy color and guides; uses specular MVs or specular hitT + matrices for reflections.
  Fixes: Everything at once, the quality leader in PT titles. Cost: Not published in ms by NVIDIA; secondary reviews say small on RTX 4090/5090. Fails: Vendor-locked; is the final temporal/upscale pass (the owner's rule 3 forbids depending on it); guide buffers must match training.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: yes. It IS the TAA/upscaler; noise hiding depends on it entirely. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Runs on RTX 50 with Vulkan via Streamline/NGX; optional arm only.
  Sources: <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/Streamline/blob/main/docs/ProgrammingGuideDLSS_RR.md>; <https://github.com/nvpro-samples/vk_denoise_dlssrr>
- **FSR Ray Regeneration** (shipped; shipped in not shipped; confidence high). AMD ML denoiser decoupled from upscaling; multiple signal types including direct and indirect specular.
  Fixes: Denoising quality comparable to neural RR on AMD. Cost: RX 9070 XT, 1080p: 2.63 ms (1 signal), 3.05 ms (2), 4.09 ms (4). Fails: RDNA4 + DX12 only; cannot run on an RTX 5070 Ti Laptop or in Vulkan.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no.  Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Not applicable (DX12, AMD only).
  Sources: <https://gpuopen.com/manuals/fsr_sdk/techniques/denoising/>
- **Geometric specular antialiasing (roughness widened by normal variance)** (shipped; shipped in UE (normal curvature to roughness), Unity HDRP (geometric specular AA), Square Enix titles; confidence high). Widen GGX roughness per pixel from screen-space normal derivatives / normal-map variance (projected-space NDF filtering, Tokuyoshi & Kaplanyan 2021; UE 'normal curvature to roughness'), so sub-pixel normal detail cannot produce a lobe narrower than the pixel footprint. Apply before ray generation and in the resolve.
  Fixes: Specular aliasing/sparkle on normal-mapped chrome and a puddle roughness map when the camera moves; removes a noise source the denoiser cannot fix. Cost: A few ALU ops per pixel; not published in ms. Fails: Slightly rougher look at distance (by design); must be consistent between raster highlight and traced reflection.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no.  Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Trivial; G-buffer/shader change.
  Sources: <https://www.jcgt.org/published/0010/02/02/paper.pdf>; <https://www.jp.square-enix.com/tech/library/pdf/ImprovedGeometricSpecularAA.pdf>
- **Ray-cone texture LOD at reflection hits** (shipped; shipped in not shipped; confidence medium). Propagate a cone (spread angle from the pixel footprint plus curvature/roughness) along the reflection ray and pick the hit texture mip from the cone width at the hit.
  Fixes: Texture aliasing/shimmer in mirror reflections of detailed surfaces under motion (speckle not caused by the lighting). Cost: Negligible (few ALU per hit); paper numbers not extracted. Fails: Curvature estimates on normal-mapped surfaces over-blur; must use geometric curvature.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no.  Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Good; manual SampleGrad/SampleLevel in the inline hit shading.
  Sources: <https://jcgt.org/published/0010/01/01/>
- **ReSTIR for specular (ReSTIR GI / ReSTIR PT)** (shipped; shipped in Cyberpunk 2077 RT Overdrive (ReSTIR DI/GI); confidence high). Reuse path samples spatially and temporally via reservoirs; ReSTIR GI reconnects at the secondary vertex (treats it as Lambertian); ReSTIR PT uses hybrid shift (random replay until a reconnectable vertex) to handle glossy.
  Fixes: Noise in multi-bounce and rough/glossy indirect. Cost: ReSTIR PT Enhanced (2026): 2-3x faster than ReSTIR PT; reservoir memory 431 MB to 265 MB at 1080p. No reflection-only ms. Fails: RTXDI's ReSTIR GI docs: poor on low-roughness specular (sparser, stable noise), recommend using the raw signal on smooth surfaces; correlation artifacts; memory.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: partly. Still needs a denoiser; shipped uses pair it with NRD or RR. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Fits (compute + ray queries) but heavy at 2560x1600 on a laptop GPU.
  Sources: <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/RTXDI/blob/main/Doc/RestirGI.md>; <https://research.nvidia.com/labs/rtr/publication/lin2026restirptenhanced/>; <https://intro-to-restir.cwyman.org/presentations/2023ReSTIR_Course_Cyberpunk_2077_Integration.pdf>
- **MIS between light sampling and BRDF-sampled reflection rays hitting emitters** (shipped; shipped in Cyberpunk 2077 RT Overdrive (heuristic), RTXDI SDK (proper MIS); confidence medium). When lamp highlights come from light sampling (NEE/ReSTIR DI/analytic) and reflection rays can also hit the emissive fitting, weight both with MIS using the pdfs of both strategies (RTXDI supports proper MIS); Cyberpunk uses a cheap heuristic blend by roughness (BRDF*(1-r^2), ReSTIR DI*r^2).
  Fixes: Double counting or missing lamp reflections, and noise: NEE is noisy on low-roughness lobes, BRDF sampling is noisy for small bright emitters on rough lobes. Cost: Extra pdf evaluation; RTXDI's full MIS 'can get expensive' (course slide). Fails: The roughness heuristic is not the general form; both halves must use consistent emitter geometry and pdfs.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no.  Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Good.
  Sources: <https://intro-to-restir.cwyman.org/presentations/2023ReSTIR_Course_Cyberpunk_2077_Integration.pdf>; <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/RTXDI>
- **Temporal stabilization pass (REBLUR TS)** (shipped; shipped in NRD ReBLUR; confidence high). After spatial filtering, a second temporal pass reprojects stabilized history with the same surface/virtual blend (virtualHistoryAmount), clamps it to a neighbourhood luma box (M1/M2 from shared memory), applies antilag, and accumulates up to maxStabilizedFrameNum.
  Fixes: Residual low-frequency shimmer after blur; would otherwise be left to TAA. Cost: Part of REBLUR; not separate. Fails: Luma-only box; another history to reproject correctly.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no.  Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Good.
  Sources: <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD/blob/master/Shaders/REBLUR_TemporalStabilization.cs.hlsl>

### B.3 The frontier (published, not yet shipped)

- **Two-level radiance caching for glossy reflections (AMD GI-1.1)** (, 2023; evidence moderate). Glossy reflections for about 1 ms extra on top of GI-1.0 at 1080p, by tracing only low-roughness pixels at half resolution, reusing the previous frame's lit screen colour at on-screen hits, a hash-grid cache otherwise, and an A-trous upscaling denoiser with dual-source temporal accumulation.
  Evidence: Measured on an RX 7900 XTX at 1080p across 4 scenes: RT 0.18-0.28 ms, denoise and upscale 0.46-0.96 ms. The only comparison is against a path tracer: darker, because the cache holds direct light only. Could beat: RageV's full-resolution trace plus full resolve for every pixel (10.6 ms at 1600x900), on cost for glossy tiles. Cost: ~1 ms at 1080p (RX 7900 XTX) Risk: Half-resolution upscaling darkened RageV's bridge cables before (RT-3.1). There is no comparison against NRD and no measurement of fast-motion stability.
  Sources: <https://gpuopen.com/download/SA2023_RealTimeReflection.pdf>; <https://gpuopen.com/learn/gi-1-1-glossy-reflection-rendering/>
- **Bounded VNDF sampling** (, 2023; evidence strong). Unbiased: generates fewer rejected, below-horizon reflection directions than spherical-cap VNDF, which lowers variance on rough, low-anisotropy surfaces.
  Evidence: Peer-reviewed (SIGGRAPH Asia 2023 TC; extended in PACMCGIT 2024) with variance comparisons. Used in Bevy Solari. Could beat: Plain VNDF or spherical caps at the same ray count, on grain in the 0.2-0.6 roughness band. Cost: About the same ALU as spherical caps. Risk: The gain is small on near-mirrors. The resolve must use the new pdf.
  Sources: <https://gpuopen.com/download/Bounded_VNDF_Sampling_for_Smith-GGX_Reflections.pdf>; <https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3651291>
- **Temporally Reliable Motion Vectors (glossy reflections)** (, 2021; evidence weak). Motion vectors for glossy reflections and shadows that follow the cause of the effect, letting temporal reuse keep working where screen-space motion vectors fail.
  Evidence: Paper results on research scenes only. Not shipped. Could beat: Surface-only reprojection. Its gain over NRD-style virtual motion is unclear. Cost:  Risk: Extra passes and complexity; no production evidence.
  Sources: <https://zheng95z.github.io/publications/trmv21>
- **Neural denoisers (DLSS RR 4.5 transformer, FSR Ray Regeneration)** (, 2025; evidence strong). The best reflection stability and detail per ms in shipped path-traced games.
  Evidence: Shipped in many titles. FSR RR has published ms (2.63 ms for one signal at 1080p on an RX 9070 XT). DLSS RR has no official ms; reviews show about the same cost as the older model. Could beat: Any hand-built denoiser, on quality. Cost:  Risk: Breaks the owner's rules 3 and 4 as a dependency: RR is the TAA/upscaler, and FSR RR is AMD/DX12-only. Usable only as an optional comparison arm.
  Sources: <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/Streamline/blob/main/docs/ProgrammingGuideDLSS_RR.md>; <https://gpuopen.com/manuals/fsr_sdk/techniques/denoising/>
- **ReSTIR PT Enhanced** (, 2026; evidence moderate). 2-3x faster ReSTIR PT, more robust shifts (footprint-based reconnection), lower memory (265 MB at 1080p).
  Evidence: NVIDIA research paper (PACMCGIT 2026). Not shipped. Could beat: Nothing in RageV's mirror/glossy reflection tier. RTXDI's own docs say ReSTIR GI is poor on low-roughness specular; PT-level reuse may help rough specular only. Cost:  Risk: Cost at 2560x1600 on a laptop GPU; needs a denoiser anyway.
  Sources: <https://research.nvidia.com/labs/rtr/publication/lin2026restirptenhanced/>; <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/RTXDI/blob/main/Doc/RestirGI.md>
- **Neural radiance cache for hit shading (NRC)** (, 2021; evidence moderate). Terminates paths into a trained cache after a short prefix, cutting bounces and noise; Half-Life 2 RTX reports +15% performance.
  Evidence: Paper: about 2.6 ms overhead at 1080p. Shipped in Half-Life 2 RTX (NVIDIA-only neural shaders). Could beat: Stochastic hit lighting for glossy reflection hits, on noise. Cost: ~2.6 ms at 1080p (paper) Risk: Vendor-specific tensor path, training lag after scene changes, and too blurry for mirror hits.
  Sources: <https://arxiv.org/abs/2106.12372>; <https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/geforce/news/rtx-remix-half-life-2-rtx-demo-launching-march-18/>

### B.4 The survey's recommended baseline and candidates

The survey's view before the comparison looked at RageV's code. The roadmap's items took these as their starting baselines where the comparison and verification agreed.

- **Baseline: Match NRD ReBLUR's specular temporal design inside RageV's own denoiser (or build NRD's specular path as a reference arm). The parts: demodulated input; a prepass that tracks by the smallest in-lobe hit distance, with misses excluded; surface-motion and virtual-motion histories (virtual motion with signed geometric curvature), both fetched with occlusion-aware Catmull-Rom sampling that falls back to bilinear; a confidence blend (lobe-sized normal test, roughness test, parallax against hit distance, a second normal test one frame back, material or primitive ID); one history counter capped by parallax and roughness; a 6-frame fast history clamping the long history; rebuilding the first 3 frames of history; a relative firefly clamp; a luminance-clamped stabilisation pass.** Why: This is the most complete reflection denoiser that has shipped and that can be inspected (public source, Vulkan-capable), and it targets RageV's exact defects: history from the wrong place at depth edges (averaged depth, bilinear fetch), ghosting against grain (no fast clamp), and speckle on young pixels. RT2-23's 23a-23c already describe most of it. Shipped by: NVIDIA NRD (Cyberpunk 2077, Portal with RTX, other RTX titles), AMD FFX Reflection Denoiser (subset: dual reprojection, variance, temporal clip). Measured by: spin_measure.py --swing=15 --rate=2 pole/bumper/tube spot scores and grain score, against RageV v1 and against an NRD-reference arm at the same ray count; after-stop rows; edge_shake.py; parked_stats.py; the bridge's glint flicker. Cost from A,B,B,A timings at 2560x1600 on the 5070 Ti Laptop, against the published 2.55 ms (diffuse+specular, 1440p, RTX 4080) as a sanity bound.
- **Baseline: A roughness split: trace one VNDF (bounded or spherical-cap) ray per pixel only below a max roughness, and above it take specular from the stored or cached bounce light; one classification per tile; ray rate varied by tile (full on mirror tiles, reduced on glossy) with the denoiser rebuilding the rest.** Why: Every top engine does this (Lumen 0.4, AMD 0.2/0.6, Metro SH, BFV variable-rate tracing). It is the only route to RT2-27's 4 ms at native 2560x1600. Shipped by: UE5 Lumen, Battlefield V, Metro Exodus EE, AMD FidelityFX. Measured by: RT2-27's first arm (tile coverage per roughness class in the garage and bridge), a roughness-ramp scene with no seam from 0.2 to 0.8 against the reference renderer, and ms per pass.
- **Baseline: Hit shading with low noise: screen-space reuse of the lit frame for on-screen hits; otherwise deterministic analytic or baked light at the hit (lightmap or probe for baked lights, a shadow ray per realtime light, or a cache when the cone footprint is larger than a cache cell). Stochastic light sampling at mirror hits only with its own accumulation.** Why: At roughness near 0 the reflection ray is deterministic, so any remaining grain on chrome comes from hit shading. Shipped engines shade hits from a cache, the screen or analytic lights, and 'Hit Lighting' is an expensive quality mode. Shipped by: UE5 Lumen (screen traces + surface cache / hit lighting), Metro Exodus EE, Battlefield V, AMD GI-1.1. Measured by: The grain score on chrome poles with the reflection accumulator off, before and after, isolating hit-shading noise; bias against the 16-ray truth harness per region.
- **Baseline: Inputs that stop specular aliasing: geometric specular AA (roughness widened by normal variance) applied before ray generation and in the resolve, and ray-cone texture LOD at hits.** Why: These remove speckle sources the denoiser cannot distinguish from signal. They are general, cheap, and shipped (UE, HDRP, Square Enix). Shipped by: Unreal Engine, Unity HDRP, Square Enix engines. Measured by: The wet-floor and pole grain score and bright-speck count during the swing with the reflection denoiser off (raw signal); a parked diff against the reference renderer to bound the added roughness bias.
- **Baseline: The final TAA does anti-aliasing only. The reflection signal reaches it already converged, and mirror pixels optionally carry virtual-surface motion (a PSR-style guide) so the AA pass does not smear them.** Why: The owner's rule 3. NRD, the FFX denoiser and Lumen each keep their own reflection history, and NRD documents PSR guides for mirrors. Shipped by: NRD integrations, Cyberpunk 2077 PT (PSR), Bevy Solari. Measured by: With taa_resolve's memory off, young reflection pixels settle within the parked noise floor (RT2-0 gate 8); the pole and bumper scores do not change when the TAA feedback changes.
- **Candidate: A temporal-gradient confidence (A-SVGF style) as the reflected-content check under any motion, fed into the history counter as NRD's IN_SPEC_CONFIDENCE.** Why better: It replaces the pile of tuned trust tests (direction, hit-distance, struck-object and colour-box ramps listed in RT2-23) with a measured change signal. It needs no thresholds on scale or brightness and catches changes in lighting as well as in geometry. Evidence: A-SVGF (HPG 2018) shows a large reduction in lag and ghosting; it shipped in Quake II RTX; NRD's README calls a confidence input 'highly recommended'. Risk: Needs deterministic re-evaluation with the previous frame's seeds and geometry; reconstructing sparse gradients adds a pass. Measured by: emitter_lag.py reflection share, the car stop (watch_arm.py car 20), window drive, spin_measure ghost scores, and the cost of the gradient samples as a share of the ray budget.
- **Candidate: Dual-source reprojection blended by colour similarity to the current neighbourhood (Stachowiak 2018 / AMD GI-1.1 / FFX), instead of NRD's full confidence stack.** Why better: Fewer passes and constants, and cheaper: AMD's whole denoise plus upscale is 0.46-0.96 ms at 1080p. Evidence: Measured on an RX 7900 XTX (AMD SA2023). Not compared head-to-head with NRD. Risk: A colour match can accept the wrong history when the wrong image has a similar colour, and it reacts to lighting change as if it were disocclusion. Measured by: The same spin_measure and after-stop rows as the NRD-style arm, at the same ray count, plus ms.
- **Candidate: PSR / virtual-surface guides for the mirror tier (roughness near 0): the reflected surface's position, motion and normal written into the guides the accumulator and TAA use.** Why better: Exact motion for planar reflectors such as the wet floor and puddles, which removes the virtual-motion guesswork where it matters most. Evidence: Documented in NRD's README. Shipped in Cyberpunk PT and Portal RTX (secondary). Bevy found no benefit from specular motion vectors when RR was used (weak, different pipeline). Risk: Curved chrome poles; it rewrites the guide buffers every pass consumes. Measured by: Floor and puddle smear and grain scores in spin_measure; pole behaviour, since NRD warns of artefacts on curved mirrors; cost of the extra bounces.
- **Candidate: Screen-space reuse of hit radiance (the previous frame's lit colour when the hit is on screen) plus a cone-footprint-gated cache for off-screen glossy hits.** Why better: Removes the noise of stochastic NEE at hits and adds multi-bounce light for most garage reflections, at a cost below a full hit shade. Evidence: AMD SA2023 (measured). Lumen and BFV screen hybrids (shipped). The SHaRC footprint rule (documented). Risk: View-dependent radiance reused across directions; disocclusion flicker at screen edges. Measured by: Grain score on chrome and on the floor with the accumulator off; region bias against the 16-ray reference; ms.
- **Candidate: NRD itself built in as a reference arm (NRI/Vulkan), behind a flag.** Why better: It turns 'top-dog baseline' into a measured number in RageV's own scenes. A native v2 then has to beat it on spin_measure and on ms. Evidence: Public SDK with published cost (2.55 ms diffuse+specular at 1440p on an RTX 4080). Risk: Integration work; the rule against third-party types in public headers (wrap it internally); NRD carries its own tuned constants, including frame-rate compensation. Measured by: All RT2-23 exit gates, NRD arm against v1 against v2.

### B.5 RageV against them, topic by topic

- **Ray direction sampler.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: Default draws GGX half-vectors from the whole distribution and replaces any below-horizon result with the exact mirror direction; Heitz-2018 VNDF exists behind a switch that is off (judged by eye during a confounded bisection). Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_trace.rvshader:322-329`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_trace.rvshader:284-321`, `RageV/src/RageV/Core/EngineConfig.h:459`
  Top engines: Lumen importance-samples the visible GGX lobe; Snowdrop samples GGX and demodulates per Heitz's VNDF paper; NRD requires a sampler that never casts below the surface; bounded VNDF (2023) lowers variance further at the same ALU. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/lumen_2022.txt:2725`; `build/rt_research/sources/snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:817-841`; `phase1_B_reflections.json techniques[0]`
  Why: Mirror substitution stacks every rejected draw on one direction (a lamp there returns full weight): a known bias and speckle source. VNDF is shipped by the top engines.
- **Sampling density handed to the resolve.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: The trace always writes the old D*NoH/(4VoH) density, and for multi-ray texels the mean direction with the first ray's pdf, whichever sampler drew the ray. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_trace.rvshader:904-911`
  Top engines: NRD and SSSR-class resolves need the true pdf of the sampler used; ratio estimators are biased otherwise. Sources: `phase1_B_reflections.json techniques[0].failure_modes`; `phase1_B_reflections.json techniques[6]`
  Why: A density mismatch confounds the firefly credibility cap and any future true ratio weights (REFL-11).
- **Where rays are spent (resolution, rate, tiles).** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: Every pixel inside the gloss window gets at least one ray at output resolution from a full-screen fragment pass; RT-9 adds rays per 16x16 tile but never removes any, runs only while an instance moves (camera motion is opt-in, off), and reads last frame's tile at the same screen position. Code: `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:2356-2369`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:2393-2437`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_trace.rvshader:777-794`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_budget.rvshader:95-162`
  Top engines: Lumen: tile classify and compact (z-order), reflections at 1 rpp only on Epic, less on High. Snowdrop: variable mixed resolution per 16x16 tile (1/16, 1/4, 1/2, full) from luminance, variance, hit distance, roughness and specularity, smoothed with last frame's quality (saved 28%). BFV: variable-rate tracing. AMD: half resolution below roughness 0.2. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/lumen_2022.txt:2825-2846`; `build/rt_research/sources/lumen_2022.txt:2900-2910`; `build/rt_research/sources/snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:888-975`; `build/rt_research/sources/snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:1170-1200`
  Why: The top engines spend fewer rays where the lobe is wide and reconstruct the rest. RageV only adds rays. Snowdrop's weights are hand-tuned, so they are not a general form either.
- **Rough surfaces and the roughness split.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: Traced share is 1 - smoothstep(0.25, 0.6, roughness) at High; the remainder is the baked probe, measured about a third brighter than the trace, cross-faded between 0.25 and 0.6; nothing traced above 0.6. Code: `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:266-273`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:5985-5989`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:6204`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:6241`
  Top engines: Lumen: 0-0.3 traced; 0.3-0.4 traced with shortened rays and the World Radiance Cache on a miss (road reflections 16% cheaper); 0.4-1.0 resamples the Screen Radiance Cache by GGX importance sampling (50-70% cheaper). Snowdrop: from 0.7 lerp to the RTGI SH toward the lobe's dominant direction, SH only at 0.9 or above (PreferDarkerLerp hides the seam). AMD: 0.2 and 0.6. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/lumen_2022.txt:2764-2815`; `build/rt_research/sources/snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:1086-1114`; `scratchpad amd_glossy.txt:350-390`
  Why: The top engines take rough specular from the same GI data the diffuse uses, so it agrees with it. RageV's rough fallback is a probe that disagrees in brightness and never shows movers.
- **Screen-space tracing before the ray query.** Comparison: missing. Verified: missing. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: None in RT mode; every reflection ray is a hardware ray query. SSR exists only as the raster fallback. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_trace.rvshader:692-702`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/ssr_trace.rvshader:1-18`
  Top engines: Lumen, Snowdrop, BFV, AMD and AC Shadows march screen space first and ray-trace only failures. Snowdrop: 27% less specular trace time, but visible discontinuities between the screen and world results and false hits behind thin objects. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:1020-1040`; `build/rt_research/sources/snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:1170-1200`
  Why: The saving is mostly traversal cost on console-class RT. The screen march brings thin-object and discontinuity errors that ray-traced visibility does not have. The part worth taking is shading visible hits from the screen (see hit shading).
- **Hit shading.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: Each hit walks its cell's lights, keeps ONE by weighted reservoir sampling (seeded by hit position and frame) and casts one shadow ray; lights are points; the hit's specular is the probe through split-sum; Static hits take fully baked lights from the field; texture fetches at mip 0. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:2810-2842`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:3098-3171`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:3307-3327`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:2763-2784`, `RageV/src/RageV/Core/EngineConfig.h:466`
  Top engines: Snowdrop: reproject the hit to the previous frame and reuse its lighting if visible; else sun+CSM, probes and every point light from a BVH (deterministic), emissive clamped per wave. AMD GI-1.1: previous-frame pixel at visible hits, else hash-grid cache or area light. Lumen: Surface Cache by default; hit lighting cost 11.54 ms against 2.44 ms on the Matrix (PS5 1080p) with no substantial gain there. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:648-683`; `build/rt_research/sources/lumen_2022.txt:1855-1867`; `scratchpad amd_glossy.txt:186-196`
  Why: A one-light stochastic pick makes even a roughness-0 ray noisy. The top engines shade hits deterministically or from the screen or a cache.
- **Lamps inside reflections (NEE toward emitters).** Comparison: better (claimed). Verified: B-OB1, partly better: ahead of Lumen 2022 and Snowdrop only; behind RTXDI-class path tracers until RT2-11 (its MIS aims at rectangle stand-ins). Confidence: medium.
  RageV: Every emitter row is scored per ray and one is picked in one pass by weighted reservoir sampling (no cap since 2026-09-25); aimed sample and lobe ray combined by the power heuristic; the lobe ray keeps only its MIS share of a struck lamp. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_trace.rvshader:398-560`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_trace.rvshader:568-589`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/reflection_emitters.glsl:157-180`
  Top engines: Lumen 2022 lists explicit emissive sampling as future work. Snowdrop suppresses emissive fireflies with a per-wave clamp, which is biased. PT titles use RTXDI with MIS; Cyberpunk uses a roughness heuristic in place of proper MIS. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/lumen_2022.txt:2996-3000`; `build/rt_research/sources/snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:655-676`; `phase1_B_reflections.json engines[7]`
  Why: Proper MIS with every emitter aimed at beats clamping or luck (Lumen 2022, Snowdrop) and equals RTXDI-class path tracers. The scoring loop is linear in emitters, which RT2-12 fixes.
- **Spatial pooling of lamp samples (ReSTIR at the reflector).** Comparison: equal. Verified: equal. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: The resolve pools up to 8 neighbours' lamp draws, re-weighs each from the texel with pairwise MIS over the samplers' own densities, keeps one, and casts one shadow ray. +2.1 ms at 1600x900; the owner confirmed lower grain. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_resolve.rvshader:169-378`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_resolve.rvshader:443-457`, `RageV/src/RageV/Core/EngineConfig.h:402`
  Top engines: RTXDI spatial and temporal reuse in PT titles, where direct and specular share one light-sampling structure; nothing equivalent in Lumen or Snowdrop reflections. Sources: `phase1_B_reflections.json techniques[16]`; `phase1_B_reflections.json engines[7]`
  Why: The estimator matches RTXDI's spatial reuse (pairwise MIS is correct in form), but it has no temporal reuse and it duplicates the direct pass's own light selection, so the pooling is paid for twice.
- **Each fixture counted once in specular.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: low.
  RageV: The lamp's highlight appears twice. DirectTrace shades the Light's analytic GGX highlight (4-frame surface-reprojected memory). The reflection trace also sees the glowing mesh. Neither is weighted against the other (RT2-0 arm E states this; no suppression found in the lit shader's reflection block). Code: `docs/RT-SERIES-2.md:442`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:5985-6257`
  Top engines: PT titles (Cyberpunk: ReSTIR DI plus BRDF paths with a roughness-heuristic MIS) count each emitter once. UE leaves double counting to the artist. MegaLights shades lamp specular as visible-sample weights times pre-integrated irradiance. Sources: `phase1_B_reflections.json techniques[17]`; `build/rt_research/sources/megalights_2025.txt:745-760`
  Why: Counting a fixture twice is a bias and a second noise source (the analytic highlight lags on the surface's motion). The path-traced top engines count each emitter once.
- **Demodulation.** Comparison: equal. Verified: equal. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: The traced picture carries the reflectance's hue only (magnitude 1); the split-sum magnitude (luminance, times occlusion) is applied at composite. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_trace.rvshader:847-884`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:6250-6257`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_composite.rvshader:108-110`
  Top engines: Snowdrop demodulates with the RT Gems ch.32 approximation and re-applies it. MegaLights factors out albedo and EnvBRDF. NRD integrations do the same. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:988-1010`; `build/rt_research/sources/megalights_2025.txt:765-785`
  Why: Same principle. RageV's hue-in-signal is a small deviation (a history crossing a coloured-metal edge carries the wrong hue), and the comment notes an unexplained 4x magnitude disagreement.
- **Spatial reuse (resolve).** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: Ratio-style reuse over a Vogel disc sized to a fifth of the lobe footprint (24-64 taps). Weights are this lobe's density over the neighbour's density at the re-aimed direction, capped at 4. Measured 3.91 ms at 1600x900 (garage). Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_resolve.rvshader:119-137`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_resolve.rvshader:402-438`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_resolve.rvshader:543-634`
  Top engines: Lumen: spatial reuse reweights neighbours by BRDF (5.4 cvars: 5 samples within 8 px, secondary source), then a bilateral filter only on high-variance areas, forced at double strength on disocclusions. BFV: 13-16 taps, 1.45 ms. SSSR: true ratio estimator. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/lumen_2022.txt:2724-2758`; `phase1_B_reflections.json techniques[6]`
  Why: Same method, but the top engines use far fewer taps. RageV's resolve costs as much as the trace and runs on every pixel, settled or not.
- **Firefly handling.** Comparison: equal. Verified: equal. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: Relative caps in the resolve (mean + 3 sigma of the taps, never below the second-brightest tap, opened by density credibility) and in the accumulator (3 sigma of the 8 neighbours), plus an absolute 64-per-channel cap on every ray and a blanket exemption for taps whose light is more than half emission. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_resolve.rvshader:466-618`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_accumulate.rvshader:1435-1448`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_trace.rvshader:900-902`
  Top engines: NRD: relative anti-firefly against neighbourhood statistics plus a history-relative clamp on young pixels. Lumen: tonemapped weighting in the last bilateral (not in spatial reuse, where it 'would crush highlights'); absolute MaxRayIntensity (cvar, secondary). Snowdrop: per-wave emissive clamp. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/lumen_2022.txt:2748-2758`; `build/rt_research/sources/snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:655-676`; `phase1_B_reflections.json techniques[14]`
  Why: RageV's relative clamps beat the absolute clamps used by Lumen and Snowdrop. Its 64 cap (a hue shift on the tubes) and the emission exemption are worse than NRD's fully relative form.
- **Depth used to place the virtual image (tracking hit distance).** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: The trace writes the mean ray distance with misses as 1e4. The resolve writes a weighted mean of re-aimed distances. The accumulator clamps it to 1e3 and blends it with the value stored at the surface's old place (history share up to 7/8). Only the resolve's disc sizing uses a 3x3 minimum. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_trace.rvshader:703`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_trace.rvshader:835`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_trace.rvshader:900-901`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_resolve.rvshader:407-417`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_resolve.rvshader:620-631`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_accumulate.rvshader:1405`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_accumulate.rvshader:1502`
  Top engines: NRD prepass keeps the minimum in-lobe hitT over lobe-weighted neighbours as a separate tracking distance, excludes misses, and never divides by the pdf. Sources: `phase1_B_reflections.json techniques[7]`
  Why: A mean that mixes the sky and foreground places the image where nothing is. RT-24 measured an isolated nearest-hit arm as inert or worse, while the ghost was dominated by the roughness-pattern drag, so it must be re-measured inside v2.
- **Reflector curvature.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: Unsigned curvature taken from shading (normal-mapped) normals two texels out, minus a 0.15/m allowance; convex formula only. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_accumulate.rvshader:533-553`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_accumulate.rvshader:1411-1415`
  Top engines: NRD: signed curvature from geometry, negative clamped, with the thin-lens (mirror) equation. Sources: `phase1_B_reflections.json techniques[8]`
  Why: Normal-map detail reads as a curved mirror (REFL-04), and there is no concave case.
- **History candidates and how they combine.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: One candidate is picked: the image history, else the surface history. A curved self-mover takes the surface history when its colour is nearer the fresh mean (kSurfaceNearer). A lobe-agreement weight scales trust when the image slid (RT-24 change 2). Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_accumulate.rvshader:1482-1601`
  Top engines: NRD blends surface-motion and virtual-motion histories by a confidence (a lobe-sized normal test, a roughness test, parallax against hit distance, a normal test one frame back, material ID). AMD, FFX and Stachowiak 2018 blend by brightness difference. Sources: `phase1_B_reflections.json techniques[9]`
  Why: Picking one candidate pops and leaves the grain-against-ghost trade to memory length (REFL-05). The blend is the shipped answer. RageV's two-layer and follow-hit variants were a different idea and measured worse.
- **History fetch filter.** Comparison: equal. Verified: equal. Confidence: low.
  RageV: Bilinear inside one object, the texel alone across an object edge, and a 3x3 neighbour search when the centre is refused. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_accumulate.rvshader:1047-1062`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_accumulate.rvshader:1098-1246`
  Top engines: NRD: occlusion-aware Catmull-Rom with a re-weighted bilinear fallback. FFX: a 3x3 search on low confidence. RageV's own TAA uses Catmull-Rom. Sources: `phase1_B_reflections.json techniques[11]`; `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/taa_resolve.rvshader:1151-1157`
  Why: RT-24 measured Catmull-Rom (with and without a 2x2 clamp) worse in this accumulator: parked shimmer, sharpness 0.29. The occlusion awareness is already present through the id and plane tests.
- **History length control.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: About 20 rules that depend on speed and texel travel: travel hyperbola, smear cap (SmearTexels 6), slack with a mirror factor, a floor that shrinks with travel (fix 2), direction and match confidence, and two counters (trust in the alpha, blend count in the id lane). Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_accumulate.rvshader:1031-1039`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_accumulate.rvshader:1692-1759`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_accumulate.rvshader:1778-1783`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/Renderer3D.h:347-356`
  Top engines: NRD: one counter, capped by the parallax angle against the lobe half-angle and a roughness curve, with an external confidence. Its frame-rate compensation constant would break the owner's rule 2. Sources: `phase1_B_reflections.json techniques[10]`
  Why: One principled cap instead of speed-keyed dials. The general form in the RT2-23 audit note is NRD's rule without its frame-rate constant.
- **Checking the reflected content under camera-only motion.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: The struck-identity test runs only when an instance moved. The hit-distance test starts at about a 35% change at roughness 0.21. The box is 6-12 spreads wide on glossy surfaces. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_accumulate.rvshader:687-717`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/Renderer3D.cpp:7638-7640`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_accumulate.rvshader:865-875`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_accumulate.rvshader:1633-1636`
  Top engines: NRD's parallax and prev-prev normal tests, material ID and fast-history clamp run under any motion. Sources: `phase1_B_reflections.json techniques[9]`
  Why: REFL-05, confirmed. Arm I was never built.
- **Anti-lag / change detection.** Comparison: better (claimed). Verified: B-OB2, equal: NRD's README prescribes the same re-traced gradient and its sample implements it. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: Measured change: last frame's recorded rays re-lit with last frame's seeds against this frame's scene; the filtered changed share shortens the blend count. Code: `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:2515-2587`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_trace.rvshader:885-897`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_accumulate.rvshader:1785-1802`
  Top engines: A-SVGF in Quake II RTX. NRD accepts a confidence input but does not produce one. Lumen and Snowdrop reflections use colour or neighbourhood heuristics. Sources: `phase1_B_reflections.json techniques[13]`
  Why: A measured, number-light change signal that fires on nothing when nothing changed. Among the reflection denoisers surveyed, none of the top engines ships one.
- **Grain against ghosting: fast history.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: None. RT2-0 arm H (a fast-history clamp) was dropped because parked flicker doubled. Code: `docs/RT-SERIES-2.md:415-417`
  Top engines: NRD: a 6-frame luminance fast history clamps the 30-frame history to mean plus or minus 2 sigma. Sources: `phase1_B_reflections.json techniques[12]`
  Why: The mechanism is sound. RageV's attempt probably sized the clamp from a noisy one-ray neighbourhood; its width must come from the fast history's own temporal spread.
- **Young and disoccluded pixels (history fix).** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: A young blur only where the reflector moves on its own (MovingRadius 4) plus a blur gated by uncertainty. A texel made young by camera motion gets no fix. Fix 1 hands young grain to TAA. Code: `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/Renderer3D.cpp:7359-7376`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_blur.rvshader:259-341`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:6193-6204`
  Top engines: NRD: history fix for the first 3 frames (sparse 5x5 kernel, stride shrinking with history). Lumen: the bilateral is forced at double strength on newly revealed areas. Sources: `phase1_B_reflections.json techniques[13]`; `build/rt_research/sources/lumen_2022.txt:2748-2758`
  Why: Without it the no-reliance-on-TAA rule fails (D9).
- **Post-temporal spatial filter.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: Three 7x7 a-trous passes (strides 1, 2, 4). The radius fades in across fixed relative-error edges 0.02-0.20, capped by the lobe footprint and MovingRadius. The edge stop uses the raw moments; the propagated variance lane is written but never weighs anything. Hard roughness cut 0.2, normal power 8, plane tolerance in metres. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_blur.rvshader:329-341`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_blur.rvshader:349-441`
  Top engines: ReBLUR: radius from roughness, hit distance and history, shrinking as history grows, with lobe-angle normal, roughness, plane and hitT weights. ReLAX/SVGF: variance-guided a-trous. Lumen: bilateral only where variance is high. Sources: `phase1_B_reflections.json techniques[15]`; `build/rt_research/sources/lumen_2022.txt:2748-2758`
  Why: Fixed gates switch each texel between no blur and full blur independently: speckle at speed (audit, live). REFL-15.
- **Temporal stabilisation after the blur.** Comparison: missing. Verified: missing. Confidence: high.
  RageV: None. The blurred picture goes straight to the composite, then TAA. Code: `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:2592-2600`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:3788-3812`
  Top engines: ReBLUR TS: a second temporal pass with the same surface and virtual blend, a luminance box and antilag. Sources: `phase1_B_reflections.json techniques[20]`
  Why: This is the step that would otherwise be left to the frame TAA.
- **Self-sufficiency of the reflection signal (owner rule 3).** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: The composite runs before TAA, and TAA filters the reflection again (now with the surface's motion). Fix 1 explicitly relies on TAA for young grain. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_composite.rvshader:106-120`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:6193-6204`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:3916`
  Top engines: NRD reflections are self-sufficient. Lumen states that the full-frame TAA 'cleans it up a bit more' after its bilateral. MegaLights passes well-sampled pixels straight to TSR. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/lumen_2022.txt:2747`; `build/rt_research/sources/megalights_2025.txt:955-966`
  Why: Only NRD meets the owner's rule. Lumen and MegaLights lean on TSR just as RageV leans on TAA, so against them this is equal.
- **Specular anti-aliasing input.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: Tokuyoshi-Kaplanyan geometric specular AA filters roughness for the analytic lights only. The G-buffer lane the trace, resolve and accumulator read holds the unfiltered roughness. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:4445-4488`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:4496`
  Top engines: UE applies normal-curvature-to-roughness to the material roughness every path reads; HDRP applies geometric specular AA. Sources: `phase1_B_reflections.json techniques[21]`
  Why: The traced lobe and the analytic highlight use different roughness, and the traced lobe can be narrower than the pixel: an aliasing source the denoiser cannot remove.
- **Texture LOD at reflection hits.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: Mip 0 for albedo, roughness, emissive and metallic at every hit. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:2763`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:2775`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:2781`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:2784`
  Top engines: Ray cones pick the mip from the cone width at the hit (JCGT 2021). Sources: `phase1_B_reflections.json techniques[22]`
  Why: Mip 0 on a distant mirror hit aliases texture into speckle under motion.
- **Pass structure and cost.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: Budget, trace, resolve with lamp pool, record and re-light, accumulate and 3 blurs, all full-screen fragment passes. Garage at 1600x900: trace 3.82, resolve 3.91, accumulate 0.94, blurs 1.97, plus the lamp pool +2.1: about 12.7 ms, about 36 ms at native (inferred). Code: `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:2353-2601`, `docs/RT-SERIES-2.md:1977`
  Top engines: Snowdrop reflections: 2.132 ms at 2160p on an RTX 4080 (denoise 69%); 2.895 ms at 1440p on Xbox Series X. Lumen: Matrix reflections 2.30 ms (High) and 4.62 ms (Epic, 1 rpp) at 1080p on PS5. AMD: about 1 ms extra at 1080p on an RX 7900 XTX. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:1233-1250`; `build/rt_research/sources/lumen_2022.txt:2958-2970`; `scratchpad amd_glossy.txt:385-392`
  Why: The top engines cost 5 to 10 times less per pixel, normalised roughly for GPU. Scenes differ, so the size of the gap is approximate.
- **Accumulating on the unjittered grid (the reflection anti-aliases itself).** Comparison: not comparable. Verified: not comparable. Confidence: low.
  RageV: History is kept on the unjittered grid; the fresh jittered sample is one part of the average. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_accumulate.rvshader:1071-1089`
  Top engines: NRD takes jittered inputs and relies on its own reprojection; no top engine documents this exact design. Sources: `phase1_B_reflections.json techniques[11]`
  Why: A sound RageV-specific choice. Keep it.
- **Half-float history precision.** Comparison: equal. Verified: equal. Confidence: high.
  RageV: Stochastic rounding of every running average onto the half-float grid. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_accumulate.rvshader:1954-1998`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/half_float.glsl:1-93`
  Top engines: MegaLights: stochastic float quantisation into R11G11B10. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/megalights_2025.txt:815-825`
  Why: Same idea, independently arrived at.
- **Translucent-layer reflections.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: The nearest glass pane runs a second full-resolution copy of the whole chain with the moving layer forced on (REFL-09). Code: `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/Renderer3D.cpp:7320-7325`
  Top engines: Lumen: the front translucent layer is depth-peeled into a minimal G-buffer, runs the pipeline only on valid pixels, forced to mirror with the denoiser off; deeper layers use the World Radiance Cache. Snowdrop: a second layer (water or clearcoat) only on PC max settings. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/lumen_2022.txt:2858-2890`; `build/rt_research/sources/snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:1045-1055`
  Why: The top engines pay per layer pixel, not per screen. Owned by RT2-31 (area G).
- **Raster fallback SSR.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: One mirror ray per pixel at half resolution through Hi-Z, a disc blur sized by roughness in the resolve, no temporal history of its own. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/ssr_trace.rvshader:1-18`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/ssr_resolve.rvshader:1-10`
  Top engines: SSSR: stochastic GGX rays, ratio-estimator resolve, temporal reprojection by the hit. Sources: `phase1_B_reflections.json engines[5]`
  Why: By the owner's rules the raster fallback is kept at parity, not improved (D1, RT-FIRST §4). Its fixed values go to RT2-44.
- **Neural joint denoisers (DLSS RR, FSR Ray Regeneration).** Comparison: not comparable. Verified: not comparable. Confidence: high.
  RageV: None; DLSS RR is allowed only as a measured arm (D5). Code: `docs/RT-SERIES-2.md:2043`
  Top engines: Lead on quality in path-traced titles; RR replaces the upscaler and TAA; FSR RR is RDNA4 and DX12 only. Sources: `phase1_B_reflections.json techniques[18]`; `phase1_B_reflections.json techniques[19]`
  Why: It is the final temporal pass itself, so it conflicts with owner rule 3 by construction.

### B.6 Candidates that could beat both

- **B-BB1: Footprint-terminated reflection rays: one estimator across all roughness. A ray continues until the lobe's cone footprint at the ray's current length exceeds the stored-light cell there (the bake field, plus RT2-25's realtime cache where built). Only then does it read the lobe's radiance from the cell. A near-mirror ray never terminates early. A roughness-0.9 ray terminates at once, which is the stored-light lookup.**
  Why better than both: Lumen switches estimators at fixed roughness bands (0.3, 0.4) and Snowdrop hides its seam with PreferDarkerLerp. Both are discontinuous in roughness by construction. Snowdrop's ray lengths are tuned in metres (50 m to 5 m), which breaks owner rule 2. RageV today cross-fades against a probe a third too bright. Footprint termination is continuous in roughness, scale-free and needs no cutoff. Evidence: The SHaRC termination rule, from phase 1 (NVIDIA SDK). Lumen's shortened glossy rays with a World Radiance Cache fallback saved 16% on the Matrix roads, and its screen-cache resampling for 0.4-1.0 saved 50-70%. Snowdrop's probe-cache fallback cut specular tracing by 51%. Risk: The bake field stores irradiance plus a dominant direction, not directional radiance, so a view-dependent lobe of moderate roughness hitting it is approximate. The garage probe/trace brightness disagreement may reappear as a field/trace disagreement, and the reference renderer decides. Dynamic objects are not in the bake. Cost: Saves trace time on rough tiles, amount unknown until RT2-27's coverage arm. Adds a few ALU per ray step for the footprint test and one cache fetch.
  The comparison's gate: (1) A roughness-ramp scene (roughness 0.05 to 1.0, lit by a tube and a Realtime light), diffed against the RT2-2 reference renderer: no step larger than 1 display level between adjacent roughness bands, and region bias no worse than v1. (2) Garage floor, walls and car diff images against the reference: bias no worse than v1. (3) Trace ms from A,B,B,A timings at 2560x1600: lower than v1. (4) Camp (no bake): no regression, because rays just continue there.
  Verification: **partly true.** Snowdrop is not discontinuous in roughness by construction. It fades and dithers, with a PreferDarkerLerp bias. The metre-tuned lengths are still a valid objection. In SHaRC's rule a roughness-0.9 ray does not stop 'at once': the footprint starts at zero, so the ray needs about half a cell, and the rule is applied at hit vertices. The idea needs a directional radiance cache (RT2-25). The bake field cannot supply a lobe's radiance. It stays a candidate with a measurement gate.
  In the roadmap: RT2-27.
- **B-BB2: Ray rate per tile from the lobe footprint: the trace spends no more rays on a resolve disc than the resolve can gather (rays per texel = min(1, T / (pi rho^2)), with rho the disc radius in texels and T the resolve's tap count). Rates are quantised to 1, 1/2 (checkerboard), 1/4 and 1/16 for coherent dispatch, and traded within one fixed average ray budget read at the reprojected position.**
  Why better than both: Snowdrop's tile quality uses five hand-tuned weights ('needs a lot of tweaking'). BFV uses an importance LUT, AMD fixed roughness thresholds. RageV's RT-9 only adds rays and reads the wrong tile under motion. The footprint rule follows from the ratio estimator itself: rays inside one disc are pooled anyway. Evidence: Stachowiak 2015 ratio estimator (reuse within the lobe). Snowdrop's mixed resolution cut 28% of specular cost, shipped. AMD traces glossy at half resolution (0.18-0.28 ms at 1080p). Risk: A low rate at disocclusions under fast motion. Reduced resolution darkened thin bright features before (RT-3.1). A tile's rate must be decided before its hit distance is known, so the footprint comes from last frame's reprojected image distance. Cost: Proportional to the glossy share at reduced rate. Unmeasured until RT2-27's first arm.
  The comparison's gate: RT2-27's gates, plus: the owner-speed swing (spin_measure.py --swing=45 --rate=3) with speckle_count.py motion dots and spin_measure grain and edges no worse than v1 at full rate. The RT-3.1 cable band on the bridge no worse than v1 (the 0.58-level darkening must not return). Parked garage diff against the v1 full-rate picture within the parked noise floor (parked_stats.py).
  Verification: **plausible.** The claim that the rule 'follows from the ratio estimator' is an argument, not a result. Neighbouring texels would share fewer rays, so their resolve outputs correlate and the noise becomes blotchy at low frequency, which the temporal and blur stages must then remove. T (the tap count) is itself a preset value. This needs its measurement gate.
  In the roadmap: RT2-27 (with the blotch gate at 1/4 and 1/16).
- **B-BB3: One specular signal with MIS across passes. A lamp's specular is sampled once, by the shared light-candidate structure (RT2-12), and weighted by the power heuristic against the reflection's lobe ray. The lobe ray's struck emission keeps the complementary weight, using the candidate structure's evaluable pdf. Both halves go into the one demodulated specular signal and one specular denoiser with virtual-image reprojection. The diffuse half of direct light stays with RT2-24.**
  Why better than both: MegaLights shades lamp specular as visible-sample weights times pre-integrated irradiance, which its own authors call an approximation that fails for specular, reprojected by the surface. UE otherwise leaves double counting to the artist. Cyberpunk uses a roughness heuristic, not proper MIS. RageV today counts a fixture twice (analytic highlight plus traced emitter), runs lamp selection twice (DirectTrace and the reflection's own reservoir), and pays +2.1 ms for a lamp pool that RT2-12's reuse would provide once. Evidence: NRD's integration guidance, from phase 1: specular is direct NEE plus indirect, denoised together with the indirect hitT. Cyberpunk PT feeds ReSTIR DI and BRDF paths into one specular denoiser. RageV's own RT-11 MIS inside reflections took the wet floor from 13.31 to 9.69 against the 16-ray truth. The audit found the direct highlight's 4-frame surface-reprojected memory smearing poles and floor (PairMemory 4, live). Risk: It crosses areas A and B: RT2-24 must stop shading specular through its visibility ratio, and RT2-12 must expose pdf(point | shading point). Near-mirror lamp images then depend on the lobe ray alone, as today. Cost: Removes the lamp pool (+2.1 ms at 1600x900) and the reflection's per-ray emitter scoring loop. Adds one lobe-pdf evaluation per direct sample.
  The comparison's gate: (1) Fixture counted once: under the tubes, the sum of arms (direct specular only plus reflection emitters only) against the full picture is exact, and the full picture diffed against the RT2-2 reference renderer is near zero or favours baked. (2) Owner-speed swing: speckle_count dots at or below today's 513/85 and pole edge score at or below 9.51. (3) emitter_lag.py reflection share and floor share no worse. (4) Cost below today's direct-specular plus lamp-pool cost, A,B,B,A.
  Verification: **partly true.** The direction is sound, but it is RTXDI/NRD's documented practice (proper MIS is an RTXDI option), so it does not go beyond the top engines. MIS is only possible if each fixture has one representation the lobe ray can strike. An analytic Light has no geometry, so a Light paired with an emissive mesh must become one emitter, or it is still counted twice. The MegaLights wording must be corrected, and the UE statement is unsupported.
  In the roadmap: RT2-23g (step 23g of RT2-23 until the roadmap's second critic; split out because it needs RT2-24's step 1 first).
- **B-BB4: Measured change (re-lighting last frame's recorded rays) as the history-confidence input of the v2 specular denoiser, in place of every colour-box, identity and hit-distance ramp that exists only to guess whether the content changed.**
  Why better than both: NRD takes a confidence input but ships no generator; its docs call one highly recommended. Lumen, Snowdrop and AMD infer change from colour or neighbourhood heuristics. RageV already generates a measured change signal for reflections. Evidence: A-SVGF (HPG 2018) shipped in Quake II RTX. RageV's measured change fires on nothing parked; it fed TAA and cut the RT-22 floor trail from 9.2 to 4.8 levels. Risk: The re-light must stay bit-identical to the trace (RT-23's defect). It measures a sparse 3x3 block sample and needs its filter's reconstruction. Cost: Already paid today (record, re-light, filter).
  The comparison's gate: watch_arm.py car 20 (the car's stop) and light 8, judged live. emitter_lag.py reflection share lower than v1. Parked garage diff 0 against v1. Cost of the record and re-light unchanged or lower at native.
  Verification: **refuted.** Measured change as the confidence input is the NRD-recommended baseline, not something that beats it. It measures the lighting change at last frame's recorded sample points (1/3 resolution). It cannot detect a history fetched from the wrong place (virtual-motion error, disocclusion, a different struck object that is lit the same). Replacing the identity, colour-box and hit-distance tests with it is unsupported.
  In the roadmap: refuted as beyond the baseline; RT2-23 step 23b uses it as NRD's documented input.
- **B-BB5: Keep RT-24's number-free lobe-agreement weight, (narrow over wide lobe width in texels) squared, gated by how far the image slid, as v2's roughness confidence term instead of NRD's tuned lobe and roughness fractions.**
  Why better than both: NRD's roughness and lobe weights are convention constants (lobeAngleFraction 0.15, roughness fraction 0.15, per phase 1). RageV's is derived from the blur widths of the two lobes. Evidence: RT-24 measured it equal to the tuned version it replaced on the slow orbit at 1 and 16 rays, parked bit-identical. The owner judged ghosting 'not noticeable' with it. Risk: Validated only on the garage floor's puddle pattern. Cost: Negligible.
  The comparison's gate: Pan against orbit parity at 16 rays: the pole score with memory on is at or below the score with memory off in both. The NRD reference arm, if built, no better on the same test.
  Verification: **plausible.** The comparison with NRD mixes a spatial-filter parameter with a temporal confidence. The weight is fed by the image distance (perAlpha uses `image`), so it inherits B-C3's defect: misses averaged in at 1e4 and clamped to 1e3. It was only ever shown equal. It needs a gate showing it is better.
  In the roadmap: RT2-23 step 23b, as an arm against NRD's form.
