- **B-C1** (confirmed). Claim: Reflection hits keep one light by weighted reservoir sampling with one shadow ray, seeded by hit position and frame (HitLightSampling on by default), so even a roughness-0 reflection ray's hit shading changes every frame.
  Evidence: pbr_fragment.glsl:2831-2842 is inside TraceSurface (starts at 2615), which reflection_trace.rvshader:693 and 808 call. The keep flag is RayRates.w bit 25, set from HitLightSampling (Renderer3D.cpp:4137; EngineConfig.h:466 defaults it to true). The seed hashes the hit position XOR RV_TRACE_FRAME. Outside the relight variant, RV_TRACE_FRAME is ray_shadow_trace.glsl:29 mod(GlobalIllumination.y,1024), and GlobalIllumination.y is the frame counter (Renderer3D.cpp:8051). The reservoir keep and the one shadow ray are at 3098-3171.
  Correction: The kept light changes frame to frame only when the hit sees two or more lights with non-zero worth. With one light the result is deterministic.
- **B-C2** (confirmed). Claim: The tube's specular is counted twice: DirectTrace's analytic highlight and the traced glowing mesh, with no weighting of one against the other.
  Evidence: The lit shader adds DirectTrace's specular to Lo (pbr_fragment.glsl:5594-5596). The traced reflection goes in separately, through the ambient share and the composite weight (6196-6257). DirectTerm (direct_trace.rvshader:329-472) has no per-light specular switch, and Light.h has no specular flag. The reflection's MIS (reflection_trace.rvshader:382-589) weighs only the area-emitter rectangles against the lobe ray. It never involves the analytic Light. Nothing links a Light to its emissive mesh.
- **B-C3** (confirmed). Claim: The virtual-image depth is a mean that includes misses as 1e4, re-averaged by the resolve, clamped to 1e3 and blended with the value stored at the surface's old place.
  Evidence: reflection_trace.rvshader:703 and 835 record a miss as 1.0e4, and 901 writes travelledSum/rays into o_Reflection.a. The resolve averages distanceHere*w into o_Resolved.a (reflection_resolve.rvshader:609, 627). The accumulator applies min(fresh.a,1e3) at reflection_accumulate.rvshader:1405, then mixes with atSurface.reflector.a at 1502 (mix weight 1/min(past+1,8)).
- **B-C4** (confirmed). Claim: The default sampler substitutes the mirror direction for below-horizon draws. VNDF is off, and the written pdf is always D*NoH/(4VoH), with a mean direction for multi-ray texels.
  Evidence: reflection_trace.rvshader:322-329: the non-VNDF branch returns the mirror direction when dot(L,N)<=0.02. ReflectionVndf=false (EngineConfig.h:459). The pdf at 905-907 is always D*NoH/(4VoH), and 909 writes normalize(directionSum) when rays>1 while keeping the first ray's pdf.
  Correction: The cut-off is 0.02, not 0. The written pdf is also wrong for the mirror-substituted draws, and it would be wrong for VNDF if that were switched on.
- **B-C5** (confirmed). Claim: The accumulator picks one history candidate and never blends. The struck-identity test runs only when an instance moved.
  Evidence: reflection_accumulate.rvshader:1526-1601: c is either the image history or atSurface (choice 1.0/0.5). The two are never blended, only multiplied through matchConfidence. IdentityConfidence returns 1.0 when Change.y<0.5 (687-690). Change.y is freshIdentity && (RayAnyMoving||RayAnyMovingLast) (Renderer3D.cpp:7638-7640), and RayAnyMoving is set only by moving ray instances (5647-5650). Camera motion never counts.
- **B-C6** (confirmed). Claim: The lamp pool uses pairwise MIS over up to 8 neighbours with one shadow ray, is skipped for multi-ray texels, and costs +2.1 ms at 1600x900.
  Evidence: PooledLamp (reflection_resolve.rvshader:255-378) uses Bitterli pairwise MIS: canonical m=(1/k)Σ(c/k)/(p_j+c/k), neighbour m=(p_j/k)/(p_j+c/k). It takes k<=8, one reservoir and one TraceShadowFrom. It is skipped when u_Travel.w>=1.5 (rays>1) at 443-446. The pool size is clamped to 0-8 (Renderer3D.cpp:7169), and the default is 8 (EngineConfig.h:402).
  Correction: The pool is also skipped for mirror texels (alpha<1e-4, 418-419) and where the disc reach is under half a texel (440-441). The +2.1 ms is the owner's recorded figure (EngineConfig comment; RT-SERIES-2.md:869). It was not re-measured here.
- **B-C7** (confirmed). Claim: Geometric specular AA filters only the analytic lights' roughness. The G-buffer surface lane the trace reads holds the unfiltered roughness.
  Evidence: pbr_fragment.glsl:4462-4488 computes shadingRoughness (Tokuyoshi-Kaplanyan), and the comment states 'Analytic lights only'. o_Surface at 4496 writes the unfiltered roughness, and that is the lane reflection_trace reads.
- **B-C8** (confirmed). Claim: The blur uses fixed relative-error gates 0.02-0.20 and a hard roughness cut of 0.2. The propagated variance is written but never weighs a tap.
  Evidence: reflection_blur.rvshader:334 and 340 use smoothstep(0.02,0.20); 411 applies abs(rn-roughness)<0.2?1:0. The luminance edge stop (424-425) uses `variance`, which is recomputed each pass from the accumulator moments (296-327). The propagated u_VarianceIn is read only by VarianceAt (216-221) to write o_Variance (434, 441). It never enters a tap weight.
- **B-C9** (partly true). Claim: Every reflection pass is a full-screen fragment pass at output times supersample resolution, and the budget pass runs only when an instance moved (camera motion off by default).
  Evidence: The trace, resolve and blur targets use Scale=supersample (FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:2365), which is the scene resolution (supersample=1 unless SSAA, 422-424). The budget pass is gated on anythingMoved = AnyInstanceMoved || (ConfidenceRaysOnCameraMotion && camera moved) (2393-2400), and ConfidenceRaysOnCameraMotion=false (EngineConfig.h:489).
  Correction: Not every reflection pass runs full-screen. The ReflectionBudget pass writes a 16x16 tile map, and the measured-change relight and record passes run on 1/3-resolution blocks (2519-2520). The trace, resolve, accumulate and blur are full-resolution fragment passes.
- **B-C10** (confirmed). Claim: Hits read every texture at mip 0.
  Evidence: pbr_fragment.glsl:2763, 2775, 2781, 2784 and 2791 are all textureLod(...,0.0). The sky at 2655 is also mip 0.
- **B-C11** (partly true). Claim: Section 10's audit missed three fixed values: the resolve's 0.9 normal cut, and the blur's 0.2 roughness cut and normal power 8.
  Evidence: Section 10 of RT-SERIES-2.md (lines 2310 onward) has no row for the resolve's dot(N,Nn)<0.9 (reflection_resolve.rvshader:562), the blur's roughness cut of 0.2, or pow(...,8) (reflection_blur.rvshader:409-411). The audit did find all three, though: build/rt24/audit_phase1.json has 'normal agreement threshold dot(N, Nn) < 0.9' (resolve item, then line 313), 'roughness-difference cut' (blur 411) and 'normal edge-stop exponent' (blur 409).
  Correction: The audit did not miss these values. It found them and marked them 'other' or 'unclear'. They were lost between audit_phase1.json and the section 10 table, so section 10 is incomplete.
- **B-C12** (confirmed). Claim: NRD's integration guidance: denoise direct NEE specular and indirect specular together as one specular signal, using the indirect hit distance for tracking.
  Evidence: NRD README (github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD), section 'COMBINED DENOISING OF DIRECT AND INDIRECT LIGHTING': for the specular signal it says to use the indirect hitT for both direct and indirect lighting, because the NEE light's distance is not in the lobe.
  Correction: This is guidance for when you combine the two. NRD does not require combining. The README also notes RELAX works well with RTXDI signals.
- **B-C13** (confirmed). Claim: Snowdrop's RT reflections cost 2.132 ms at 2160p on an RTX 4080 (denoise 69%) and 2.895 ms at 1440p on Xbox Series X. The trace resolution glyph lost in extraction is probably quarter resolution.
  Evidence: snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:1233-1250 gives 2.895 ms on XSX at 1440p and 2.132 ms on RTX 4080 at 2160p, with the 4080 denoise at 1.473 ms (69%). The lost glyph on line 1246 is byte 0xBC, which is '¼' in Latin-1. The talk also says consoles trace at quarter resolution (lines 829-831).
  Correction: The trace fraction is certain, not just probable: '¼ resolution'.
- **B-C14** (confirmed). Claim: Lumen's reflection chain: visible-GGX rays, BRDF-reweighted spatial reuse, temporal accumulation, then a bilateral filter on high-variance areas (double strength at disocclusions, tonemapped weights), then full-frame TAA 'cleans it up a bit more'. Roughness bands 0-0.3 traced, 0.3-0.4 shortened rays with the World Radiance Cache (road reflections 16% cheaper), 0.4-1 Screen Radiance Cache (50-70% cheaper).
  Evidence: lumen_2022.txt:2724-2815 lists, in order: visible-GGX ray generation, spatial reuse reweighted by the BRDF, temporal accumulation, then the bilateral filter (high variance after reuse, forced on at double strength on disocclusion, tonemapped weights), then TAA, which 'cleans it up a bit more'. Roughness bands: 0.3-0.4 uses shortened rays with the World Radiance Cache (another 16% off the roads). 0.4-1 resamples the Screen Radiance Cache (50-70% cheaper).
- **B-C15** (confirmed). Claim: Snowdrop's hit shader reprojects the hit into the previous frame and reuses its lighting when visible; otherwise sun with CSM, probes and every point light from a light BVH, with emissive clamped per wave.
  Evidence: snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:648-683 covers reprojection into the previous frame, reuse when visible, and otherwise sun+CSM, probes (cache probes for specular), point lights from a BVH, and the per-wave emissive clamp. Lines 819-831 say reflections use the same pipeline as the GI, with the differences listed.
  Correction: The slide is in the RTGI section. It applies to reflections only through the talk's 'same pipeline' statement. Xbox Series S uses no point lights.
- **B-C16** (confirmed). Claim: MegaLights shades lamp specular as visible-sample weights times pre-integrated light irradiance (the authors call it an approximation), demodulates by EnvBRDF, and passes well-sampled pixels straight to TSR.
  Evidence: megalights_2025.txt:735-760: visible-sample weights times pre-integrated irradiance. The authors say separating shadowing from shading 'is not correct' and cite Heitz 2018. 765-785: EnvBRDF is factored out. 955-966: when 80% of the energy is sampled, the history weight and spatial filtering are reduced, 'maybe even' passing straight to TSR.
  Correction: The talk frames TSR pass-through as reduced denoising that can go as far as pass-through. It is not described as an unconditional path.
- **B-C17** (confirmed). Claim: The SHaRC rule: use the cache only when the lobe's cone footprint at the hit exceeds the voxel size.
  Evidence: SHaRC docs/Integration.md: for a glossy lobe, use the cache when the cone spread 2*len*sqrt(0.5*a^2/(1-a^2)) (a = roughness squared) exceeds the voxel resolution. Also keep tracing while the segment is shorter than GetVoxelSize().
  Correction: The test is made at path vertices (hits) against cells hashed on surfaces, with a minimum segment length. It is not a mid-air cut-off.
- **B-C18** (confirmed). Claim: Lumen's Matrix demo: hit lighting cost 11.54 ms against 2.44 ms for the surface cache (PS5, 1080p), with no substantial qualitative benefit there.
  Evidence: lumen_2022.txt:1845-1867, Matrix Awakens on PS5 at 1080p: reflection tracing costs 2.44 ms with the surface cache and 11.54 ms with hit lighting. The talk says hit lighting gave no substantial qualitative benefit there, because the demo depends on the SkyLight.
- **B-C19** (confirmed). Claim: AMD GI-1.1 traces glossy reflections at half resolution below roughness 0.2, uses the previous frame's pixel at visible hits and a hash-grid cache otherwise, for about 1 ms extra at 1080p on an RX 7900 XTX.
  Evidence: The session scratchpad amd_glossy.txt (Eto, 'Real-Time Rendering of Glossy Reflections Using Ray Tracing and Two-Level Radiance Caching'): screen probes above rrt, ray tracing below. Lines 186-196: half resolution, the previous-frame pixel when the hit is visible, otherwise the area light or the hash-grid cache. Lines 350-392: rrt=0.2, RX 7900 XTX at 1080p, about 1 ms overhead, RT reflection 0.18-0.28 ms.
  Correction: This is an AMD research paper built on GI-1.0/Capsaicin. No shipped title uses it.
- **B-OB1** (partly true). Claim: RageV is BETTER than the top engines at: Lamps inside reflections (NEE toward emitters). Ours: Every emitter row is scored per ray and one is picked in one pass by weighted reservoir sampling (no cap since 2026-09-25); aimed sample and lobe ray combined by the power heuristic; the lobe ray keeps only its MIS share of a struck lamp. Theirs: Lumen 2022 lists explicit emissive sampling as future work. Snowdrop suppresses emissive fireflies with a per-wave clamp, which is biased. PT titles use RTXDI with MIS; Cyberpunk uses a roughness heuristic in place of proper MIS. Why: Proper MIS with every emitter aimed at beats clamping or luck (Lumen 2022, Snowdrop) and equals RTXDI-class path tracers. The scoring loop is linear in emitters, which RT2-12 fixes.
  Evidence: The code half holds: WRS over every row with no cap (reflection_trace.rvshader:398-437; kMaxAreaEmitters no longer exists in RageV/src), the power heuristic (568-589), and StruckEmitterShare. Two limits come from the emitter list (Scene.cpp:2219-2256). Each 'emitter' is an AABB rectangle proxy stepped out by half its thickness, so the aimed pdf describes the proxy while the lobe ray strikes the real mesh shell, and the MIS pair only approximates one estimator. Emitters with strength <= 1 are not listed. On the other side: Lumen's future-work line (lumen_2022.txt:2996-3000) is about emissive sampling in the downsampled radiance cache (the GI final gather), not reflections. The Snowdrop clamp is confirmed (655-676). Cyberpunk's heuristic comes from phase-1 secondary sources.
  Correction: RageV is ahead of Lumen 2022 and Snowdrop at this. It is not ahead of 'the top engines'. The claim itself concedes it only equals RTXDI-class path tracers, and ReSTIR DI over real emissive triangles with spatiotemporal reuse beats one linear-scan candidate per ray on proxy rectangles. 'Proper MIS' should read 'MIS against a rectangle proxy'.
- **B-OB2** (refuted). Claim: RageV is BETTER than the top engines at: Anti-lag / change detection. Ours: Measured change: last frame's recorded rays re-lit with last frame's seeds against this frame's scene; the filtered changed share shortens the blend count. Theirs: A-SVGF in Quake II RTX. NRD accepts a confidence input but does not produce one. Lumen and Snowdrop reflections use colour or neighbourhood heuristics. Why: A measured, number-light change signal that fires on nothing when nothing changed. Among the reflection denoisers surveyed, none of the top engines ships one.
  Evidence: The RageV half holds: MeasuredChange=true (EngineConfig.h:563), relight and record on 1/3-resolution blocks (FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:2515-2587), and blendFrames capped by 1/measured (reflection_accumulate.rvshader:1785-1802). The NRD README, however, prescribes this exact method: history confidence from the gradient between last frame's stored radiance and last frame's sample re-traced this frame with the previous frame's RNG seed, with spatial blurring of the gradients. It points to the NRD sample's 'SHARC Update' and 'ConfidenceBlur' passes as the implementation. A-SVGF shipped in Quake II RTX.
  Correction: RageV matches NRD's documented, sample-implemented practice and A-SVGF. It is ahead only of Lumen and Snowdrop's heuristics. 'NRD does not produce one' holds for the library binary but is misleading, because the SDK sample ships a generator.
- **B-BB1** (partly true). Claim: Could beat both RageV and the shipped baseline: Footprint-terminated reflection rays: one estimator across all roughness. A ray continues until the lobe's cone footprint at the ray's current length exceeds the stored-light cell there (the bake field, plus RT2-25's realtime cache where built). Only then does it read the lobe's radiance from the cell. A near-mirror ray never terminates early. A roughness-0.9 ray terminates at once, which is the stored-light lookup.. Why: Lumen switches estimators at fixed roughness bands (0.3, 0.4) and Snowdrop hides its seam with PreferDarkerLerp. Both are discontinuous in roughness by construction. Snowdrop's ray lengths are tuned in metres (50 m to 5 m), which breaks owner rule 2. RageV today cross-fades against a probe a third too bright. Footprint termination is continuous in roughness, scale-free and needs no cutoff.
  Evidence: The SHaRC rule is confirmed (Integration.md). Lumen's bands (0.3/0.4) are confirmed. Snowdrop (snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:852-880): the ray range is a smooth function of roughness, dithered with +/-0.045 roughness noise 'to not have a straight cut', and floored at twice the cache spacing. The GI blend (1096-1099) is a linear fade saturate((Max-r)*InvFade) through PreferDarkerLerp. RageV's bake field is an irradiance field (irradiance_fill.rvshader: three rgba16f tiles per cell), which has no directional resolution for a glossy lobe.
  Correction: Snowdrop is not discontinuous in roughness by construction. It fades and dithers, with a PreferDarkerLerp bias. The metre-tuned lengths are still a valid objection. In SHaRC's rule a roughness-0.9 ray does not stop 'at once': the footprint starts at zero, so the ray needs about half a cell, and the rule is applied at hit vertices. The idea needs a directional radiance cache (RT2-25). The bake field cannot supply a lobe's radiance. It stays a candidate with a measurement gate.
- **B-BB2** (plausible). Claim: Could beat both RageV and the shipped baseline: Ray rate per tile from the lobe footprint: the trace spends no more rays on a resolve disc than the resolve can gather (rays per texel = min(1, T / (pi rho^2)), with rho the disc radius in texels and T the resolve's tap count). Rates are quantised to 1, 1/2 (checkerboard), 1/4 and 1/16 for coherent dispatch, and traded within one fixed average ray budget read at the reprojected position.. Why: Snowdrop's tile quality uses five hand-tuned weights ('needs a lot of tweaking'). BFV uses an importance LUT, AMD fixed roughness thresholds. RageV's RT-9 only adds rays and reads the wrong tile under motion. The footprint rule follows from the ratio estimator itself: rays inside one disc are pooled anyway.
  Evidence: Snowdrop's mixed resolution: -28% total (table at snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:1169-1181, 4.480 to 3.246 ms). Its tile quality uses five weights and 'needs a lot of tweaking' (916-933). The AMD half-resolution glossy trace costs 0.18-0.28 ms. RT-9 only raises rays above one (FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:2385-2392), and the trace reads the tile map at the current texel with no reprojection (reflection_trace.rvshader:782-784).
  Correction: The claim that the rule 'follows from the ratio estimator' is an argument, not a result. Neighbouring texels would share fewer rays, so their resolve outputs correlate and the noise becomes blotchy at low frequency, which the temporal and blur stages must then remove. T (the tap count) is itself a preset value. This needs its measurement gate.
- **B-BB3** (partly true). Claim: Could beat both RageV and the shipped baseline: One specular signal with MIS across passes. A lamp's specular is sampled once, by the shared light-candidate structure (RT2-12), and weighted by the power heuristic against the reflection's lobe ray. The lobe ray's struck emission keeps the complementary weight, using the candidate structure's evaluable pdf. Both halves go into the one demodulated specular signal and one specular denoiser with virtual-image reprojection. The diffuse half of direct light stays with RT2-24.. Why: MegaLights shades lamp specular as visible-sample weights times pre-integrated irradiance, which its own authors call an approximation that fails for specular, reprojected by the surface. UE otherwise leaves double counting to the artist. Cyberpunk uses a roughness heuristic, not proper MIS. RageV today counts a fixture twice (analytic highlight plus traced emitter), runs lamp selection twice (DirectTrace and the reflection's own reservoir), and pays +2.1 ms for a lamp pool that RT2-12's reuse would provide once.
  Evidence: The NRD combined-denoising guidance is confirmed. RageV's double count is confirmed (B-C2), and so is the 4-frame surface-reprojected direct-specular twin (Renderer3D.cpp:7414 PairMemory=4, DirectSignal Kind::Diffuse). The RT-11 13.31->9.69 figure is recorded (RT-SERIES.md:171). MegaLights (megalights_2025.txt:745-760) says only that the approximation 'is not correct' in general. It does not say it 'fails for specular'. 'UE leaves double counting to the artist' appears in no source.
  Correction: The direction is sound, but it is RTXDI/NRD's documented practice (proper MIS is an RTXDI option), so it does not go beyond the top engines. MIS is only possible if each fixture has one representation the lobe ray can strike. An analytic Light has no geometry, so a Light paired with an emissive mesh must become one emitter, or it is still counted twice. The MegaLights wording must be corrected, and the UE statement is unsupported.
- **B-BB4** (refuted). Claim: Could beat both RageV and the shipped baseline: Measured change (re-lighting last frame's recorded rays) as the history-confidence input of the v2 specular denoiser, in place of every colour-box, identity and hit-distance ramp that exists only to guess whether the content changed.. Why: NRD takes a confidence input but ships no generator; its docs call one highly recommended. Lumen, Snowdrop and AMD infer change from colour or neighbourhood heuristics. RageV already generates a measured change signal for reflections.
  Evidence: The NRD README describes this exact generator for IN_SPEC_CONFIDENCE (same seeds, gradient, spatial blur) and calls it 'easy and cheap to compute'. It also says the denoisers keep their own anti-lag and that confidence is 'the best way to mitigate temporal lags', which does not mean it replaces the other tests. The cited 9.2->4.8 floor-trail gain came from feeding the change map to TAA (HANDOFF.md:216; RT-SERIES.md:76), so it depends on TAA.
  Correction: Measured change as the confidence input is the NRD-recommended baseline, not something that beats it. It measures the lighting change at last frame's recorded sample points (1/3 resolution). It cannot detect a history fetched from the wrong place (virtual-motion error, disocclusion, a different struck object that is lit the same). Replacing the identity, colour-box and hit-distance tests with it is unsupported.
- **B-BB5** (plausible). Claim: Could beat both RageV and the shipped baseline: Keep RT-24's number-free lobe-agreement weight, (narrow over wide lobe width in texels) squared, gated by how far the image slid, as v2's roughness confidence term instead of NRD's tuned lobe and roughness fractions.. Why: NRD's roughness and lobe weights are convention constants (lobeAngleFraction 0.15, roughness fraction 0.15, per phase 1). RageV's is derived from the blur widths of the two lobes.
  Evidence: reflection_accumulate.rvshader:1582-1596 computes the weight from the lobe widths, (narrow/wide)^2 gated by min(slide,1). Its only evidence is that it measured equal to the tuned version and parked bit-identical (the comment itself). NRD ReBLUR lobeAngleFraction=0.15 and roughnessFraction=0.15 (NRDSettings.h), but ReLAX's lobeAngleFraction is 0.5, and both are spatial-filter rejection fractions, not the temporal candidate confidence.
  Correction: The comparison with NRD mixes a spatial-filter parameter with a temporal confidence. The weight is fed by the image distance (perAlpha uses `image`), so it inherits B-C3's defect: misses averaged in at 1e4 and clamped to 1e3. It was only ever shown equal. It needs a gate showing it is better.

### B.8 Design changes the verification required

Each was applied to the roadmap's items on 2026-09-25 (section 9 lists them).

- **C2. Tile classification, ray rate and roughness tiers (one estimator).** Footprint termination must name a store that can answer a glossy lobe. The bake field is an irradiance field, so C2 depends on RT2-25's directional realtime cache, or it must fall back to prefiltered probes. Apply SHaRC's rule as written: test at hit vertices, with the minimum-segment rule, and drop 'a roughness-0.9 ray terminates at once'. Remove the claim that Snowdrop is discontinuous by construction (it fades and dithers). The objection that stands is metre-tuned lengths plus the PreferDarkerLerp bias. Keep the footprint ray rate as a measured candidate. Add a gate for correlated (blotchy) resolve noise at 1/4 and 1/16 rate, not only speckle. Rests on: B-BB1, B-BB2, B-C9.
- **C4. One specular signal and the lamps (cross-area with A).** MIS is only unbiased if the light sampler and the struck-emitter pdf describe the same surface. Today's emitters are AABB-rectangle proxies with a strength>1 membership rule. C4 must depend on RT2-11 sampling the real emitting surface and on emitters being members because they emit anything. Lights that have a fixture mesh need an explicit single-representation rule, since an analytic Light cannot be struck by a lobe ray. Present C4 as matching RTXDI/NRD practice, not beating the top engines, and correct the MegaLights citation. Rests on: B-OB1, B-BB3.
- **C5. Spatial pre-pass (resolve).** 'No emission-share exemption, because every emitter is now aimed at' is false today. Emitters with strength<=1 (Scene.cpp:2224) and any surface outside the list are found only by lobe rays. Make the exemption's removal conditional on RT2-11's membership change. Rests on: B-OB1.
- **C6. Temporal accumulation (v2).** Present measured change as the NRD-documented confidence input, not as a beyond-baseline feature, and keep the geometric and identity tests alongside it. Confidence measures lighting change, not reprojection correctness. Fix the image distance first (misses flagged, a tracking minimum over the disc; B-C3), then re-derive the lobe-agreement weight on it. Add a gate that the weight beats NRD's form, since equality is all that has been shown. Rests on: B-BB4, B-BB5, B-OB2.
- **C7. Young pixels, spatial filter and stabilisation.** The stabilisation anti-lag through measured change is fine as NRD practice. Its supporting evidence (the 9.2->4.8 trail) came from feeding TAA, so the C7 gate with TAA memory off must re-measure the lag benefit without TAA. Rests on: B-BB4.
- **C0. Finish RT-24 inside today's chain (attribution, not new machinery).** Add the three dropped values (resolve 0.9 normal cut, blur 0.2 roughness cut, blur normal power 8) to section 10 from audit_phase1.json. Also check whether other 'other' or 'unclear' items were lost in the same way. Rests on: B-C11.

### B.9 New findings from the verification

- **B-N1.** The NRD README documents RageV's measured-change method exactly: last frame's sample re-traced with the previous frame's RNG seed, the gradient taken against the stored radiance, then blurred spatially. The NRD sample implements it (the 'SHARC Update' and 'ConfidenceBlur' passes). This undermines OB2 and BB4.
- **B-N2.** Area emitters are AABB-rectangle proxies (thinnest box axis taken as the normal, point stepped out by half the thickness), listed only when strength > 1 (Scene.cpp:2219-2256). The reflection's MIS pairs the proxy's pdf with lobe-ray hits on the real mesh shell (StruckEmitterShare evaluates the rectangle pdf at the shell point), so it is approximate, not 'proper' MIS.
- **B-N3.** Snowdrop's reflection ray length is a smooth function of roughness, dithered with +/-0.045 roughness noise to avoid a hard cut and floored at twice the cache spacing. The RT-to-GI seam is a linear fade through PreferDarkerLerp, not a band switch.
- **B-N4.** Lumen 2022's 'explicit sampling of emissive' future-work item refers to the downsampled screen radiance cache (the GI final gather), not reflections.
- **B-N5.** RageV's bake 'field' is an irradiance field (irradiance_fill.rvshader, three rgba16f tiles per cell), so it cannot supply a glossy lobe's radiance for footprint termination.
- **B-N6.** The lamp pool is also skipped at mirror texels (alpha<1e-4) and where the disc reach is under half a texel, not only for multi-ray texels (reflection_resolve.rvshader:418-419, 440-441).
- **B-N7.** The RT-24 hard-coded audit found the three 'missed' values in audit_phase1.json. Section 10 dropped them, so other audit items marked 'other' or 'unclear' may have been lost in the same way.
- **B-N8.** RT-9's tile map is read at the current texel's UV (reflection_trace.rvshader:782-784). It was built from last frame's picture and is never reprojected.
- **B-N9.** The Snowdrop '¼ resolution' glyph is byte 0xBC in the extracted text, so it is certainly quarter resolution. The footnote covers the RTX 4080 column too.
- **B-N10.** MegaLights' authors say only that separating shadowing from shading 'is not correct'. BB3's 'fails for specular' paraphrase is not in the source.

### B.10 The comparison's corrections to the survey

- Phase 1 called Lumen hit lighting '~5x slower than surface cache on PS5' a weak secondary claim. The primary talk confirms it: Matrix Awakens reflection tracing 11.54 ms with hit lighting against 2.44 ms with the Surface Cache (4.7x, PS5 1080p), and Epic says hit lighting gave no substantial qualitative benefit there (lumen_2022.txt:1855-1867).
- Phase 1's Lumen roughness split was incomplete. The primary gives three bands: 0-0.3 traced; 0.3-0.4 traced with shortened rays and the World Radiance Cache (32x32 directions) on a miss, -16% on roads; 0.4-1.0 no rays, GGX-importance-sampled resample of the 8x8 Screen Radiance Cache, -50-70% (lumen_2022.txt:2764-2815).
- Phase 1 listed Lumen's bilateral as the last denoiser step. The primary adds that it runs only on high-variance areas after spatial reuse, is forced at double strength on disocclusions, uses tonemapped weighting there because it would crush highlights in spatial reuse, and that full-frame TAA then 'cleans it up a bit more'. So Lumen's reflections do not meet the owner's rule 3 (lumen_2022.txt:2745-2758).
- Lumen's reflection temporal reprojection method (hit-depth reprojection) could not be confirmed from the 2022 talk: its reflection section names only 'temporal accumulation'. It stays unverified.
- Lumen 2022 did not sample emissive surfaces explicitly (listed as future work, lumen_2022.txt:2996-3000). On lamp reflections RageV's aimed NEE is ahead of that version.
- Lumen 2022 runs reflections on the front translucent layer on depth-peeled pixels only, forced to mirror with the denoiser disabled; deeper layers use the World Radiance Cache (lumen_2022.txt:2858-2890). This matters for RT2-31 (area G).
- Snowdrop was marked unread in phase 1. It is now read. RT reflections: GGX sampling, a screen-space trace, then world-space RT, then a light-cache fallback. Hits reproject into the previous frame for lighting, else sun, CSM, probes and BVH point lights. Variable mixed resolution per 16x16 tile (1/16 to full) from five tuned weights. Demodulation before denoising. Five denoisers including ReBLUR, ReLAX and FFX. Roughness 0.9 or above uses the RTGI SH, with a PreferDarkerLerp from 0.7. Ray length is tuned in metres (50 m at roughness 0.1 down to 5 m at 0.7), which breaks owner rule 2. 2.132 ms at 2160p on an RTX 4080, denoising 67-79% of it (snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:811-1114, 1233-1250).
- Phase 1's AMD GI-1.1 figures match the paper text in the session scratchpad: half-resolution RT below roughness 0.2, 0.6 for diffuse, previous-frame pixel at visible hits, hash-grid fallback, about 1 ms reflection overhead at 1080p on an RX 7900 XTX (amd_glossy.txt:186-196, 350-392).
- RT-SERIES-2 and REFL-03 cite reflection_trace.rvshader:826/949/1013 for the miss-as-10km mean and the 64 cap. In the working tree these are now lines 703, 835 and 900-902. The defect is unchanged.
- RT-SERIES-2 section 10's reflection audit missed three fixed values: the resolve's neighbour normal cut dot(N, Nn) < 0.9 (reflection_resolve.rvshader:562), and the blur's hard roughness cut |rn - roughness| < 0.2 and normal power 8 (reflection_blur.rvshader:409-411).
- The task text says sharp_study.py is in tools/scripts/garage. It is only in the session scratchpad (with drift_field.py and render_authored.py) and must be moved into the repo before any gate cites it.
- The headers of reflection_composite.rvshader:1-31 and reflection_accumulate.rvshader:49-54 still say the picture is added after TAA. The composite runs before the TAA resolve (FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:3788-3812 against 3916), as REFL-01 found. Still stale.

### B.11 Questions left open

As the survey recorded them. Where the verification later read a primary talk the survey could not fetch, the verification's verdict above supersedes the line here.

- Lumen SIGGRAPH 2022 slides (PDF over 10 MB) could not be read. Lumen's reflection temporal reprojection method (hit-depth reprojection) and its timings are from memory, not verified. The UE 5.5 'new Lumen reflection denoiser' internals are unpublished.
- Snowdrop (GDC 2024), Assassin's Creed Shadows (SIGGRAPH 2025) and MegaLights (SIGGRAPH 2025) slide PDFs are over 10 MB and were not read, so their reflection denoisers, hit shading and ms are unknown.
- No primary technical source was found for Insomniac's RT reflections, Alan Wake 2's reflection denoiser, or id Tech's (Indiana Jones, Doom: The Dark Ages) reflections. Only secondary coverage exists.
- DLSS Ray Reconstruction's cost in ms is not officially published. NRD's specular-only cost is not published separately. No reference numbers exist for an RTX 5070 Ti Laptop at 2560x1600.
- The Battlefield V slides give per-pass ms (6.29 ms total) but no GPU or resolution.
- The PICA PICA chapter (60 MB) was not read; its reflection resolution, tap counts and timings are from memory (medium confidence).
- The claim that Lumen hit lighting is ~5x slower than surface cache on PS5 comes from secondary coverage; the primary talk was not located.
- Whether SER-equivalent reordering is available for inline ray queries on Vulkan (VK_NV/EXT invocation reorder appears to be RT-pipeline-only) was not verified against the spec.
- No independent head-to-head of NRD ReBLUR, AMD FFX, Lumen and DLSS RR on the same scene was found. 'Better per ms' claims between them are unmeasured.
- Claims the verification could not settle without a measurement or a source: B-BB2, B-BB5 (each is gated in the roadmap item named above).

## C. Bounce light (GI), radiance caches, the bake

**In short.** The top engines that ship ray-traced bounce light without a bake use one ray per traced pixel, a world cache every hit reads, harmonic output and short rays; RageV casts up to 4 rays, shades every hit in full, has no cache, and in baked scenes runs no live bounce at all. The shipped bakes place sparse bricks automatically; RageV's uses 8 hand-placed boxes. The verified verdicts, with the candidates tied to their items, are in the roadmap's section 11.

**Counts.** Comparison topics: 13 behind, 4 better (claimed), 3 missing, 3 not comparable, 2 equal. Verification of 24 claims: 13 confirmed, 7 partly true, 2 refuted, 1 unsupported, 1 plausible.

**The survey, in its own summary.** Every AAA engine that shipped ray-traced GI without a bake in 2024-2025 is built the same way. A cheap gather runs at reduced resolution: one ray per pixel, or screen probes, with short rays. Behind it sits a world-space cache that every ray hit and every ray miss reads. Multi-bounce comes from feeding last frame's cache into hit shading. The signal gets its own spatial and temporal denoiser before TAA. id Tech 8 (Doom: The Dark Ages) is the leanest version:; Cascaded irradiance volumes, updated by ray tracing.; A spatial-hash radiance cache with 25 cm cells plus distance LOD.; A final gather of 1 ray per pixel at half or quarter resolution. It does no shading at the hit: it reads screen lighting first, then the hash cache, then the volumes.; About 1.7 ms at 4K on an RTX 4080. It has no bake and uses no disk. Snowdrop (Avatar) and Anvil (AC Shadows) use DDGI-style probe cascades as the cache. Their per-pixel rays run at quarter resolution, try a screen-space trace first, and are cut to twice the probe spacing. AC Shadows costs 2.65 ms at 1440p on an RTX 4070, plus about 1 ms of probe update. Lumen uses screen probes (1/16 to 1/4 ray per pixel at 1080p) over a world radiance cache and a card surface cache. It costs 2.8 to 5.5 ms on PS5 at 1080p. It is designed around TSR and cannot be combined with lightmap GI. The path-traced modes (Cyberpunk, Indiana Jones, Doom) use ReSTIR GI and/or the SHaRC hash cache. All of them lean on DLSS Ray Reconstruction, which breaks the no-TAA rule. NRC ships only in RTX Remix titles; it is a binary library and NVIDIA-only. For the bake itself, what engines ship is:; directional (spherical-harmonic) lightmaps;; sparse 4x4x4-brick probe volumes, sampled per pixel, with probes moved out of walls and bad probes filled from neighbours (Unity APV, UE volumetric lightmaps, id Tech 7);; per-object stale-lighting tracking (UE's "lighting needs to be rebuilt"). The owner's two-layer design (D4) has only partial precedents. Unity lets baked GI run alongside Enlighten realtime GI, which is CPU radiosity, not ray tracing. UE Stationary lights keep live direct light over a baked bounce. I found no shipped ray-traced engine that adds a bounce layer for Realtime lights only on top of a bake. So the "light adds up" argument must be proved by diff images. The research frontier does not yet clearly beat the id/Snowdrop pattern on quality per ms for a mostly-static scene: on-surface caches (+5 to 10% over GI-1.0), Split Radiance Cascades (13 ms, research), ReSTIR PT Enhanced (12 to 21 ms at 1080p), and neural irradiance volumes (about 1 ms, static lighting only). Each needs measuring in RageV before it can be adopted.

**The comparison, in its own summary.** Where RageV is ahead. Its bake stores more than the shipped volume bakes: nine spherical-harmonic numbers per colour, the baked lamps' direct light kept apart from the bounce, a lamp direction recovered for highlights, and neighbour-visibility bits against leaks. It is solved by the same hit shader the frame uses, so the bake and the frame cannot drift apart. The per-pixel bounce aims rays at every glowing surface; Lumen 2022 lists that as future work. Its anti-lag re-traces the same rays to measure what changed, where the shipped engines guess. The Hybrid light (live up close, baked further out) has no shipped equal. Where the shipped engines are ahead: the live bounce.; RageV casts 4 rays per traced pixel. id, Snowdrop and Anvil cast 1.; It shades every hit in full and walks every light to do it.; It stores plain colour, so normal-map detail is lost when the bounce is scaled up. The shipped engines store spherical harmonics and evaluate them with the full-resolution normal.; No world cache is read at any hit. Every shipped engine has one.; It relies on fixed clamps tuned on one scene. On the bake, Unity and Unreal place sparse bricks automatically. RageV allows at most 8 hand-placed boxes, does not blend where they nest, and cannot tell that a moved wall has made the bake stale. It also has no lightmaps (one UV set), so baked contact detail is coarser. What could beat both, each to be measured: 1. The one-ray gather also reads the baked field where its rays land. Baked lamps then get contact-scale bounce without lightmaps. RageV measured this arrangement as its best picture (error 0.73, against 5.73 for the field alone) and rejected it only for cost, when every sample cost several rays. 2. A correction for moving objects: take out the baked bounce they block and add the bounce they give. 3. The same measured-change test applied to each cell of the cache. Roadmap changes:; RT2-25 is amended, not replaced. The two-layer rule stays, stated per light path, with the sky as a light. The gather becomes 1 ray per traced pixel with spherical-harmonic output. The cache is decided: a spatial hash whose cell size comes from the pixel footprint, so nothing is in metres. Every hit is shaded through one contract. Probe capture moves to RT2-27.; The ~1 ms proposal for the live layer is below every shipped figure at this pixel count; 2.0 ms is the realistic ceiling.; RT2-26 is amended. Automatically placed sparse bricks replace hand-placed volumes. Moving cells out of walls becomes a measured arm: RageV measured it helping one leak test and hurting the sun-lit ones, and Snowdrop shipped without it. Bake hits get their sky light occluded. The stale-bake check names the objects that changed.; RT2-30 gains the deletions this frees.

**The verification, in its own summary.** Of the 11 code claims, 9 are confirmed and 2 are partly true. C-C5: the upsample does lose normal-map detail, but 'SH recovers it' is unproven. C-C9: lightmaps do need a second UV set, but Lumen cards are UV-less projections. C-C1, C-C2 and C-C4 are confirmed with nuances: the GI ray count is a per-tile ceiling under the editor's budget; a baked reflection probe still freezes Realtime light that was on at capture; the trace-only light walk still uses the 16-byte cull record. Of the industry claims, Snowdrop, Lumen and id Tech 8 check out against the primary texts (the Sousa PDF was fetched and read). The AC Shadows numbers (C-C16) stay unsupported because the PDF could not be loaded. C-C17's numbers are right but selectively quoted: on the showroom's bright bay the field-terminated hybrid lost to realtime (1.00 against 0.51). Of the 'ours better' verdicts, two fail and two are only partly true. Anti-lag (C-OB1) is refuted: NRD's README documents the same A-SVGF re-trace recipe. Field storage (C-OB3) is refuted: UE Volumetric Lightmaps also store L2 SH. Emitter NEE (C-OB2) is partly true: it is ahead of Lumen, Snowdrop and id only after its own admitted defects are fixed, and it clamps too. Bake consistency (C-OB4) is partly true: several fill-only branches exist, and the bake inherits the frame's approximations. Of the beats-both ideas, C-BB2 is plausible and needs a gate. C-BB1 is partly true: the evidence is mixed and the fetch cost is understated (~20 to ~150 fetches per read, not one). C-BB3 is partly true: shipped DDGI already restarts per probe on a measured change. Required design changes: the cache anti-lag rationale and gate, the bake-gather gates and cost, the hit-contract rationale plus the ambient leak at bake hits, the claims about emitters and field storage, and the Section 7 'second UV set' line for Lumen cards.

### C.1 What the top engines ship

- **Unreal Engine 5 - Lumen (SWRT/HWRT).** Screen-space radiance cache: probes are placed on pixels on a 16-pixel grid, with adaptive extra probes. Each stores 8x8 octahedral radiance and is importance-sampled, then spatially filtered and temporally accumulated. A sparse world-space radiance cache, in clipmaps with 32x32 directional resolution, supplies distant lighting. Rays trace screen-space first, then mesh SDF within about 2 m, then global SDF or HWRT, and fall back to the world cache on a short-ray miss. A card-based surface cache supplies lighting at hits. Multi-bounce comes from surface-cache feedback. Surface-cache update budget is 1024x1024 texels of direct light and 512x512 texels of indirect light per frame, chosen by priority (LastUsed - LastUpdated). The world cache updates a fixed number of probes from a GPU priority queue. Lumen GI cannot be combined with lightmap static lighting.
  Numbers: PS5 1080p internal, TSR to 4K. Final gather is 1/16 ray per pixel at High and 1/4 at Epic. 'Land of Nanite' (SWRT): 2.77 ms total at High, 4.63 ms at Epic. Matrix Awakens (HWRT + far field): surface cache 1.03/1.17 ms, final gather 3.05/5.52 ms (High/Epic). Official budgets at 1080p internal for GI, reflections, translucency and fog: 4 ms for 60 fps, 8 ms for 30 fps. No PC native-resolution numbers are published.
  Status: shipped (many UE5 titles)
  Sources: <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2022/SIGGRAPH2022-Advances-Lumen-Wright%20et%20al.pdf>; <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/lumen-technical-details-in-unreal-engine>; <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/lumen-performance-guide-for-unreal-engine>
- **id Tech 8 (DOOM: The Dark Ages; an early iteration in Indiana Jones and the Great Circle).** Fully realtime, with no bake. It replaced id Tech 7's path-traced lightmaps, which would have cost an estimated 44-110 GB of disk.; A world-space cascaded light grid (16^3 cells x 8 cascades) lets ray hits outside the frustum be lit.; Cascaded irradiance volumes (16^3 x 6 cascades, plus hand-placed 10^3 local volumes) trace 64/32/16 visibility rays per probe. The updates are interleaved: 1 cascade plus 1 volume per frame.; Each probe-ray hit writes to a spatial-hash world radiance cache (25 cm cells plus distance LOD, 14 MB). About 20k entries are shaded per frame with the same light loop as raster, and previous-frame volumes give multi-bounce.; The final gather traces 1 cosine ray per pixel at reduced resolution. It does no shading: screen-space cache if the hit is on screen and unoccluded, else the hash cache, else the volumes. It stores 2-band SH per pixel.; A separable bilateral denoise, a 4-tap bilateral upscale and a temporal filter follow, with TAA as an extra filter at the end.
  Numbers: Shipped hotspot, serial cost:; PC at 4K (RTX 4080): world sampling 0.091 ms, radiance cache 0.109, volumes 0.07, final gather 0.5, denoise 0.52, upscale 0.445; about 1.7 ms total.; PS5 at 1440p: about 2.05 ms serial, 1.55 ms with async.; Xbox Series S at 900p: about 2.11 ms serial, 1.71 ms with async. Memory: probe atlas 64 MB; light grid 30 MB.
  Status: shipped
  Sources: <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/SOUSA_SIGGRAPH_2025_Final.pdf>; <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/index.html>
- **id Tech path-tracing modes (Indiana Jones PT, DOOM: The Dark Ages PT).** A path tracer with the SHaRC hash radiance cache. In Indiana Jones, the SHaRC update pass traces up to 4 bounces and the main trace up to 2 bounces, then reuses the cache. Denoising is DLSS Ray Reconstruction. It runs at 1080p internal with 4K output.
  Numbers: Indiana Jones on an RTX 5080 at 1080p internal, 4K output, DLSS RR Performance: SharcUpdate 0.89 ms (1.99 ms without opacity micromaps), TraceMain 3.58 ms (7.90 ms without). With SER, TraceMain went from 4.07 to 3.08 ms.
  Status: shipped (optional PC mode)
  Sources: <https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/path-tracing-optimizations-in-indiana-jones-opacity-micromaps-and-compaction-of-dynamic-blass/>; <https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/path-tracing-optimization-in-indiana-jones-shader-execution-reordering-and-live-state-reductions/>; <https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/geforce/news/doom-the-dark-ages-path-tracing-dlss-ray-reconstruction-update/>
- **Snowdrop (Avatar: Frontiers of Pandora).** DDGI-derived probe cascades used as a light cache.; 4 cascades of 32x32x8 probes with spacing 2/8/64/1024 m, following the camera with a forward offset.; 400 rays per probe; rays reach twice the next cascade's spacing.; Each probe keeps a probe G-buffer, so it can be relit without being retraced. The budget is 1024 probes per frame: about 15% retraced, about 85% relit only.; Hits are shaded with a simplified BSDF: sun via CSM plus a shadow ray, analytic lights via a light BVH, indirect from other probes.; Per-pixel GI is 1 cosine ray per pixel at quarter, half or full resolution (consoles use quarter). It traces screen-space first, then HW rays of limited length, then falls back to the probe cache. Output is SH.; Denoising uses the in-house Modular Snowdrop Denoiser (recurrent blur); NRD ReBLUR and ReLAX are also available.
  Numbers: Probe update, async: RTX 4080 0.258 ms; Xbox Series X 0.715 ms; Series S 1.868 ms. Diffuse RTGI on Series X at 1440p, quarter resolution: tracing 1.462 ms (screen-space 0.442 + world 1.020), lighting 0.842, denoise 1.573, total 3.877 ms. The baseline without screen tracing and cache fallback was 7.211 ms. The cache fallback alone cut tracing by 52%.
  Status: shipped
  Sources: <https://media.gdcvault.com/gdc2024/Slides/GDC+slide+presentations/Kuenlib_Quentin_Raytracing_In_Snowdrop.pdf>; <https://www.massive.se/blog/games-technology/snowdrops-ray-tracing-shines-a-light-on-pandora/>
- **Anvil (Assassin's Creed Shadows).** Same family as Snowdrop.; 5 cascades of 16x16x8 probes (10,240 probes) with 2 to 32 m spacing and toroidal addressing.; Each probe keeps a probe G-buffer, a visibility map, a radiance cache and an irradiance cache.; 1024 probes per frame (512 in 60 fps mode or on Series S). About 16% are traced; the rest are relit and convolved only. Probes are relocated and classified indoor or outdoor.; Per-pixel diffuse is 1 ray per pixel at quarter resolution with a range of twice the cascade's probe spacing, then SH output, MSD denoise and bilateral upscale.; A baked diffuse GI path also ships as an alternative on some platforms and modes. The two are not layered.
  Numbers: Probe update: RTX 4070, 512 probes, 0.97 ms serial / 0.42 ms async; PS5, 1024 probes, 1.24 ms / 0.91 ms. Diffuse GI at quarter resolution: RTX 4070 at 1440p 2.65 ms serial / 1.90 ms async (screen-space 0.27, world 0.55, lighting 0.97, denoise 0.86); PS5 at 1440p 7.12 ms / 4.58 ms. Probe memory: 73 MB.
  Status: shipped
  Sources: <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/Advances%202025%20-%20Raytracing%20the%20world%20of%20Assassin's%20Creed%20Shadows.pdf>
- **4A Engine (Metro Exodus Enhanced Edition).** One diffuse ray per pixel from the rasterized G-buffer. A world-space DDGI-style probe grid is updated by rays, and it samples its own previous frame, which gives infinite bounces over time. Ray hits read the grid. Up to 256 analytic lights plus emissive surfaces are handled, with world-grid light culling. Output is SH, with a non-linear denoiser.
  Numbers: Not published (DLSS is used to lower internal resolution).
  Status: shipped
  Sources: <https://www.4a-games.com.mt/4a-dna/in-depth-technical-dive-into-metro-exodus-pc-enhanced-edition>
- **REDengine (Cyberpunk 2077 RT: Overdrive).** Path tracing with ReSTIR DI (RTXDI) and ReSTIR GI, a SHaRC world cache for later bounces, and NRD or DLSS Ray Reconstruction for denoising.
  Numbers: Not published in sources I could read. The GDC 2024 talk requires a login. NVIDIA describes SHaRC's cost there as negligible (vendor claim).
  Status: shipped (optional PC mode)
  Sources: <https://intro-to-restir.cwyman.org/presentations/2023ReSTIR_Course_Cyberpunk_2077_Integration.pdf>; <https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/on-demand/session/gdc24-gdc1002/>; <https://x.com/acmarrs/status/1770652409792282784>
- **Frostbite / EA SEED GIBS (EA SPORTS College Football 25).** Surfels are spawned on screen-visible geometry at constant screen density and persist across frames. They sit in a camera-perspective acceleration structure and are updated by HW ray tracing, with a global ray budget, ray guiding (a 6x6 per-surfel function), variance-based ray counts and spatial filtering. Characters use ray-traced GPU probes.
  Numbers: 2021 talk, PS5 with 4K output and screen passes at 1080p: about 7 ms total in a large vista, about 11 ms while converging with a 1M-ray budget, filtering about 0.2 ms. College Football 25 shipped at 60 fps on PS5, XSX and XSS; no ms published.
  Status: shipped
  Sources: <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2021/SIGGRAPH%20Advances%202021%20-%20Surfel%20GI.pdf>; <https://www.ea.com/seed/news/siggraph21-global-illumination-surfels>; <https://www.ea.com/technology/news/gibs-lighting-ea-sports-college-football-25>
- **Unity (HDRP/URP).** The bake:; Adaptive Probe Volumes: 4x4x4-probe bricks at 1/3/9/27 m spacing, sampled per pixel from the 8 nearest probes.; Leak fixes: Virtual Offset, Dilation, up to 4 Rendering Layers, Adjustment Volumes, and a Leak Reduction mode.; Lighting Scenarios can be blended at runtime. Sky occlusion allows a dynamic sky.; Mixed lighting modes: Baked Indirect, Shadowmask/Distance Shadowmask (at most 4 lights per texel) and Subtractive. Enlighten realtime GI (precomputed visibility, CPU radiosity, multi-frame latency) can run alongside baked GI, with Realtime lights adding realtime indirect light. Unity calls this rarely advisable, and Unity 6 is the last release with Enlighten. HDRP ray-traced GI: Performance mode traces 1 ray per pixel at full or quarter resolution; Quality mode uses more samples and bounces; misses and the last bounce fall back to reflection probes and/or sky.
  Numbers: Not published in the docs read.
  Status: shipped
  Sources: <https://docs.unity3d.com/Packages/com.unity.render-pipelines.high-definition@17.1/manual/probevolumes-concept.html>; <https://docs.unity3d.com/Packages/com.unity.render-pipelines.high-definition@17.0/manual/probevolumes-fixissues.html>; <https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/Manual/lighting-mode.html>; <https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/Manual/choose-a-lighting-setup.html>; <https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.2/Documentation/Manual/realtime-gi-using-enlighten.html>; <https://docs.unity3d.com/Packages/com.unity.render-pipelines.high-definition@17.0/manual/reference-screen-space-global-illumination.html>; <https://unity.com/blog/engine-platform/new-ways-of-applying-global-illumination-in-unity-6>
- **Unreal Engine static/stationary lighting (Lightmass, GPU Lightmass).** The bake:; Lightmaps plus Volumetric Lightmaps: 4x4x4-cell bricks refined near geometry, 3rd-order SH, per-pixel interpolation. Stationary lights:; Direct light and shadows are live; indirect light is baked, with a distance-field shadowmask. At most 4 may overlap.; Color and intensity can change without a rebuild; the docs say other changes invalidate the bake. Stale bake and rebake:; The editor tracks unbuilt light interactions and warns 'Lighting needs to be rebuilt (N unbuilt objects)'.; GPU Lightmass 'Bake What You See' re-bakes visible tiles interactively.
  Numbers: Not published in the docs read.
  Status: shipped
  Sources: <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/stationary-light-mobility-in-unreal-engine>; <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/volumetric-lightmaps-in-unreal-engine>; <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/gpu-lightmass-global-illumination-in-unreal-engine>; <https://forums.unrealengine.com/t/lighting-needs-to-be-rebuilt-197-unbuilt-objects/440544>
- **NVIDIA RTX Remix titles (Half-Life 2 RTX demo, Portal RTX), Quake III RTX demo.** A path tracer with the Neural Radiance Cache: paths end after about 2-3 bounces and the rest is inferred. Denoising is DLSS Ray Reconstruction.
  Numbers: NVIDIA claims up to 15% faster in HL2 RTX (vendor claim); no ms published.
  Status: shipped (mods/demos)
  Sources: <https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/geforce/news/rtx-remix-half-life-2-rtx-demo-launching-march-18/>; <https://www.dsogaming.com/patches/portal-rtx-now-supports-dlss-4-rtx-neural-radiance-cache/>; <https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/geforce/news/gdc-2026-nvidia-geforce-rtx-announcements/>
- **THE FINALS (Embark, UE5).** RTXGI DDGI probe volumes for realtime GI that responds to destruction.
  Numbers: Not published.
  Status: shipped
  Sources: <https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/geforce/news/the-finals-released-reflex-dlss-3-ray-tracing-geforce-rtx/>; <https://github.com/NVIDIAGameWorks/RTXGI-DDGI>
- **Path of Exile 2 (Grinding Gear Games).** Screen-space Radiance Cascades GI (Sannikov). Each cascade level has half the probe density and twice the directions, and the levels are merged.
  Numbers: Not published (secondary sources only confirm it shipped).
  Status: shipped (early access Dec 2024)
  Sources: <https://www.pathofexile.com/forum/view-thread/3448733>; <https://radiance.wiki/>
- **AMD FidelityFX / research (GI-1.0, GI-1.1, Brixelizer GI, FSR Radiance Caching).** - GI-1.0: screen probes (8x8 tiles, 8x8 octahedral, filled temporally over 4 frames) plus a hash-grid second cache.; GI-1.1: adds glossy reflections.; Brixelizer GI: a compute-only sparse SDF version.; FSR Radiance Caching: an online-trained neural cache, DX12 only, RX 9000 only.
  Numbers: GI-1.0: 1.93-3.12 ms for the GI pipeline at 1080p on an RX 6900 XT; direct plus indirect 3.5/4.2 ms (Kitchen/Sponza). In the paper's own comparison: GI-1.0 HW 2.4 ms against Lumen SW 2.6 ms.
  Status: demo / SDK (FSR RC shown in a Darktide demo only)
  Sources: <https://gpuopen.com/download/publications/GPUOpen2022_GI1_0.pdf>; <https://arxiv.org/abs/2310.19855>; <https://gpuopen.com/learn/gi-1-1-glossy-reflection-rendering/>; <https://gpuopen.com/manuals/fidelityfx_sdk/techniques/brixelizer-gi/>; <https://gpuopen.com/amd-fsr-radiancecaching/>

### C.2 The techniques behind them

- **Reduced-resolution per-pixel final gather with short rays and cache fallback (id / Snowdrop / Anvil / Metro pattern)** (shipped; shipped in DOOM: The Dark Ages, Indiana Jones and the Great Circle (early iteration), Avatar: Frontiers of Pandora, Assassin's Creed Shadows, Metro Exodus Enhanced Edition; confidence high). Each traced pixel shoots one cosine-weighted ray; id uses blue noise. The ray is first marched in screen space, and continues as a HW ray only if that fails. The HW ray length is tied to the cache's cell or probe spacing: twice the spacing in Snowdrop and AC. On a miss, or at the hit, the ray reads a world-space cache instead of tracing further. id goes furthest and does no shading at the hit at all: previous-frame screen lighting, then the hash cache, then irradiance volumes. The result is stored as L1 SH per traced pixel. It is denoised at trace resolution and bilaterally upsampled, with the SH evaluated against full-resolution normals.
  Fixes: Noise and cost of per-pixel indirect. Keeps contact-scale detail and normal-map detail that probes alone lose. Short rays plus cache fallback remove most of the tracing cost. Cost: id Tech 8, RTX 4080 at 4K, final gather 0.5 ms + denoise 0.52 + upscale 0.445 (Sousa 2025). AC Shadows, RTX 4070 at 1440p, quarter resolution: 2.65 ms serial. Snowdrop, Xbox Series X at 1440p, quarter resolution: 3.88 ms; cache fallback cut tracing 52% (Kuenlin 2024). Fails: Disocclusion noise (AC added an extra Poisson spatial pass for disoccluded pixels). Leaks and lost detail where the cache is coarser than the short ray. Screen-space and world-space results can disagree at their seam. Thin geometry is missed by screen tracing.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: partly. Each engine runs its own spatial and temporal denoiser. id says TAA acts as an additional temporal filter at the end, so some residual may be hidden by TAA. This must be measured with TAA off. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Excellent: compute or fragment ray queries with a screen march first. Nothing needs any-hit (AC replaced alpha-test with scaled opaque triangles). Hit shading can be deferred to a separate pass keyed by hit data, as Snowdrop does.
  Needs: Screen-space tracer, world cache (next rows), SH output, bilateral upsample with depth and normal weights, a diffuse denoiser.
  Sources: <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/SOUSA_SIGGRAPH_2025_Final.pdf>; <https://media.gdcvault.com/gdc2024/Slides/GDC+slide+presentations/Kuenlib_Quentin_Raytracing_In_Snowdrop.pdf>; <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/Advances%202025%20-%20Raytracing%20the%20world%20of%20Assassin's%20Creed%20Shadows.pdf>; <https://www.4a-games.com.mt/4a-dna/in-depth-technical-dive-into-metro-exodus-pc-enhanced-edition>
- **Screen-space radiance cache / screen probes (Lumen final gather, AMD GI-1.0)** (shipped; shipped in Unreal Engine 5 titles (Lumen); confidence high). Hemispherical probes are placed on G-buffer pixels: every 16 pixels in Lumen, adaptively refined; one per 8x8 tile in GI-1.0, filled over 4 frames. Each probe stores 8x8 octahedral radiance and is importance-sampled from BRDF and lighting (product importance sampling). The probes are filtered in probe space and temporally reused, then interpolated to pixels. Rays are shortened and interpolate from a world cache on a miss.
  Fixes: Amortises rays below 1 per pixel while keeping leak-free placement on surfaces. More directional detail than world probes. Cost: Lumen, PS5 at 1080p: final gather 2.07 ms at 1/16 ray per pixel and 3.46 ms at 1/4 ('Land of Nanite'), 3.05/5.52 ms in Matrix Awakens (Wright 2022). GI-1.0: 1.93-3.12 ms at 1080p on an RX 6900 XT. Fails: Lag after disocclusion. Probe jitter needs reliable temporal rejection. Small lights missed at low ray counts. Lumen's 1/16 ray per pixel relies on heavy temporal reuse.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: partly. Lumen's measurements are all at 1080p with TSR to 4K; Epic's slides say the full-frame TAA cleans it up further. GI-1.0 amortises probes over 4 frames with its own temporal step. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Good. Probe tracing is a compute ray query per probe texel. Lumen's software-RT parts are not needed with hardware RT.
  Needs: G-buffer depth and normals, probe atlas, a world cache for misses, temporal reprojection with depth and normal rejection.
  Sources: <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2022/SIGGRAPH2022-Advances-Lumen-Wright%20et%20al.pdf>; <https://gpuopen.com/download/publications/GPUOpen2022_GI1_0.pdf>
- **DDGI-style ray-traced irradiance probe cascades (with visibility, relocation, classification, cached probe G-buffer)** (shipped; shipped in Avatar: Frontiers of Pandora, Assassin's Creed Shadows, THE FINALS (RTXGI), Metro Exodus EE (DDGI-like), DOOM: The Dark Ages (irradiance volumes updated from a hash cache); confidence high). World-space probe grids, cascaded around the camera with toroidal addressing, trace N rays per probe each update. They store octahedral irradiance plus depth moments, and sampling weights 8 probes by a Chebyshev visibility test. Hysteresis blends new results into old. Multi-bounce comes from hits reading the probes themselves. Production extensions (Majercik 2021):; probe relocation out of walls;; probe classification that disables useless probes;; a self-shadow bias;; infinite scrolling cascades. Snowdrop and AC also cache each probe's ray-hit G-buffer, so most updates only relight: AC traces about 16% of the budget.
  Fixes: A world cache readable from any hit or miss, anywhere, including off-screen and for reflections and translucency. Cheap multi-bounce. Cost: DDGI 2019 paper: about 6 ms for the frame's GI at 1080p on an RTX 2080 Ti, updating every probe every frame. Production numbers: Snowdrop probe update 0.258 ms on an RTX 4080 at 1024 probes per frame; AC 0.97 ms serial on an RTX 4070 at 512 probes per frame. Fails: Leaks where walls are thinner than the probe spacing. Probes stuck inside geometry go black, which Snowdrop fixes with a validity volume. Low spatial detail with no contact AO. Lag when lights change fast. Fixed cascade spacings (2/8/64/1024 m) are scene-scale constants.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no. Hysteresis is the probe's own accumulator; interpolation is smooth, with no per-pixel noise. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Excellent. The RTXGI-DDGI SDK ships Vulkan, and probe tracing is a compute ray query.
  Needs: Probe atlas (irradiance, depth moments, optional radiance), per-frame update budget and priority, toroidal scrolling, relocation and classification passes.
  Sources: <https://www.jcgt.org/published/0008/02/01/paper-lowres.pdf>; <https://arxiv.org/abs/2009.10796>; <https://github.com/NVIDIAGameWorks/RTXGI-DDGI>; <https://media.gdcvault.com/gdc2024/Slides/GDC+slide+presentations/Kuenlib_Quentin_Raytracing_In_Snowdrop.pdf>; <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/Advances%202025%20-%20Raytracing%20the%20world%20of%20Assassin's%20Creed%20Shadows.pdf>
- **Spatial-hash radiance cache (NVIDIA SHaRC; id Tech 8 world radiance cache; GI-1.0 hash cells)** (public_sdk; shipped in Cyberpunk 2077 RT Overdrive, Indiana Jones and the Great Circle (PT), DOOM: The Dark Ages (PT, and id's own hash cache in its default GI); confidence high). World positions are quantised into voxels, and voxel size grows with camera distance on a logarithmic level scheme. The voxels are hashed into a fixed 1D array, and collisions are handled by linear probing. SHaRC runs three passes:; Update: sparse paths, about one pixel per 5x5 block (about 4%); each bounce segment is accumulated independently.; Resolve: temporal accumulation, capped at a maximum number of frames, and eviction of stale entries.; Query: a path terminates into the cache at a hit whose segment is longer than the voxel size, or where a glossy lobe's cone spread exceeds the voxel. Optional modes: responsive lighting, and SH directional encoding. id Tech 8 uses 25 cm cells plus LOD, shading about 20k active entries per frame with the full light loop.
  Fixes: Radiance at any hit point, anywhere, at fixed memory. Paths can be cut short for multi-bounce and for reflections' secondary hits. It scales to large worlds without a probe grid. Cost: Indiana Jones on an RTX 5080 at 1080p internal: SharcUpdate 0.89 ms with up to 4 bounces. SHaRC memory: 40 B per entry (64 B with SH), so 160 MiB at the suggested 2^22 entries. id Tech 8 cache: 14 MB, 0.109 ms on an RTX 4080 at 4K. Fails: Hash collisions and overflow. Occupancy climbs with fast camera motion, so stale entries must be evicted more aggressively. Voxel-scale blockiness if queried too close, hence the segment-length rule. Directional error on glossy hits without SH mode. Lag from temporal accumulation, which responsive mode trades away. GI-1.0's cells store only direct light, which limits multi-bounce off screen.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: partly. The cache has its own temporal accumulation. The shipped PT titles that use it denoise the per-pixel image with DLSS RR. id's version sits behind its own denoiser. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Excellent. SHaRC is shader-only headers with a GLSL helper. It needs native fp16 in shaders (the 16-bit storage/arithmetic features) and optionally 64-bit atomics (else a lock buffer). Update and query are ordinary ray-query loops in compute.
  Needs: fp16 support, 3 UAV buffers, an update budget (fraction of pixels), a scene-scale parameter (see risk under rule 2).
  Sources: <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/SHARC/blob/main/docs/Integration.md>; <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/RTXGI>; <https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/path-tracing-optimizations-in-indiana-jones-opacity-micromaps-and-compaction-of-dynamic-blass/>; <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/SOUSA_SIGGRAPH_2025_Final.pdf>
- **Surface cache / on-surface caches (Lumen cards; Enlighten-style lightmap outputs; OSC-GI)** (shipped; shipped in Unreal Engine 5 (Lumen surface cache), Enlighten titles (realtime lightmaps); confidence medium). Lighting is cached in a surface parameterisation rather than in space. Lumen captures mesh cards into a 4096^2 atlas. Its lighting update runs on a fixed per-frame texel budget, by priority, with indirect light gathered from sparse hemisphere probes on the cards and accumulated over at most 4 frames. OSC-GI stores a primary cache (incoming radiance) and a secondary cache (outgoing radiance) in unique-UV islands.
  Fixes: No leaks from spatial interpolation, because the cache lives on the surface. Multi-bounce by self-gather. Reusable by any ray hit. Cost: Lumen surface cache on PS5 at 1080p: 0.47-1.17 ms (Wright 2022). OSC-GI on an RTX 3090 Ti at 1080p: 3.02 ms final for glossy Sponza; claims +5-10% speed and quality over GI-1.0 (Tatzgern 2024). Fails: Coverage holes: Lumen cards miss parts of the mesh, which then go black in reflections. Needs a parameterisation. Skinned and deforming meshes are handled poorly (Lumen gives skeletal meshes 6 box cards). Ghosting when lighting updates are budget-limited.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no. The cache is persistent and has its own accumulation; the final screen pass still needs a filter. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Good. Hits look up the atlas by instance/primitive/UV. RageV already has lightmap UVs for Static meshes.
  Needs: Unique surface parameterisation (lightmap UVs), atlas allocation and paging, a fallback for dynamic or unparameterised meshes.
  Sources: <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2022/SIGGRAPH2022-Advances-Lumen-Wright%20et%20al.pdf>; <https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3675382>; <https://www.highperformancegraphics.org/slides24/hpg24_oscgi.pdf>
- **Neural Radiance Cache (NVIDIA NRC; AMD FSR Radiance Caching)** (shipped; shipped in Half-Life 2 RTX demo, Portal RTX, Quake III Arena RTX demo; confidence medium). A small network (hash-grid encoding plus an MLP) is trained online every frame from low-resolution training paths traced to full length, with self-training for multi-bounce. At full resolution, paths terminate into the network once the ray-cone spread passes a threshold, and inference is resolved in a separate pass.
  Fixes: Noise and length of multi-bounce paths, including glossy. No precompute; adapts to change. Cost: About 2.6 ms for training plus queries at 1920x1080 on an RTX 3090 (Müller 2021). Shipped HL2 RTX: up to 15% faster overall (NVIDIA claim). Fails: Training lag and bias. Behaves like a black box. NVIDIA NRC is a binary library (NRC_Vulkan.dll plus CUDA DLLs) that needs Tensor Cores and is marked experimental. AMD's version is DX12-only on RX 9000.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: partly. The cache itself does not; every shipped title pairs it with DLSS RR. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Possible (it supports Vulkan) but awkward. It is built around HLSL layout rules (-fvk-use-dx-layout), a CUDA runtime, and separate update and query path-tracer passes. It is vendor-locked.
  Needs: NVIDIA RTX with Tensor Cores; a driver of at least 555; path-tracer passes at two resolutions.
  Sources: <https://arxiv.org/abs/2106.12372>; <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/RTXGI/blob/main/Docs/NrcGuide.md>; <https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/geforce/news/rtx-remix-half-life-2-rtx-demo-launching-march-18/>; <https://gpuopen.com/amd-fsr-radiancecaching/>
- **ReSTIR GI (Ouyang et al. 2021)** (shipped; shipped in Cyberpunk 2077 RT Overdrive; confidence high). Each pixel traces one indirect sample path and stores its first secondary vertex (position, normal, radiance) in a reservoir. The reservoirs are resampled from last frame's reprojected reservoir and from spatial neighbours, with Jacobian-corrected reconnection at the secondary vertex. Later bounces can terminate into a radiance cache.
  Fixes: Much better quality per ray for the one-bounce-plus indirect term: MSE 9.3x to 166x lower than 1 sample per pixel of path tracing at equal sample count. Cost: RTX 3090 at 1080p: 8.9 ms for 1 sample per pixel ReSTIR GI against 8.0 ms for plain path tracing, MSE 15.1x lower. A UE4 integration adds 8-18 ms per frame at 1080p on an RTX 3090. RTX 3080 at 1920x1050: initial samples 3.2 ms for 1 bounce (4.2 ms for 2), plus 4.6 ms for reuse. The authors themselves say typical games allow under 2 ms. Fails: Correlation artifacts (blotches) and flicker under fast motion. Temporal lag. Screen-space only: off-screen samples are lost. Needs a strong denoiser.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: partly. Needs a dedicated denoiser (NRD or DLSS RR in Cyberpunk); does not need TAA as such. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Good. RTXDI 2.0+ ships a ReSTIR GI library (Vulkan-capable via Donut); it is compute plus ray queries.
  Needs: Reservoir buffers (tens of bytes per pixel, double-buffered), motion vectors, a visibility-ray budget for reuse.
  Sources: <https://d1qx31qr3h6wln.cloudfront.net/publications/ReSTIR%20GI.pdf>; <https://research.nvidia.com/publication/2021-06_restir-gi-path-resampling-real-time-path-tracing>; <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/RTXDI>
- **ReSTIR PT (Lin et al. 2022) and ReSTIR PT Enhanced (2026)** (public_sdk; shipped in not shipped; confidence high). Generalised resampled importance sampling over whole paths, with hybrid shift mappings (reconnect or replay) so reuse works for glossy multi-bounce. The Enhanced version pairs spatial neighbours (halving shift cost), uses reconnection thresholds based on ray footprint, and uses duplication maps to limit correlation.
  Fixes: Full multi-bounce diffuse and glossy indirect at 1 sample per pixel with low error. Cost: Original: 40-80 ms per frame on an RTX 3090 (per the 2026 paper). Enhanced: 12.6-21.4 ms at 1920x1080 on an RTX 5880 Ada, 2-3x faster than the original. Fails: Still far over a 60 fps native budget; correlation; memory (265 MB of reservoirs at 1080p after the Enhanced paper's savings).
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: partly. Research images are shown raw; products would pair it with a denoiser or DLSS RR. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Possible; RTXDI 3.0 includes ReSTIR PT.
  Needs: A full path tracer, large reservoir memory.
  Sources: <https://github.com/DQLin/ReSTIR_PT>; <https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3528223.3530158>; <https://research.nvidia.com/labs/rtr/publication/lin2026restirptenhanced/>; <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/RTXDI>
- **Surfel GI (EA SEED GIBS; Kajiya-style)** (shipped; shipped in EA SPORTS College Football 25; confidence medium). Surfels are spawned on screen-visible surfaces to keep a constant screen-space density, and are grown or removed with distance. They are indexed in a camera-perspective grid and persist off-screen. Rays go to surfels by a global budget weighted by contribution and variance, with ray guiding from a small per-surfel directional function, spatial filtering, and radial depth for occlusion.
  Fixes: A persistent, resolution-independent cache on surfaces, with no lightmap UVs needed; dynamic and skinned geometry. Cost: PS5 with 4K output (1080p screen passes): about 7 ms in a large vista, about 11 ms while converging with a 1M-ray budget (SEED 2021). The shipped College Football 25 hit 60 fps; no ms given. Fails: Surfel spawn and gap-fill cost spikes. Convergence time when a view is new. Needs fallback probes for geometry that surfels cannot cover.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no. Surfels are their own persistent accumulator. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Good (compute plus ray queries).
  Needs: Surfel allocator, spatial grid, per-surfel directional data.
  Sources: <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2021/SIGGRAPH%20Advances%202021%20-%20Surfel%20GI.pdf>; <https://www.ea.com/technology/news/gibs-lighting-ea-sports-college-football-25>
- **Radiance Cascades (Sannikov; screen-space in PoE2; Split RC in 3D)** (shipped; shipped in Path of Exile 2 (screen-space/2.5D only); confidence medium). Radiance is stored in cascades of probes. Each level has fewer probes but more directions and a longer ray interval, following the penumbra observation: near light needs spatial detail, far light needs angular detail. Adjacent levels are merged into a full radiance field. Split RC (2026) stores world-space probes in a sparse hashmap and deposits screen-traced rays into cascades by hit distance.
  Fixes: Noise-free, detail-at-all-scales diffuse GI including medium-scale AO in a single frame. Cost: PoE2: not published. Split RC: 13.2 ms single-frame for San Miguel on an RTX 3080 Laptop, of which 6.1 ms is 1-sample-per-pixel tracing (CUDA/OptiX research code, fp32, unoptimised). Fails: Interpolation leaks during merging. Misses detail below the base probe spacing. Overblurs hard shadows. 3D memory cost is why shipped versions are 2D or screen-space.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no. Designed to work in a single frame; temporal accumulation is optional. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Plausible (compute plus ray queries), but no Vulkan or game implementation exists in 3D.
  Needs: Sparse probe hashmap, cascade merge passes.
  Sources: <https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.20384>; <https://radiance.wiki/>; <https://www.pathofexile.com/forum/view-thread/3448733>
- **Baked GI storage: directional lightmaps plus sparse brick probe volumes (APV / UE Volumetric Lightmap / id Tech 7)** (shipped; shipped in Unity 6 (APV), Unreal Engine (Volumetric Lightmaps), DOOM Eternal (id Tech 7); confidence high). Static surfaces store L1 (2-band) SH irradiance in lightmaps. id Tech 7 used BC6H L0 plus 3x BC1 L1, Oodle-compressed. Everything else samples a sparse probe volume: 4x4x4-probe bricks refined near geometry and interpolated per pixel. Leak control:; Virtual Offset / relocation moves probes out of walls.; Dilation fills invalid probes from valid neighbours.; Rendering-layer masks (up to 4) and normal/view bias at sampling. Extras: Lighting Scenarios can be blended; sky occlusion lets the sky change at runtime.
  Fixes: Near-free runtime indirect for static lights, with memory adapted to geometry and no hand-placed volume caps. Cost: Runtime not published in docs (8-probe trilinear fetch per pixel). id Tech 7 Doom Eternal: about 500 MB compressed per level, 4-10 h bakes; estimated 44-110 GB for Doom: The Dark Ages had they kept it. Fails: Stale when geometry or lights change. Leaks through thin walls. Seams and block-compression banding (id noted BC artefacts; RageV's audit lists a BC6H fixed box-fit banding). Streaming complexity.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no. Deterministic data, no noise. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Bake-time tracing fits ray queries; runtime is plain texture sampling.
  Needs: Lightmap UVs, brick allocator and indirection texture, a bake pipeline.
  Sources: <https://docs.unity3d.com/Packages/com.unity.render-pipelines.high-definition@17.1/manual/probevolumes-concept.html>; <https://docs.unity3d.com/Packages/com.unity.render-pipelines.high-definition@17.0/manual/probevolumes-fixissues.html>; <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/volumetric-lightmaps-in-unreal-engine>; <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/SOUSA_SIGGRAPH_2025_Final.pdf>
- **Mixing baked and realtime lights (Unity mixed modes, UE Stationary, Unity baked + Enlighten realtime)** (shipped; shipped in Unity (all pipelines), Unreal Engine (Stationary lights); confidence high). - Unity Baked Indirect: mixed lights give live direct light and baked bounce.; Unity Shadowmask: also bakes static shadows for up to 4 lights per texel.; Unity Subtractive: bakes almost everything.; UE Stationary: live direct light and shadows, baked indirect, at most 4 overlapping; color and intensity may change at runtime without a rebuild.; Unity (Built-in) can run baked GI and Enlighten realtime GI together: baked lights feed lightmaps, and Realtime lights feed realtime indirect through Enlighten. Unity calls this rarely advisable and for experts only.
  Fixes: Cheap bounce for the static majority, with live direct light where it matters. The closest shipped precedent for RageV's two layers that add up (D4) is Unity's combination. Cost: Not published. Fails: Double counting or missing bounce when a light's category is wrong. Changing a baked or mixed light's properties leaves its bounce stale (UE: anything beyond color and intensity invalidates the bake). Shadowmask channel limit. Unity notes the two GI systems differ in resolution and technique, so their results disagree.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no. n/a Fit with Vulkan ray queries: n/a (policy, not a trace technique). The RT equivalent is RageV's realtime layer.
  Needs: Per-light mobility flag; a rule that each light's bounce comes from exactly one layer.
