  Sources: <https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/Manual/lighting-mode.html>; <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/stationary-light-mobility-in-unreal-engine>; <https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/Manual/choose-a-lighting-setup.html>
- **Precomputed-visibility realtime radiosity (Enlighten)** (shipped; shipped in Unity (until Unity 6), Frostbite-era titles (historically); confidence medium). An offline pass splits static geometry into clusters and computes the form factors between clusters (geometry only, no materials). At runtime, a CPU solver iterates radiosity with any light setup, one bounce per iteration, asynchronously over several frames. It outputs lightmaps, probes and cubemaps.
  Fixes: Lights, including 'baked' ones, can change or switch without a re-bake. Materials and colours can change too. Cost: Not published in the docs read. The solve runs on the CPU with multi-frame latency. Fails: Static geometry only. Latency makes it unsuitable for fast-changing lights. Coarse clusters. Deprecated after Unity 6.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no. n/a Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Not a ray-query technique. The idea (bake visibility, relight live) maps onto RT as 'trace once, relight many' (the probe G-buffer caching row).
  Needs: Precompute of static geometry; CPU budget.
  Sources: <https://enlighten.atlassian.net/wiki/spaces/SDK402/pages/2485158180/Understanding+Global+Illumination>; <https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.2/Documentation/Manual/realtime-gi-using-enlighten.html>
- **Stale-bake detection and partial rebake** (shipped; shipped in Unreal Engine editor, Unity editor; confidence medium). UE tracks each primitive-light interaction's build state. Moving or adding static geometry or a static light marks those interactions unbuilt, and the viewport shows a count; DumpUnbuiltLightInteractions lists them. GPU Lightmass 'Bake What You See' re-bakes the visible tiles interactively, but saves only those. Unity 6 dropped continuous auto-generate in favour of 'Auto Initialize Lighting' (bake only when data is missing). Its GPU baker takes a snapshot of the scene state at the moment Generate is pressed.
  Fixes: A bake that is silently wrong after edits. Cost: Editor-only. Fails: False positives: UE forums report the warning persisting after rebuilds. It cannot see edits outside what it tracks (e.g. material albedo, unless hashed).
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no. n/a Fit with Vulkan ray queries: n/a
  Needs: A per-object/per-light hash or GUID of the bake inputs (transform, mesh, material, light parameters).
  Sources: <https://forums.unrealengine.com/t/lighting-needs-to-be-rebuilt-197-unbuilt-objects/440544>; <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/gpu-lightmass-global-illumination-in-unreal-engine>; <https://unity.com/blog/engine-platform/new-ways-of-applying-global-illumination-in-unity-6>
- **Per-signal diffuse denoiser with history confidence (NRD ReBLUR/ReLAX; Snowdrop MSD; id bilateral + temporal)** (public_sdk; shipped in Cyberpunk 2077 (NRD path), Avatar (MSD; NRD available), DOOM: The Dark Ages (own filter); confidence high). Temporal accumulation per signal with limited history (NRD recommends about 32 frames, up to 60 with confidence input), plus anti-lag. An optional per-pixel history-confidence input (0 = reset) comes from measured change. Spatial blur is guided by hit distance, normal and depth. An SH mode keeps directional detail when the signal is denoised below render resolution.
  Fixes: Noise from about 1 ray per pixel or less, with no reliance on TAA. Cost: NRD on an RTX 4080 at 1440p native: REBLUR_DIFFUSE_SPECULAR 2.55 ms (3.40 in SH mode), RELAX_DIFFUSE_SPECULAR 3.25 ms (4.80). Diffuse denoise elsewhere: Snowdrop 1.573 ms on Xbox Series X at 1440p quarter resolution; AC 0.86 ms on an RTX 4070 at 1440p; id 0.52 ms on an RTX 4080 at 4K. Fails: Lag without a confidence input. Disocclusion noise. Fixed clamps can remove real energy.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no. This is the signal's own accumulator; TAA is anti-aliasing only. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: n/a (compute); NRD supports Vulkan.
  Needs: Motion vectors, normal and roughness guides, hit distance, and a confidence signal from change detection.
  Sources: <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD>; <https://media.gdcvault.com/gdc2024/Slides/GDC+slide+presentations/Kuenlib_Quentin_Raytracing_In_Snowdrop.pdf>; <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/SOUSA_SIGGRAPH_2025_Final.pdf>
- **Multi-bounce by cache feedback (shared hit-shading contract)** (shipped; shipped in DOOM: The Dark Ages, Avatar, AC Shadows, Metro Exodus EE, Lumen (surface-cache feedback); confidence high). Every ray-hit shader (probe update, per-pixel gather, reflections) uses one lighting function: direct light from a world-space light structure plus indirect from the previous frame's world cache.; Snowdrop: simplified Lambert + GGX, sun via CSM plus a shadow ray, analytic lights via a light BVH, indirect from other probes.; id: the same light loop as raster, through a cascaded world light grid, with previous-frame irradiance volumes. Each frame adds one more bounce.
  Fixes: Four different answers for the same surface depending on who looks at it. Infinite bounces at the cost of one. Cost: id world light grid: about 0.1 ms on PC (0.2 ms on consoles), 30 MB, 16^3 cells x 8 cascades (first cascade 32 m, 2 m cells), 64 ids per cell. Fails: Bounce lags one frame per bounce. Energy runaway if the cache is not energy-conserving. Out-of-frustum lights need a world light structure.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no. n/a Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Excellent; deferred hit shading keyed by packed hit data (id uses 128 bits per hit).
  Needs: A world-space light index (grid or BVH) usable off-frustum; a cache readable at any position.
  Sources: <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/SOUSA_SIGGRAPH_2025_Final.pdf>; <https://media.gdcvault.com/gdc2024/Slides/GDC+slide+presentations/Kuenlib_Quentin_Raytracing_In_Snowdrop.pdf>

### C.3 The frontier (published, not yet shipped)

- **Split Radiance Cascades** (Freeman & Sannikov 2026, 2026; evidence weak). Radiance Cascades in 3D world space with a sparse probe hashmap. Rays are split into cascade intervals by hit distance. Gives better detail than DDGI (medium-scale AO, penumbrae) in a single frame.
  Evidence: Research paper (arXiv, July 2026). Visual comparisons against DDGI and a path-traced reference. 13.2 ms single-frame on an RTX 3080 Laptop (6.1 ms of it tracing), in CUDA/OptiX with fp32 storage. Diffuse only. Could beat: DDGI-style probe cascades as the world cache, on contact detail. Cost: 13.2 ms on an RTX 3080 Laptop (research, unoptimised) Risk: No game implementation. Leaks during merging. Cost is far above budget. Diffuse only.
  Sources: <https://arxiv.org/abs/2607.20384>
- **ReSTIR PT Enhanced** (Lin et al. 2026, 2026; evidence moderate). 2-3x faster than ReSTIR PT, with better robustness (footprint-based reconnection, paired spatial reuse, duplication maps).
  Evidence: I3D 2026 paper. 12.6-21.4 ms at 1080p on an RTX 5880 Ada, against 30-50 ms for the original. Measured with FLIP. Could beat: ReSTIR GI plus SHaRC path-traced modes, on quality. Cost: 12.6-21.4 ms at 1920x1080 on an RTX 5880 Ada Risk: Far over a 16.6 ms frame at native 2560x1600 on a laptop GPU. Would need a denoiser.
  Sources: <https://research.nvidia.com/labs/rtr/publication/lin2026restirptenhanced/>
- **On-Surface Caches GI (OSC-GI)** (Tatzgern et al. 2024, 2024; evidence moderate). A two-level radiance cache held only in on-surface texture space (primary: incoming radiance; secondary: outgoing radiance). Leak-free, infinite bounces, glossy support. Claims 5-10% better speed and quality than GI-1.0.
  Evidence: HPG 2024 / PACMCGIT paper. RTX 3090 Ti at 1080p in Falcor. Compared with GI-1.0 and path-traced references. Could beat: Hash-grid or probe world caches, on leaks in thin-walled interiors like the garage. Cost: About 3 ms final at 1080p on an RTX 3090 Ti (Sponza, glossy) Risk: Needs unique UV islands; RageV's lightmap UVs may serve. Dynamic and skinned meshes need a fallback. Single research group.
  Sources: <https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3675382>; <https://www.highperformancegraphics.org/slides24/hpg24_oscgi.pdf>
- **Neural Irradiance Volume** (Coomans et al. 2026, 2026; evidence weak). A precomputed neural 5D irradiance field replacing probe grids. About 1 ms at full HD, 1-5 MB for medium scenes, at least 10x quality at equal memory compared with probe grids.
  Evidence: CGF 2026 paper (abstract-level numbers read; full tables not verified). Could beat: Sparse-brick probe volumes as the storage format for the baked layer. Cost: About 1 ms at 1080p on consumer GPUs (abstract) Risk: Static lighting only, so the same staleness as any bake. Inference cost at 2560x1600. Training-time pipeline.
  Sources: <https://arxiv.org/abs/2602.12949>; <https://onlinelibrary.wiley.com/doi/10.1111/cgf.70400>
- **Neural Light Grid (Activision)** (Activision Research 2024, 2024; evidence weak). A production-oriented learned irradiance volume: per-probe weighting functions, generated by a pre-trained network, with about 100x faster precompute.
  Evidence: SIGGRAPH 2024 talk and technical memo. Shipping status not stated on the page read. Could beat: Classic baked probe volumes, on leaks and memory. Cost: not published (in the page read) Risk: Static lighting only; unclear shipping status.
  Sources: <https://research.activision.com/publications/2024/08/Neural_Light_Grid>
- **NRC for path-tracing tails** (Müller et al. 2021; RTXGI 2.x, 2021; evidence moderate). An online-trained neural cache ends paths early with less bias than hash grids and supports glossy transport.
  Evidence: SIGGRAPH 2021 paper (2.6 ms at 1080p on an RTX 3090). Shipped in RTX Remix titles, with NVIDIA claiming up to +15% performance (vendor claim). No independent quality-per-ms comparison against SHaRC was found. Could beat: SHaRC as the tail cache. Cost: About 2.6 ms at 1080p on an RTX 3090 (paper) Risk: Binary-only, CUDA, Tensor Cores, NVIDIA-only. Experimental. Built for path tracers; shipped titles lean on DLSS RR.
  Sources: <https://arxiv.org/abs/2106.12372>; <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/RTXGI/blob/main/Docs/NrcGuide.md>
- **GI-1.1 two-level cache for glossy** (Eto et al. 2023, 2023; evidence moderate). Glossy reflections from the same two-level cache: screen probes for rough, hash-grid rays for smoother.
  Evidence: SIGGRAPH Asia 2023 paper and Capsaicin source. OSC-GI notes that its hash cells hold only direct light, so off-screen multi-bounce darkens. Could beat: Separate rough-reflection probes (relevant to RT2-27, which sits outside this area). Cost: not published (in the page read) Risk: The multi-bounce darkening noted above.
  Sources: <https://gpuopen.com/learn/gi-1-1-glossy-reflection-rendering/>; <https://github.com/GPUOpen-LibrariesAndSDKs/Capsaicin>
- **AMD FSR Radiance Caching** (AMD GPUOpen 2025, 2025; evidence weak). An online-learned radiance cache for path tracing.
  Evidence: SDK documentation only; a Darktide demo. No ms figures. Could beat: SHaRC, NRC Cost: not published Risk: DX12 only and RX 9000+ only, so not usable in a Vulkan engine on an NVIDIA GPU.
  Sources: <https://gpuopen.com/amd-fsr-radiancecaching/>

### C.4 The survey's recommended baseline and candidates

The survey's view before the comparison looked at RageV's code. The roadmap's items took these as their starting baselines where the comparison and verification agreed.

- **Baseline: The realtime layer's gather should follow the shipped RT-GI final-gather pattern:; 1 cosine ray per traced pixel at reduced resolution, with the owner choosing half or quarter.; Screen-space trace first.; A HW ray-query continuation whose length is tied to the world cache's cell size at that position.; On a miss or at the hit, read the world cache instead of tracing further or reading the sky.; Output L1 SH per traced pixel and bilaterally upsample, evaluating the SH with full-resolution normals.** Why: This is what id Tech 8, Snowdrop, Anvil and Metro EE ship at 60 fps on consoles. The cache fallback alone halved Snowdrop's tracing time. id's final gather does no hit shading at all. RageV's current realtime pass traces 1 ray per pixel at half resolution but treats a miss as sky and shades hits in several different ways (RT2-25). Shipped by: DOOM: The Dark Ages (id Tech 8), Avatar (Snowdrop), Assassin's Creed Shadows (Anvil), Metro Exodus EE. Measured by: - --pass-timings at native 2560x1600 on the owner's GPU, A,B,B,A: gather, lighting, denoise and upsample separately, at both half and quarter resolution.; Diff images against the reference renderer on the garage with its Realtime lights on, the camp, and the bridge cameras.; Leak scenes in check_gi.py at 0.000.; The same diffs with TAA off, to prove no reliance on TAA. Published anchors: AC 2.65 ms (RTX 4070, 1440p, quarter resolution); id about 1.5 ms for gather + denoise + upscale (RTX 4080, 4K).
- **Baseline: One world-space radiance cache read by every ray hit and miss: the realtime layer's rays, reflection hits, probe captures and bake hits. It should hold radiance from Realtime lights only, so it matches the D4 layering. Cell size should grow with camera distance from the pixel footprint (SHaRC's logarithmic levels) rather than use fixed metres. Update it within a fixed per-frame budget chosen by priority (how recently used minus how recently updated). Multi-bounce comes from hit shading reading the previous frame's cache.** Why: Every shipped RT-GI system has exactly one such cache: id's hash cache plus irradiance volumes, Snowdrop/AC's probe light cache, Lumen's world radiance cache plus surface cache, and SHaRC in the path-traced titles. RageV's only world store (the 24x12x24 1 m box) is off and read by no hit, and fixed-metre cells break owner rule 2 on the kilometre-scale bridge. Shipped by: DOOM: The Dark Ages, Indiana Jones PT, Cyberpunk 2077 RT Overdrive, Avatar, AC Shadows, UE5 Lumen. Measured by: - VRAM: SHaRC is 40 B per entry, so 2^20 entries = 40 MiB; id used 14 MB.; Update cost under --pass-timings.; emitter_lag.py on RT-22's moving cube, as frames to converge.; Hash occupancy during camera moves.; Diffs of reflection hits and second bounce against the reference.; Bridge cameras at kilometre distance, to prove no scale tuning.
- **Baseline: One hit-shading function used by the realtime layer, reflections, bake hits and probe captures. Direct light comes from a world-space light structure that works outside the frustum (id's cascaded light grid, Snowdrop's light BVH), with occluded shadow rays. Indirect comes from the cache (Realtime lights) plus the bake (baked lights).** Why: Snowdrop and id each use one lighting loop for every hit. RageV today lights a surface four different ways (RT2-25). Shipped by: DOOM: The Dark Ages, Avatar, AC Shadows. Measured by: Render one surface point four ways (lit pixel, traced hit, bake hit, probe capture) and diff each against the reference renderer; all four should agree within grain. Record the cost of the light lookup at hits.
- **Baseline: A per-signal diffuse denoiser for the realtime layer. It keeps its own temporal history with a history-confidence input from measured change, a relative outlier stage instead of fixed clamps, and SH-mode denoising when traced below full resolution.** Why: NRD, MSD and id's filter all own the GI signal's history; TAA only anti-aliases. RageV's fixed clamps (4 and 32) were tuned on the showroom (owner rules 2 and 3). Shipped by: Avatar (MSD), AC Shadows, DOOM: The Dark Ages, Cyberpunk 2077 (NRD path). Measured by: - Denoise ms at native 2560x1600 (anchor: NRD ReBLUR diffuse + specular 2.55 ms on an RTX 4080 at 1440p).; Frames-to-converge after a light switch.; Flicker metric on still cameras with TAA off.; Energy check: the mean against the reference with no brightening.
- **Baseline: The bake format:; Directional (L1 SH) lightmaps for Static meshes.; Sparse 4x4x4-probe bricks, refined where geometry is dense and sampled per pixel.; Probes relocated out of walls, invalid probes dilated from neighbours.; Nested volumes blended.; Baked lights stay in the bake, with their bounce counted only there.** Why: Unity APV and UE Volumetric Lightmaps both ship exactly this, and RT2-26 already plans it. It removes the 8-volume cap and the leak case that cell size alone cannot fix. Shipped by: Unity 6 (APV), Unreal Engine (Volumetric Lightmaps), DOOM Eternal (id Tech 7 SH lightmaps). Measured by: - Leak scenes at 0.000, including a wall thinner than the cell.; Contact-scale bleeding share below today's 1.9% of pixels.; Bake time and disk size.; Runtime cost of the per-pixel brick lookup.
- **Baseline: Stale-bake detection with per-object and per-light granularity. Hash each Static object's transform, mesh and material, and each baked light's parameters, into the bake stamp. Warn in the editor and list the offenders. Optionally re-bake visible regions first.** Why: UE's 'lighting needs to be rebuilt' check with a list of unbuilt interactions is the shipped baseline. GPU Lightmass 'Bake What You See' shows interactive partial rebakes are practical. Shipped by: Unreal Engine editor. Measured by: Moving or recolouring a Static object raises the warning and names it; moving a dynamic object does not. Time from edit to refreshed bake for one region.
- **Candidate: A final gather with no hit shading (id Tech 8). Realtime-layer rays do not shade their hits. They read previous-frame screen lighting if the hit is on screen and unoccluded, else the world cache, else the probe fallback. Full shading runs only on the cache's update budget (about 20k entries per frame).** Why better: It removes the per-hit light loop from about 1 million rays per frame at half of 2560x1600. id's gather costs 0.5 ms at 4K on an RTX 4080. Evidence: Sousa, SIGGRAPH 2025: shipped on all platforms at 60 Hz or more, with timings per platform. Risk: Bounce lags by a frame. Screen-space reads can leak where occlusion checks fail. Hits read a coarser cache than per-hit shading would give. Measured by: A,B,B,A at native resolution against RageV's per-hit shading. Diff against the reference on garage and camp. Lag of a moving light in emitter_lag.py (screen and cache lighting is one frame old).
- **Candidate: Trace once, relight many. Cache ray-hit G-buffers (hit position, normal, material) for world-cache entries or probes. When only lights change, relight from the cached hits without re-tracing. Re-trace a small share each frame plus anything a moved object invalidates.** Why better: Scenes are mostly static with a few Realtime lights, so most updates only need relighting. AC traces about 16% of updated probes and Snowdrop about 15%; the rest are relit only. Evidence: Kuenlin GDC 2024 and Leblanc/Conte SIGGRAPH 2025 (shipped). Risk: Stale hit G-buffers under dynamic geometry need an invalidation rule that does not depend on speed (owner rule 2). Measured by: Cache update ms with and without hit caching while a Realtime light moves. Correctness when a dynamic object moves through probe rays: diff against the reference.
- **Candidate: An on-surface cache for the realtime layer on Static geometry: store the realtime layer's outgoing radiance in a lightmap-UV atlas (OSC-GI, Lumen surface cache). Keep the hash cache only for dynamic and unparameterised hits.** Why better: A cache on the surface cannot leak through thin walls, which matters for the garage and its thin ceiling fittings. RageV already owns lightmap UVs and an atlas pipeline. OSC-GI reports +5-10% speed and quality over GI-1.0. Evidence: HPG 2024 paper (research); Lumen surface cache (shipped); Enlighten realtime lightmaps (shipped). Risk: Two cache types to maintain. Card or UV coverage holes go black, as Lumen documents. Dynamic meshes need the hash path anyway. Measured by: - Leak scenes and contact-scale bleeding against the hash-grid cache.; VRAM of the atlas.; Update cost under --pass-timings.; Diff against the reference on the garage.
- **Candidate: ReSTIR GI reuse on the realtime layer's samples: temporal plus spatial reservoirs on the half-resolution rays.** Why better: MSE 9-166x lower than plain sampling at equal sample count; could allow quarter resolution at half-resolution quality. Evidence: Ouyang et al. HPG 2021; shipped in Cyberpunk RT Overdrive (with a denoiser or RR). Risk: Cost is well above the roughly 1 ms target proposed in RT2-25. Correlation blotches. Reuse is screen-space only. Measured by: MSE or diff against the reference, at equal ms, against plain rays plus the denoiser. Reuse pass ms at native resolution (anchor: 4.6 ms at 1920x1050 on an RTX 3080 for reuse alone). Flicker with TAA off.
- **Candidate: A per-light layer for switchable baked lights. Store each switchable baked or Hybrid light's bounce (or its light group's) in its own coefficient layer, so switching or dimming scales or removes that layer instead of discarding the bake. The live realtime layer can take over the light meanwhile.** Why better: This targets the accepted defect 'switching a baked light discards its bake'. Unity already blends whole Lighting Scenarios at runtime, and Enlighten shows relighting on precomputed transport is viable. Evidence: Weak for this exact form: Unity scenario blending covers whole scenes, not per-light layers, and I found no shipped per-light bake layers. Risk: Memory grows with the number of switchable lights. Needs an owner decision, since it changes D4's accepted trade-off. Measured by: - Memory per layer.; Diff against the reference after switching each lamp (near zero, or favouring baked).; No double counting where the realtime layer takes over.
- **Candidate: Integrate the SHaRC library directly as the realtime layer's world cache, rather than writing a custom cache.** Why better: Shader-only, with GLSL helpers, Vulkan-capable, and shipped in three path-traced AAA titles. It has logarithmic distance-based cell size (matching owner rule 2), stale eviction, a responsive-lighting mode, and optional SH directionality. Evidence: NVIDIA SHaRC integration guide; Indiana Jones SharcUpdate 0.89 ms on an RTX 5080 at 1080p. Risk: Designed around a path tracer's update pass (about 4% of pixels tracing multi-bounce paths). Needs fp16 shader support. Irradiance-style use (the diffuse final gather) must be validated. Collision behaviour in large scenes such as the bridge. Measured by: Integration cost. VRAM at a chosen entry count. Occupancy (10-20% typical for a static camera, per NVIDIA). Update and resolve ms. Diff against a custom cache on the same scenes.

### C.5 RageV against them, topic by topic

- **Per-pixel gather: rays and trace resolution.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: The project runs RTGI High: 4 cosine rays per traced pixel, with the target at width/2 x height/2 (a quarter of the pixels). Each sample also casts one aimed emitter shadow ray, and its hit is shaded in full (one kept light plus its shadow ray). Measured 2.8-3.0 ms of trace at 1600x900 in the garage when realtime is forced on. Code: `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:363-383`, `SampleProject/SampleProject.rvproject:20-21`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/rtgi_trace.rvshader:374-668`, `docs/RT-SERIES-2.md:1344`
  Top engines: 1 cosine ray per traced pixel at a quarter of the pixels (Snowdrop consoles, AC Shadows), or 1 ray at reduced resolution (id Tech 8). Snowdrop: 3.877 ms total at 1440p on Xbox Series X. AC: 2.65 ms serial at 1440p on an RTX 4070. id: about 1.47 ms for gather, denoise and upscale at 4K on an RTX 4080. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:465-476,1149-1167`; <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/SOUSA_SIGGRAPH_2025_Final.pdf>; <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/Advances%202025%20-%20Raytracing%20the%20world%20of%20Assassin's%20Creed%20Shadows.pdf>
  Why: At the same traced-pixel count RageV casts 4x the gather rays, and each one pays full hit shading. The shipped engines spend 1 ray and let a cache and the denoiser carry the rest. RageV's 'half resolution' is the industry's 'quarter resolution' (the divisor is per axis), which phase 1 missed.
- **Screen-space tracing or previous-frame screen lighting at hits.** Comparison: missing. Verified: missing. Confidence: high.
  RageV: None. Every gather ray is a hardware ray query from the G-buffer point. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/rtgi_trace.rvshader:595-597`
  Top engines: Snowdrop and AC march in screen space first and continue with a hardware ray from where the march stopped. Snowdrop's hit shader first reprojects the hit into the previous frame and reuses that lighting if valid. id reads previous-frame screen lighting before its hash cache. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:536-613,660-676`; <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/SOUSA_SIGGRAPH_2025_Final.pdf>
  Why: It is a shipped cost lever: Snowdrop's tracing went from 4.621 to 1.462 ms with screen tracing plus the cache fallback. Under D4 it cannot be copied as it stands, though. The previous lit frame holds the baked lights' light, so a realtime-layer hit that read it would count baked light twice. It needs a lighting lane holding only the realtime-owned light. Recorded as a deliberate deviation from the baseline, not adopted now.
- **Ray length and what a miss returns.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: A fixed reach of 250 m, measured on the camp and hard-coded. A miss adds nothing; sky reaches the pixel through the probe term instead. On the 2-bounce path, a second-ray miss returns the raw sky whether or not the ray could have left the scene. Code: `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:866-882`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/rtgi_trace.rvshader:361-363,597-602,620-630`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:2651-2657`
  Top engines: Rays are cut to twice the probe spacing of the cascade the ray starts in (Snowdrop, AC). On a miss they sample the probe cache at the ray's end point and read the sky only outside every cascade. SHaRC ends a path in the cache when the segment is longer than the voxel. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:498-531,615-640`; <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/SHARC/blob/main/docs/Integration.md>
  Why: Short rays that end in a cache cut Snowdrop's world-space tracing by 59% (2.468 to 1.020 ms). RageV's 250 m is a scale constant (owner rule 2), and its miss rule treats 'did not hit within 250 m' as 'saw the sky'.
- **Shading at a gather hit.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: Full shading at every hit: textured material, a Lambert-only walk over every light in the scene (trace-only passes compile out the cluster and world-grid lookups), one kept light with a shadow ray, the hit's emission, and one probe's irradiance as the light arriving there. That probe irradiance has no occlusion. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:2800-2802`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:2851,2936-2941`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:3214-3327`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/rtgi_trace.rvshader:617-647`
  Top engines: id's final gather does no shading at the hit: screen lighting, else the hash cache, else the irradiance volumes. Snowdrop reuses previous-frame screen lighting, else lights the hit with a simplified BSDF and an untextured one-material-per-mesh RT world: sun via cascaded shadow maps plus a shadow ray, point lights via a light BVH, indirect light from probes. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:36-57,648-676`; <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/SOUSA_SIGGRAPH_2025_Final.pdf>
  Why: RageV's hits cost the most in the frame's GI budget and scale with the scene's light count, which GI-06 confirms. Its textured hits are more faithful than Snowdrop's untextured RT world, but the shipped engines buy that fidelity back through caches that are shaded once and read many times.
- **World-space radiance cache read at hits.** Comparison: missing. Verified: missing. Confidence: high.
  RageV: A 24x12x24 box of 1 m cells follows the camera: 64 rays per cell, 65,536 rays a frame, a fixed 5% blend. It exists only when the source is Realtime, so it is off in every shipped scene. Its texture does not scroll with the box, and no frame hit reads its bounce: frame hits fetch the field and keep only the fully baked direct light. Code: `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/RuntimeIrradianceField.h:43-46,74-100`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/RuntimeIrradianceField.cpp:58-60,91-106`, `RageV/src/RageV/Scene/Scene.cpp:3579-3600`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:3274-3303`
  Top engines: Every shipped RT-GI system has exactly one cache that hits and misses read:; id: spatial-hash cache with 25 cm cells plus distance LOD, 14 MB, about 20k entries shaded a frame.; Snowdrop and AC: cascaded probe caches, 1024 probes a frame, about 15% retraced and the rest relit from a stored probe G-buffer.; Lumen: a world radiance cache (32x32 directions per probe, a fixed number updated from a GPU priority queue) plus the surface cache. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:150-160,230-262`; `build/rt_research/sources/lumen_2022.txt:2500-2552`; <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/SOUSA_SIGGRAPH_2025_Final.pdf>
  Why: The pieces exist (the fill solver, sweeps with feedback, visibility bits) but they are not connected to any reader. The fixed metre cells break rule 2 at the bridge's scale.
- **Multi-bounce.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: Realtime: one bounce, plus one probe's unoccluded irradiance as the light arriving at the hit. GiBounces=2 traces a second ray instead. Bake: every sweep's rays read the previous sweep's field (Jacobi swap pair), so the stored field converges to multi-bounce. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/rtgi_trace.rvshader:617-630`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/Renderer3D.cpp:2957-2990`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:3236-3267`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/PostSettings.h:651`
  Top engines: Feedback from the cache: hit shading reads last frame's cache, and each frame adds a bounce (id, Snowdrop, Metro EE, Lumen's surface cache). Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/lumen_2022.txt:1360-1378`; `build/rt_research/sources/snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:285-305`
  Why: The bake's feedback solve equals the industry's. The realtime layer has no cache to close the loop, so any Realtime light gets one bounce plus a probe standing in for the rest.
- **Gather output encoding and upsample.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: Albedo-free RGB irradiance for the one G-buffer normal the trace sampled. It is upsampled by a 4-tap joint bilateral filter weighted by depth only; a normal weight was tried and measured out on AO. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/rtgi_trace.rvshader:312,687`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/signal_upsample.rvshader:17-24,95-114`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:2155-2181,2306-2320`
  Top engines: L1 spherical harmonics per traced pixel: Snowdrop packs it into 64 bits in YCoCg and applies it as a directional light through the full BSDF; id stores 2-band SH. It is denoised at trace resolution and evaluated with the full-resolution normal. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:688-716,786-802`; <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/SOUSA_SIGGRAPH_2025_Final.pdf>
  Why: A quarter-pixel gather that stores irradiance for one normal loses normal-map detail at 2x2-pixel scale. Storing SH gets it back at the full-resolution evaluation.
- **GI denoiser: anti-lag.** Comparison: better (claimed). Verified: C-OB1, equal: NRD documents the same recipe (and under the baked default it never runs). Confidence: medium.
  RageV: The shared signal contract runs at the trace grid. Measured change re-traces the same rays in a record and re-light pass to find where the light really changed: 0.21 ms at 1 ray, and the share of a switched light still held ten frames later fell from 77% to 12.6%. Code: `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:2213-2303`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/rtgi_trace.rvshader:234-259,670-688`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/Renderer3D.cpp:7909-7938`
  Top engines: NRD takes a history-confidence input that the application must supply. Lumen detects traces that hit fast-moving objects and speeds its accumulation up there. Snowdrop invalidates probes on events (objects moving, time of day). Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/lumen_2022.txt:2568-2590`; `build/rt_research/sources/snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:240-257`; <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD>
  Why: RageV measures the change with the same rays (an A-SVGF-style temporal gradient) and does not infer it from motion or events. Among the shipped engines, only Q2RTX-class research renderers do this.
- **GI outliers and clamps.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: Fixed clamps in scene units: 4.0 per hemisphere sample and 32 per aimed-emitter sample, the second chosen from showroom-ceiling measurements. The fresh-sample firefly bound is compiled out for the accumulator's diffuse kind. BoundWidth 6 is justified by an upsample that RT-3.1 removed. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/rtgi_trace.rvshader:369-371,562-588,649-665`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/Renderer3D.cpp:7926-7936`
  Top engines: Relative bounds. Snowdrop clamps emissive hits against the wave's average luminance and an exposure reference. NRD bounds against local statistics and history. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:662-672`; <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD>
  Why: Fixed clamps remove real energy in bright scenes and do nothing in dim ones, which breaks rule 2.
- **Emissive surfaces in the gather (NEE).** Comparison: better (claimed). Verified: C-OB2, partly better: ahead of Lumen 2022, Snowdrop and id only once its own defects are fixed; it clamps too. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: Every sample aims a shadow ray at an emitter rectangle, using an aiming table over the emissive map's cells. RT-24 (uncommitted) lifted the 16-row cap to every emitter. The pick between emitters is still uniform, although the reflections now use RT-24's weighted reservoir pick. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/rtgi_trace.rvshader:365-367,383-386,409-464,517-519`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/reflection_emitters.glsl:157`
  Top engines: Lumen 2022 does not sample emissive meshes explicitly and lists it as future work. The Matrix night mode was its stress case, handled through the world cache's directional resolution. id's and Snowdrop's gathers take emitters from caches, and Snowdrop clamps emissive fireflies. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/lumen_2022.txt:2553-2560,2998-3006`
  Why: Aiming at small bright fittings removes most of the variance that caches blur or clamp. Two caveats: gi-s1 (fittings also counted as analytic lights) and gi-s3 (bounding-rectangle power) must be fixed in RT2-11 first, and the uniform pick should become the RT-24 reservoir pick.
- **Light lookup at ray hits.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: Trace-only passes (gather, bake, reflections, water) walk every light's cull record. The C++ binds the world grid, but the shader compiles its lookup out. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:2851-2941`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/rtgi_trace.rvshader:40`
  Top engines: Snowdrop uses a light BVH that works anywhere in the world. id uses a cascaded world light grid (16^3 x 8, about 0.1 ms). Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:295-305,652-660`; <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/SOUSA_SIGGRAPH_2025_Final.pdf>
  Why: Cost grows with the scene's light count, and there are about 190 positional lights on the bridge. RT2-12 fixes this.
- **Composition of indirect terms at the pixel.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: ambient = kD*albedo*((flat + probe-or-sky)*skyVisible + irradiance)*AO*screenAO. Under the GI signal the field's sky visibility is never applied, because the bounce is 'answered' everywhere. A local probe's irradiance, which already contains the room's light, is added as 'sky'. AO multiplies the traced bounce and the stored direct light. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:5620-5634`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:5691-5715,5763-5787`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:5854-5855`
  Top engines: One rule per engine, with each term counted once. Lumen: skylight plus the world cache beyond 2 m plus the screen cache near, and contact AO applied after the temporal filter. Snowdrop applies GI as an SH light through the full BSDF. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/lumen_2022.txt:2398-2425,2585-2590`; `build/rt_research/sources/snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:786-802`
  Why: RageV double counts (GI-03), and AO darkens light it should not touch (GI-14).
- **Baked field: what a cell stores.** Comparison: better (claimed). Verified: C-OB3, equal: Unreal's volumetric lightmaps also store L2 harmonics; the separate baked-lamp set is a different trade. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: Per cell:; 9 L2 SH coefficients of bounce (irradiance/pi), with sky visibility in each coefficient's alpha;; 9 more coefficients of the fully and hybrid-baked lamps' direct light;; a visibility tile holding a 6-neighbour bitmask and an alive flag. That is 19 RGBA16F tiles, 152 B a cell. A dominant lamp direction is recovered from the direct light's first band to give baked lamps a highlight. Code: `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/IrradianceVolume.h:136-159`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:1690-1744`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/irradiance_fill.rvshader:296-379,488-534`
  Top engines: Unity APV stores L1 by default (L2 optional). UE Volumetric Lightmaps store 3rd-order SH bricks. Directional lightmaps (UE, id Tech 7 L1) supply the highlight direction for static surfaces. Sources: <https://docs.unity3d.com/Packages/com.unity.render-pipelines.high-definition@17.1/manual/probevolumes-concept.html>; <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/volumetric-lightmaps-in-unreal-engine>
  Why: RageV measured L2 against an ambient cube (the cube was 30% dark on single lamps). Keeping direct light apart and deriving the highlight gives baked lamps a specular term without lightmaps. The cost is memory: 152 B a cell against APV's L1.
- **Baked field: placement, coverage and scaling.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: Hand-placed boxes, at most 8 (the bridge uses all 8), packed into one resident atlas. Where volumes nest the deepest wins with no blend. Every field also allocates a zero-filled solve twin. Two byte-identical files are written, one rt and one ss. Code: `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/Renderer3D.h:86`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:1767-1802`, `RageV/src/RageV/Scene/Scene.cpp:3823-3831`
  Top engines: Unity APV places sparse 4x4x4-probe bricks automatically, refined near geometry, blends lighting scenarios, and streams. UE Volumetric Lightmaps use bricks refined near geometry. Sources: <https://docs.unity3d.com/Packages/com.unity.render-pipelines.high-definition@17.1/manual/probevolumes-concept.html>; <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/volumetric-lightmaps-in-unreal-engine>
  Why: The cap, the seams, the hand placement and the memory all scale badly (GI-12). Static surfaces outside every box get no baked bounce at all.
- **Baked field: leak control.** Comparison: equal. Verified: equal. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: The lookup steps half a cell along the normal, and a further step off a buried anchor. Corners are refused by the neighbour-visibility bits, a facing weight and the alive flag, and the solve reads a single cell at a buried anchor. Virtual offset (relocation) was built and measured on 2026-08-26: it fixed the point-light sealed room (8.4 to 0.2 levels) and made the sun-lit fixtures worse (3.2 to 15.6), so it was reverted. A sealed room still leaks 3.4 levels where cells are coarser than the wall. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:1855-1946,2228-2329`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/irradiance_fill.rvshader:138-148,398-419,488-534`, `docs/BAKING-ROADMAP.md:95-104`
  Top engines: Unity APV: Virtual Offset, Dilation, rendering-layer masks, Leak Reduction mode. DDGI: Chebyshev depth moments, relocation and classification. Snowdrop ships relocation as an option but did not use it in Avatar ('no significant benefit'). Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:176-182`; <https://docs.unity3d.com/Packages/com.unity.render-pipelines.high-definition@17.0/manual/probevolumes-fixissues.html>
  Why: RageV's visibility bits and buried-cell handling match the shipped tools in kind. It lacks dilation and a working relocation, but its own measurement shows relocation is a trade-off, not a sure win.
- **Contact-scale baked detail (lightmaps).** Comparison: missing. Verified: missing. Confidence: high.
  RageV: None: MeshVertex has a single UV set, so lightmaps cannot be represented, and the roadmap does not plan them. About 1.9% of pixels bleed at contact scale. Code: `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/Mesh.h:17-22`, `docs/BAKING-ROADMAP.md:30-36`, `docs/RT-SERIES-2.md:393`
  Top engines: UE, Unity and id Tech 7 ship directional lightmaps for static surfaces alongside probe volumes for dynamic objects. Sources: <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/volumetric-lightmaps-in-unreal-engine>; <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/SOUSA_SIGGRAPH_2025_Final.pdf>
  Why: A volume-only bake cannot resolve contact bounce below its cell spacing (1 m in the garage, 5-32 m on the bridge). beats_both item 1 is the proposed substitute.
- **Mixed lighting: which light's bounce comes from where.** Comparison: not comparable. Verified: not comparable. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: Mobilities: Realtime, HalfBake (live direct, baked bounce), FullBake (both baked) and HybridFullBake (live within a radius, baked beyond). The bake skips Realtime lights. When a bake is honoured the whole realtime chain is dropped, so Realtime lights bounce nothing. Code: `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/Light.h:42-48`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:295-307,2963-2969`, `RageV/src/RageV/Scene/Scene.cpp:1887-1912`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:902-913`
  Top engines: UE Stationary: live direct, baked indirect, at most 4 overlapping. Unity: Baked Indirect and Shadowmask modes; baked GI can run beside Enlighten realtime GI, which Unity calls rarely advisable. No shipped ray-traced engine layers a realtime bounce for Realtime lights on top of a bake. Sources: <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/stationary-light-mobility-in-unreal-engine>; <https://docs.unity3d.com/6000.0/Documentation/Manual/lighting-mode.html>
  Why: Hybrid's distance split has no shipped equal (ours better there). A missing Realtime-light bounce over a bake is ours_missing today. D4's target has no ray-traced precedent, so its additivity must be proved by diff images.
- **Stale-bake detection.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: The stamp is the volume layout plus a hash of the lights and the environment. The code comment says it does not catch a moved wall or an edited material. Code: `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/BakedLighting.h:48-72`, `RageV/src/RageV/Scene/Scene.cpp:4250-4259`
  Top engines: UE tracks every primitive-light interaction and warns 'Lighting needs to be rebuilt (N unbuilt objects)', with a command that lists them. GPU Lightmass re-bakes what is visible. Sources: <https://forums.unrealengine.com/t/lighting-needs-to-be-rebuilt-197-unbuilt-objects/440544>; <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/gpu-lightmass-global-illumination-in-unreal-engine>
  Why: Today a stale bake is silently wrong.
- **Bake solver consistency with the frame.** Comparison: better (claimed). Verified: C-OB4, partly better: consistent with the frame by construction, less accurate than a reference baker. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: The bake is solved by the frame's own TraceSurface/ShadeTraced (irradiance_fill includes pbr_fragment.glsl), with 1M rays a frame in a child process. Two defects: the sky at bake hits is unoccluded (slot 0), and the solve's shadow rays see only the static half of the scene. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/irradiance_fill.rvshader:3-11,229,279,357-361`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:3255-3267`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/Renderer3D.cpp:2945-2951`
  Top engines: UE Lightmass and GPU Lightmass are separate renderers from the runtime. Unity's GPU baker is likewise separate. Sources: <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/gpu-lightmass-global-illumination-in-unreal-engine>
  Why: One hit shader means the bake cannot drift from the frame's lighting model. The unoccluded sky at bake hits is a real defect (the audit row), and the leak fixtures cannot see it because their skies are black.
- **Reflection probes as a lighting source.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: Probes are captured by raster before the TLAS is built, so every shadow ray answers 'lit'. Their irradiance is used as the pixel's diffuse 'sky' and as the light arriving at every traced hit. Code: `RageVRuntime/src/RuntimeLayer.cpp:493-499`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:3197-3201,5630-5634`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/rtgi_trace.rvshader:211-230`
  Top engines: Lumen replaces probes with its radiance caches. Snowdrop's hits read the probe cache, not reflection captures. UE and Unity keep raster reflection captures for specular fallback only. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/lumen_2022.txt:2398-2425`; `build/rt_research/sources/snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:286-305`
  Why: The probe is about a third too bright (GI-04) and enters diffuse light twice.
- **AO and indirect light.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: RTAO with a 0.5 m radius multiplies everything: the traced bounce, the probe sky and the stored direct light of baked lamps. Code: `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/PostSettings.h:694`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/rtao_compute.rvshader:372`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:5854-5855`
  Top engines: Lumen applies its shortest-range (16-pixel) occlusion after the temporal filter as contact detail on the gathered indirect light. Baked engines apply AO to indirect and ambient, never to direct light. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/lumen_2022.txt:2585-2590`
  Why: AO darkens baked lamps' direct light only where it is baked (GI-14), and on top of a gather it double-darkens contact regions.
- **Per-tile adaptive ray budget for the gather.** Comparison: equal. Verified: equal. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: The gather reads a per-tile ray allocation from last frame's budget map. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/rtgi_trace.rvshader:332-347`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:2176-2181`
  Top engines: Lumen uses adaptive downsampling of screen probes (extra probes where geometry is detailed). id and Snowdrop use a fixed 1 ray per traced pixel. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/lumen_2022.txt:2354-2370`
  Why: The mechanisms are comparable. At 1 ray per traced pixel the budget map becomes a skip or extra map rather than a 1-to-4 range.
- **Realtime GI at kilometre scale.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: The runtime cache reaches 12 m. The bridge has no probes, so hits get only sky. It has never been measured on the bridge. Code: `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/RuntimeIrradianceField.h:43-46`, `docs/RT-SERIES-2-FINDINGS.md:1394-1415`
  Top engines: Snowdrop cascades span 2/8/64/1024 m spacing, 32x32x8 km at the coarsest. id's hash cache uses distance LOD. SHaRC uses logarithmic distance levels. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:217-228`; <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/SHARC/blob/main/docs/Integration.md>
  Why: The shipped engines cover distance. Their spacings are still scene-scale constants (Snowdrop) or carry a scene-scale parameter (SHaRC); a footprint-derived cell size would beat both on rule 2.
- **Path-tracing-tier GI: ReSTIR GI or PT, NRC, SHaRC.** Comparison: not comparable. Verified: not comparable. Confidence: high.
  RageV: Not built. ReSTIR GI was rejected twice (RT-SERIES-2 section 7.6) and NRC is ruled out (7.4). Code: `docs/RT-SERIES-2.md:2044-2046`
  Top engines: Cyberpunk, Indiana Jones and Doom path-tracing modes use ReSTIR GI and/or SHaRC with DLSS Ray Reconstruction. ReSTIR GI reuse alone costs 4.6 ms at 1920x1050 on an RTX 3080. Sources: <https://d1qx31qr3h6wln.cloudfront.net/publications/ReSTIR%20GI.pdf>; <https://developer.nvidia.com/blog/path-tracing-optimizations-in-indiana-jones-opacity-micromaps-and-compaction-of-dynamic-blass/>
  Why: A different tier. It relies on DLSS RR (rule 3) and exceeds a native 2560x1600 budget on this laptop.
- **Surface caches (Lumen cards, OSC-GI) and surfels (GIBS).** Comparison: not comparable. Verified: not comparable. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: None. Code: `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/Mesh.h:17-22`
  Top engines: Lumen's surface cache: 0.47-1.17 ms on PS5 at 1080p, with budgets of 1024^2 direct texels and 512^2 indirect texels a frame and at most 4 frames of accumulation. OSC-GI is research that needs unique UVs. GIBS surfels shipped in College Football 25, at about 7 ms on PS5 in the 2021 talk. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/lumen_2022.txt:1305-1315,1405-1415,2913-2960`; <https://dl.acm.org/doi/10.1145/3675382>
  Why: Both surface-cache forms need a unique parameterisation that RageV's single-UV vertex does not have, and surfels cost several times the budget. The hash cache gets the on-surface benefit without UVs.

### C.6 Candidates that could beat both

- **C-BB1: Gather the bake per pixel. The realtime layer's one ray per traced pixel reads, at its hit, the baked field's stored light (bounce plus far direct from baked lamps) as well as the realtime cache. Its result replaces the receiver-side field read wherever the gather has an estimate. The bake's content is unchanged; only where it is sampled moves, from the receiver's cell to the hit point one bounce away.**
  Why better than both: Unity and UE get contact-scale baked bounce only through lightmaps, which RageV cannot represent. id, Snowdrop and Anvil have no bake. This gives baked lamps per-pixel contact bounce and indirect shadows from a volume-only bake. It uses the ray the realtime layer already casts, at roughly the cost of one field fetch per hit. Evidence: RageV's own measurement (ENGINE-NOTES 7ct, cited at Scene.cpp:1893-1901 and in GI-01's skeptic note): traced rays ending in the field scored error 0.73 against 1.48 for realtime alone and 5.73 for the field alone, on the fixture. It was rejected only because it cost 8.7 ms against 3.9 ms baked on the showroom, with the old 4-ray, full-hit-shade gather. The per-hit field read measured +0.55 ms on a 1.5 ms frame with the careful 32-fetch path (pbr_fragment.glsl:1660-1666); the fast path is 9 fetches. Risk: If the gather does not run in every view, baked contact detail pops in and out. Field cells inside walls read at hits could reintroduce leaks; the careful read with visibility bits must be used. Cost: +0.2-0.4 ms at native on top of the gather; the gather itself 1.5-2.0 ms if it must run in every view.
  The comparison's gate: - Converged with --aa=none on the garage and the RT2-2e bright-sky room: the share of pixels off --reference-render by more than grain falls from today's 1.9% contact bleed.; Leak fixtures in check_gi.py stay at 0.000.; A,B,B,A at native: the field read at hits adds at most 0.3 ms on top of the gather.; If the gather runs only near Realtime lights (OD1), a diff image of the frame before and after the layer switches on must show no step beyond grain.
  Verification: **partly true.** The reasoning holds but the evidence is mixed, so this needs a measurement gate. (a) It must be gated on the showroom bright bay, where the one real-scene measurement had it losing. (b) 'Roughly the cost of one field fetch per hit' is wrong: it is ~20 fetches at best and ~150 on the careful path, and must be re-measured. (c) 'Anvil has no bake' is inaccurate: the phase-1 survey records a baked diffuse GI path shipping as an alternative on some platforms (not layered). (d) It reopens the owner's 'Baked means baked' ruling (Scene.cpp:1887-1901), because it needs the gather to run in baked scenes.
  In the roadmap: RT2-25 step 25e (D18).
- **C-BB2: A differential correction for moving objects over the bake. When a realtime-layer ray hits a dynamic surface, it subtracts the radiance the baked field says arrived from that direction (L2 SH turned back into low-order radiance) and adds the dynamic surface's own radiance under all lights.**
  Why better than both: UE and Unity leave dynamic objects neither bouncing baked light nor blocking baked bounce, apart from AO. Fully realtime engines get it right but pay for all light live. This keeps the bake and still makes a moving car block and bounce baked lamps' light. Evidence: Differential rendering (Debevec 1998) is established for inserting objects into captured lighting. RageV's field already stores L2 SH (pbr_fragment.glsl:1690-1715) and the bake already excludes dynamic geometry (irradiance_fill.rvshader:357-361; Scene.cpp:3748). No shipped game precedent was found; this is reasoning from the literature. Risk: SH ringing gives negative or overshooting differences, which must be clamped. It widens when the realtime layer runs (OD2). The field's radiance per direction is low-order, so small bright baked sources are subtracted inaccurately. Cost: 0.1-0.3 ms; needs the layer running while dynamic objects are near.
  The comparison's gate: - Scene: the garage car moved through a baked-lamp pool (RT-22's moving cube as the minimum). Converged --aa=none frames against --reference-render with the object in place: the diff around the object improves on the AO-only baseline.; No negative light: the count of pixels clamped at zero is recorded.; Cost at most 0.3 ms at native when dynamic objects are in view.
  Verification: **plausible.** Unmeasured, with real risks. The field stores irradiance, so recovering radiance means deconvolving L2 (ringing, negatives). The subtraction must use both the bounce set and the far-direct set. With 1 ray per traced pixel, a car covering a small solid angle will be noisy. It also requires the gather to run in baked scenes. It needs a gate (RT-22's moving cube against --reference-render, no region brighter than the reference, with --aa=none).
  In the roadmap: RT2-25 step 25f (D19).
- **C-BB3: Drive each cell of the realtime cache by measured change. When a cell is re-shaded, compare the fresh estimate with the stored mean using the cell's own variance; restart the cell's average when the difference exceeds the noise, and otherwise accumulate up to a sample cap.**
  Why better than both: Shipped caches react either by a fixed hysteresis or accumulation cap (Lumen caps its surface cache at 4 frames; SHaRC has a max-frames setting and a responsive mode) or by events (Snowdrop invalidation). Both trade lag against noise with a constant. Per-cell change testing is frame-rate independent and needs no event plumbing, and it extends RageV's measured-change idea from pixels to world cells. Evidence: RageV's measured change on the pixel signal took the switched light still held after 10 frames from 77% to 12.6% for 0.21 ms (docs/RT-SERIES-2-FINDINGS.md:1233). The audit's general form for the old cache asks for this too (RT-SERIES-2.md:2485-2487). Risk: Variance estimates are poor at low sample counts, which can cause false restarts; RT2-6's precision rules apply. Cost: Near zero: one comparison and a variance lane per updated cell.
  The comparison's gate: - emitter_lag.py (tools/scripts/garage/session_2026_09_15/emitter_lag.py) with --frame-time pinned to 0.000001 (0 means the wall clock): frames to converge after a Realtime light switches is no worse than the pixel signal's.; Parked cameras with --aa=none: temporal flicker of the gi-light debug view is no worse than a fixed-cap cache at equal memory.
  Verification: **partly true.** The 'why' misstates the baseline. Shipped DDGI caches do react to measured change, but with a fixed ratio threshold. What is new is only a noise-relative (variance-based) threshold and a sample-count cap. The cited evidence (the pixel re-trace, 77% to 12.6%) is a different mechanism: an exact same-ray replay, not a statistical test on a cell's variance. It must be measured against a DDGI-style ratio threshold, not against a fixed hysteresis.
  In the roadmap: RT2-25 step 25c, against DDGI's per-probe threshold as the baseline arm.

| C-BB1 | beats_both | major | partly true | RageV's own measurement (ENGINE-NOTES 7ct, cited at Scene.cpp:1893-1901 and in GI-01's skeptic note): traced rays ending in the field scored error 0.73 against 1.48 for realtime alone and 5.73 for the field alone, on the fixture. It was rejected only because it cost 8.7 ms against 3.9 ms baked on the showroom, with the old 4-ray, full-hit-shade gather. The per-hit field read measured +0.55 ms on a 1.5 ms frame with the careful 32-fetch path (pbr_fragment.glsl:1660-1666); the fast path is 9 fetches. |
| C-BB2 | beats_both | major | plausible | Differential rendering (Debevec 1998) is established for inserting objects into captured lighting. RageV's field already stores L2 SH (pbr_fragment.glsl:1690-1715) and the bake already excludes dynamic geometry (irradiance_fill.rvshader:357-361; Scene.cpp:3748). No shipped game precedent was found; this is reasoning from the literature. |
| C-BB3 | beats_both | major | partly true | RageV's measured change on the pixel signal took the switched light still held after 10 frames from 77% to 12.6% for 0.21 ms (docs/RT-SERIES-2-FINDINGS.md:1233). The audit's general form for the old cache asks for this too (RT-SERIES-2.md:2485-2487). |

- **C-C1** (confirmed). Claim: The project's RTGI High is 4 rays per traced pixel with the trace target at width/2 x height/2. So RageV's 'half resolution' is the industry's 'quarter resolution', at 4x the rays.
  Evidence: FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:359-383: RayDetailDivisor is 4 at Low and 2 otherwise, RayDetailRays is 4 at High and 2 otherwise. The comment reads 'High is four rays at half'. FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:2157-2164 sizes GiRaw at desc.Width/2 x desc.Height/2. SSAA is the only thing that scales the scene target (FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:417-424), and the project uses TAA. SampleProject.rvproject sets RayTracedGlobalIllumination: High. rtgi_trace.rvshader:341-346 takes that count as the ray count.
  Correction: 4 is a ceiling, not a fixed count. The editor binds a ray budget (EditorLayer.cpp:683,843), which makes Renderer3D.cpp:2361 send Rays as negative, and rtgi_trace.rvshader:343-346 then clamps each tile to the budget map's allocation, between 1 and 4. The conclusion (half per axis = the industry's 'quarter resolution', at up to 4x the rays) holds.
- **C-C2** (confirmed). Claim: Under an honoured Baked source the whole realtime GI chain is off, and the bake solve skips Realtime lights, so Realtime lights bounce nothing in the garage and on the bridge.
  Evidence: Scene.cpp:1887-1912: SetRayTracedGlobalIllumination(giDetail != Off && !bakedHonoured). FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:902-913: bakedOnly drops the whole indirect chain. Scene.cpp:3579-3596: the runtime field is withdrawn whenever WantsBakedGi(). pbr_fragment.glsl:2963-2970: under RV_IRRADIANCE_FILL the solve skips lights with Params.w < 0.5 (Realtime). The garage (showroom.rage) has 7 Realtime lights out of 30, and the bridge has 14 out of 190.
  Correction: One path is left, and it is stale rather than live. Under Baked, the lit pass still adds the reflection probe's irradiance as 'skyDiffuse' (pbr_fragment.glsl:5620-5634, 5854-5855). The garage's single probe is Update: Baked with a 42 m influence, and it is recaptured only when moved or on request, never on a light change (Scene.cpp:3270-3306). So any Realtime light that was on when the probe was captured is frozen into it: unshadowed, never updated. No live bounce exists for Realtime lights. Also, most of the garage's Realtime lights are at Intensity 0 in the saved scene (mode scripts switch them).
- **C-C3** (confirmed). Claim: Frame hits (ShadeTraced outside the fill) read only the fully baked direct light from the field and discard its bounce and sky. The light arriving at a gather hit is one probe's unoccluded irradiance.
  Evidence: pbr_fragment.glsl:3263-3301 (#else branch, i.e. every frame compile): VolumeIrradiance returns hitBounce, hitSky and hitDirect; only 'ambientLight += hitDirect' is used, and hitBounce and hitSky are dropped. This happens only for static hits and only when IrradianceExtents.x > 0.5. rtgi_trace.rvshader:617-618: with bounces < 2, arriving = ProbeIrradiance(first.Normal, GiProbeSlotAt(first.Position)), which is one probe cube with no visibility test at the hit. PostSettings.h:651 sets GiBounces = 1 by default, so that is the live path. ShadeTraced also adds the flat u_Scene.Ambient.
- **C-C4** (confirmed). Claim: Trace-only passes walk every light: the cluster and world-grid lookups are inside #ifndef RV_TRACE_ONLY, and rtgi_trace and irradiance_fill define RV_TRACE_ONLY.
  Evidence: pbr_fragment.glsl:2851-2938: the cluster lookup and the world-grid lookup are both inside #ifndef RV_TRACE_ONLY, and the #else branch loops i < hitLightCount. rtgi_trace.rvshader:39 and irradiance_fill.rvshader:32 both #define RV_TRACE_ONLY.
  Correction: The loop runs over every light, but in the frame's trace passes RV_LIGHT_CULL (pbr_fragment.glsl:492-531, 2942-2945) still rejects out-of-range and baked lamps from a 16-byte record before the 80-byte read. The cost is O(all lights) cheap reads, not O(all lights) full shading. In the fill, RV_LIGHT_CULL is off by design, so it does read every light in full.
- **C-C5** (partly true). Claim: The GI upsample weights taps by depth only and carries RGB irradiance, so normal-map detail is lost at 2x2 scale. Storing SH would recover it.
  Evidence: signal_upsample.rvshader:90-114: the weight is bilinear x 1/(eps + relative depth difference), with no normal term. The comment at lines 17-23 records a normal term that was measured and removed. The output is RGB plus validity. rtgi_trace.rvshader:279-312 takes the normal from ONE snapped full-res texel, and 687 writes RGB. So normal-map variation inside a 2x2 block is lost: confirmed.
  Correction: 'Storing SH would recover it' is not established in RageV. It is id's stated rationale (Sousa 2025 p.21: '2-band SH ... maintain normal maps details'), but L1 SH at the trace grid recovers only the low-order directional response. It needs the SH-arm gate the design already has.
- **C-C6** (confirmed). Claim: Relocation (virtual offset) was built and measured on 2026-08-26: the point-light sealed room went from 8.4 to 0.2 levels, and the sun-lit fixtures went from 3.2 to 15.6. It was reverted.
  Evidence: irradiance_fill.rvshader:138-148 comment: the APV/DDGI-style lift was implemented and run on 2026-08-26. The point-light room went from 8.4 to 0.2 levels, and the sun-lit rooms from 3.2 to 15.6 at 1 m cells. The same numbers are in ENGINE-NOTES.md:13853-13855 and HANDOFF.md:5758-5760. There is no virtual offset in the current fill.
  Correction: This is a trace-origin lift (APV virtual offset), not DDGI probe relocation. The comment itself says it needs its own pass over the alive, dead and visibility logic.
- **C-C7** (confirmed). Claim: The baked field is L2 SH (9 coefficients) of bounce plus a separate L2 set of baked lamps' direct light plus a neighbour-bitmask tile, 19 RGBA16F tiles. The Chebyshev distance test was replaced, although comments still describe L1 and Chebyshev.
  Evidence: IrradianceVolume.h:136-159: kTiles = 19 (tiles 0-8 bounce with sky in alpha, 9-17 direct, 18 visibility and alive), in an RGBA16F texture (IrradianceVolume.cpp:185). irradiance_fill.rvshader:488-534 writes the 6-neighbour bitmask into tile 18. The stale comments are real: IrradianceVolume.h:25-44 still says first-order SH, 'five tiles' and 32 Chebyshev tiles, pbr_fragment.glsl:1645-1653 says L1, and 2033-2041 describes Chebyshev, which the code no longer uses.
  Correction: There is one more stale comment: pbr_fragment.glsl:1702 says 'Tile 9 carries the neighbour-visibility bits', but RV_FIELD_VISIBILITY_TILE is 18 and tile 9 is the first direct tile.
- **C-C8** (confirmed). Claim: Bake hits get unoccluded sky irradiance (probe slot 0), which a sealed room under a non-black sky would leak into its bounce.
  Evidence: irradiance_fill.rvshader:279: ShadeTraced(hit, ProbeIrradiance(hit.Normal, 0.0), 0.0), so every bake hit receives slot-0 (sky) irradiance with no visibility. The traced flavour also adds the previous sweep's field unoccluded (pbr_fragment.glsl:3241-3262). The lit pass applies skyVisible only to (ambient + skyDiffuse), never to the stored bounce (pbr_fragment.glsl:5854-5855), so leaked sky in the bounce is never removed. All four irradiance_leak*.rage fixtures have a black sky (SkyHorizon, Zenith and Ground all [0,0,0]).
  Correction: Same class of leak, not in the claim: ShadeTraced also adds the flat u_Scene.Ambient at bake hits unoccluded (pbr_fragment.glsl:3221). The fixtures have AmbientIntensity 0, so they cannot see that either. The size of either leak is unmeasured.
- **C-C9** (partly true). Claim: MeshVertex has one UV set, so lightmaps and UV-based surface caches (OSC-GI, Lumen cards) cannot be represented without a vertex-format change.
  Evidence: Mesh.h:17-22: MeshVertex is Position/Normal/TexCoord, one UV set. BAKING-ROADMAP.md:30-36 calls lightmaps unrepresentable. The lightmap half is confirmed.
  Correction: Wrong for Lumen cards. lumen_2022.txt:959-962 says cards are projection-based and need no vertex attributes. They are captured at runtime by rasterising into cards with orthographic cameras (1175-1176), and the talk rejects UVs explicitly (907, 944). A Lumen-style surface cache needs card placement and a capture pass, not a second UV set. OSC-GI was not checked (no source in the phase files).
- **C-C10** (confirmed). Claim: The bake stamp covers the volume layout and a light and environment hash only. Moved Static geometry and edited materials are invisible to it.
  Evidence: BakedLighting.h:48-68: the Stamp holds the box (Centre, Extents, Axes), Width/Height/Depth/Tiles, the Lighting hash and the Layout hash. Scene.cpp:4250-4258 (LightingHash): the comment says outright that it 'does not catch a moved wall or an edited material'.
- **C-C11** (confirmed). Claim: RT-24's uncommitted change lifts the GI emitter cap to every emitter, but the gather still picks between emitters uniformly.
  Evidence: git diff of rtgi_trace.rvshader: clamp(...,0,16) became max(int(u_Gi.Emitters+0.5),0). In Renderer3D.cpp's diff the Min(...,16u) caps and the kMaxAreaEmitters early-out are removed. rtgi_trace.rvshader:383-386 still picks int(GiRandom(seed)*emitters), which is uniform. The change is uncommitted (git status M).
- **C-C12** (confirmed). Claim: Snowdrop: probe update costs 0.258 ms on an RTX 4080 at 1024 probes a frame (about 15% retraced, 85% relit). Diffuse RTGI on Xbox Series X at 1440p (quarter resolution on consoles) is 3.877 ms against a 7.211 ms baseline. Tracing went 4.621 to 3.054 to 1.462 ms as screen tracing and then the cache fallback were added.
  Evidence: snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:233-262: 1024 probes a frame, about 15% (~162) fully retraced and about 85% (~862) relit only. snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:1133-1140: probe total on an RTX 4080 is 0.258 ms. snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:465-470: consoles trace at quarter resolution. snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:1143-1164: Series X at 1440p, diffuse total 7.211 baseline to 3.877 ms, and tracing 4.621, then 3.054 (+ screen space), then 1.462 ms (+ cache volume fallback).
- **C-C13** (confirmed). Claim: Snowdrop shipped Avatar without probe relocation ('no significant benefit'). Its RT world uses one untextured material per mesh. Its hit shader reuses previous-frame screen lighting when the hit reprojects.
  Evidence: snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:176-182: probe relocation is optional and 'didn't gave any significant benefit in Avatar so it was not used'. snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:38-52: RT world uses lower LODs and one simple material per mesh with no textures (terrain is ray marched separately and can use textures). snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:650-676: the hit shader reprojects to the previous frame and samples that frame's lighting if it matches, and otherwise lights with the sun, probes and point lights.
- **C-C14** (confirmed). Claim: Lumen's world radiance cache uses 32x32-direction probes with a fixed number updated from a GPU priority queue. Screen probes are 8x8 at 1/16 resolution per axis. The surface cache's budgets are 1024^2 direct and 512^2 indirect texels with a 4-frame accumulation cap. Emissive meshes are not explicitly sampled (future work).
  Evidence: lumen_2022.txt:2517-2524: world probes are 32x32 and screen probes 8x8. lumen_2022.txt:2398-2399: the screen cache runs at 1/16 resolution per axis. lumen_2022.txt:2540-2552: a fixed number of probes is picked from a GPU priority queue. lumen_2022.txt:1303-1312: surface-cache budgets of 1024x1024 direct and 512x512 indirect texels, priority LastUsed - LastUpdated. lumen_2022.txt:1405: 4-frame temporal accumulation. lumen_2022.txt:2557-2560 and 2998-3004: emissive meshes are not explicitly sampled, and doing so is future work.
  Correction: This is Lumen as of 2022. megalights_2025.txt:117-140 confirms that UE still treats emissive meshes as a GI problem in 2025.
- **C-C15** (confirmed). Claim: id Tech 8 (Doom: The Dark Ages): a final gather of 1 ray per pixel with no hit shading (screen lighting, then the hash cache, then irradiance volumes), about 1.47 ms for gather, denoise and upscale at 4K on an RTX 4080, and a 14 MB hash cache with 25 cm cells plus distance LOD. Not in the local sources; unverified by this agent.
  Evidence: Sousa SIGGRAPH 2025 PDF (fetched; text extracted), p.11: 'final gather step ... ( 0 shading involved )'. p.21: 1 ray per pixel at (1/2)x(1/2) or (1/4)x(1/4) depending on quality; screen space if the hit is in the frustum and unoccluded, then the world radiance cache, then the irradiance volumes; 2-band SH in 3 x RGBA16F. Radiance cache page: '25cm3 cells quantization + cells LODs', '14 MB'. p.22 timing table, PC 4K (RTX 4080): Final Gather 0.5, Denoise 0.52, Upscale 0.445 ms. That sums to 1.465 ms; the full GI total is ~1.7 ms serial.
  Correction: This is a hotspot measurement (mission 4, large vista). The full per-frame GI cost at 4K is ~1.7 ms, including world sampling 0.091, radiance cache 0.109 and volumes 0.07.
- **C-C16** (unsupported). Claim: AC Shadows diffuse GI costs 2.65 ms serial at 1440p quarter resolution on an RTX 4070, with a ray range of twice the cascade spacing. Not in the local sources; unverified by this agent.
  Evidence: Could not read the primary source: the AC Shadows Advances 2025 PDF exceeds WebFetch's 10 MB limit, and downloading it by other means needs the owner's permission. The phase-1 survey's breakdown (screen-space 0.27 + world 0.55 + lighting 0.97 + denoise 0.86 = 2.65 ms serial, 1.90 async, RTX 4070 at 1440p quarter resolution) is internally consistent, but I did not confirm it from the PDF. The 'ray range = 2x cascade spacing' figure is also unconfirmed.
  Correction: Keep it as 'reported by the phase-1 survey, unverified' until someone reads the PDF.
- **C-C17** (partly true). Claim: RageV's 'rays ending in the field' arrangement scored error 0.73 against 1.48 (realtime) and 5.73 (field alone), and cost 8.7 ms against 7.2 ms realtime and 3.9 ms baked on the showroom.
  Evidence: ENGINE-NOTES.md:13669-13711 (7ct): on the irradiance_field fixture, field-only 5.73, one traced bounce 1.48 (~1.8 ms), bounce terminating in the field 0.73 (1.87 ms). Showroom costs: realtime ~7.2 ms, hybrid ~8.7 ms, field-only 3.9 ms. Scene.cpp:1893-1901 records the removal for cost.
  Correction: The numbers are right but selectively quoted. They pair the fixture's error with the showroom's cost. On the showroom itself the hybrid LOST on quality: mode 2 (bright bay) scored realtime 0.51, hybrid 1.00, field-only 2.52; in mode 1 all three were within 0.24. On the fixture the hybrid cost only +0.07 ms. The measurement also predates the L2 field (2026-09-02), so both the error and the cost are for an older field format.
- **C-OB1** (refuted). Claim: RageV is BETTER than the top engines at: GI denoiser: anti-lag. Ours: The shared signal contract runs at the trace grid. Measured change re-traces the same rays in a record and re-light pass to find where the light really changed: 0.21 ms at 1 ray, and the share of a switched light still held ten frames later fell from 77% to 12.6%. Theirs: NRD takes a history-confidence input that the application must supply. Lumen detects traces that hit fast-moving objects and speeds its accumulation up there. Snowdrop invalidates probes on events (objects moving, time of day). Why: RageV measures the change with the same rays (an A-SVGF-style temporal gradient) and does not infer it from motion or events. Among the shipped engines, only Q2RTX-class research renderers do this.
  Evidence: The RageV half holds. FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:2211-2272 runs GiRecord/GiRelight when config.MeasuredChange (default true, EngineConfig.h:563). RT-MEASURED-CHANGE.md:311-313 gives the bounce holding 77.4% vs 12.6% ten frames after the switch with the camera parked (23.8% vs 9.1% moving), and 344-346 gives the cost as 0.21 ms at one ray (Balanced/Performance), 0.59 ms at Quality. The other half fails. The NRD README (fetched) recommends computing IN_DIFF_CONFIDENCE from a gradient between stored and re-traced previous-frame radiance using the previous frame's RNG seed, at reduced resolution. That is the same A-SVGF re-trace RageV does, offered as NRD's documented recipe, and Q2RTX is a shipped game using it.
  Correction: This is equal to the published NRD/A-SVGF practice, not better. And under the project's own default (RayTracedGiSource: Baked, honoured), the GI chain, including this anti-lag, does not run at all. The project runs RtOptimisation: Quality, so the relevant cost is ~0.59 ms while moving, not 0.21.
- **C-OB2** (partly true). Claim: RageV is BETTER than the top engines at: Emissive surfaces in the gather (NEE). Ours: Every sample aims a shadow ray at an emitter rectangle, using an aiming table over the emissive map's cells. RT-24 (uncommitted) lifted the 16-row cap to every emitter. The pick between emitters is still uniform, although the reflections now use RT-24's weighted reservoir pick. Theirs: Lumen 2022 does not sample emissive meshes explicitly and lists it as future work. The Matrix night mode was its stress case, handled through the world cache's directional resolution. id's and Snowdrop's gathers take emitters from caches, and Snowdrop clamps emissive fireflies. Why: Aiming at small bright fittings removes most of the variance that caches blur or clamp. Two caveats: gi-s1 (fittings also counted as analytic lights) and gi-s3 (bounding-rectangle power) must be fixed in RT2-11 first, and the uniform pick should become the RT-24 reservoir pick.
  Evidence: The RageV half holds. rtgi_trace.rvshader:383-470: a uniform pick of one emitter, then CDF-table aiming over emissive-map cells, then a shadow ray. The emitter cap is removed in the uncommitted diff. Against Lumen: lumen_2022.txt:2557-2560 and 2998-3004 (no explicit emissive sampling), and megalights_2025.txt:117-140 (still true in 2025). Snowdrop adds emission with firefly suppression (snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:660-668).
  Correction: 'Better' has not been earned today. (1) The claim concedes two open correctness defects: fittings counted twice (gi-s1) and bounding-rectangle power (gi-s3). (2) RageV also clamps its emitter estimate (kNeeClamp = 32, kIndirectClamp = 4, rtgi_trace.rvshader:371,585), the same class of bias as Snowdrop's firefly suppression, so 'removes variance that caches ... clamp' cuts both ways. (3) Shipped path-traced modes (ReSTIR/RTXDI-class, which importance-samples emissive triangles) were not checked from a primary source, so 'better than the top engines' covers at most the raster-hybrid engines (Lumen, Snowdrop, id).
- **C-OB3** (refuted). Claim: RageV is BETTER than the top engines at: Baked field: what a cell stores. Ours: Per cell:; 9 L2 SH coefficients of bounce (irradiance/pi), with sky visibility in each coefficient's alpha;; 9 more coefficients of the fully and hybrid-baked lamps' direct light;; a visibility tile holding a 6-neighbour bitmask and an alive flag. That is 19 RGBA16F tiles, 152 B a cell. A dominant lamp direction is recovered from the direct light's first band to give baked lamps a highlight. Theirs: Unity APV stores L1 by default (L2 optional). UE Volumetric Lightmaps store 3rd-order SH bricks. Directional lightmaps (UE, id Tech 7 L1) supply the highlight direction for static surfaces. Why: RageV measured L2 against an ambient cube (the cube was 30% dark on single lamps). Keeping direct light apart and deriving the highlight gives baked lamps a specular term without lightmaps. The cost is memory: 152 B a cell against APV's L1.
  Evidence: The RageV side is confirmed (C-C7: 19 RGBA16F tiles, 152 B a cell). The UE docs (fetched) say each Volumetric Lightmap sample 'uses a Third Order Spherical Harmonic', which is L2 with 9 coefficients: the same order RageV stores. Unity APV's default of L1 was not re-confirmed (the fetched page does not state the SH bands).
  Correction: This is equal to UE VLM on SH order, not better. What is RageV's own is a separate L2 set for baked lamps' direct light, plus a dominant direction taken from its first band for a highlight. Extracting a dominant light from SH is a well-known technique. It costs 2x memory and 2x fetches (9+9) per read. Call it 'a different trade, justified by 7ct's measured 30% cube deficit', not 'better than the top engines'.
- **C-OB4** (partly true). Claim: RageV is BETTER than the top engines at: Bake solver consistency with the frame. Ours: The bake is solved by the frame's own TraceSurface/ShadeTraced (irradiance_fill includes pbr_fragment.glsl), with 1M rays a frame in a child process. Two defects: the sky at bake hits is unoccluded (slot 0), and the solve's shadow rays see only the static half of the scene. Theirs: UE Lightmass and GPU Lightmass are separate renderers from the runtime. Unity's GPU baker is likewise separate. Why: One hit shader means the bake cannot drift from the frame's lighting model. The unoccluded sky at bake hits is a real defect (the audit row), and the leak fixtures cannot see it because their skies are black.
  Evidence: irradiance_fill.rvshader:32-33 includes pbr_fragment.glsl, and line 229 calls TraceSurface and 279 ShadeTraced. Renderer3D.cpp:2951-2955: kRayBudget = 1<<20, and the comment says the Bake button is a child process (ChildProcess.cpp exists). irradiance_fill.rvshader:357-361: the solve's shadow rays use RV_RAY_MASK_SCENE, the static half only.
  Correction: 'Cannot drift from the frame' is overstated.
