Measured by: A white-furnace fixture for diffuse (albedo 1, roughness 0 to 1). ms per pass for EON against Lambert in the composition, DirectTrace and GI hits. The owner judges the look through diff images on the garage concrete and the camp.
- **Candidate: Spherical-cap VNDF sampling, plus bounded VNDF in the reflection and GI trace** Why better: Same or lower cost, and less wasted and lower-variance sampling of low-roughness lobes (wet floor). Evidence: Author claims of systematic speed-ups (spherical caps). Bounded VNDF numbers not retrieved. Risk: A PDF mismatch introduces bias into the resolve and the denoiser. Measured by: Per-pixel variance of the 16-ray truth harness on the garage puddle, before and after. Reflection trace ms A,B,B,A. Check that the PDF used by the resolve's ratio weighting matches (no bias: a converged mean diff near 0).
- **Candidate: Primary Surface Replacement guides for mirror-like pixels, fed to the reflection accumulator** Why better: Reflected surfaces get their own depth, normal and motion, so history reuse on mirrors follows the reflected geometry rather than the mirror. Evidence: Shipped in Q2RTX and Minecraft RTX; no published cost. Risk: Exact only for perfect mirrors, and a roughness cut-off conflicts with rule 2. Curved-mirror motion vectors are out of scope in NVIDIA's write-up. Measured by: The RT-24 spin-smear orbit and a camera-translation test on chrome and the still puddle: smear and ghost metrics with diff images, with the feature on and off. Its ms.

### F.5 RageV against them, topic by topic

- **Specular distribution (GGX, alpha = roughness squared).** Comparison: equal. Verified: equal. Confidence: high.
  RageV: Trowbridge-Reitz GGX with alpha = r^2 (glTF convention), divisor floor 1e-9 since RT-21 so polished highlights are not capped. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:3413-3430`
  Top engines: Filament, HDRP, Unreal, OpenPBR: GGX with alpha = r^2. Sources: <https://google.github.io/filament/main/filament.html>; <https://academysoftwarefoundation.github.io/OpenPBR/index.html>
  Why: Same distribution and parameterisation as every shipped engine.
- **Smith masking-shadowing term.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: Three different forms. Analytic lights, DirectTrace and hit specular use a separable Schlick-GGX with Karis's direct-light remap k=(r+1)^2/8. The split-sum table uses a separable Schlick-GGX with k=alpha/2. The reflection trace's sampling density uses the exact GGX G1. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:3433-3444`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:5352-5353`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/direct_trace.rvshader:454-456`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/Cubemap.cpp:252-261`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/reflection_emitters.glsl:62-86`
  Top engines: Filament and HDRP use height-correlated Smith-GGX, one form for direct light and the pre-integrated table. The Karis remap is a UE4-era approximation. Sources: <https://google.github.io/filament/main/filament.html>; <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Unity-Technologies/Graphics/master/Packages/com.unity.render-pipelines.high-definition/Runtime/Material/Lit/Lit.hlsl>
  Why: The reflection's NEE/MIS, the table weight and the lamp highlight are three different BRDFs. The resolve's pairwise MIS (RT-24) and future LTC tables need one consistent BRDF.
- **Energy compensation for rough GGX (multiple scattering).** Comparison: missing. Verified: missing. Confidence: high.
  RageV: None anywhere: no Turquin, Kulla-Conty or Fdez-Aguera term in any shader (grep). Rough metals lose energy in raster, DirectTrace, hits and the split-sum composition alike. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:6250-6251`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:5357-5359`
  Top engines: HDRP (LIT_USE_GGX_ENERGY_COMPENSATION, Turquin 2019) and Filament (1 + f0(1/r - 1) from the DFG table) ship lobe scaling. The Unreal Substrate docs state energy conservation. Sources: <https://blog.selfshadow.com/publications/turquin/ms_comp_final.pdf>; <https://google.github.io/filament/main/filament.html>
  Why: It costs one multiply-add from a table already fetched, and it is the top-dog baseline.
- **Fresnel and F0 source.** Comparison: equal. Verified: equal. Confidence: high.
  RageV: Schlick with F90 = 1 (Pow5). F0 = mix(0.08*specular, albedo, metallic), the same formula in raster, DirectTrace, hits and the reflection trace. Ambient uses Lagarde's roughness-Fresnel for kD. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:3559-3568`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:4515`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:2792-2793`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/direct_trace.rvshader:757`
  Top engines: Schlick in Filament, HDRP and UE. F82-tint in OpenPBR, F0/F90 in Substrate. Sources: none given
  Why: Same model. F82-tint only matters for metals with authored edge colour, which RageV content does not have.
- **Diffuse model and its weighting.** Comparison: equal. Verified: equal. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: Lambert. Raster and DirectTrace weight it by kD=(1-F(VdotH))(1-metallic). Ray hits use albedo*(1-metallic) with no (1-F). The wrap 'subsurface' exists only in the raster loop. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:5363`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/direct_trace.rvshader:461-469`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:2802`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:5510-5511`
  Top engines: Lambert in Unreal (legacy) and Filament; Burley in HDRP; Chan's multiple-scattering diffuse in CoD WWII; EON in OpenPBR (offline). Sources: none given
  Why: Lambert is a shipped top-dog choice. The internal inconsistency (hits lack the (1-F)) is a defect the one-BSDF step removes.
- **Split-sum environment lighting.** Comparison: equal. Verified: equal. Confidence: high.
  RageV: Karis split-sum: a 128x128 R16G16F table integrated on the CPU (512 Hammersley samples), and a GGX-importance-sampled prefiltered cube (6 roughness levels, 128 samples, filtered importance sampling). The mip is linear in perceptual roughness. Code: `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/EnvironmentIBL.cpp:41`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/EnvironmentIBL.cpp:311-316`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/EnvironmentIBL.h:21-25`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/prefilter.rvshader:101-136`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:5920`
  Top engines: The same Karis 2013 split-sum in UE, Filament (DFG table) and HDRP. Sources: none given
  Why: Same method and comparable table sizes. Only the Smith form inside the table needs to follow the BSDF.
- **Minimum roughness floor (0.045).** Comparison: equal. Verified: equal. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: clamp(r, 0.045, 1) in the G-buffer and lit variant, at hits, in macro variation and on the coat. The floor is also the 'no surface' sentinel: the clear is roughness 0, and about 17 sites in 13 shaders test surface.b <= 0. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:4203`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:2794`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:108`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:5374`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/view_reconstruction.glsl:79-89`
  Top engines: Filament clamps perceptual roughness to 0.045 on desktop and 0.089 on mobile, the mobile value documented as an fp16 guard. Sources: none given
  Why: The industry clamps too, so this is not behind the baseline. It breaks the owner's rule 2 (a fixed aliasing guard), and doubling as a sentinel is poor design. Replace it with the footprint filter and a numerical guard, and move the sentinel to the id lane.
- **Geometric specular anti-aliasing from the pixel footprint.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: Tokuyoshi-Kaplanyan 2019 deferred isotropic form, with the paper's constants (sigma^2 = 1/(2 pi), kappa = 0.18), from derivatives of the normal-mapped shading normal, in alpha^2 space. It is applied only to analytic lights: o_Surface keeps the unfiltered roughness for SSR, the traced reflection and the probe term. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:4445-4487`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:4496`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:4506-4507`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/direct_trace.rvshader:739-748`
  Top engines: HDRP applies projected-space filtering to the roughness it writes into the G-buffer, so all lighting uses it; the geometric normal feeds the filter and texture variance is handled separately (vMF). Unreal has the 'Normal Curvature to Roughness' node. Sources: <https://www.jcgt.org/published/0010/02/02/paper.pdf>; <https://docs.unity3d.com/Packages/com.unity.render-pipelines.high-definition@17.3/manual/Geometric-Specular-Anti-Aliasing.html>
  Why: The method is equal. HDRP's coverage is wider: RageV's reflection lobe and probe term still see unfiltered roughness, which is one source of chrome pole edge jitter.
- **Normal-map variance baked into roughness mips (Toksvig / vMF / LEAN).** Comparison: missing. Verified: missing. Confidence: high.
  RageV: The cook box-filters normal maps and renormalises each mip, discarding the shortening that measures variance. Roughness maps are box-filtered in raw perceptual space (BC4). The format is chosen from the file name, and normal and roughness maps are never coupled. Code: `RageV/src/RageV/IO/TextureCook.cpp:297-326`, `RageV/src/RageV/IO/TextureCook.cpp:329-356`, `RageV/src/RageV/IO/TextureCook.cpp:458-482`, `RageV/src/RageV/IO/TextureCook.cpp:499-506`
  Top engines: The Order: 1886 (vMF baked), CoD WWII (gloss from normal length), Unreal Composite Texture, HDRP TextureNormalVariance. Sources: none given
  Why: A distant wet floor with a normal map reads glossier than it should and shimmers. Filtering perceptual roughness linearly is the wrong space.
- **Environment-map level chosen from reflection-vector screen derivatives.** Comparison: better (claimed). Verified: F-OB1, equal (unsupported): an equivalent cheap guard; the same roughness widening ships elsewhere. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: The probe mip is max(roughness level, log2 of the texels per pixel the reflection vector sweeps), so small bright sources in the probe do not alias at silhouettes. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:5895-5921`
  Top engines: Not a standard feature in the phase-1 sources; HDRP and UE pick the mip from roughness. Sources: none given
  Why: Cheap, general, and removes a real aliasing source. Keep it in the composition, where the derivatives must come from the G-buffer's filtered roughness because compute has none.
- **G-buffer lane contract (typing, packing, sRGB).** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: Velocity RG16F; oct normal+roughness+metallic RGBA16F; albedo RGBA8 linear with specular in A; RG32F id lane packing the Static flag in the sign and roughness*65535 + occlusion in one float; an indirect RGBA16F lane written as zero in RT mode. 53 bytes per pixel with colour, OIT and depth. Code: `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:68-82`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:432-433`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:578-594`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:4505-4507`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:5651-5657`
  Top engines: HDRP, Battlefield V and Cyberpunk use about 16 bytes per pixel of colour lanes: sRGB8 base colour, integer or bit feature ids, a separate occlusion channel, no meanings packed into float bits. Sources: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/Unity-Technologies/Graphics/master/Packages/com.unity.render-pipelines.high-definition/Runtime/Material/Lit/Lit.hlsl>; <https://zhangdoa.com/rendering-analysis-cyberpunk-2077/>
  Why: Float-bit packing breaks under any averaging. Linear 8-bit albedo bands in the dark tones. There is no shading-model id. RageV's larger byte count is not the problem: RT-14 measured the G-buffer is not where the memory goes.
- **Normal precision in the G-buffer.** Comparison: better (claimed). Verified: F-OB2, behind: the half-float normal is coarser than HDRP's 12-bit lanes over half its range. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: Octahedral normal in two 16-bit channels (half float). The comments record that 10 bits moved chrome highlights. Code: `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:56-68`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:4501-4503`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/octahedral.glsl`
  Top engines: HDRP 12:12 oct; NRD recommends R10G10B10A2 oct; Battlefield V R10G10B10A2; Cyberpunk xyz 10-bit. Sources: none given
  Why: For mirror-like chrome and puddles the extra precision is visible. Half float is uneven across [0,1], so UNORM16 at the same 8 bytes is better still.
- **Geometric normal lane.** Comparison: missing. Verified: missing. Confidence: high.
  RageV: None. Shadow and reflection rays leave along the normal-mapped shading normal, and mirror curvature is measured from the normal-mapped normal (REFL-04). Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:4490-4496`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_trace.rvshader:665`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/ray_shadow_trace.glsl:237-239`
  Top engines: The RTXDI sample stores a geometric normal in the G-buffer. Production path tracers offset along the geometric normal (Waechter-Binder). Sources: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/NVIDIA-RTX/RTXDI/main/Doc/RtxdiApplicationBridge.md>; <https://link.springer.com/chapter/10.1007/978-1-4842-4427-2_6>
  Why: It is needed for scale-independent ray offsets and for curvature that is not polluted by the normal map.
- **Object identity lanes.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: G-buffer id exact (float32, below 2^24), but reflection and glass histories keep it in RGBA16F, exact only to 2048. CPU-list draws use their pool row + 1, which is renumbered by culling. The ray-instance identity is folded to 1..1021 and drops the entity version. Code: `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:1001-1007`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/Renderer3D.cpp:9177`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/Renderer3D.cpp:9705`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/Renderer3D.cpp:9756`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/Renderer3D.cpp:9839`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/Renderer3D.cpp:9892`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/Renderer3D.cpp:5662-5670`
  Top engines: Unreal's GPU scene gives stable primitive ids. NRD and RTXDI take integer material and instance ids. RTXDI reads the previous-frame G-buffer by stable surface. Sources: none given
  Why: At 60,000 objects only 10.1% of glossy pixels keep reflection history (measured), which fits the half-float arithmetic.
- **Shading-model / material id in the G-buffer.** Comparison: missing. Verified: missing. Confidence: high.
  RageV: None. Clearcoat, sheen, anisotropy and wrap are evaluated only in the raster light loop, and DirectTrace cannot know they exist. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:5326-5392`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/direct_trace.rvshader:453-471`
  Top engines: UE: a 4-bit ShadingModelID. HDRP: a 3-bit feature id. Substrate: tile classification. Sources: none given
  Why: It blocks RT-mode parity for the extended lobes. Latent today: none of the 458 sample materials uses them.
- **MSAA resolve of G-buffer lanes.** Comparison: not comparable. Verified: not comparable. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: Every colour attachment, including the id, velocity and normal lanes, is resolved with AVERAGE; depth with SAMPLE_ZERO. The project default is TAA, so this bites only in MSAA mode. Code: `RageV/src/Platform/Vulkan/VulkanCommandList.cpp:157`, `RageV/src/Platform/Vulkan/VulkanCommandList.cpp:191`
  Top engines: Deferred renderers in UE5 and HDRP do not offer MSAA with their deferred path; MSAA is forward-only there. Vulkan allows SAMPLE_ZERO only for integer formats (VUID-06129/06130, checked in the vendored validusage.json). Sources: none given
  Why: RageV supports a mode the deferred top dogs do not. Its current implementation is wrong at edges. How MSAA should work in RT mode is an owner decision.
- **Composition in RT mode.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: The forward lit shader rasterises every opaque draw a second time, re-samples the material (about 0.6 ms of Headland's 2.2 ms lit pass, measured), composes probes, the field and split-sum per fragment, and writes the reflection weight into the colour alpha. Code: `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:2607-2660`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:5831-5855`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:6250-6260`
  Top engines: DOOM: The Dark Ages composes deferred lighting in compute. So do Unreal (Substrate tiles) and Frostbite (tiled deferred in compute). Sources: <https://developer.microsoft.com/en-us/games/articles/2026/04/variable-rate-compute-shaders-doom-the-dark-ages/>
  Why: The second raster and material evaluation are pure waste in RT mode. The lit pass is 85% pixel-bound (RT-14).
- **Specular demodulation and remodulation for the reflection signal.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: The trace multiplies the picture by reflectance/luminance(reflectance), a hue-only tint. The lit shader sends luminance((F0 A + B) occlusion) through the colour alpha, and the composite multiplies after TAA. The resolve repeats the tint for pooled lamps. There are two shaders, different inputs (8-bit linear G-buffer albedo against full-precision albedo, decoded against interpolated normal), and a measured 7% gap. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_trace.rvshader:857-884`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_resolve.rvshader:447-456`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:6252-6257`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_composite.rvshader:108-110`
  Top engines: NRD (NRD_MaterialFactors) and Snowdrop (demodulate by the pre-integrated BRDF before denoising, remodulate at composition, Ray Tracing Gems ch. 32) use one function with the same inputs on both sides. Sources: <https://raw.githubusercontent.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD/master/README.md>; `build/rt_research/sources/snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:990-1011`
  Why: One full-colour factor from one function with the same inputs settles the 7% by construction. Anything blended into the colour alpha stops being able to corrupt the weight (materials-s1).
- **Diffuse demodulation of the direct signal.** Comparison: equal. Verified: equal. Confidence: high.
  RageV: DirectTrace's diffuse excludes albedo, and the lit pass multiplies by its full-precision albedo. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/direct_trace.rvshader:463-470`
  Top engines: NRD demodulates diffuse by albedo. Sources: none given
  Why: Same pattern.
- **Material source at ray hits.** Comparison: better (claimed). Verified: F-OB3, behind: no normal maps and the sharpest mip at hits, behind Battlefield V and Unreal's hit lighting. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: The real material record (GpuMaterial) and its maps through bindless textures at every hit, with macro variation bit-identical to raster. Hit specular includes lamp highlights, the field-derived lamp and the probe split-sum (RV_HIT_SPECULAR). Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:2757-2798`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:2764-2777`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:3307-3324`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/Material.h:244-275`
  Top engines: Snowdrop's RT world: lower LOD, 'one simple material per mesh, no texture', a simplified BSDF (verified). Metro EE: an average colour per object. Lumen surface cache by default; Hit Lighting costs 11.54 against 2.44 ms on PS5 at 1080p (verified) and 'did not provide a substantial qualitative benefit' in that demo. Battlefield V: hit shaders generated from the raster materials. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:40-58`; `build/rt_research/sources/snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:300-310`; `build/rt_research/sources/lumen_2022.txt:1857-1869`
  Why: Better material fidelity than the average-material engines and the surface cache, near Battlefield V. It is affordable because RageV's materials are 5-8 fetches, not Matrix's dozens of virtual-texture fetches.
- **Detail at ray hits: normal maps, texture LOD, terrain layers, lobes.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: Hits use the interpolated vertex normal (the flat triangle normal for posed meshes), no normal map, mip 0 for every map and the alpha test, Lambert-only for GI and bake (no RV_HIT_SPECULAR), and terrain layer 0 only. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:2717-2744`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:2763-2791`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/ray_shadow_trace.glsl:178-181`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/rtgi_trace.rvshader:40-47`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/irradiance_fill.rvshader:32-33`, `RageV/src/RageV/Scene/Scene.cpp:2937-2948`
  Top engines: Minecraft RTX uses ray cones for mip and shading LOD (Bistro frame 98 to 90 ms with isotropic cones). UE Hit Lighting runs the full material including normal maps. Battlefield V hits used SampleLevel 0. Sources: <https://www.jcgt.org/published/0010/01/01/paper-lowres.pdf>
  Why: Normal-mapped walls reflect flat, distant hits alias and thrash the cache, metals bounce nothing in GI, and terrain is wrong in reflections.
- **Tangent frame.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: No vertex tangent. Raster builds Schueler's cotangent frame from screen derivatives, so hits have no frame at all. Code: `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/Mesh.h:17-22`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:3576-3604`
  Top engines: UE and Unity use MikkTSpace vertex tangents, the convention most bakers write. Sources: none given
  Why: Baked MikkTSpace maps display slightly wrong on a derivative frame. There are no reported artefacts, and the per-triangle equivalent of RageV's frame is cheap to rebuild at hits, which keeps raster-hit parity. Low priority.
- **Raster-to-hit parity test.** Comparison: missing. Verified: missing. Confidence: high.
  RageV: None. Parity is argued in comments, for example the macro variation. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:2764-2777`
  Top engines: Battlefield V traced primary rays and required a zero difference between the hit record and the raster G-buffer. Sources: <https://developer.download.nvidia.com/video/gputechconf/gtc/2019/presentation/s91023-it-just-works-ray-traced-reflections-in-battlefield-v.pdf>
  Why: It is the instrument that keeps the one-material rule honest.
- **Hit-shading coherence (SER, binning, sorted deferred).** Comparison: not comparable. Verified: not comparable. Confidence: low.
  RageV: Inline ray queries in full-screen fragment passes, with hits shaded in place by one uber-function (divergence is in the data, not the code). No binning. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_trace.rvshader:692-702`
  Top engines: Battlefield V binned rays (0.15 ms). Lumen Hit Lighting uses sorted-deferred (trace, sort by material, retrace). Vulkan SER is ray-generation-only. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/lumen_2022.txt:1719-1731`
  Why: Unmeasured whether RageV's few-material uber-shader gains from sorting. It belongs to RT2-20's compute measurement, not assumed.
- **Visibility buffer.** Comparison: not comparable. Verified: not comparable. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: G-buffer raster with the material evaluated in the fragment shader. A visibility buffer is explicitly not the default (RT-SERIES-2 section 7 item 7). Code: `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:1638-1652`
  Top engines: DOOM: The Dark Ages and Nanite. Hable (RTX 3070, 1080p): 21-32% faster than deferred at 1-10 px per triangle, 17% slower with large triangles. Sources: none given
  Why: It depends on content. The garage and camp are large-triangle scenes where Hable's data says the visibility buffer loses.
- **Ray-origin offsets.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: A fixed 0.002 m*(1+min(slope,4)) along the shading normal (G-buffer points) or the vertex normal (hits). RTAO uses kLift/kLiftPerMetre. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:2639-2648`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/ray_shadow_trace.glsl:237-239`
  Top engines: Waechter-Binder (Ray Tracing Gems ch. 6): offsets in integer float ULPs along the geometric normal, independent of scale. Sources: none given
  Why: Metres break the owner's rule 2 and fail far from the origin. Measured harmless in the garage today (T5 parity 0.034 levels).
- **Coat, sheen and anisotropy models.** Comparison: equal. Verified: equal. Confidence: high.
  RageV: Kelemen clearcoat, Charlie sheen with Ashikhmin visibility, and anisotropic GGX (Disney remap), in the raster loop only. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:3486-3538`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:5371-5392`
  Top engines: Filament: the same trio. OpenPBR: fuzz (LTC sheen) and coat with albedo scaling. Sources: none given
  Why: The models are equal. Coverage in RT paths is missing (see the shading-model id).
- **GGX importance sampling for reflection rays.** Comparison: equal. Verified: equal. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: Heitz 2018 visible-normal sampling behind VisibleNormals(), with an exact G1 density. Halton over 64 frames with a per-texel R2 rotation. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/reflection_trace.rvshader:259-316`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/reflection_emitters.glsl:62-86`
  Top engines: Heitz VNDF is the shipped standard. Spherical caps (Dupuy-Benyoub 2023) and bounded VNDF (Eto-Tokuyoshi 2023) are frontier. Sources: none given
  Why: Standard method. The variants belong to the reflections area; the BSDF module must own the sample and pdf so one density is used everywhere.
- **Emissive in the composition.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: Not in the G-buffer. The lit pass re-samples the emissive map and adds surface.Emissive; hits read it from the material. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:6260`
  Top engines: UE's base pass writes emissive (and precomputed light) into SceneColor during the G-buffer pass. Sources: none given
  Why: A compute composition needs emissive without a second material pass. Writing it into the colour attachment during the G-buffer pass costs no lane.
- **Adaptive / layered G-buffer (Substrate) and neural materials.** Comparison: not comparable. Verified: not comparable. Confidence: high.
  RageV: A fixed material block with four lobes and no general layering. Code: `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/Material.h:86-228`
  Top engines: UE Substrate (80 bytes per pixel budget, tile classes). NVIDIA neural materials (demo) and NTC (beta). Sources: none given
  Why: RageV's content uses one standard model. Substrate's bitstream and neural decoding solve problems RageV does not have.

### F.6 Candidates that could beat both

- **F-BB1: One specular anti-aliasing budget used by every consumer. alpha'^2 = alpha^2 (the material, filtered in alpha^2 space) + sigma^2 of the normal map (Toksvig, baked into derived roughness mips at cook) + min(2 sigma_geo^2, kappa). The footprint term comes from derivatives of the smooth vertex normal at raster pixels (Tokuyoshi-Kaplanyan) and from the ray cone's footprint at hits (Akenine-Moeller). The G-buffer stores alpha', and analytic lights, the reflection lobe, the probe term and the composition all read it.**
  Why better than both: HDRP filters at pixels and bakes texture variance but has no ray cones at hits. Minecraft RTX has cones at hits but no pixel filtering. RageV today filters analytic lights only and throws normal-map variance away at cook. One equation across pixels and hits removes specular aliasing at the source for reflections too, which is exactly the no-TAA rule's need on chrome poles and the wet floor. Evidence: Tokuyoshi-Kaplanyan JCGT 2021: RMSE 12631.7 to 0.134 on its alpha=0.01 test, about 0.1 ms on an Intel iGPU. RageV's own record: without the filtered roughness the chrome poles came out three levels dark (direct_trace.rvshader:739-741). Ray cones made the Bistro frame faster (98 to 90 ms, RTX 2080). The owner-swing pole edge-jitter is 9.51 against the 16-ray reference's 8.83. Risk: Over-blur on smooth curved chrome. Silhouette pixels of the poles may widen past the gloss window and fall to the probe, which ties it to RT2-27's tiers. kappa and sigma^2 are the paper's pixel-filter constants and are flagged to the owner. Cost: About 0.05-0.1 ms in the G-buffer pass; a few ALU per hit; cook time for derived roughness mips.
  The comparison's gate: With --aa=none at the owner swing (spin_measure.py): pole edge-jitter at most 8.83 and speckle_count at most 494 dots over 32 levels. Diff image against a 16x SSAA reference of a slow orbit near zero on the poles and puddle. sharp_study.py tube sharpness on the puddle against the RT2-2 reference renderer. G-buffer pass cost at most +0.1 ms at 2560x1600 (A,B,B,A).
  Verification: **plausible.** 'HDRP has no ray cones at hits' is false. HDRP has ray-cone texture LOD at hits but compiles geometric specular AA out of the RT stage, so the novel part is feeding the cone into the roughness, not the cone itself. That HDRP bakes texture variance is unverified: TextureNormalVariance exists in the core library, but its use by Lit was not confirmed. RMSE 12631.7 to 0.134 is the non-axis-aligned projected-space filter in Figure 1, not the normal-based isotropic filter RageV uses. The pole edge-jitter 9.51 against 8.83 is not shown to be specular aliasing (RT-SERIES-2.md:2319 attributes edge jitter at speed to taa_resolve's depth-tolerance rejection). It needs the --aa=none gate as designed. Ray-cone Bistro timings and the Minecraft RTX statement were not verified.
  In the roadmap: RT2-21 step 21c.
- **F-BB2: Parity by construction: one SampleMaterial(inputs, footprint) function feeds the raster G-buffer write and every ray hit (reflection, GI, bake, water and glass hits), with a zero-difference test in scenetest. The test traces primary rays through the hit path using analytic ray differentials, so the hit reproduces raster's texture gradients, and diffs the decoded lanes against the G-buffer.**
  Why better than both: Battlefield V needed a shader generator and got parity only at mip 0 (hits used SampleLevel 0). Unreal's hit lighting diverges by design (the Ray Tracing Quality Switch, and the surface cache approximation). RageV's inline ray queries make a hit a plain function call in the same GLSL, so parity is free and holds with texture LOD. Evidence: RageV already shares ApplyMacro bit-identically between raster and hits (pbr_fragment.glsl:2764-2777) and reaches the full material record at hits (Material.h:244-275). The Battlefield V GTC 2019 slides describe the zero-difference test. Risk: The position loads that were removed for cost come back for textured hits. Cost: A refactor with no runtime cost. Loading triangle positions at textured hits (for the cone and the frame) is measured.
  The comparison's gate: Primary-ray parity test on the garage, the bridge's three cameras and the camp: the maximum decoded difference is at most one quantisation step per lane (albedo 1/255, roughness 1/65535, normal at most 0.01 degrees), with parallax as the only documented exception. Reflection and GI trace ms within +10% of before (A,B,B,A); the owner sets the final bar.
  Verification: **plausible.** Parity is not 'free' with texture LOD. Ray-differential gradients through textureGrad do not match hardware quad derivatives or anisotropic filtering bit-for-bit, which the design's own gate concedes with 'at most one quantisation step'. Today's hits are far from parity (no normal map, mip 0), so the claim is a target, not a present strength.
  In the roadmap: RT2-21 step 21d.
- **F-BB3: Split geometry normals in the G-buffer: the smooth, interpolated vertex normal (before the normal map) for mirror curvature and the reflection accumulator's facing and plane tests, and the triangle's face normal (from dFdx/dFdy of the world position, exact inside a triangle) for ray-origin offsets. Both are octahedral 16:16 in one RGBA16_UNORM lane that replaces the dead indirect lane in the RT recipe.**
  Why better than both: NRD-style curvature comes from the provided (usually normal-mapped) normal, which is REFL-04's bug: a bumpy wet floor reads as a curved mirror. Engines without a geometric lane offset along the shading normal. Face normals alone would make faceted chrome poles read as flat mirrors; the smooth normal alone cannot bound ray offsets. Evidence: REFL-04 arithmetic: at 11 m a one-degree normal-map difference reads as about 0.6/m of curvature and collapses the image distance. The lane costs zero net bytes because the RT recipe's indirect lane is written as zero and read by nothing (pbr_fragment.glsl:5651-5657). Risk: Derivatives across quad helper lanes at triangle edges. The face normal is exact inside a triangle because position is linear there; edge pixels need a check in the acne fixture. Cost: Zero net bytes; a few ALU in the G-buffer pass.
  The comparison's gate: The reflection-image-distance debug view on the wet floor matches the flat-plane distance (camera-to-floor mirror geometry) within 5%, and the pole's curvature has the right sign. RT-24 ghost scores (spin_measure.py --stage=many16, pole and bumper spots) no worse. The acne fixture at the origin and at 10 km shows zero self-shadowed texels. G-buffer pass ms unchanged within the spread.
  Verification: **plausible.** The 'zero net bytes' premise is only as good as F-C8 (it holds only with ray GI on). The motivating defect is unmeasured, and the RT-SERIES-2 plan's step G (reflection-image debug view) must confirm it first. The position derivatives lose precision far from the origin in absolute world coordinates (bridge scene), so derive them camera-relative. That NRD and other engines offset along the shading normal was not verified.
  In the roadmap: RT2-21 step 21a.
- **F-BB4: EON (energy-preserving Oren-Nayar, JCGT 2025) as the diffuse lobe in the one BSDF module, tried as a measured arm against the Lambert baseline across raster, hits, GI and the bake.**
  Why better than both: Lambert (UE legacy, Filament) has no roughness response. Burley (HDRP) is not energy-conserving without renormalisation. EON passes the white furnace test, comes with exact CLTC sampling for the GI trace, and is OpenPBR's choice. Evidence: JCGT 14(1) 2025 white-furnace analysis with GLSL supplied. No real-time ms published. Risk: A visible look change on rough surfaces, and the probe and bake integration must switch together. Adopt only on the owner's judgement, because Lambert is the shipped baseline. Cost: ALU only; unmeasured.
  The comparison's gate: Diffuse white furnace (albedo 1, roughness 0-1) at 1.0 within 2% in every path. Composition, DirectTrace and GI trace ms within +0.05 ms each (A,B,B,A). Diff images of the garage concrete and the camp, judged by the owner. The bake is re-baked and diffed.
  Verification: **plausible.** EON's roughness is a separate diffuse roughness (OpenPBR base_diffuse_roughness), not the GGX specular roughness. RageV materials have no such parameter, so the arm needs either a new material field or a stated mapping, and a mapping is a look choice the owner must judge.
  In the roadmap: section 7, item 50 (needs a diffuse-roughness input).
- **F-BB5: The octahedral normal lanes stored as RGBA16_UNORM instead of RGBA16F, in the same 8 bytes.**
  Why better than both: Half float's step is uneven across [0,1] (about 2^-11 near 1). UNORM16 is uniform at 2^-16, so about 30x finer in the worst case, against the industry's 10-12-bit lanes (NRD, Battlefield V, HDRP) and RageV's own half floats. Evidence: The in-code record says 10 bits moved chrome highlights (pbr_fragment.glsl:4501-4503; FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:56-68). A 1 mrad normal error moves a reflection 4 cm at 20 m. Risk: UNORM lanes still cannot be resolved by SAMPLE_ZERO under MSAA (VUID-06129), which is the owner's MSAA decision. Cost: None.
  The comparison's gate: A curved chrome pole's reflection diffed against a float32-normal build: at most 1 display level with UNORM16, measured against the RGBA16F build's diff. G-buffer ms unchanged.
  Verification: **plausible.** The cited in-code record is misattributed. The '10 bits moved chrome highlights' comment is about roughness packing, and the normal-format record (FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:56-68) is about the S5 neighbour test. Justify the change by the precision arithmetic plus the planned chrome-pole gate. Colour-attachment and blend support for R16G16B16A16_UNORM on the target devices was not checked here.
  In the roadmap: RT2-21 step 21a.

The owner-swing pole edge-jitter is 9.51 against the 16-ray reference's 8.83. |
| F-BB2 | beats_both | major | plausible | RageV already shares ApplyMacro bit-identically between raster and hits (pbr_fragment.glsl:2764-2777) and reaches the full material record at hits (Material.h:244-275). The Battlefield V GTC 2019 slides describe the zero-difference test. |
| F-BB3 | beats_both | major | plausible | REFL-04 arithmetic: at 11 m a one-degree normal-map difference reads as about 0.6/m of curvature and collapses the image distance. The lane costs zero net bytes because the RT recipe's indirect lane is written as zero and read by nothing (pbr_fragment.glsl:5651-5657). |
| F-BB4 | beats_both | major | plausible | JCGT 14(1) 2025 white-furnace analysis with GLSL supplied. No real-time ms published. |
| F-BB5 | beats_both | major | plausible | The in-code record says 10 bits moved chrome highlights (pbr_fragment.glsl:4501-4503; FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:56-68). A 1 mrad normal error moves a reflection 4 cm at 20 m. |

- **F-C1** (confirmed). Claim: The 0.045 roughness clamp doubles as the 'no surface' sentinel: the G-buffer clear stores roughness 0, and readers test surface.b <= 0. Removing the floor without moving the sentinel breaks about 17 sites in 13 shaders.
  Evidence: view_reconstruction.glsl:79-89 SurfaceIsEmpty returns surface.b <= 0.0, and the comment says it relies on the PBR 0.045 clamp. pbr_fragment.glsl:4203 clamp(surface.Roughness, 0.045, 1.0), written to o_Surface.b at 4496. FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:1643/2613/3748 clear the normal lane to Vec4(0). Grep finds 15 test sites plus the definition, in 13 files: direct_trace:678, importance_tiles:107, rtgi_trace:287, rtao_compute:286, reflection_blur:190,198, reflection_accumulate:509,516, reflection_resolve:143, reflection_trace:606,635, ssao_compute:198, ssgi_compute:227, ssr_trace:160, voxelgi_gather:137. Other 0.045 clamps are at pbr_fragment.glsl:108,131,2794,4380,4386,4414,5374.
  Correction: The exact count is 16 lines (15 tests plus the definition) in 12 shaders plus the include, so 'about 17 in 13' is close. The zero is not only the clear: sky, grid, particle, light_glow, ui_world, quad and debug all write o_Surface = vec4(0) in the Scene pass. A sentinel moved to the G-buffer id lane will not see those forward draws. Wherever a draw is not additive (particles, grid, world UI), the set of 'empty' pixels can change, so the diff-0 gate for moving the sentinel has to cover them.
- **F-C2** (confirmed). Claim: The Tokuyoshi-Kaplanyan filtered roughness reaches only analytic lights (the id lane's .g to DirectTrace). The traced reflection, the probe term and SSR read the unfiltered roughness from o_Surface.
  Evidence: pbr_fragment.glsl:4462-4487: the comment says 'Analytic lights only. o_Surface keeps the unfiltered value'. 4496 writes o_Surface with the raw roughness and 4506-4507 puts shadingRoughness in the id lane's .g. Probe: 5920 lod = roughness*Environment.y, and 6250 EnvBRDF(NdotV, roughness), both unfiltered. reflection_trace.rvshader:641 reads roughness = surface.b. direct_trace.rvshader:748 p.Roughness comes from the id lane, while 737 RawRoughness (surface.b) feeds the ambient Fresnel at 1391.
  Correction: The filtered value reaches more than DirectTrace. The forward lit loop uses it directly for every analytic light (specRoughness = shadingRoughness, 5182), and so does the field-derived baked-lamp highlight (5805-5806). It is still analytic-light-only in kind. The id lane is how it reaches the RT-mode DirectTrace.
- **F-C3** (confirmed). Claim: Three Smith forms are in use: Schlick-GGX k=(r+1)^2/8 for lights and hits, k=alpha/2 in the split-sum table, and the exact GGX G1 in the reflection sampling density.
  Evidence: pbr_fragment.glsl:3433-3444 has GeometrySchlickGGX k=(r+1)^2/8, used by the lit loop (5352), the hit lamp highlights (3139, 3165), the hit derived lamp (3297) and DirectTrace (direct_trace:455-456, 1381). Cubemap.cpp:252-261 GeometrySmithIBL k=roughness^2/2 (= alpha/2) feeds IntegrateEnvironmentBRDF (EnvironmentIBL.cpp:311), which the lit shader reads through u_BRDF (EnvBRDF 3407). reflection_emitters.glsl:79-84 uses the exact GGX G1 in the VNDF LobePdf.
  Correction: Besides these three GGX forms there are Kelemen (coat, 5376), Ashikhmin (sheen, 5390) and Beckmann G1 (water). The unified module must keep or deliberately replace them.
- **F-C4** (confirmed). Claim: No GGX energy compensation exists anywhere in the shaders.
  Evidence: Grepping the shaders for energy comp, multiscat, Turquin, Kulla, Ess, Fms and energy bias/scale finds only the Kulla citation in the Charlie sheen comment (pbr_fragment.glsl:3504). Every split-sum use is F0*envBRDF.x + envBRDF.y with no 1/E factor (3323, 6251, 6257, 6313).
- **F-C5** (confirmed). Claim: Hit diffuse is albedo*(1-metallic) with no (1-F), while raster and DirectTrace use kD = (1-F)(1-metallic).
  Evidence: pbr_fragment.glsl:2802 vec3 diffuse = albedo * (1.0 - metallic), used unchanged in the hit light loop (3131, 3160) and the hit ambient (surface.Diffuse * (ambientLight + arriving), 3326). With RV_HIT_SPECULAR the hit specular is added on top, so the hit double-counts at grazing angles. Raster: 5363 and 5833 kD = (1-F)(1-metallic). direct_trace.rvshader:461 does the same.
  Correction: RV_HIT_SPECULAR is defined only in reflection_trace and water_trace. GI and bake hits have neither the specular term nor the (1-F).
- **F-C6** (confirmed). Claim: The texture cook renormalises normal mips (discarding variance) and box-filters roughness maps in raw perceptual space, with no coupling of normal to roughness maps.
  Evidence: TextureCook.cpp:297-326 is a 2x2 box filter. 328-356 RenormalizeNormals rebuilds z from the averaged x,y and normalises, so the shortened length (the variance) is thrown away. BC5 stores only xy anyway. 458-482: the format and filter space come from the file name. Maps matching _roughness, _metallic and similar go to BC4 with srgbFilter=false (raw). Nothing couples normal maps to roughness maps.
  Correction: Two edge cases. A roughness map whose name misses the case-sensitive suffix list is cooked as BC1 and filtered after sRGB linearisation, which is not perceptual space. The uncooked fallback ('loaded from source', ImportCache.cpp:265) gets GPU blit mips (VulkanResources.cpp:471), with no normal renormalisation at all. The design's per-material derived chain is also a new asset kind: today the cook is per texture file, keyed by name, not per material pair.
- **F-C7** (confirmed). Claim: Today's scene target is 53 bytes a pixel (colour 8, OIT 9, velocity 4, normal 8, indirect 8, albedo 4, id 8, depth 4), and the proposed RT contract is byte-neutral because the geometry lane replaces the RT-mode indirect lane.
  Evidence: FrameGraphBuilder.cpp: colour RGBA16F (432) = 8; OIT RGBA16F+R8 (510) = 9; velocity RG16F (553) = 4; normal RGBA16F (68, 578) = 8; indirect RGBA16F (73, 590) = 8; albedo RGBA8 (78) = 4; id RG32F (82) = 8; D32 (433) = 4. The total is 53. The proposed lanes also sum to 53.
  Correction: 53 holds only when the transparent pass exists (wantTransparent). Without it the figure is 44. MSAA multiplies every lane. Byte-neutrality depends on the indirect lane really being dead, which F-C8 limits.
- **F-C8** (partly true). Claim: The indirect lane is written as zero and unread in RT mode, but read in raster mode by the screen-space GI subtraction, so only the RT recipe can drop it.
  Evidence: pbr_fragment.glsl:5639-5658 writes o_Indirect = 0 only under #ifdef RV_RAY_GI. 5871-5884 (#ifndef RV_RAY_GI) writes the SSGI subtraction value. The only reader is the SSGI compute pass (FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:4357-4363), which runs when !rayGi. RV_RAY_GI is defined when RayGlobalIlluminationOn (Renderer3D.cpp:2016). rayGi = giDetail != Off && CanTraceGlobalIllumination (823), and ResolveRayTracedGlobalIllumination (326-351) can return Off with ray tracing on (--rt-gi=off, settings Off, or no bindless).
  Correction: The lane is dead only when ray-traced GI is on, not in 'RT mode' as such. With RT reflections or direct light but GI Off, voxel or screen-space, the lit shader writes the SSGI subtraction and SSGI reads it. So the RT recipe can drop the lane only if it requires ray GI, or treats SSGI as a raster-recipe feature. The comments at FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:578-588 say the attachment count is fixed across six renderers, the UI world layer and the probe face, so a per-recipe count has to reach all of them.
- **F-C9** (confirmed). Claim: The reflection history keeps the object id in RGBA16F; CPU-list draws take pool row + 1; the ray-instance identity folds to 1..1021 without the version.
  Evidence: FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:1001-1007: the RT-6.5 object-id history is R16G16B16A16_SFLOAT. reflection_accumulate.rvshader:1390 g_ObjectId = u_SurfaceId.r and 1951/1976 o_Ident = vec4(g_ObjectId, ...). Renderer3D.cpp:9705/9756/9839/9892: instance.Extra.x = (&instance - Instances.data() + 1), which is the per-frame pool row. 5662-5670 row.Identity = ((Owner & 0xFFFFF) % 1021) + 1, or 1022 when there is no entity.
  Correction: The consequence is worse than precision alone. The pool row is not stable from frame to frame (the order of submission), and above 2048 rows a half float can no longer tell neighbouring ids apart, so SameObject (318-319) can both reject a true match and accept a false one.
- **F-C10** (confirmed). Claim: Lumen: on PS5 at 1080p, reflection tracing costs 2.44 ms with HWRT + Surface Cache against 11.54 ms with HWRT + Hit Lighting. Hit Lighting 'did not provide a substantial qualitative benefit' in Matrix Awakens, partial dynamic evaluation was 'just as expensive as full', and Hit Lighting uses sorted-deferred (sort and retrace).
  Evidence: lumen_2022.txt:1690-1699 says partial dynamic evaluation was just as expensive as full. 1700-1731: two modes, and Hit Lighting uses a modified sorted-deferred pipeline (screen trace, trace + surface cache, sort, retrace + hit lighting), exposed for reflections only at the time. 1850-1869: PS5 1080p reflection tracing 2.44 ms (surface cache) against 11.54 ms (hit lighting), with no substantial qualitative benefit.
  Correction: The 'no qualitative benefit' is tied to that demo: the talk puts it down to the strong SkyLight dependence and both modes fetching it from the surface cache. It is not a general verdict on hit lighting.
- **F-C11** (confirmed). Claim: Snowdrop's ray-tracing world uses lower LOD (LOD1-2), one simple untextured material per mesh and a simplified BSDF (no hair, cloth, clearcoat). Screen-space hits read the raster G-buffer, and reflections are demodulated per Ray Tracing Gems ch. 32.
  Evidence: snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:40-58: lower LOD (LOD1-2), one simple material per mesh, no textures, dithered alpha, terrain ray-marched separately with textures. 295-310: a simplified BSDF (Lambert + GGX), no hair, cloth or clearcoat. 565-580: screen-space hits sample the G-buffer, and bright emissive hits are checked against the RT world's averaged material. 990-1011: reflections demodulated before denoising, citing Ray Tracing Gems ch. 32 and Heitz VNDF.
  Correction: The simplified BSDF is described for probe (GI) hit lighting, not explicitly for reflections.
- **F-C12** (confirmed). Claim: HDRP writes the geometric-specular-AA-filtered roughness into the G-buffer, so all lighting (including ray-traced reflections) uses it.
  Evidence: Unity Graphics master, HDRP Runtime/Material/Lit/LitData.hlsl: under #if defined(_ENABLE_GEOMETRIC_SPECULAR_AA) && !defined(SHADER_STAGE_RAY_TRACING), surfaceData.perceptualSmoothness = GeometricNormalFiltering(..., input.tangentToWorld[2], _SpecularAAScreenSpaceVariance, _SpecularAAThreshold), before encoding. Core CommonMaterial.hlsl NormalFiltering: roughness^2 + min(2*variance, threshold^2).
  Correction: Three caveats. It is a per-material opt-in with artist variance and threshold values, not fixed constants. It uses the geometric (vertex) normal, which supports the design's smooth-normal choice. It is explicitly compiled out in the ray-tracing stage, so HDRP hits get no footprint filtering.
- **F-C13** (confirmed). Claim: UE5's deferred path and HDRP's deferred path do not support MSAA (forward only).
  Evidence: Unity HDRP 14 manual, Anti-Aliasing page: HDRP supports MSAA only for forward rendering, and ray tracing does not support MSAA. Epic's 'Anti-Aliasing and Upscaling' page: MSAA is only available with the Forward Renderer on desktop/console.
- **F-C14** (partly true). Claim: Filament applies its energy-compensation factor to the IBL specular as well as to direct lights. Its desktop MIN_PERCEPTUAL_ROUGHNESS of 0.045 is a precision guard, not an aliasing fix.
  Evidence: filament shaders/src/surface_light_indirect.fs evaluateIBL has 'Fr *= pixel.energyCompensation;' (the first half is confirmed). shaders/src/surface_material.fs: mobile MIN_PERCEPTUAL_ROUGHNESS 0.089 is commented as an fp16 precision limit, and the desktop 0.045 has no comment. The Filament document gives the fp16 reason for clamping and also says the clamp reduces specular aliasing at low roughness and keeps a minimum highlight size.
  Correction: Filament documents two reasons for the roughness clamp, precision and specular aliasing, so 'not an aliasing fix' is wrong. The desktop 0.045 has no stated derivation. Removing RageV's floor removes an aliasing guard as well as a sentinel, so the footprint filter must fully replace it, which the design's --aa=none gates must show.
- **F-C15** (confirmed). Claim: Vulkan permits only NONE, AVERAGE or CUSTOM resolves for non-integer (including UNORM) colour formats, and SAMPLE_ZERO only for integer formats.
  Evidence: RageV/vendor/Vulkan-Headers/registry/validusage.json. VUID-VkRenderingAttachmentInfo-imageView-06129: non-integer colour format, resolveMode NONE, CUSTOM_BIT_EXT or AVERAGE. -06130: integer colour format, NONE, CUSTOM_BIT_EXT or SAMPLE_ZERO.
  Correction: This applies to dynamic rendering (VkRenderingAttachmentInfo). CUSTOM needs VK_EXT_custom_resolve.
- **F-C16** (partly true). Claim: The Tokuyoshi-Kaplanyan deferred listing uses SIGMA2 = 0.15915494 and KAPPA = 0.18, matching RageV's constants.
  Evidence: The cited I3D 2019 PDF (Square Enix) gives kappa = 0.18 but defines the pixel filter with sigma = 0.5 pixels, so sigma^2 = 0.25; it has no 0.15915494. The JCGT 10(2) 2021 follow-up (Tokuyoshi & Kaplanyan, 'Stable Geometric Specular Antialiasing with Projected-Space NDF Filtering') has 'float SIGMA2 = 0.15915494; float KAPPA = 0.18;' in its deferred IsotropicNDFFiltering listings. RageV pbr_fragment.glsl:4474-4486 uses 0.15915494 and 0.18 with the ddx/ddy sum form.
  Correction: The constants match JCGT 2021, not the cited 2019 paper. Phase 2 calls it 'Tokuyoshi-Kaplanyan 2019 ... the paper's sigma^2 = 1/(2 pi)', and that citation is wrong. RageV implements the plain derivative-sum listing, not the ddx_fine/neighbouring-direction variant of the same paper.
- **F-OB1** (unsupported). Claim: RageV is BETTER than the top engines at: Environment-map level chosen from reflection-vector screen derivatives. Ours: The probe mip is max(roughness level, log2 of the texels per pixel the reflection vector sweeps), so small bright sources in the probe do not alias at silhouettes. Theirs: Not a standard feature in the phase-1 sources; HDRP and UE pick the mip from roughness. Why: Cheap, general, and removes a real aliasing source. Keep it in the composition, where the derivatives must come from the G-buffer's filtered roughness because compute has none.
  Evidence: The code half is confirmed: pbr_fragment.glsl:5895-5921, lod = max(roughness*Environment.y, log2(texelsPerPixel)) from dFdx/dFdy of the reflection vector, in the forward lit pass, which is live in RT mode because the probe is still blended with the traced share. The industry half rests only on absence from the phase-1 sources. Shipped engines address the same silhouette sweep by widening roughness from normal curvature (HDRP geometric specular AA written to the G-buffer, F-C12; Unreal's normal-curvature-to-roughness). Hardware implicit-LOD cube sampling does this automatically.
  Correction: This is not shown to be 'better'. It is an equivalent, cheap guard. The composition note is also wrong: in compute the reflection-vector derivatives can be finite-differenced from neighbouring G-buffer normals and depth (RageV already does neighbour differencing for Curvature()), so they need not come from filtered roughness. Filtered roughness caps at kappa and would not reproduce the max() term.
- **F-OB2** (refuted). Claim: RageV is BETTER than the top engines at: Normal precision in the G-buffer. Ours: Octahedral normal in two 16-bit channels (half float). The comments record that 10 bits moved chrome highlights. Theirs: HDRP 12:12 oct; NRD recommends R10G10B10A2 oct; Battlefield V R10G10B10A2; Cyberpunk xyz 10-bit. Why: For mirror-like chrome and puddles the extra precision is visible. Half float is uneven across [0,1], so UNORM16 at the same 8 bytes is better still.
  Evidence: FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:56-68: the move from 8-bit to RGBA16F normals was made because 8 bits broke the S5 neighbour normal test, not because of chrome highlights. The 'ten bits were coarse enough to move chrome's highlights' comment (pbr_fragment.glsl:4501-4503) is about the analytic-light roughness packed into the id lane, not the normal. octahedral.glsl maps the normal into [0,1]. A half float in [0.5,1) has a step of 2^-11 (about 4.9e-4). HDRP's 12:12 octahedral step is 1/4095 (about 2.4e-4).
  Correction: RageV's RGBA16F octahedral normal is coarser than HDRP's 12:12 over half the octahedral square (about 11 effective bits) and finer only than 10-bit lanes. It is not better than the top engines. The advantage exists only after the proposed UNORM16 change (F-BB5). The chrome-highlight evidence belongs to roughness precision.
- **F-OB3** (refuted). Claim: RageV is BETTER than the top engines at: Material source at ray hits. Ours: The real material record (GpuMaterial) and its maps through bindless textures at every hit, with macro variation bit-identical to raster. Hit specular includes lamp highlights, the field-derived lamp and the probe split-sum (RV_HIT_SPECULAR). Theirs: Snowdrop's RT world: lower LOD, 'one simple material per mesh, no texture', a simplified BSDF (verified). Metro EE: an average colour per object. Lumen surface cache by default; Hit Lighting costs 11.54 against 2.44 ms on PS5 at 1080p (verified) and 'did not provide a substantial qualitative benefit' in that demo. Battlefield V: hit shaders generated from the raster materials. Why: Better material fidelity than the average-material engines and the surface cache, near Battlefield V. It is affordable because RageV's materials are 5-8 fetches, not Matrix's dozens of virtual-texture fetches.
  Evidence: pbr_fragment.glsl:2757-2798: hits read the GpuMaterial record and maps, but every map is textureLod(..., 0.0) (mip 0, so it aliases under minification). The normal is the interpolated vertex normal or the face normal, with no normal map (2717-2744). ApplyMacro is shared (2777). RV_HIT_SPECULAR is only in reflection_trace and water_trace, and GI and bake hits are Lambert without a roughness read. Snowdrop and the Lumen surface cache (F-C10, F-C11) are confirmed as simpler. But Battlefield V hit shaders are generated from the raster materials (by the claim's own statement), and UE's Hit Lighting is a shipped option that evaluates full materials.
  Correction: RageV is richer than average-material RT worlds (Snowdrop) and the default surface cache, and below Battlefield V and UE Hit Lighting: no normal maps, mip 0 only, terrain layer 0, no specular on GI hits. 'Better than the top engines' does not hold. At best it is 'above the cheap defaults, behind full-material hit shading'. Metro EE's 'average colour per object' was not verified from a primary source.
- **F-BB1** (plausible). Claim: Could beat both RageV and the shipped baseline: One specular anti-aliasing budget used by every consumer. alpha'^2 = alpha^2 (the material, filtered in alpha^2 space) + sigma^2 of the normal map (Toksvig, baked into derived roughness mips at cook) + min(2 sigma_geo^2, kappa). The footprint term comes from derivatives of the smooth vertex normal at raster pixels (Tokuyoshi-Kaplanyan) and from the ray cone's footprint at hits (Akenine-Moeller). The G-buffer stores alpha', and analytic lights, the reflection lobe, the probe term and the composition all read it.. Why: HDRP filters at pixels and bakes texture variance but has no ray cones at hits. Minecraft RTX has cones at hits but no pixel filtering. RageV today filters analytic lights only and throws normal-map variance away at cook. One equation across pixels and hits removes specular aliasing at the source for reflections too, which is exactly the no-TAA rule's need on chrome poles and the wet floor.
  Evidence: Supporting: RageV already ships the pixel filter for analytic lights only (F-C2), and the cook discards variance (F-C6). The JCGT 2021 costs hold: about 0.1 ms for normal-based isotropic filtering, Table 3, forward shading on Intel Iris Plus. direct_trace.rvshader:739-741 records that the chrome poles came out three levels dark without the filtered roughness. Contradicting: HDRP's RaytracingIntersection.hlsl defines struct RayCone {width, spreadAngle} with USE_RAY_CONE_LOD and triangle/UV areas for texture LOD at hits.
  Correction: 'HDRP has no ray cones at hits' is false. HDRP has ray-cone texture LOD at hits but compiles geometric specular AA out of the RT stage, so the novel part is feeding the cone into the roughness, not the cone itself. That HDRP bakes texture variance is unverified: TextureNormalVariance exists in the core library, but its use by Lit was not confirmed. RMSE 12631.7 to 0.134 is the non-axis-aligned projected-space filter in Figure 1, not the normal-based isotropic filter RageV uses. The pole edge-jitter 9.51 against 8.83 is not shown to be specular aliasing (RT-SERIES-2.md:2319 attributes edge jitter at speed to taa_resolve's depth-tolerance rejection). It needs the --aa=none gate as designed. Ray-cone Bistro timings and the Minecraft RTX statement were not verified.
- **F-BB2** (plausible). Claim: Could beat both RageV and the shipped baseline: Parity by construction: one SampleMaterial(inputs, footprint) function feeds the raster G-buffer write and every ray hit (reflection, GI, bake, water and glass hits), with a zero-difference test in scenetest. The test traces primary rays through the hit path using analytic ray differentials, so the hit reproduces raster's texture gradients, and diffs the decoded lanes against the G-buffer.. Why: Battlefield V needed a shader generator and got parity only at mip 0 (hits used SampleLevel 0). Unreal's hit lighting diverges by design (the Ray Tracing Quality Switch, and the surface cache approximation). RageV's inline ray queries make a hit a plain function call in the same GLSL, so parity is free and holds with texture LOD.
  Evidence: ApplyMacro is shared between raster and hits (pbr_fragment.glsl:2777), and hits reach the full GpuMaterial through bindless (2757). Inline ray queries make a hit a GLSL call in the same module, which is architecturally true. The Battlefield V GTC 2019 zero-difference test and 'SampleLevel 0 at hits' were not verified from a primary source.
  Correction: Parity is not 'free' with texture LOD. Ray-differential gradients through textureGrad do not match hardware quad derivatives or anisotropic filtering bit-for-bit, which the design's own gate concedes with 'at most one quantisation step'. Today's hits are far from parity (no normal map, mip 0), so the claim is a target, not a present strength.
- **F-BB3** (plausible). Claim: Could beat both RageV and the shipped baseline: Split geometry normals in the G-buffer: the smooth, interpolated vertex normal (before the normal map) for mirror curvature and the reflection accumulator's facing and plane tests, and the triangle's face normal (from dFdx/dFdy of the world position, exact inside a triangle) for ray-origin offsets. Both are octahedral 16:16 in one RGBA16_UNORM lane that replaces the dead indirect lane in the RT recipe.. Why: NRD-style curvature comes from the provided (usually normal-mapped) normal, which is REFL-04's bug: a bumpy wet floor reads as a curved mirror. Engines without a geometric lane offset along the shading normal. Face normals alone would make faceted chrome poles read as flat mirrors; the smooth normal alone cannot bound ray offsets.
  Evidence: REFL-04 (RT-SERIES-2-FINDINGS.md:1777-1792): Curvature() reads the normal-mapped lane. The arithmetic checks: 1 degree over 2 x 1.4 cm is about 0.62/m. The finding's own skeptic note says the consequence is unmeasured. The face normal from dFdx/dFdy of the position is exact for a planar triangle inside a quad.
  Correction: The 'zero net bytes' premise is only as good as F-C8 (it holds only with ray GI on). The motivating defect is unmeasured, and the RT-SERIES-2 plan's step G (reflection-image debug view) must confirm it first. The position derivatives lose precision far from the origin in absolute world coordinates (bridge scene), so derive them camera-relative. That NRD and other engines offset along the shading normal was not verified.
- **F-BB4** (plausible). Claim: Could beat both RageV and the shipped baseline: EON (energy-preserving Oren-Nayar, JCGT 2025) as the diffuse lobe in the one BSDF module, tried as a measured arm against the Lambert baseline across raster, hits, GI and the bake.. Why: Lambert (UE legacy, Filament) has no roughness response. Burley (HDRP) is not energy-conserving without renormalisation. EON passes the white furnace test, comes with exact CLTC sampling for the GI trace, and is OpenPBR's choice.
  Evidence: EON (JCGT 14(1) 2025) is a published energy-preserving Oren-Nayar with a furnace analysis. It is proposed only as a measured arm, and the phase-2 BSDF module keeps Lambert as the baseline. No real-time cost is published.
  Correction: EON's roughness is a separate diffuse roughness (OpenPBR base_diffuse_roughness), not the GGX specular roughness. RageV materials have no such parameter, so the arm needs either a new material field or a stated mapping, and a mapping is a look choice the owner must judge.
- **F-BB5** (plausible). Claim: Could beat both RageV and the shipped baseline: The octahedral normal lanes stored as RGBA16_UNORM instead of RGBA16F, in the same 8 bytes.. Why: Half float's step is uneven across [0,1] (about 2^-11 near 1). UNORM16 is uniform at 2^-16, so about 30x finer in the worst case, against the industry's 10-12-bit lanes (NRD, Battlefield V, HDRP) and RageV's own half floats.
  Evidence: The arithmetic holds: a half float in [0.5,1) steps 2^-11, UNORM16 steps 2^-16, a ratio of 32 at the same 8 bytes. A 1 mrad normal error gives a 2 mrad reflection error, about 4 cm at 20 m.
  Correction: The cited in-code record is misattributed. The '10 bits moved chrome highlights' comment is about roughness packing, and the normal-format record (FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:56-68) is about the S5 neighbour test. Justify the change by the precision arithmetic plus the planned chrome-pole gate. Colour-attachment and blend support for R16G16B16A16_UNORM on the target devices was not checked here.

### F.8 Design changes the verification required

Each was applied to the roadmap's items on 2026-09-25 (section 9 lists them).

- **G-buffer contract (RT recipe), described once as data.** Make the geometry lane's replacement of the indirect lane conditional on ray-traced GI. Either the RT recipe requires rayGi, or SSGI and voxel GI are declared raster-recipe only. State that 53 bytes assumes the transparent pass (44 without) and scales with MSAA. Justify UNORM16 normals by the S5 test record and precision arithmetic, not by a chrome-highlight record (that one is about roughness), and add a format-feature check. For the sentinel move to id == 0, list the seven non-PBR shaders that write o_Surface = 0 in the Scene pass and make the diff-0 gate cover pixels where particles, grid or world UI cover a surface. Rests on: F-C8, F-C7, F-OB2, F-BB5, F-C1.
- **Roughness pipeline and specular anti-aliasing.** Cite JCGT 10(2) 2021 for sigma^2 = 1/(2 pi) and kappa = 0.18 (the I3D 2019 paper uses sigma = 0.5). Drop 'HDRP has no ray cones at hits' as the novelty: HDRP has ray-cone texture LOD but no footprint roughness filtering at hits. Note that the 0.045 floor is also an aliasing guard (Filament documents both reasons), so the --aa=none gates are what justify its removal. Do not use the 9.51/8.83 pole edge-jitter as a specular-aliasing target until it is separated from taa_resolve's depth-tolerance rejection. The cook work creates a per-material derived texture, a new asset kind keyed differently from today's per-file, name-classified cook. Include the name-mismatch case (roughness filtered as sRGB colour) and the uncooked blit-mip fallback. Rests on: F-C16, F-C14, F-BB1, F-C12, F-C6.
- **Material detail at ray hits.** Present this as closing the gap to Battlefield V and UE Hit Lighting, not keeping a lead: today's hits have no normal maps, sample mip 0 only and have no specular on GI or bake hits. Word the parity gate as 'within one quantisation step', because textureGrad from ray differentials is not bit-identical to quad derivatives or anisotropic filtering. Rests on: F-OB3, F-BB2.
- **Composition (RT mode).** Do not claim the environment-mip-from-derivatives term as an industry lead. In the compute composition, keep it by finite-differencing the reflection vector from neighbouring G-buffer normals and depth rather than 'from the filtered roughness', which caps at kappa and cannot reproduce the max() term. Or treat dropping it as a measured arm. Removing the RT-mode indirect lane in 'replaces' must carry the rayGi condition. Rests on: F-OB1, F-C8.
- **Ray origins.** The face-normal source (geometry lane) is byte-neutral only under ray GI. Otherwise either derive the face normal from depth in each consumer or accept +8 bytes. Compute position derivatives camera-relative, for precision at bridge-scale distances. Rests on: F-BB3, F-C8.
- **One BSDF module (include/bsdf.glsl).** No refutation: C3, C4 and C5 hold. Add Kelemen (coat), Ashikhmin (sheen) and the water Beckmann G1 to the inventory of forms the module owns. If EON is ever tried, it needs a diffuse-roughness source separate from GGX roughness. Rests on: F-C3, F-C5, F-BB4.

### F.9 New findings from the verification

- **F-N1.** HDRP compiles geometric specular AA out of the ray-tracing stage (LitData.hlsl: !defined(SHADER_STAGE_RAY_TRACING)) but does have ray-cone texture LOD at hits (RaytracingIntersection.hlsl RayCone, USE_RAY_CONE_LOD). Phase 2 has both halves of this wrong or missing.
- **F-N2.** HDRP documentation says ray tracing does not support MSAA at all, and HDRP supports MSAA only in forward. This bears on 'the owner's MSAA decision' in the composition design: RageV's owner-chosen MSAA 4x has no shipped RT-engine precedent.
- **F-N3.** The reflection history's same-object test (reflection_accumulate.rvshader:318-319, 1390, 1951) compares per-frame pool rows (Renderer3D.cpp:9705 etc.), which change with submission order, so 'same object' is unreliable even below the half-float 2048 limit. This is beyond the precision issue F-C9 lists.
- **F-N4.** In the G-buffer variant (RV_GBUFFER), pbr_fragment.glsl returns at 4509, before the o_Indirect write. The G-buffer pass binds only velocity, surface, albedo and id, so this is harmless today, but the indirect-lane zero write happens only in the later forward Scene pass.
- **F-N5.** The JCGT 2021 figures phase 2 cites (RMSE 12631.7 to 0.134) are for the non-axis-aligned projected-space filter, not the normal-based isotropic filter RageV uses. The 0.1 ms cost figure is for the isotropic filter, in forward shading, on Intel Iris Plus.
- **F-N6.** TextureCook chooses the filter space from case-sensitive name suffixes. A roughness map named without '_roughness' is cooked as BC1 and box-filtered in linearised sRGB space.

### F.10 The comparison's corrections to the survey

- Phase 1 recommends 'specular AA from the pixel's footprint in place of the fixed 0.045 floor' as though RageV had none. RageV already ships Tokuyoshi-Kaplanyan 2019 (deferred isotropic, the paper's sigma^2 = 1/(2 pi) and kappa = 0.18) at pbr_fragment.glsl:4445-4487. The real gaps are coverage (analytic lights only; o_Surface keeps the unfiltered roughness, 4462-4496) and normal-map variance discarded at cook (TextureCook.cpp:329-356).
- Lumen hit-lighting cost, marked 'secondary source, not verified' in phase 1, is now verified in the primary text: PS5 1080p reflection tracing 2.44 ms (HWRT + Surface Cache) against 11.54 ms (HWRT + Hit Lighting), 4.7x (lumen_2022.txt:1857-1869). Added context from the same talk: Hit Lighting 'did not provide a substantial qualitative benefit' in that sky-dominated demo, 'partial dynamic evaluation was just as expensive as full dynamic evaluation' (1695-1696), and Hit Lighting is a sort-and-retrace sorted-deferred pipeline (1719-1731).
- Snowdrop: phase 1 said the RT world uses an 'average material computed from each artist-authored material, often depending on runtime inputs'. The GDC 2024 text says: lower LOD (LOD1-2), 'one simple material per mesh, no texture', and a simplified BSDF with no hair, cloth or clearcoat (snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:40-58, 300-310). The 'runtime inputs' part is not in this talk and stays unverified. Also found: screen-space hits read the raster G-buffer, then fall back to the RT world (565-580); reflections are demodulated per Ray Tracing Gems ch. 32 (990-1011); and there are two reflection layers (824, 838).
- Phase 1's baseline lane (R10G10B10A2 octahedral normal, the NRD packing) would regress RageV's mirror precision. RageV moved to 16-bit channels because 10 bits moved chrome highlights (pbr_fragment.glsl:4501-4503; FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:56-68). The correction is to keep 8 bytes and switch half float to UNORM16.
- RT-SERIES-2's RT2-21 lane list has both 'albedo (sRGB, 8 bits)' and 'F0 and metalness'. F0 is derived from albedo, specular and metallic by one formula already shared by every path (pbr_fragment.glsl:4515; direct_trace.rvshader:757; reflection_trace.rvshader:862-863), so the F0 lane is redundant. Phase 1 flagged this correctly.
- RT-SERIES-2 RT2-21 says float lanes can be resolved 'by a small shader that takes sample zero'. That also has to cover the proposed UNORM lanes: Vulkan's SAMPLE_ZERO applies only to integer formats, and UNORM is not integer (vendored validusage.json VUID-06129/06130).
- Phase 1's count of 'five different BSDFs' understates the drift. There are three Smith forms (pbr_fragment.glsl:3433-3444; Cubemap.cpp:252-261; reflection_emitters.glsl:62-86) and a hit diffuse without (1-F) (pbr_fragment.glsl:2802), in addition to MAT-02's list.
- Stale line citations in RT-SERIES-2 against the working tree: the reflection trace's tint is now reflection_trace.rvshader:857-884, not 961-998. The CPU-path id fill sites are Renderer3D.cpp:9705, 9756, 9839 and 9892, not 9673. The struck-identity fold is at Renderer3D.cpp:5662-5670.

### F.11 Questions left open

As the survey recorded them. Where the verification later read a primary talk the survey could not fetch, the verification's verdict above supersedes the line here.

- The research is incomplete: the session's web-search limit (200 calls, shared with the other agents in this workflow) ran out partway through, so everything after that point came from direct fetches of known URLs.
- Unreal legacy (non-Substrate) GGX energy compensation: whether it exists, since which version, and under which cvar names could not be confirmed. The source is private, the docs pages rendered empty, and a 2022 forum thread said it did not exist then.
- Which diffuse model Unreal Substrate uses (Chan or Lambert) is unverified.
- The G-buffer layout and material-at-hits details for DOOM: The Dark Ages (the visibility-buffer and path-tracing talks), the Snowdrop 2024 slides, the SEED PICA PICA chapter and the Frostbite 2014 notes were not read: each PDF was over the 10 MB fetch limit.
- How Cyberpunk 2077 RT Overdrive, Alan Wake 2 and Indiana Jones shade materials at hits (material LOD, texture LOD) was not found in public text.
- The Lumen hit-lighting against surface-cache figures (11.54 against 2.44 ms, PS5) come only from a secondary summary of SIGGRAPH 2022.
- The Battlefield V 6.29 ms breakdown has no GPU or resolution in the slide text.
- No real-time ms figures found for EON, the Chan diffuse, the bounded VNDF sampler or NTC decode-on-sample.
- Call of Duty WWII course notes (Chan 2018): the Activision CDN refused the connection, so only the summary page was read.
- AMD FidelityFX Hybrid Reflections hit shading, and HDRP's deferred ray-tracing mode with ray binning: the docs URLs returned 404 or did not cover it.
- The RTX 5070 Ti Laptop's real memory bandwidth, needed to turn G-buffer bytes into ms, should be measured on the device rather than taken from a spec sheet.
- Whether VK_NV_cooperative_vector is usable from fragment shaders as well as compute (relevant only if neural materials are ever tried) was not checked.
- Claims the verification could not settle without a measurement or a source: F-OB1, F-BB1, F-BB2, F-BB3, F-BB4, F-BB5 (each is gated in the roadmap item named above).

## G. Glass, water, fog, particles

**In short.** The nearest glass pane runs the shared direct and reflection passes with its own histories, which is Snowdrop's shape. Everything else is behind: glass and water are not in the ray structure, glass cannot tint, the sea's rays are averaged only by TAA, the waves are a fixed grid, fog is one pass after TAA, and particles are unlit. The "many lamps on water" lead was refuted. The verified verdicts, with the candidates tied to their items, are in the roadmap's section 11.

**Counts.** Comparison topics: 11 behind, 6 equal, 5 missing, 2 not comparable, 1 better (claimed). Verification of 22 claims: 12 confirmed, 5 plausible, 4 partly true, 1 refuted.

**The survey, in its own summary.** In shipped RT-era engines, glass, water, fog and particles are still mostly rasterized and lit from caches. They are not path traced. The shipped pattern has five parts: (1) The nearest see-through layer is pulled into a small G-buffer and gets the full RT reflection pipeline. Examples: UE5 Lumen High Quality Translucency Reflections (depth-peeled front layer, forced mirror, denoiser off), Snowdrop's second reflection layer for water and clearcoat (traced and denoised on its own), and BFV's extra rays on water. (2) Deeper layers, particles and fog are lit from a camera-froxel volume or a world radiance cache, each filtered in space and time by its own accumulator. Examples: the Lumen Volumetric Final Gather (SH2), id Tech 8's froxel irradiance volume, GIBS probes, UE's translucency lighting volume, and MegaLights, which casts a fixed number of shadow rays per voxel and then denoises. (3) Order independence comes from a transmittance-integral OIT: MBOIT at three resolutions in Alan Wake 2, or AVBOIT in Call of Duty (2025). The alternative is a heavy A-buffer (Black Ops 4). (4) Refraction is screen-space (UE Single Layer Water, Alan Wake 2). The exception is path-traced remasters, where RTX Remix replaces the primary surface with the refracted one (PSTR), so the normal denoisers see through glass. (5) Fog is a froxel grid with its own jittered temporal reprojection. RT-first variants add ReSTIR per froxel with up to 128 frames of accumulation (RTX Remix). AC Shadows evaluates fog cheaply at reflection-ray hits. Water surfaces come from FFT spectra or baked sims. AC Shadows filters their normal and roughness by the pixel's footprint. Caustics are procedural, built from the simulation's normals (HDRP); ray-traced caustics exist only in NVIDIA's UE branch and its book chapter. Anchor costs:; UE volumetric fog: 1 ms on PS4, 3 ms on a GTX 970.; At 4K on PS5: AVBOIT with culling 4.97 ms, WBOIT 5.91 ms, no OIT 4.40 ms.; SIGMA translucent-shadow denoiser: 0.45 ms at 1440p on an RTX 4080.; Snowdrop RT reflections: 2.13 ms at 2160p on an RTX 4080; the second layer doubles this. TAA reliance:; Clean without TAA: froxel fog, the OIT methods and cache-lit layers.; Depend on TAA or an upscaler: stochastic or dithered transparency, the NRD sample's 'denoising-free' glass and Lumen's undenoised front layer.; DLSS RR does not denoise a transparency overlay, so transparent surfaces must arrive clean. Baseline for RageV: one extracted nearest layer on the shared RT passes with its own history; cache-lit back layers and particles; a froxel volume with its own accumulator; analytic fog along ray segments; a transmittance-integral OIT. Candidates that could beat it: PSTR-style surface replacement, AVBOIT, and froxel ReSTIR. Each needs measuring in RageV.

**The comparison, in its own summary.** Where RageV is ahead: the nearest pane of glass already runs the shared direct-light and reflection passes with its own histories. That is Snowdrop's shape, and it holds more steadily than Unreal's front layer, which runs with the denoiser off. The sea lights about a hundred shadowed lamps per pixel, which no source shows any shipped engine doing. The lamp glow disc is an energy-correct way to stop lamps smaller than a pixel from popping, a problem the sources never address. For thin glass, the surface seen through the pane is already rasterised and fully lit, so RageV gets the benefit of RTX Remix's primary surface replacement (PSTR) for free. Where the others are ahead:; Particles are unlit.; Fog is one pass after TAA over the opaque depth. Reflections carry no fog, glass is fogged at the depth of what lies behind it, and the sky stops at the far clip.; Glass casts no shadow, is not tinted, and its coverage comes from how bright its reflection is.; The sea's mirror and refraction rays are traced inside its draw, and the only thing averaging them is TAA.; The waves are a 32-wave sum on a fixed 3 m grid with no level of detail.; There is no lamp light scattered in the fog.; The panes behind the nearest only settle because of TAA, which the owner accepted as exception D11. What beats both: 1. Refraction: screen-space refraction of the fully lit backdrop first, and a traced ray only where screen space fails. Each traced ray has its own accumulator. 2. The nearest see-through surface is composited exactly, and weighted-blended transparency (WBOIT) is kept only for what lies behind it. 3.
