Shadow rays through glass need only one query: they multiply the transmittance in any order, because any opaque hit ends the ray at zero. This needs one geometry flag that RageV does not set yet. 4. One analytic fog integral is applied to every path segment, and each segment knows which medium it runs through. This removes the fog-floor crutch. 5. Panes behind the nearest get one fixed mirror ray each, with no random draw, so they settle without TAA. This needs the owner to reopen D11. Roadmap changes:; RT2-13 splits. The opaque clean-up stays in M2. Moving the panes behind the nearest folds into RT2-31.; RT2-31 gains the exact composite, the refraction pass with its fallback, and a rule that one set of full-frame histories is shared by glass and water.; RT2-32 gains a spectrum-driven generator on a clipmap, with roughness and caustics derived from the waves.; RT2-33 gains fog along every segment, glows that follow the lamp's own emission and a ray visibility test, and particles lit from a new world-space lighting cache (the MegaLights structure).; Two live defects move early into RT2-6: the glass coverage and the fog stopping at the far clip.; Seven entries are added to section 7, the not-doing list.; Three owner decisions: reopen D11; replace the absolute mirror cap that produces the accepted sea's white shine; FFT or Gerstner waves.

**The verification, in its own summary.** Of the 15 code and industry claims (C1-C15), 12 are confirmed and 3 are partly true. The partly-true ones are G-C4, G-C8 and G-C15. G-C4: the accepted white-shine accumulator lives only on the unmerged wip branch; main bounds each sample in the draw and relies on TAA. G-C8: direct light has 5 history lanes, not 4 (168 B/px total), and the histories are allocated even with no glass. G-C15: the refraction push is already in audit 2 (RT-SERIES-2.md:2587); only the 0.32 wave-speed factor is new. The verdict G-OB1 (RageV better at many lamps on water) is refuted: it rests on absence of evidence, is bridge-only, and its own memories break rule 2. Of the beats-both claims, G-BB3 is partly true. Its logic (G-C3) holds, but NO_DUPLICATE is not the only requirement: glass and water are not in the TLAS at all, RayTraverse is shared, RTAO forces opaque, and the flag is per mesh BLAS. BB1, BB2, BB4, BB5 and BB6 are plausible with corrections. BB1: id Tech 8 is not a precedent, and Snowdrop's seams must be gated. BB2: the AVBOIT cost framing is inverted, since WBOIT is the slowest in that table. BB4: 'exact' fails with MaxOpacity 0.42 on the bridge. BB5: 'stored light' for Realtime lights does not exist yet. BB6: only GPU emitters have state slots, and GPU simulation is off by default. Target-design changes are needed in the traversal, layer composite, layer signals, refraction, fog and back-layers components. Files checked: C:/Users/ism19/Code/RageV/RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl, include/ray_shadow_trace.glsl, fog.rvshader, water_accumulate.rvshader, particle_sim.rvshader, C:/Users/ism19/Code/RageV/RageV/src/Platform/Vulkan/VulkanResources.cpp, RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/FrameGraphBuilder.cpp, Renderer3D.cpp, RenderSettings.h, RayShadows.cpp, Scene/Scene.cpp, Core/EngineConfig.h, docs/RT-SERIES-2.md, and the sources/*.txt talks plus the AVBOIT PDF.

### G.1 What the top engines ship

- **Unreal Engine 5.x (Lumen, MegaLights, Single Layer Water, Volumetric Fog, Heterogeneous Volumes).** Single Layer Water: its own pass after deferred lighting. Refraction distorts scene colour/depth reads; absorption and scattering coefficients per channel plus a phase G; Lumen reflections are forced to mirror on water (the reflection pipeline is run again for water). Translucency: the frontmost layer is depth-peeled into a minimal G-buffer, and the tile-based reflection pipeline is re-run on those pixels, forced to mirror with the denoiser disabled. Other layers read glossy reflections from the World Radiance Cache. GI on translucency and fog comes from the Volumetric Final Gather: a froxel probe grid, 3x3 or 4x4 octahedral traces, spatial and temporal filter, SH2 irradiance, about 1/8 of the opaque gather budget. Classic Translucency Lighting Volume (cascaded, 64^3 by default) for direct light. MegaLights samples lights stochastically on the lighting volume and on volumetric fog, with a fixed number of shadow rays per voxel and then a denoiser, plus a per-pixel front-layer pass. HWRT translucent refractions are optional (hit lighting, Max Refraction Bounces). Volumetric fog is a froxel grid with heavy temporal reprojection and per-frame sub-voxel jitter. Heterogeneous Volumes are experimental. Bloom is standard (Gaussian) or FFT convolution.
  Numbers: Volumetric fog: 1 ms on PS4 at High, 3 ms on a GTX 970 at Epic (8x the voxels). Translucency Lighting Volume: doubling its size costs 8x. MegaLights front-layer translucency can double the lighting cost when it covers most of the screen. MegaLights does not support water. Lumen 2022 per-effect costs exist for opaque only; no translucency ms published.
  Status: shipped (engine features; Heterogeneous Volumes experimental)
  Sources: <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2022/SIGGRAPH2022-Advances-Lumen-Wright%20et%20al.pdf>; <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/lumen-global-illumination-and-reflections-in-unreal-engine>; <https://dlss.download.nvidia.com/uebinarypackages/Documentation/UE5+Raytracing+Guideline+v5.4.pdf>; <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/megalights-in-unreal-engine>; <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/single-layer-water-shading-model-in-unreal-engine>; <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/volumetric-fog-in-unreal-engine>; <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/lit-translucency-in-unreal-engine>; <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/heterogeneous-volumes-in-unreal-engine>; <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/bloom-in-unreal-engine>
- **NVIDIA RTX Remix runtime (dxvk-remix; Portal with RTX, Half-Life 2 RTX demo, community remasters).** Path tracer on Vulkan. Primary Surface Replacement for reflection (PSRR) and transmission (PSTR): the G-buffer surface is replaced by the surface seen through mirrors or smooth glass, up to 10 bounces each by default, with 'split approximations' for rough and outgoing transmission events. Coloured translucent shadows for direct and indirect light exist but are off by default. Particles and decals are resolved by separate 'unordered' approximations, including in indirect rays. Opacity micromaps are built for particle billboards. Volumetrics: a froxel grid at 1/8 resolution with 64 depth slices (exponent 2) and a default range of 20 m; 32 initial RIS candidates; temporal and spatial ReSTIR reuse; visibility rays with visibility reuse; accumulation up to 128 frames in YCoCg; a firefly luminance clamp. Denoising splits direct and indirect (NRD) or uses DLSS RR.
  Numbers: No costs published in the sources read. Defaults: pstrMaxBounces 10, psrrMaxBounces 10; froxel grid resolution scale 8, 64 slices, 20 m range; ReSTIR spatial reuse of 8 neighbours within 0.8 froxels; max accumulation 128 frames.
  Status: shipped (open-source runtime used by shipped Remix titles; the specific title list was not re-verified here)
  Sources: <https://github.com/NVIDIAGameWorks/dxvk-remix/blob/main/RtxOptions.md>; <https://github.com/NVIDIAGameWorks/dxvk-remix/blob/main/src/dxvk/rtx_render/rtx_options.h>; <https://github.com/NVIDIAGameWorks/dxvk-remix/blob/main/src/dxvk/rtx_render/rtx_global_volumetrics.h>
- **NVIDIA NRD / NRD-Sample (reference integration).** The NRD README says NRD is not designed for volumetrics or transparency. PSR is the recommended handling for mirrors and delta events: virtual viewZ along the view vector, mirrored normals, curvature-aware motion, and a 2-10x larger disocclusion threshold. The sample's glass is 'denoising-free': multi-bounce reflection and refraction, lit by an L1 cache (the previous denoised frame) and an L2 cache (SHARC), with dithering left for TAA or an upscaler to resolve. SIGMA_SHADOW_TRANSLUCENCY denoises coloured shadows from a 'pseudo translucency' input.
  Numbers: RTX 4080 at 1440p native: REBLUR diffuse+specular 2.55 ms, RELAX 3.25 ms, SIGMA shadow 0.40 ms, SIGMA shadow translucency 0.45 ms. Sample: about 150 FPS day and 110 FPS night in Bistro, same GPU and resolution.
  Status: public SDK / demo
  Sources: <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD>; <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD-Sample>
- **id Tech 8 (Doom: The Dark Ages).** Transparencies (particles, glass) are lit from a 'transparencies froxel irradiance volume' (2-band SH), written during the volumetric-fog light-scattering dispatch (fog based on Wronski 2014). Lights, probes and decals for the RT world, particles and glass come from a world-space cascaded light grid (16^3 cells x 8 cascades). RT specular is SSR first, then RT, only on very smooth surfaces, with its own temporal upscale. Reflection probes on transparents are re-fitted with the froxel irradiance.
  Numbers: Froxel irradiance volume 50 MB (25 MB on lower specs). Light grid about 30 MB. RT specular about 2 ms, disabled on consoles. GPU and resolution not stated on those slides.
  Status: shipped
  Sources: <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/SOUSA_SIGGRAPH_2025_Final.pdf>
- **Snowdrop (Avatar: Frontiers of Pandora).** RT reflections carry up to two layers: the G-buffer layer plus a water/clearcoat layer. The two are traced and denoised separately (PC max settings only). Mixed-resolution tracing is chosen per tile by a quality metric, and water tiles mostly get full resolution. Specular is demodulated before denoising. The low-LOD RT world makes mirror and still-water reflections discontinuous where screen-space and world-space hits differ.
  Numbers: RT reflections 2.132 ms on an RTX 4080 at 2160p, traced at reduced resolution (the fraction is garbled in the extracted text). The second layer effectively doubles RT reflection cost.
  Status: shipped
  Sources: <https://media.gdcvault.com/gdc2024/Slides/GDC+slide+presentations/Kuenlib_Quentin_Raytracing_In_Snowdrop.pdf>
- **Anvil (Assassin's Creed Shadows).** Per-pixel indirect specular, shipped at half resolution. Water normal and roughness are adapted to the world-space projected pixel size to reduce aliasing, and water gets no diffuse evaluation. Screen-space march length is extended on water. Reflection misses fall back to a dynamic 128x128 far cubemap (terrain, sky, clouds, far entities) that updates one face per frame. Fog is evaluated at specular hits with a simplified single-step integration: near fog is skipped, and fog is skipped entirely for diffuse. When the material is translucent, rays behind screen geometry go to the BVH.
  Numbers: Far cubemap 128x128, one face per frame. Share of per-pixel ms for water or fog not published.
  Status: shipped
  Sources: <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/Advances%202025%20-%20Raytracing%20the%20world%20of%20Assassin's%20Creed%20Shadows.pdf>
- **Frostbite / EA SEED (Battlefield V RT reflections, GIBS, PICA PICA).** BFV: variable-rate tracing gives more rays to water and grazing angles. Particles in reflections come from a separate particle TLAS traced after the opaque hit (tMax = the opaque hit), accumulated with WBOIT-style weights inside an any-hit shader that ignores the hit. Odd billboards are rotated 90 degrees to look volumetric. GIBS lights transparents from a probe volume projected to SH, using the same adaptive MSME integrator as the surfels. PICA PICA (demo): glass refracts per Snell with IOR transitions from the hit kind, tinted, with chromatic aberration; clear glass needs no filtering, rough glass uses cone sampling plus more samples or temporal filtering; transparent shadows were not handled; translucency is computed in texture space and accumulated over time.
  Numbers: BFV particle ray-gen loop 0.96 ms before the any-hit optimisation (GPU not in the extracted slide text). GIBS and PICA transparency costs not published.
  Status: BFV shipped; GIBS shipped later in Frostbite (per the 2024 talk title); PICA PICA demo
  Sources: <https://developer.download.nvidia.com/video/gputechconf/gtc/2019/presentation/s91023-it-just-works-ray-traced-reflections-in-battlefield-v.pdf>; <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2021/SIGGRAPH%20Advances%202021%20-%20Surfel%20GI.pdf>; <https://media.contentapi.ea.com/content/dam/ea/seed/presentations/gdc2018-seed-shiny-pixels-and-beyond-real-time-raytracing-at-seed.pdf>
- **Northlight (Alan Wake 2).** Transparency is drawn in three resolutions with MBOIT, so fog, transparent geometry and effects blend seamlessly. Transparents get per-pixel lighting, and reflections are affected by fog. Water refracts with a non-uniform blur. Path-traced indirect lighting and DLSS RR on PC.
  Numbers: not published
  Status: shipped
  Sources: <https://www.remedygames.com/article/how-northlight-makes-alan-wake-2-shine>
- **IW engine (Call of Duty).** Black Ops 4: an exact 9-layer A-buffer; meshes raster through UAVs; VFX use a software compute rasterizer; overflow blends into one layer. SIGGRAPH 2025: AVBOIT. Transparencies are splatted into a low-resolution froxel extinction buffer and integrated into a 3D transmittance LUT; all transparencies then render in any order weighted by that LUT. Slices are redistributed from per-frame occupancy, zero-transmittance depth enables an early cull, and RGB extinction is kept only for coloured sources.
  Numbers: BO4 A-buffer: about 530 MB (mono) or 754 MB (RGB) at 4K, and over 2.5x the cost of non-OIT. AVBOIT at 4K on PS5: total 5.667 ms, 4.967 ms with culling, against MBOIT 5.705, WBOIT 5.908 and no OIT 4.4. Content test at 4K on PS5: no OIT 2.8, AVBOIT mono 2.95, RGB 3.11 ms. GTX 1060 at 1080p: AVBOIT RGB 12.2 vs no OIT 11.2 ms.
  Status: BO4 A-buffer shipped. The AVBOIT abstract says CoD moved to OIT while supporting live releases; the exact title is not named in the extracted slides.
  Sources: <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/AVBOIT_SIG2025_MDROBOT-final.pdf>; <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/index.html>
- **Decima (Horizon Forbidden West water).** Breaking waves come from baked Houdini sims, instanced, played back and blended to fit the coastline, plus wave particles. Tessellation is raised around wavefronts, and foam is carried in vertex colour. PS4/PS5 raster; no RT.
  Numbers: no costs in the extracted notes
  Status: shipped
  Sources: <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2022/SIGGRAPH2022-Advances-Water-Malan.pdf>
- **Unity HDRP.** Water system with FFT simulation bands: ocean 2 swell bands plus 1 ripple band, river 2, pool 1. Caustics are computed from one chosen simulation band via a virtual plane distance and resolution; they do not apply to transparents underwater. Recursive Rendering is a replacement pipeline for flagged glass: multi-bounce reflection and refraction, rays ignore smoothness, and objects beyond Max Ray Length show sky. It cannot be combined with other RT effects on the same object.
  Numbers: not published
  Status: shipped (engine features)
  Sources: <https://docs.unity3d.com/Packages/com.unity.render-pipelines.high-definition@17.0/manual/water-water-system-simulation.html>; <https://docs.unity3d.com/Packages/com.unity.render-pipelines.high-definition@17.0/manual/water-caustics-in-the-water-system.html>; <https://docs.unity3d.com/Packages/com.unity.render-pipelines.high-definition@17.0/manual/Ray-Tracing-Recursive-Rendering.html>
- **REDengine (Cyberpunk 2077 RT Overdrive), 4A (Metro Exodus EE), id Tech 7/Motor (Indiana Jones).** No primary source read here describes how glass, particles, water or volumetric fog enter their path tracers. The Metro EE technical article covers only emissive surfaces and denoising. The Cyberpunk ReSTIR integration talk has no transparency content. The NVIDIA Cyberpunk article gives only frame rates.
  Numbers: Cyberpunk Overdrive frame rates are with DLSS 3 only (e.g. 4K on an RTX 4090 at 103 FPS with frame generation); these cannot be used as costs.
  Status: shipped (area handling unresolved)
  Sources: <https://www.nvidia.com/en-us/geforce/news/cyberpunk-2077-ray-tracing-overdrive-update-launches-april-11/>; <https://intro-to-restir.cwyman.org/presentations/2023ReSTIR_Course_Cyberpunk_2077_Integration.pdf>; <https://www.4a-games.com.mt/4a-dna/in-depth-technical-dive-into-metro-exodus-pc-enhanced-edition>
- **AMD FidelityFX.** The Hybrid Reflections documentation does not describe any transparency, glass or water path. FSR asks for a reactive mask (particles and alpha-blended surfaces that write no depth or motion) and a transparency-and-composition mask; without them the upscaler ghosts or blurs these surfaces.
  Numbers: not published
  Status: public SDK
  Sources: <https://gpuopen.com/fidelityfx-hybrid-reflections/>; <https://gpuopen.com/manuals/fidelityfx_sdk/techniques/super-resolution-temporal/>

### G.2 The techniques behind them

- **Front-layer transmissive G-buffer on the shared RT reflection pipeline (Lumen High Quality Translucency Reflections)** (shipped; shipped in Unreal Engine 5.x; confidence high). Depth-peel the frontmost translucent surface into a minimal G-buffer. Run the normal tile-based reflection pipeline again, only on tiles holding that layer. Reflections are forced to mirror and the denoiser is disabled to save cost. The forward translucency pass then reads the result; deeper layers read the radiance cache instead.
  Fixes: Mirror reflections on glass without tracing inside the pixel shader. The reflection code is shared with opaque surfaces. Cost: Not published separately (the NVIDIA UE5.4 guide says only 'additional GPU cost'). MegaLights documentation: a front-layer per-pixel lighting pass can double lighting cost when translucency covers most of the screen. Fails: Only one layer gets sharp reflections; back layers are glossy-only. Mirror reflections are forced regardless of roughness. With the denoiser off, stochastic hit lighting is left to TSR. The depth-peel is an extra geometry pass.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: partly. Mirror rays need no spatial filter. Any stochastic lighting at the hit is left un-denoised for TSR to average. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Good: a compute or fragment pass over a compacted list of layer pixels, tracing with inline ray queries. This is the shape RT2-31 plans; the difference is that RageV plans a history for the layer.
  Needs: A translucent depth pre-pass or depth peel, a compact tile list, and a reflection pipeline that can run on part of the screen.
  Sources: <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2022/SIGGRAPH2022-Advances-Lumen-Wright%20et%20al.pdf>; <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/lumen-global-illumination-and-reflections-in-unreal-engine>; <https://dlss.download.nvidia.com/uebinarypackages/Documentation/UE5+Raytracing+Guideline+v5.4.pdf>; <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/megalights-in-unreal-engine>
- **Cache-lit deeper translucent layers, particles and fog (froxel or world radiance cache)** (shipped; shipped in Unreal Engine 5.x, Doom: The Dark Ages (id Tech 8), Frostbite (GIBS); confidence high). Lumen: a froxel probe grid covers the view frustum. Visible probes (HZB-tested) trace 3x3 or 4x4 octahedral rays, which are spatially filtered, temporally accumulated and pre-integrated to SH2 irradiance. Forward translucency and volumetric fog interpolate it. Shortened froxel rays fall back to a world radiance cache with 16 world rays per froxel ray. id Tech 8: the fog light-scattering dispatch also writes a 2-band SH froxel irradiance volume, which particles and glass read. GIBS: probes in a volume gather radiance and project it to SH with the same adaptive MSME integrator used for surfels.
  Fixes: Lighting for any number of layers and for particles, with no per-pixel rays and no per-pixel noise. Cost: Lumen: about 1/8 of the opaque final-gather budget (no ms given). id Tech 8: 50 MB of VRAM (25 MB on lower specs). GIBS: not published. Fails: Low spatial resolution: flat look, leaks through thin walls, no sharp specular. Temporal lag follows moving lights. Froxel range and slice distribution are dials tied to scene scale.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no. Each cache has its own spatial and temporal filter. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Good: compute probe tracing with inline ray queries; lookups are texture fetches.
  Needs: A froxel or probe structure and a radiance cache to fall back to. RageV already has a probe/irradiance field and a GI cache; the question is whether particles and back panes read it.
  Sources: <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2022/SIGGRAPH2022-Advances-Lumen-Wright%20et%20al.pdf>; <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/SOUSA_SIGGRAPH_2025_Final.pdf>; <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2021/SIGGRAPH%20Advances%202021%20-%20Surfel%20GI.pdf>
- **Translucency lighting volume with stochastic many-light sampling (UE TLV plus MegaLights)** (shipped; shipped in Unreal Engine 5.x (MegaLights from 5.5; maturity status not verified here); confidence high). Cascaded volume textures aligned to the view frustum (64^3 by default, inner and outer distance cvars) are lit once and read by translucent pixels in a single forward pass. Fourier opacity maps give volumetric self-shadowing. MegaLights importance-samples lights per voxel and traces a fixed number of shadow rays per voxel, then denoises. This work is shared with volumetric fog, and a per-pixel front-layer path exists for high-quality direct light.
  Fixes: Many shadowed lights on particles, fog and translucency at a fixed cost. Cost: Doubling the volume size costs 8x the lighting. The MegaLights front layer can double total lighting cost at full-screen coverage. No ms published. Fails: TLV-lit surfaces have no direct specular, and the volume resolution is too low for glass or water surfaces. Fourier opacity maps ring on dense media. MegaLights warns of blurring and ghosting under complex lighting and does not support water. The inner and outer distances are world-scale dials.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no. The volume has its own denoiser. The MegaLights per-pixel denoiser is separate from TSR. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Good: compute per froxel with inline shadow queries. This matches RageV's shared light-candidate structure (RT2-12/13).
  Needs: A light-candidate structure that can be sampled per voxel, plus shadow rays.
  Sources: <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/lit-translucency-in-unreal-engine>; <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/megalights-in-unreal-engine>; <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/MegaLights_Stochastic_Direct_Lighting_2025.pdf>
- **Second RT reflection layer for water and clearcoat, denoised separately (Snowdrop)** (shipped; shipped in Avatar: Frontiers of Pandora; confidence high). A second reflection target holds the water or clearcoat lobe, traced and denoised independently of the G-buffer layer. Trace resolution is chosen per tile by a quality metric from roughness, variance, luminance and distance; water tiles mostly get full resolution. Radiance is demodulated by a pre-integrated specular term before denoising.
  Fixes: Stable, denoised reflections on water sitting over an opaque surface that has its own reflection. Cost: RT reflections 2.132 ms on an RTX 4080 at 2160p, traced at reduced resolution; the second layer effectively doubles this (PC max settings only). Fails: The low-LOD RT world shows a seam between screen-space and world-space hits on still water. The cost is a full second chain.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no. Own denoiser per layer; it upscales the traced resolution itself. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Good.
  Needs: A second set of reflection histories at the layer's resolution.
  Sources: <https://media.gdcvault.com/gdc2024/Slides/GDC+slide+presentations/Kuenlib_Quentin_Raytracing_In_Snowdrop.pdf>
- **Primary Surface Replacement for reflection and transmission (PSR/PSTR)** (shipped; shipped in RTX Remix titles (e.g. Portal with RTX); confidence medium). From the primary hit, follow delta events (perfect mirror, smooth glass or water refraction) until the first non-delta surface. Write that surface into the G-buffer as the 'primary' surface, with a virtual depth along the view ray, normals mirrored through the delta events, and motion vectors of the virtual position adjusted for curvature. The whole opaque lighting and denoising pipeline then runs on what is seen through the glass. RTX Remix defaults to 10 bounces for each kind and adds split approximations for rough or outgoing transmission. NRD asks for a 2-10x larger disocclusion threshold on PSR pixels.
  Fixes: No separate refraction pass or accumulator is needed for smooth glass or still water; the seen-through surface gets the full lighting and history. It also stops shading a surface that is then fully covered (the seabed case in RT2-32). Cost: Not published. Denoiser cost per pixel is unchanged; the extra cost is the delta-chain rays. Fails: A pixel keeps only one path: the reflected or the transmitted one. The other lobe of a Fresnel mix still needs its own signal. Curved or animated surfaces (waves, bumpy glass) break reprojection; Remix has a pstrNormalDetailThreshold for normal-mapped glass disocclusion. Rough transmission is approximated.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: partly. It relies on the main denoisers (NRD or DLSS RR in Remix) seeing a consistent virtual surface, not on TAA as such. Wavy water is the risky case. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Good: Remix itself is Vulkan and uses ray queries in compute passes.
  Needs: The G-buffer pass must be able to trace delta chains, and there must be virtual motion-vector support.
  Sources: <https://github.com/NVIDIAGameWorks/dxvk-remix/blob/main/src/dxvk/rtx_render/rtx_options.h>; <https://github.com/NVIDIAGameWorks/dxvk-remix/blob/main/RtxOptions.md>; <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD>
- **Ray-traced refraction through glass (Snell rays with IOR transitions)** (shipped; shipped in Unreal Engine 5 (optional), Unity HDRP (Recursive Rendering), PICA PICA (demo); confidence high). From the camera ray or the G-buffer, refract by the IOR ratio and use the front/back face of each hit to track air-to-glass transitions. Trace and light the hit, then tint by the glass colour, optionally with chromatic aberration. Rough glass samples a cone and needs more samples or temporal filtering. UE offers HWRT translucent refractions with hit lighting and a Max Refraction Bounces setting. HDRP Recursive Rendering replaces raster for flagged objects.
  Fixes: Correct refraction of off-screen and occluded content, which screen-space refraction cannot see. Cost: Not published; the UE guide says only that GPU cost increases. Fails: Rough transmission is noisy. PICA PICA had no transparent shadows. HDRP shows sky beyond Max Ray Length and blocks other RT effects on those objects. Multiple overlapping panes multiply the rays.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: partly. Clear glass needs no filtering; rough glass needs an accumulator, its own or TAA. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Good: rayQueryGetIntersectionFrontFaceEXT gives the transition.
  Needs: Glass in the ray structure, with front/back-face information.
  Sources: <https://media.contentapi.ea.com/content/dam/ea/seed/presentations/gdc2018-seed-shiny-pixels-and-beyond-real-time-raytracing-at-seed.pdf>; <https://dlss.download.nvidia.com/uebinarypackages/Documentation/UE5+Raytracing+Guideline+v5.4.pdf>; <https://docs.unity3d.com/Packages/com.unity.render-pipelines.high-definition@17.0/manual/Ray-Tracing-Recursive-Rendering.html>
- **Coloured (translucent) shadows from shadow-ray transmittance** (shipped; shipped in RTX Remix (optional), NvRTX UE branch (RTXDI transparent shadows); confidence high). A shadow ray does not stop at glass or water. It multiplies their RGB transmittance and continues to the light or to an opaque blocker. Remix exposes this for direct and indirect light (off by default); NvRTX adds it to RTXDI. NRD's SIGMA_SHADOW_TRANSLUCENCY denoises it from a pseudo-translucency value plus penumbra.
  Fixes: Glass that casts tinted shadows. Today RageV's glass casts no shadow at all (RT2-31). Cost: SIGMA_SHADOW_TRANSLUCENCY 0.45 ms at 1440p on an RTX 4080. Ray cost: not published. Fails: Each translucent candidate adds a shader round trip. Transmittance ignores refraction, so shadows are not caustics.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no. Visibility is deterministic per ray; the stochastic part is light selection, handled by the shadow denoiser. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Good, with no any-hit needed. Multiplying transmittance is order-independent, so a rayQueryProceed candidate loop over non-opaque candidates can accumulate the product. It must not commit candidates, and it must bound tMax by the opaque hit: either a first opaque-only query, or by committing opaque hits and discarding translucent candidates beyond them.
  Needs: Transmissive geometry in the TLAS with a separate instance mask or non-opaque flag.
  Sources: <https://github.com/NVIDIAGameWorks/dxvk-remix/blob/main/RtxOptions.md>; <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD>; <https://dlss.download.nvidia.com/uebinarypackages/Documentation/UE5+Raytracing+Guideline+v5.4.pdf>
- **Particles and transparents inside RT reflections (separate TLAS or unordered resolve)** (shipped; shipped in Battlefield V, RTX Remix titles; confidence high). BFV: after the opaque reflection hit, trace a particle-only TLAS with tMax set to the opaque distance, and accumulate colour, alpha and weight with WBOIT-style weights in an any-hit that ignores the hit. Odd billboards are rotated 90 degrees to look volumetric. Remix: 'unordered' approximations resolve particles and decals in indirect rays, and opacity micromaps are built for billboards.
  Fixes: Smoke and fire appear in reflections without sorting. Cost: BFV's first ray-gen loop version cost 0.96 ms before the any-hit optimisation (GPU not in the extracted text). Fails: Billboards seen side-on; weighted-average colour errors; cost of many candidates.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no. Order-independent and noise-free per ray; it rides on the reflection signal's own denoiser. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Good: a candidate loop in rayQuery replaces the any-hit. VK_EXT_opacity_micromap works with ray queries for alpha-tested cards.
  Needs: Particles in a TLAS or an instance mask.
  Sources: <https://developer.download.nvidia.com/video/gputechconf/gtc/2019/presentation/s91023-it-just-works-ray-traced-reflections-in-battlefield-v.pdf>; <https://github.com/NVIDIAGameWorks/dxvk-remix/blob/main/RtxOptions.md>
- **Weighted Blended OIT (WBOIT)** (shipped; shipped in many titles; BFV used its weights inside RT reflections; confidence high). One unsorted pass blends premultiplied colour times a weight into RGBA16F and the product of (1 - alpha) into an R8 'revealage' target. A full-screen pass then composites. The weight combines colour/alpha with a depth term tuned to an expected depth range; the published example scales depth by 200 units.
  Fixes: No sorting and cheap; monochrome transmission for smoke and particles. Cost: Measured in the AVBOIT test at 4K on PS5 (shared framework): WBOIT total 5.908 ms vs no-OIT 4.4 ms. The blog claims it is faster than sorting but gives no numbers. Fails: The depth weight is tuned to depth range and opacity, which conflicts with the owner's general-solutions rule unless it is derived from content. No refraction. Monochrome transmission only. High-alpha near layers do not hide far ones (glass behind glass bleeds). Not shift-invariant. Designed for alpha of roughly 0.2-0.9 and 1-100 layers.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no. Deterministic, no noise. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: n/a (raster); RageV already has it.
  Needs: MRT with float blending.
  Sources: <https://jcgt.org/published/0002/02/09/>; <https://casual-effects.blogspot.com/2014/03/weighted-blended-order-independent.html>; <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/AVBOIT_SIG2025_MDROBOT-final.pdf>
- **Moment-based OIT (MBOIT)** (shipped; shipped in Alan Wake 2 (MBOIT at three resolutions); confidence high). Two additive passes. The first accumulates power (or trigonometric) moments of the logarithm of transmittance over depth. The second reconstructs transmittance at each fragment from the moments and composites. Variants use 4, 6 or 8 power moments or up to 4 trigonometric moments, and the method also handles participating media and transparent shadows.
  Fixes: Better depth ordering than WBOIT without per-pixel lists. Cost: 4K on PS5 (AVBOIT framework): MBOIT 5.705 ms total vs no OIT 4.4. Rank-2 prepass output 5 x FP16 (x3 for RGB). Fails: Not invariant to depth complexity: bright or blue layers pierce through. Upsampling artifacts at silhouettes in the low-resolution prepass. RGB is costly.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no. Deterministic. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: n/a (raster).
  Needs: Float additive blending, two passes.
  Sources: <https://momentsingraphics.de/I3D2018.html>; <https://www.remedygames.com/article/how-northlight-makes-alan-wake-2-shine>; <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/AVBOIT_SIG2025_MDROBOT-final.pdf>
- **Exact A-buffer / per-pixel lists / k-buffer** (shipped; shipped in Call of Duty: Black Ops 4; confidence high). Store up to N fragments per pixel (colour, transmittance, depth) through UAVs, then sort and resolve. Black Ops 4 used a 9-layer buffer, rasterized VFX in software in compute, and blended overflow into one layer.
  Fixes: Exact ordering, coloured transmittance. Cost: BO4: about 530 MB (mono) or 754 MB (RGB) at 4K, and over 2.5x slower than non-OIT with current content. Fails: Memory and bandwidth. Overflow on modern non-camera-facing VFX. No multi-resolution. Heavy maintenance.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no. Deterministic. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: n/a (raster).
  Needs: Atomics, large buffers.
  Sources: <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/AVBOIT_SIG2025_MDROBOT-final.pdf>
- **Stochastic / hashed / dithered transparency** (shipped; shipped in common in games (per the AVBOIT survey); confidence high). Each fragment is kept or discarded with probability equal to its alpha (random or hashed coverage masks). The result converges over MSAA samples, or over frames.
  Fixes: Cheap order independence, and it writes depth and motion. Cost: Cheap (AVBOIT survey); no ms. Fails: Noise; needs many samples or temporal accumulation.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: yes. Only converges through TAA or supersampling. It breaks rule 3 as a lighting signal unless it has its own accumulator. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: n/a
  Needs: none
  Sources: <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/AVBOIT_SIG2025_MDROBOT-final.pdf>
- **Adaptive Voxel-Based OIT (AVBOIT)** (shipped; shipped in Call of Duty (title not named in the extracted slides); confidence medium). Transparent draws splat their extinction into a low-resolution froxel extinction buffer. The measured setup used 480x270 at 4K, 8-bit x 128 slices, a logarithmic slice curve, and slices redistributed from per-frame occupancy. A compute pass integrates this along view rays into a 3D transmittance LUT. Every transparent surface then renders in any order, multiplying its opacity by the LUT transmittance at its position. A final resolve normalises accumulated colour and extinction over the opaque background. Zero-transmittance depth culls hidden transparent pixels, and RGB extinction is stored only for coloured sources. It is the same framework as volumetric fog, so fog, smoke and glass share one transmittance.
  Fixes: Correct occlusion of glass behind glass and smoke, stable results, coloured transparency at about 1.05-1.1x the cost of monochrome, and no content sorting. Cost: 4K on PS5: 5.667 ms, 4.967 ms with culling, vs WBOIT 5.908, MBOIT 5.705 and no OIT 4.4. Content test: mono 2.95, RGB 3.11 vs no OIT 2.8 ms. GTX 1060 at 1080p: 12.2 vs 11.2 ms without OIT. One quoted configuration uses 66.5 MB. Fails: Close-depth overlaps (UI, artist-intended manual sort order). Precision limits of slice discretisation and splatting. Occlusion can be tricky. Memory grows with precision; sparse textures are listed as future work.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no. Deterministic; 'stable' per the talk. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: n/a for tracing. It fits Vulkan compute, and its extinction volume could also feed shadow rays through smoke (unmeasured).
  Needs: Compute atomics or rasterised splatting into a 3D texture, and a froxel slice curve.
  Sources: <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/AVBOIT_SIG2025_MDROBOT-final.pdf>; <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/index.html>
- **Wavelet transparency / phenomenological OIT** (paper; shipped in not shipped; confidence low). Represents transmittance over depth with wavelets and extends phenomenological OIT, which also approximates refraction and chromatic aberration.
  Fixes: Closer to an A-buffer for smoke coverage at MBOIT-class cost, with more plausible refraction. Cost: Claimed comparable or better than moment transparency; no ms captured here. Fails: Not shipped; approximate.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no.  Fit with Vulkan ray queries: 
  Sources: <https://arxiv.org/abs/2201.00094>
- **Single Layer Water shading (UE)** (shipped; shipped in Unreal Engine 5; confidence high). A dedicated pass after opaque lighting. It reads scene depth and colour below the surface and distorts them for refraction, optionally from a downsampled copy. Absorption and scattering coefficients per channel, with a phase G, attenuate by the depth of water travelled. Reflections come from SSR, or from Lumen forced to mirror.
  Fixes: Cheap, stable water with a physical medium below the surface. Cost: Not published. Fails: Screen-space refraction cannot see off-screen or occluded content. Only one layer. Translucency behind water is limited. MegaLights does not support it.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no. Deterministic, apart from the reflection signal. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Refraction is raster; the reflection part fits ray queries.
  Needs: An opaque colour and depth copy.
  Sources: <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/single-layer-water-shading-model-in-unreal-engine>; <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/lumen-global-illumination-and-reflections-in-unreal-engine>
- **Ocean surface generation: FFT spectra, multi-band cascades, baked sims** (shipped; shipped in Unity HDRP, Horizon Forbidden West; confidence medium). Tessendorf: a Phillips (or later empirical) spectrum is animated with the deep-water dispersion relation, omega^2 = g k, and inverse-FFT'd to height and choppy horizontal displacement on tiled grids of about 64^2 to 512^2. HDRP runs 1-3 bands (swell plus ripples) per body type. Horizon Forbidden West instances baked Houdini sims deformed to the coastline, plus wave particles, with tessellation raised around wavefronts.
  Fixes: Plausible wave motion from physical dials such as wind, fetch and depth, so every constant can come from the spectrum. Cost: Not published in the sources read. Fails: Visible tiling with too few bands. Choppy displacement self-intersects. Normal-map aliasing and glitter at distance need footprint filtering. Baked sims cost content authoring.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no. The surface itself is deterministic, but high-frequency normals shimmer unless filtered (see the next technique). Fit with Vulkan ray queries: n/a for the generator; see the BVH entry.
  Needs: GPU FFT, and an LOD grid (clipmap or quadtree) for displacement.
  Sources: <https://jtessen.people.clemson.edu/reports/papers_files/coursenotes2004.pdf>; <https://docs.unity3d.com/Packages/com.unity.render-pipelines.high-definition@17.0/manual/water-water-system-simulation.html>; <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2022/SIGGRAPH2022-Advances-Water-Malan.pdf>
- **Footprint-filtered water normals and roughness** (shipped; shipped in Assassin's Creed Shadows; confidence medium). Wave detail smaller than a pixel is folded into roughness. AC Shadows adapts water normal and roughness using the world-space projected size of the pixel before importance-sampling reflection rays. Bruneton et al. 2010 derive the unresolved slope variance directly from the wave spectrum to move smoothly from geometry to normal map to BRDF (that paper was not re-read here).
  Fixes: Distant sparkle and aliasing on water without TAA, and a stable glint field. It is the general form of RageV's Cox-Munk footprint constants. Cost: Not published; a few ALU per pixel. Fails: Needs correct derivatives or ray-cone footprints on reflected and refracted paths. Over-blurring if the spectrum's variance is wrong.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no. This is the non-TAA fix for glint aliasing. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Good; for compute passes, ray-cone footprints replace derivatives.
  Needs: Spectrum slope variance per band; a pixel footprint (screen derivatives or ray cones).
  Sources: <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/Advances%202025%20-%20Raytracing%20the%20world%20of%20Assassin's%20Creed%20Shadows.pdf>; <https://inria.hal.science/inria-00443630>
- **Displaced water in the ray structure (refit, rebuild or cluster acceleration structures)** (demo; shipped in not shipped; confidence low). Three options: refit or rebuild a BLAS each frame for the displaced LOD grid; use NVIDIA cluster acceleration structures (VK_NV_cluster_acceleration_structure), where the vk_tessellated_clusters sample tessellates and displaces clusters each frame; or keep water out of the BVH and fake it (Remix has a displacement PSR option, off by default).
  Fixes: Lets other surfaces reflect the sea, water cast shadows, and rays hit the actual wave surface. Cost: Not published: the sample gives no build times. Fails: The sample forces facet shading with tessellation, caps clusters at 256 vertices, and culls whole instances, which causes shadow pops. A per-frame BLAS rebuild at kilometre scale is unmeasured. Mismatch between the raster surface and the BVH surface causes self-hit acne.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no.  Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Probably fine: cluster AS feed ordinary BLAS/TLAS, but whether ray queries can traverse them was not verified here.
  Needs: RTX GPU; driver 572.16+ and Vulkan SDK 1.4.309+ for cluster AS.
  Sources: <https://github.com/nvpro-samples/vk_tessellated_clusters>; <https://github.com/NVIDIAGameWorks/dxvk-remix/blob/main/RtxOptions.md>
- **Caustics: procedural from the wave simulation, or ray-traced photon splatting** (shipped; shipped in Unity HDRP (procedural); confidence medium). HDRP computes caustics from a chosen simulation band, projected through a virtual plane at a set distance into a caustics texture of chosen resolution. NVIDIA's UE branch and Ray Tracing Gems II chapter 30 trace photons from lights through refractive meshes or water and splat them, with temporal filtering. The chapter could not be retrieved here.
  Fixes: Light patterns under water. Cost: HDRP: not published. RT caustics: not retrieved. Fails: HDRP caustics ignore current maps and water masks, do not reach transparents underwater, and use the opaque position. Photon methods are noisy and need their own accumulator.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no. The procedural version is deterministic; photon splats need their own temporal filter. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: The photon pass fits compute with ray queries.
  Needs: Wave normals; for RT, water in the BVH.
  Sources: <https://docs.unity3d.com/Packages/com.unity.render-pipelines.high-definition@17.0/manual/water-caustics-in-the-water-system.html>; <https://link.springer.com/chapter/10.1007/978-1-4842-7185-8_30>
- **Froxel volumetric fog with its own jittered temporal reprojection** (shipped; shipped in Assassin's Creed IV, Unreal Engine 4/5, Doom: The Dark Ages (Wronski-based), Frostbite (Hillaire 2015); confidence high). A camera-aligned 3D grid: AC4 used 160x90x64 or 160x90x128. Media density (height falloff, noise, particle injection) is written, each froxel is lit (shadow maps or rays, light lists), and in-scattering and transmittance are integrated front to back into a 3D LUT. Opaque surfaces, every transparent layer and particles read it with one trilinear fetch. UE jitters sub-voxel positions every frame and reprojects heavily, while AC4 listed that as future work. Lumen adds GI to fog through the same froxel final gather.
  Fixes: Fog and shafts that apply identically to opaque and transparent surfaces, stable at low resolution. Cost: AC4: about 1.1 ms total (1.6 ms at double resolution), with density plus lighting about 0.43 ms, on its console target (the platform is not named in the slide text). UE: 1 ms on PS4 at High, 3 ms on a GTX 970 at Epic (8x the voxels). Fails: Lighting trails from fast-changing lights (UE). Low resolution means soft or leaking shadows. The range and slice curve are tied to scene scale. The camera frustum only covers primary rays, so reflection and refraction rays need another path.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no. It has its own history in 3D, which reprojects more easily than 2D; it does not need TAA. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Good: shadow rays per froxel from compute (MegaLights-style), or shadow maps on the raster path.
  Needs: Compute plus 3D textures; shadowing per froxel.
  Sources: <https://bartwronski.com/wp-content/uploads/2014/08/bwronski_volumetric_fog_siggraph2014.pdf>; <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/volumetric-fog-in-unreal-engine>; <https://www.ea.com/frostbite/news/physically-based-unified-volumetric-rendering-in-frostbite>; <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2022/SIGGRAPH2022-Advances-Lumen-Wright%20et%20al.pdf>
- **ReSTIR-sampled froxel volumetrics (RTX Remix) and volumetric ReSTIR (Lin 2021)** (shipped; shipped in RTX Remix runtime (Lin 2021 is a paper); confidence medium). Remix: for each froxel (1/8 screen resolution, 64 exponential slices), pick lights by RIS from 32 candidates. Resample temporally (history capped at M = 2) and spatially (8 neighbours within 0.8 froxels), trace visibility with visibility reuse, and accumulate radiance for up to 128 frames with a firefly clamp. Lin 2021: path-space ReSTIR for heterogeneous volumes, using a cheap biased transmittance in resampling and an unbiased final evaluation of one path per pixel.
  Fixes: Many shadowed lights in fog, with traced visibility and no shadow maps: the RT-first form of froxel fog. Cost: Remix: not published. Lin 2021: 55 ms single scattering and 142 ms three-bounce on the bunny cloud (paper results on an RTX 3090 per the repo; resolution not captured). Fails: The Remix default range of 20 m is a scale dial that the bridge scene would break. Temporal lag across up to 128 frames. The Lin method is far too slow for 60 FPS at 2560x1600.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no. Own reservoirs and accumulation. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Good: Remix is Vulkan compute with ray queries.
  Needs: A light-candidate structure and visibility rays.
  Sources: <https://github.com/NVIDIAGameWorks/dxvk-remix/blob/main/src/dxvk/rtx_render/rtx_global_volumetrics.h>; <https://dqlin.xyz/pubs/2021-sa-VOR/>; <https://github.com/DQLin/VolumetricReSTIRRelease>
- **Fog along traced ray segments (fog at reflection and refraction hits)** (shipped; shipped in Assassin's Creed Shadows; confidence high). At each reflection or refraction hit, apply transmittance and in-scatter for the segment from the ray origin to the hit. AC Shadows uses a simplified single-step evaluation for specular hits only, skips near fog, and skips it for diffuse. The general form is the analytic height-fog integral along an arbitrary segment, integrated to infinity for sky misses.
  Fixes: A far tower fogged in its reflection on the water. This is exactly RT2-33's first bullet. Cost: Not published; cheap (ALU per hit). Fails: The single-step version misses local light shafts inside the segment; it is height fog only.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no. Deterministic. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: Good.
  Needs: The fog parameters available in the hit shading.
  Sources: <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/Advances%202025%20-%20Raytracing%20the%20world%20of%20Assassin's%20Creed%20Shadows.pdf>
- **Sky and aerial-perspective LUTs (Hillaire 2020)** (shipped; shipped in Unreal Engine (Sky Atmosphere); confidence medium). Transmittance, multiple-scattering and sky-view LUTs, plus a small camera-frustum aerial-perspective volume, give atmospheric fog to infinity.
  Fixes: Fog on the sky and at kilometre distances without a far-clip hack. Cost: Not re-read here (the paper PDF exceeded the fetch limit). Fails: The aerial-perspective volume covers only the camera frustum, so reflection and refraction hits outside it need the analytic path.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no.  Fit with Vulkan ray queries: 
  Sources: <https://sebh.github.io/publications/egsr2020.pdf>; <https://github.com/sebh/UnrealEngineSkyAtmosphere>
- **Heterogeneous volumes (UE)** (shipped; shipped in Unreal Engine 5 (experimental); confidence medium). Sparse volume textures are ray-marched with a dense internal lighting cache (up to 1024x1024x512) and marched shadows. They can composite with translucency and are supported by the path tracer.
  Fixes: Smoke and explosions as real media rather than cards. Cost: Not published; scaled through the lighting downsample factor, step factor and shading rate. Fails: Experimental: blocky at the default shading rate, dropped voxels under memory pressure, and problems where volumes overlap.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: unknown.  Fit with Vulkan ray queries: 
  Sources: <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/heterogeneous-volumes-in-unreal-engine>
- **Light glows: fog in-scatter plus bloom/convolution instead of billboards** (shipped; shipped in Unreal Engine (bloom and volumetric fog light scattering); confidence medium). Physically, the halo around a lamp is (a) in-scattering of that lamp in the medium near the view ray, which a froxel volume or an analytic airlight integral gives, plus (b) the camera or eye point-spread function, which is bloom. UE's standard bloom is a Gaussian mip chain that is cheap but brightens the image overall; convolution bloom uses an FFT with a kernel image to model glare in the eye or lens. Billboard glows with fixed cones are the legacy form.
  Fixes: Glows follow each light's own emission and the medium, with no fixed side-glow shares (RT2-33's audit item). Cost: Convolution bloom is costly and suited to cinematics or high-end hardware (UE); no ms. Analytic airlight: not measured here. Fails: Gaussian bloom is not energy conserving. In-scatter needs the lamp's shadowing in the medium.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: no.  Fit with Vulkan ray queries: 
  Sources: <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/bloom-in-unreal-engine>; <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/volumetric-fog-in-unreal-engine>
- **Upscaler/denoiser transparency hooks (evidence of what TAA-class passes need)** (public_sdk; shipped in not shipped; confidence high). DLSS RR takes an optional transparency overlay, which is only upscaled, never denoised. It also takes a 'colour before transparency' guide against particle ghosting and asks for refraction to be applied to guide buffers, with background motion vectors, and for constant guides on noisy glossy refraction. FSR asks for reactive and transparency-and-composition masks, or it ghosts and blurs particles and alpha-blended surfaces.
  Fixes: Ghosting of transparents in temporal passes. Cost: n/a Fails: Even vendor temporal passes cannot clean noisy transparent signals; they expect transparents to arrive clean.
  Leans on TAA or an upscaler: yes. This is the strongest vendor evidence for rule 3: transparent and particle signals must be converged before the final temporal pass. Fit with Vulkan ray queries: 
  Sources: <https://github.com/NVIDIA/DLSS/blob/main/doc/DLSS-RR%20Integration%20Guide.pdf>; <https://gpuopen.com/manuals/fidelityfx_sdk/techniques/super-resolution-temporal/>

### G.3 The frontier (published, not yet shipped)

- **AVBOIT: froxel-extinction OIT shared with the fog framework** (Drobot, SIGGRAPH 2025 Advances, 2025; evidence strong). Order-independent transparency at close to no-OIT cost, with correct glass-behind-glass occlusion, coloured transmittance at about 1.05x, and early culling of hidden transparent pixels.
  Evidence: Measured at 4K on PS5 in the same framework: AVBOIT+cull 4.967 ms vs WBOIT 5.908, MBOIT 5.705 and no OIT 4.4. Content test: mono 2.95 and RGB 3.11 vs 2.8 ms. GTX 1060 at 1080p: 12.2 vs 11.2 ms. Side-by-side captures at 150 m distance show MBOIT and WBOIT failing to occlude background glass. Could beat: WBOIT (RageV's planned tier for smoke, particles and deeper panes) and MBOIT (Alan Wake 2). Cost: About 0.5-1 ms over no-OIT at 4K on PS5; tens of MB of VRAM. Risk: Slice-curve parameters are scale dials unless derived from occupancy, which AVBOIT already does adaptively. Close-depth layering is lost. The exact shipped title is not named.
  Sources: <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/AVBOIT_SIG2025_MDROBOT-final.pdf>
- **Primary Surface Replacement for transmission (PSTR)** (NVIDIA RTX Remix, NRD, 2022; evidence moderate). Smooth glass and still water become see-through for the entire opaque pipeline: the refracted surface gets full RT lighting, GI, reflections and their accumulators, with no separate refraction pass.
  Evidence: Open-source Remix runtime defaults (pstrMaxBounces 10) and NRD's PSR guidance on virtual depth, motion and disocclusion thresholds. No published cost or quality study. Could beat: RT2-31's plan of a dedicated refraction pass plus its own accumulator on the transmissive layer, and RT2-32's full shading of a seabed that is then covered. Cost: Not published. Risk: Only one path per pixel, so the reflection lobe still needs a signal. Animated wave normals break reprojection. Rough transmission is approximated.
  Sources: <https://github.com/NVIDIAGameWorks/dxvk-remix/blob/main/src/dxvk/rtx_render/rtx_options.h>; <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD>
- **ReSTIR per froxel for volumetric lighting** (NVIDIA RTX Remix; Lin, Wyman, Yuksel 2021, 2022; evidence moderate). Many shadowed lights in fog with traced visibility and no shadow maps, stabilised by ReSTIR plus long accumulation.
  Evidence: Shipped defaults in the open-source Remix runtime. Lin 2021 is the path-traced version: 55 ms single scattering on an RTX 3090 (resolution not captured). Could beat: Froxel fog lit by shadow maps (UE classic) and the post-TAA single fog pass RageV has today. Cost: Not published for Remix. Risk: The default 20 m range is a scale dial. 128-frame histories lag moving lights.
  Sources: <https://github.com/NVIDIAGameWorks/dxvk-remix/blob/main/src/dxvk/rtx_render/rtx_global_volumetrics.h>; <https://dqlin.xyz/pubs/2021-sa-VOR/>
- **MegaLights stochastic lighting of the lighting volume and fog** (Epic Games, 2025; evidence moderate). The same importance sampling and shadow-ray budget as opaque surfaces, applied per voxel for fog and back translucent layers, denoised, and shared between the two.
  Evidence: UE documentation and the SIGGRAPH 2025 talk (the slides exceeded the fetch limit, so no ms here). Could beat: Per-light shadow maps in fog, and RageV's unlit or loop-lit particles. Cost: Not published here; the front layer can double lighting cost. Risk: Blurring and ghosting under complex lighting; no water support.
  Sources: <https://dev.epicgames.com/documentation/en-us/unreal-engine/megalights-in-unreal-engine>; <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/MegaLights_Stochastic_Direct_Lighting_2025.pdf>
- **Denoising-free glass lit from two-level radiance caches (NRD sample)** (NVIDIA, 2024; evidence moderate). Multi-bounce glass reflection and refraction with no denoiser: rays end in the previous denoised frame (L1) or in SHARC (L2).
  Evidence: NRD-Sample README: about 110-150 FPS in Bistro at 1440p on an RTX 4080 for the whole sample. Could beat: Separate glass accumulators. Cost: Not isolated. Risk: Relies on dithering resolved by TAA or an upscaler, which conflicts with rule 3.
  Sources: <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD-Sample>; <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD>
- **Cluster acceleration structures for displaced, tessellated surfaces** (NVIDIA nvpro-samples, 2025; evidence weak). Per-frame displaced tessellation can be put into the ray structure cheaply.
  Evidence: nvpro sample only; no build-time numbers. Could beat: Leaving the sea out of the ray structure (today), or a full per-frame BLAS rebuild. Cost: Not published. Risk: Facet shading, the 256-vertex cluster limit, whole-instance culling, NVIDIA-only extension.
  Sources: <https://github.com/nvpro-samples/vk_tessellated_clusters>
- **Wavelet transparency** (Aizenshtein, Smal, McGuire, 2022; evidence weak). A-buffer-like smoke coverage at moment-transparency cost, plus a better refraction approximation.
  Evidence: arXiv preprint claims only; no independent numbers captured. Could beat: MBOIT/WBOIT Cost: Claimed comparable to MBOIT. Risk: Unshipped.
  Sources: <https://arxiv.org/abs/2201.00094>

### G.4 The survey's recommended baseline and candidates

The survey's view before the comparison looked at RageV's code. The roadmap's items took these as their starting baselines where the comparison and verification agreed.

- **Baseline: The nearest transmissive surface (glass or water) extracted into a compact layer that runs the same RT passes as opaque (reflections, direct light), each signal with its own history.** Why: This is what UE (the front-layer reflection pipeline) and Snowdrop (a second, separately denoised water/clearcoat layer) ship. It removes the rays traced inside the draw. RT2-31 already plans this shape, and it goes further than UE, which turns the denoiser off. Shipped by: Unreal Engine 5 (Lumen High Quality Translucency Reflections), Snowdrop (Avatar: Frontiers of Pandora), Battlefield V (more rays on water). Measured by: Per-pixel diff of the garage close-up glass and the pier against the reference renderer. Convergence under --aa=none, both still and moving. Layer cost at RT2-19's measured coverages (against today's +0.45 ms at the close-up). History VRAM compared with today's 96 bytes per pixel at full frame size.
- **Baseline: Every layer behind the nearest, plus particles, lit from the existing radiance/irradiance cache or a froxel irradiance volume, with no per-pixel rays.** Why: UE, id Tech 8 and Frostbite GIBS all light back layers and particles from a filtered cache. This would remove the in-draw back-pane reflection rays that are RageV's one named TAA exception (D11). Shipped by: Unreal Engine 5, Doom: The Dark Ages, Frostbite (GIBS). Measured by: Diff the back panes against today's in-draw rays and against the reference. Check convergence without TAA. Measure the cost of the cache lookups against the current in-draw rays at the garage close-up.
- **Baseline: A transmittance-integral OIT for smoke, particles and deeper panes, with the depth weighting derived from content rather than a fixed metre range.** Why: Alan Wake 2 ships MBOIT and Call of Duty ships AVBOIT. WBOIT's published weight uses a fixed depth scale, which conflicts with the general-solutions rule, and it cannot hide glass behind glass. Shipped by: Alan Wake 2 (MBOIT), Call of Duty (AVBOIT; BO4 A-buffer before it). Measured by: A,B,B,A timing of WBOIT against MBOIT or AVBOIT at 2560x1600 on the garage and bridge, plus diff images of layered glass and smoke against a sorted reference.
- **Baseline: Water as a surface generator (spectrum/FFT bands on an LOD grid), with pixel-footprint-filtered normals and roughness, screen-space refraction with backdrop plus a per-channel absorption/scattering medium, and reflections through the shared reflection pipeline.** Why: This is what UE Single Layer Water, AC Shadows (water filtering), HDRP (FFT bands) and Snowdrop (reflection layer) ship. RT refraction of water appears only in Remix-class path tracers. Shipped by: Unreal Engine 5, Assassin's Creed Shadows, Unity HDRP, Avatar: Frontiers of Pandora, Horizon Forbidden West (surface generator). Measured by: check_glint_flicker.py on the bridge without TAA. Diffs at the three composed bridge cameras. Sea cost at Headland against the proposed at most 4 ms at native resolution.
- **Baseline: Fog as a froxel volume with its own jittered temporal accumulation (lit by the shared light candidates with rays), plus analytic height fog along every traced segment and aerial perspective to infinity. Applied before the final temporal pass, to opaque and transmissive surfaces alike.** Why: UE, id Tech 8, AC4 and Frostbite ship froxel fog with their own history. AC Shadows fogs specular hits. MegaLights and Remix light froxels with rays. Shipped by: Unreal Engine 5, Doom: The Dark Ages, Assassin's Creed Shadows, RTX Remix. Measured by: Fog-off frames byte-identical. A Headland diff with the far bridge fogged in its sea reflection. Froxel pass ms at 2560x1600. Trail length from a moving lamp at a fixed history weight.
- **Baseline: Particles lit from the cache plus direct light from the light candidates. Particles and emissive VFX visible in reflections through an order-independent weighted accumulation in the ray query.** Why: UE (TLV plus MegaLights), id Tech 8 (froxel irradiance) and BFV (particle TLAS with WBOIT weights) ship this. Shipped by: Unreal Engine 5, Doom: The Dark Ages, Battlefield V, RTX Remix. Measured by: A lit-particle diff and the cost of the particle pass. Reflection cost with and without the particle traversal.
- **Baseline: Tinted shadows from shadow-ray transmittance through glass and water, denoised like other shadows.** Why: Shipped as an option in Remix and NvRTX. SIGMA shows the denoising cost is small (0.45 ms at 1440p on an RTX 4080). Shipped by: RTX Remix (optional), NvRTX / RTXDI (optional). Measured by: Shadow-pass ms with transmissive instances in the TLAS, and the tinted-shadow diff against the reference renderer.
- **Candidate: PSTR/PSR: replace the primary surface with the refracted (or reflected) surface for smooth glass and calm water, so the existing opaque passes and accumulators shade what is behind.** Why better: It could remove the planned refraction pass and its history, and stop shading the covered seabed, with only one set of accumulators. Evidence: Shipped in the Remix runtime (default 10 transmission bounces); NRD documents the virtual-motion rules. No cost or quality numbers published. Risk: Only one path per pixel; the reflection lobe still needs the transmissive-layer signal. Wave normals break reprojection. Measured by: On the garage glass and the pier: compare PSTR against RT2-31's separate refraction pass for ms, VRAM and per-pixel diff against the reference, still and with a moving camera under --aa=none. Measure disocclusion and ghosting on wavy water.
- **Candidate: AVBOIT in place of WBOIT for smoke, particles and deeper panes, sharing its froxel extinction with the fog volume.** Why better: Measured faster than WBOIT and MBOIT at 4K on PS5 (4.97 vs 5.91 and 5.71 ms). It occludes glass behind glass correctly, handles RGB at about 1.05x, and derives its slices from occupancy, which fits the general-solutions rule. Evidence: Drobot, SIGGRAPH 2025 Advances (measured tables). Risk: Implementation size. The close-overlap layering that artists may rely on is lost. Measured by: An A,B,B,A timing against RageV's WBOIT at 2560x1600 on the RTX 5070 Ti Laptop, and a diff of the car windows and bridge smoke against a sorted reference.
- **Candidate: Back panes read the radiance cache instead of tracing in-draw rays.** Why better: It would remove the D11 TAA exception and lower the cost; this is how UE ships it. Evidence: UE front-layer plus radiance cache (Lumen 2022). Risk: Glossy-only reflections on the back panes; the owner decides whether that is acceptable. Measured by: Owner-judged diff of the back panes against today's rays at the garage close-up, and convergence under --aa=none.
- **Candidate: A froxel volume lit by ReSTIR (or MegaLights-style fixed rays per froxel) from the shared light-candidate structure, with its own accumulator.** Why better: RT-first; no shadow maps; the lamps' own emission makes the glows. It replaces the post-TAA fog pass and the billboard glows. Evidence: Remix runtime defaults; UE MegaLights docs. No ms published. Risk: Scale dials (range, slice curve) must be derived from the camera and the content. Temporal lag. Measured by: Froxel pass ms at 2560x1600 with the bridge's 190 lights. Moving-lamp trail length. Glow diff against a reference with fog path tracing.
- **Candidate: Water roughness from the spectrum's unresolved slope variance under the pixel or ray-cone footprint.** Why better: This is the general form of today's Cox-Munk footprint constants; one estimate applies to primary, reflected and refracted views. Evidence: AC Shadows ships footprint filtering (not spectrum-derived); Bruneton 2010 is a paper (not re-read). Risk: Needs per-band variance from the generator. Measured by: check_glint_flicker.py without TAA, and diffs at Glitter and Headland, against the current constants.
- **Candidate: A displaced water proxy in the TLAS via a per-frame refit of an LOD grid or cluster AS.** Why better: The bridge metal can reflect the sea, and water can shadow and refract with real hits. Evidence: nvpro sample only; no numbers. Risk: A mismatch between the raster surface and the BVH surface; the extension is NVIDIA-only. Measured by: BLAS build or refit ms per frame at kilometre scale, TLAS memory, and ray cost at Headland.

### G.5 RageV against them, topic by topic

- **Nearest see-through surface on the shared RT passes (front layer).** Comparison: equal. Verified: equal. Confidence: high.
  RageV: RT-13: the nearest glass pane goes into its own single-sample G-buffer layer. DirectTrace and the whole reflection chain then run again on it: budget, trace, resolve, record, re-light, filters, accumulate and three blurs. Histories are full-frame: 6 RGBA16F lanes x 2 = 96 B/px for reflections, and about 72 B/px for direct light. The glass call passes a literal true for the moving layer. Water sits on a second, separate layer (WaterSurface, RGBA32F lanes) with its own private passes. Code: `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:662-684`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:2668-2682`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:2729-2737`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:2786-2791`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:3007-3027`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:4599-4613`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:6079-6093`
  Top engines: UE5 Lumen depth-peels the frontmost translucent layer into a minimal G-buffer and re-runs the reflection pipeline only on those pixels, forced to mirror with the denoiser disabled. Snowdrop (Avatar) keeps a second reflection layer for water and clearcoat, traced and denoised separately. That effectively doubles the RT reflection cost, so it ships at PC max settings only (2.132 ms at 2160p on an RTX 4080). Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/lumen_2022.txt:2855-2878`; `build/rt_research/sources/snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:1043-1054`; `build/rt_research/sources/snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:1233-1243`
  Why: Same design as Snowdrop: one history per layer, which UE lacks because it leaves the front layer's noise to TSR. RageV is more stable than UE but costs more memory and time than both: a full-frame duplicate chain for a layer that covers a small share of the garage frame, and water on a second, private layer.
- **Panes behind the nearest and other deeper layers.** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: medium.
  RageV: Lit by the old in-shader light loop. Outside the baked field it uses the S4 sampler, whose salt moves only under TAA jitter. Inside the field it traces one soft-shadow ray per casting lamp. Reflections come from 1-8 GGX lobe rays per pixel inside the draw, seeded by the frame counter, with hit NEE also seeded by the frame counter. There is no accumulator: this is the owner's named TAA exception, D11. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:4804-4819`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:6096-6125`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:2838-2842`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:3370`
  Top engines: Lumen: every layer after the front one interpolates glossy reflections from the World Radiance Cache. The probes it needs are marked by rasterising the translucent surfaces at low resolution, and no pixel traces rays. MegaLights: translucency is lit from a world-space voxel grid of SH2 (spherical harmonics), with K shadow-ray samples per voxel plus temporal and spatial filtering. Glass specular comes from the SH's dominant direction. id Tech 8: a froxel SH2 irradiance volume. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/lumen_2022.txt:2880-2890`; `build/rt_research/sources/megalights_2025.txt:1326-1340`; `build/rt_research/sources/megalights_2025.txt:1429-1518`; <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/SOUSA_SIGGRAPH_2025_Final.pdf>
  Why: RageV's panes are sharper within a frame, but they converge only through TAA (rule 3), and their cost grows with the number of lamps and panes. The others are deterministic at a fixed cost and pay with lower-frequency lighting.
- **See-through surfaces in the ray structure, and tinted shadows.** Comparison: missing. Verified: missing. Confidence: high.
  RageV: Blended materials are skipped when the TLAS is built, and no water body is ever inserted. So glass casts no shadow, car windows are missing from floor reflections, and the bridge's metal cannot reflect the sea. A candidate/confirm traversal loop exists for alpha-tested geometry. BLAS geometry flags set only OPAQUE and never NO_DUPLICATE_ANY_HIT. Code: `RageV/src/RageV/Scene/Scene.cpp:2892-2910`, `RageV/src/RageV/Renderer/Material.h:80-83`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/ray_shadow_trace.glsl:100-203`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/ray_shadow_trace.glsl:243-253`, `RageV/src/Platform/Vulkan/VulkanResources.cpp:979`
  Top engines: Lumen's HWRT surface-cache path skips translucent meshes entirely (MaxTranslucentSkipCount), so shipped Lumen reflections also lose the glass. RTX Remix and NvRTX offer coloured translucent shadows, off by default, through any-hit shaders. NRD's SIGMA translucency shadow denoiser costs 0.45 ms at 1440p on an RTX 4080. Sources: `build/rt_research/sources/lumen_2022.txt:1749-1766`; <https://github.com/NVIDIAGameWorks/dxvk-remix/blob/main/RtxOptions.md>; <https://github.com/NVIDIA-RTX/NRD>
  Why: Tinted shadows, and water that rays can see, exist only as options in path-tracing runtimes. Against mainstream shipped reflections (Lumen), RageV is equal in missing the glass.
- **Glass composite equation (coverage and tint).** Comparison: behind. Verified: behind. Confidence: high.
  RageV: coverage = alpha + reflectance*(1-alpha), where reflectance is the largest channel of the reflected radiance itself. So windows hide the interior behind bright reflections and show it behind dim ones. One scalar revealage: glass can dim what is behind it but never tint it. Code: `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:6313-6316`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/include/pbr_fragment.glsl:6477-6486`, `RageVEditor/assets/shaders/oit_resolve.rvshader:48-65`
  Top engines: Standard practice: the transmission weight is 1-F from Fresnel or directional albedo, times an RGB transmission colour. AVBOIT keeps RGB extinction for coloured sources at about 1.05x the cost. Sources: <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/AVBOIT_SIG2025_MDROBOT-final.pdf>; <https://jcgt.org/published/0002/02/09/>

  RageV: WBOIT with weight = coverage x clamp(k/d). k is 20 m for meshes and 90 m for particles, which contradicts the code's own 'same unit-weight distance' comment. The 1/d exponent was measured against sorted alpha. The nearest pane and every sea crossing go into the same weighted average: at Glitter, three to five crossings are averaged, about 10% too bright. Black Ops 4 used an exact 9-layer A-buffer (about 530-754 MB at 4K). UE and Unity default to sorting per object. Wavelet OIT is a paper only. Sources: <https://advances.realtimerendering.com/s2025/content/AVBOIT_SIG2025_MDROBOT-final.pdf>; <https://www.remedygames.com/article/how-northlight-makes-alan-wake-2-shine>

  RageV: Only water refracts. In RT mode, a Snell ray is traced for each 2x2 quad inside the water draw, with the IOR hard-coded at 0.7519. It is lit by the simplified ShadeTraced (one NEE light per hit, seeded by the frame counter), reaches min(300 m, -ln(floor)/sigma_min), and is used that frame with no accumulator. The quad's tracing lane moves only while TAA jitters. In raster mode, one push along the refracted ray, clamped to 0.25-4 m, is reprojected into the backdrop, with a fallback when it lands off screen or on an occluder. The seabed underneath is G-buffered, traced and lit, then covered. Glass does not refract.
