### G.6 Candidates that could beat both

- **G-BB1: Refraction in screen space first, traced only where that fails: march the refracted ray across the backdrop's depth and read the fully lit, accumulated opaque frame. A traced ray, with its own accumulator, is used only for points where the march leaves the screen, lands in front of the surface, or fails the local depth-gradient test.**
  Why better than both: UE Single Layer Water uses screen space only, so it misses bottoms that are occluded or off screen. RageV re-shades every sea pixel's bottom with a cheaper shade, per quad, with no accumulator, and throws away the lit seabed. The hybrid gets opaque-quality lighting on the common path, correct geometry where screen space fails, and far fewer rays. It is the screen-space-then-RT pattern that id Tech 8, AC Shadows and Snowdrop ship for reflections, applied to refraction. Evidence: Snowdrop and AC Shadows ship screen-space-then-world-space reflections. RageV's own raster form already has the guards (pbr_fragment.glsl:6440-6447) but uses a single push clamped to 0.25-4 m instead of a march. Looking down at water, the refracted ray bends toward the normal, so its bottom point is usually on screen. Risk: A seam between screen-space and traced answers on calm water (Snowdrop reports this for mirror reflections). The raster march needs the backdrop before the layer composite. Cost: Expected below today's per-quad traced refraction in the water draw (part of the 5.58 ms Transparent pass at Headland, 1600x900). Unmeasured.
  The comparison's gate: Pier (shallow) and Headland, per-pixel diff against the reference renderer, calm and wavy: within grain, with no structure along the boundary between screen-space and traced points. Report the share of traced fallback points. A,B,B,A against today's in-draw rays. Converges under --aa=none.
  Verification: **plausible.** id Tech 8's pattern (phase1) traces in world space for GI and then reads screen lighting at the hit. That is not screen-march-then-trace for reflections, so drop it as a precedent. Snowdrop itself reports seams where screen-space and world-space hits differ, so the seam gate is load-bearing. UE has optional HWRT translucent refraction, so 'screen space only' holds for Single Layer Water specifically. It needs a measurement gate.
  In the roadmap: RT2-31 step 31e.
- **G-BB2: Composite the nearest see-through surface exactly, per pixel: final = its radiance + RGB transmittance x the backdrop. Weighted-blended OIT is kept only for what lies behind it: back panes, particles and smoke.**
  Why better than both: Today, WBOIT averages the nearest pane with the panes behind it and averages three to five sea crossings (about 10% too bright at Glitter). MBOIT and AVBOIT fix ordering approximately at +0.5-1.3 ms at 4K on PS5. An exact front layer removes the dominant error in this content, one pane or the sea, at close to no cost, because the layer is already rasterised for RT-13. Evidence: RageV already rasterises the nearest pane and the sea into single-layer targets (FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:626-684). The failure cases in the AVBOIT paper are glass behind glass, which the exact front layer handles, and the particle_weighted header's measurements show where the WBOIT weight falls short. Risk: Particles and glows need splitting at the layer depth. The back layers keep WBOIT's approximation. Cost: About one full-screen pass; the existing ResolveTransparent is replaced, not added to.
  The comparison's gate: A fixture with glass over glass and a particle in front of glass, against a sorted reference: front-layer error 0, back layers no worse than today. At Glitter the over-brightness is gone (mean and red/blue shown to the owner). Composite cost at most 0.1 ms at 2560x1600.
  Verification: **plausible.** The cost framing is wrong. In the AVBOIT table, WBOIT (5.908) costs more than MBOIT (5.705) and AVBOIT+Cull (4.967), so against RageV's current method those are not '+0.5-1.3 ms'. 'Close to no cost' is unmeasured: the glass layer today holds velocity, normal, albedo and id only, so radiance and RGB-transmittance lanes and a shading step must be added. Particles and glows in front of the layer need their own path; today they draw into the opaque target before the transparent pass (G-C9).
  In the roadmap: RT2-31 step 31b.
- **G-BB3: Shadow rays through glass and water in one query: the candidate loop multiplies the RGB transmittance of each transmissive candidate in any order and never confirms them. Any confirmed opaque hit ends the ray with TerminateOnFirstHit and makes visibility zero. The only requirement is VK_GEOMETRY_NO_DUPLICATE_ANY_HIT_INVOCATION_BIT_KHR on transmissive geometry.**
  Why better than both: RageV's glass casts no shadow today. Remix and NvRTX need an any-hit stage, which RageV does not have, and phase 1 assumed the product must be bounded by the opaque hit (two queries). For visibility rays that bound is unnecessary: an opaque hit zeroes the result whatever order the candidates came in. That makes it one traversal at the cost of the existing alpha-test loop. Evidence: ray_shadow_trace.glsl:192-203 already runs a candidate loop, and 243-253 already uses TerminateOnFirstHit. VulkanResources.cpp:979 sets only OPAQUE_BIT, so duplicate candidate reports are allowed today; that is harmless for an alpha test but would double-count a product. Risk: Cost where many transmissive surfaces overlap. The shadow ratio must carry RGB (RT2-24). Cost: One transmittance lookup per transmissive candidate; unmeasured.
  The comparison's gate: The camp (no transmissive material) byte-identical. Garage DirectTrace within +5% (A,B,B,A at 2560x1600). A tinted-pane test scene: floor shadow diffed against the reference renderer, near zero.
  Verification: **partly true.** NO_DUPLICATE is not 'the only requirement'. Glass and water must first enter the TLAS with a mask bit of their own. Every ray type that must not see them (AO with gl_RayFlagsOpaqueEXT, reflection, GI, irradiance_fill, water_shade) must drop that bit or branch in RayTraverse. Water needs a ray stand-in because its displacement exists only in the vertex stage. The flag's per-mesh BLAS granularity must be resolved (G-C2).
  In the roadmap: RT2-31 step 31a.
- **G-BB4: One analytic height-fog integral, applied to every path segment and aware of each segment's medium: opaque surfaces from the nearest see-through surface (or the camera), the layer from the camera, each WBOIT fragment by T*C + coverage*S, every reflection and air-side refraction segment, glows from the camera to the lamp, and the sky integrated to infinity. Segments behind a water surface use the water medium, not air.**
  Why better than both: RageV fogs once after TAA over the opaque depth, so reflections get none and glass is fogged at the depth behind it. AC Shadows uses one simplified step at specular hits and skips near fog. Froxel volumes cover only the camera frustum, so reflection rays need another path anyway. The closed form is exact for height fog, has no noise, costs almost nothing, and removes the sea's dependence on FogFloor. Evidence: The closed form, floor clip and sky branch already exist at fog.rvshader:174-244. For one thin layer, fogging the layer's premultiplied colour as T*C + a*S over an already-fogged background is algebraically exact. Risk: Still no lamp light scattered in the fog; that stays WR-11. Rough reflections take fog at one representative distance. Cost: ALU only; the post pass is deleted.
  The comparison's gate: Fog off: byte-identical. Headland: the far bridge fogged in its sea reflection, diffed against a reference renderer with height fog along segments, within grain. With FogFloor ignored in a test arm, no haze appears under the sea. Cost no worse than today's Fog pass (A,B,B,A).
  Verification: **plausible.** 'Algebraically exact' fails with the MaxOpacity cap. The fog is (1-exp(-optical))*MaxOpacity (fog.rvshader:~249), which does not compose over segments, and the bridge profile uses FogMaxOpacity 0.42. Direction-dependent inscatter (SkyAffect/SkyOcclusion) must be taken per segment direction. Also the inscatter is a colour, not lit and not shadowed, so it is exact only for RageV's own fog model.
  In the roadmap: RT2-33, with the cap applied once per camera path; that changes what the dial means (its value stays 0.42), so it is adopted only after the owner's named live review at Headland.
- **G-BB5: The panes behind the nearest, when mirror-smooth, get one fixed mirror ray each: no lobe draw, and a hit shaded only by the deterministic part of the hit contract (stored light plus emissive). Rougher panes read stored light. Nothing then relies on TAA.**
  Why better than both: UE's deeper layers get only glossy, cache-interpolated reflections. RageV's are sharper but random every frame and averaged only by TAA (the D11 exception). A single mirror ray is exact for smooth glass and holds still in every AA mode, and it costs fewer rays than today's 1-8 lobe rays with NEE. Evidence: The noise today comes from the GGX lobe seed (pbr_fragment.glsl:3370-3378) and the hit NEE seed (2838-2842), both driven by the frame counter. A mirror ray has no lobe noise. Risk: Reflected lamps lose their sampled direct light where they are not emissive geometry. This is a taste call for the owner (D11). Cost: At most one ray per back-pane pixel, against today's up to 8.
  The comparison's gate: Garage close-up: owner-judged diff against today and against the reference. Under --aa=none and MSAA 4x, the panes behind the nearest do not change frame to frame on a still camera, and speckle_count.py on their pixels at the owner swing is no worse than on the nearest pane.
  Verification: **plausible.** 'Stored light' at a hit has to exist first. The one world-space light store is off in every shipped scene and read by no ray hit (RT-SERIES-2.md:1345), so today the deterministic part is probe/bake plus emissive, and Realtime lamps would drop out of back-pane reflections. A single mirror ray is exact only as roughness goes to 0, so the mirror-tier threshold must come from lobe width against pixel footprint.
  In the roadmap: RT2-31 step 31f (D28).
- **G-BB6: Per-particle light accumulator: light each particle once per frame at its centre from the candidate structure and stored light, and average it in the particle's own GPU state slot. The history moves with the particle, so nothing is reprojected.**
  Why better than both: It avoids the camera-dependent instability that MegaLights reports for froxel lighting and the cell structure of a world grid, at a cost that scales with the number of particles. Evidence: particle_sim.rvshader keeps a persistent per-slot Particle state (Spin.zw unused). MegaLights reports froxel lighting of translucency as unstable when the camera moves (megalights_2025.txt:1383-1402). Risk: CPU-simulated emitters need a stable particle id. Cost: One sample and one shadow ray per particle; unmeasured.
  The comparison's gate: Only if the translucency cache shows cell structure on the camp smoke: the camp smoke diffed against the reference renderer, with emitter_lag.py no worse.
  Verification: **plausible.** Only GPU-simulated emitters have a state slot, and SimulateOnGpu defaults to false (Components.h:1053). CPU emitters need an equivalent. Two floats hold RGB+count only as packed halves. One centre sample cannot vary light across a large sprite, and the history lags a particle crossing a shadow edge. Needs a gate against the world-grid cache.
  In the roadmap: RT2-48, its per-particle arm.

RageV's own raster form already has the guards (pbr_fragment.glsl:6440-6447) but uses a single push clamped to 0.25-4 m instead of a march. Looking down at water, the refracted ray bends toward the normal, so its bottom point is usually on screen. |

The failure cases in the AVBOIT paper are glass behind glass, which the exact front layer handles, and the particle_weighted header's measurements show where the WBOIT weight falls short. |

VulkanResources.cpp:979 sets only OPAQUE_BIT, so duplicate candidate reports are allowed today; that is harmless for an alpha test but would double-count a product. |
| G-BB4 | beats_both | major | plausible | The closed form, floor clip and sky branch already exist at fog.rvshader:174-244. For one thin layer, fogging the layer's premultiplied colour as T*C + a*S over an already-fogged background is algebraically exact. |
| G-BB5 | beats_both | major | plausible | The noise today comes from the GGX lobe seed (pbr_fragment.glsl:3370-3378) and the hit NEE seed (2838-2842), both driven by the frame counter. A mirror ray has no lobe noise. |
| G-BB6 | beats_both | major | plausible | particle_sim.rvshader keeps a persistent per-slot Particle state (Spin.zw unused). MegaLights reports froxel lighting of translucency as unstable when the camera moves (megalights_2025.txt:1383-1402). |

- **G-C1** (confirmed). Claim: Glass coverage is taken from the largest channel of the reflected radiance, not from reflectance, so window opacity follows the lighting level.
  Evidence: pbr_fragment.glsl:6313 `reflected = prefiltered * (F0*envBRDF.x+envBRDF.y)*occlusion*screenOcclusion` (prefiltered is probe or traced radiance, 5927-6159); 6316 `reflectance = clamp(max(reflected.r,g,b),0,1)`; 6477 `coverage = alpha + reflectance*(1-alpha)`. The glass-signal variant (6079-6095) only swaps prefiltered, so it is still radiance. Water sets alpha=1 (6471), so this matters for glass only.
- **G-C2** (confirmed). Claim: BLAS geometry sets only VK_GEOMETRY_OPAQUE_BIT_KHR and never NO_DUPLICATE_ANY_HIT_INVOCATION, so a non-opaque candidate may be reported more than once. That is harmless for the alpha test but would double-count a transmittance product.
  Evidence: VulkanResources.cpp:979 `flags = geometry.Opaque ? OPAQUE_BIT : 0`; every BLAS is built with Opaque=true (RHITypes.h:201; Mesh.cpp:219-227); per-instance FORCE_NO_OPAQUE only for masked and emitter instances (VulkanResources.cpp:1263, RayShadows.cpp:283-289). The Vulkan spec (raytraversal.adoc, 'Triangle and Generated Intersection Candidates') says the confirmation operation for non-opaque candidates 'may be executed multiple times ... unless VK_GEOMETRY_NO_DUPLICATE_ANY_HIT_INVOCATION_BIT_KHR', and the same subsection describes the ray-query return. So a ray query can see a candidate twice.
  Correction: The flag is set per BLAS geometry, and a BLAS is built once per mesh and shared by every material that uses it (RHITypes.h:222-231). So 'on transmissive geometry' means either the flag on every BLAS or a separate BLAS for meshes used as glass.
- **G-C3** (confirmed). Claim: For visibility rays, an unordered product of transmissive candidate transmittances, with TerminateOnFirstHit on opaque, is exact; no opaque-bounded second query is needed. It requires no-duplicate reporting.
  Evidence: Logic checked. With TerminateOnFirstHit (ray_shadow_trace.glsl:250-251), a committed opaque or confirmed masked hit ends traversal, and the result is 0 whatever candidates came before it. With no opaque hit, every candidate in [tMin,tMax] is reported, so an order-independent product is exact once each candidate is reported only once. A second query bounded by the opaque hit is only needed for closest-hit or radiance rays, not for binary visibility.
  Correction: The product is per triangle crossing, so a closed pane with two faces multiplies twice. That is a material modelling choice, not a traversal error.
- **G-C4** (partly true). Claim: The bridge's accepted sea mirror gets its white shine from clamping samples at probe*8 + ReflectionFloor 0.5 before accumulation.
  Evidence: wip/2026-09-21-rt10-and-sea:docs/SEA-ACCUMULATOR-HANDOFF.md lines 54-64 and 73-75: the per-channel bound min(traced, prefiltered*8 + floor) with floor 0.5 'is the white shine', and moving it before the write gives 'white and the lines thin'. bridge_cinematic.rvpostprofile:42 has ReflectionFloor 0.5, which goes to Indirect.z through FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:826. In the working tree, pbr_fragment.glsl:6155-6157 applies the same bound.
  Correction: The accumulator that bounds before accumulating exists only on the unmerged branch (be4abf6; main's water_trace.rvshader has no bound). In the working tree the bound is applied in the draw to each per-quad sample, and only TAA averages the result. The same bound block (6096-6157) also clamps glass's in-draw rays, not only the sea's.
- **G-C5** (confirmed). Claim: Fog runs after TAA over the opaque depth, the sky is fogged to FarClip, and no traced hit carries fog.
  Evidence: TAA resolve is declared at FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:3916 and Fog at 4889, and Fog samples sceneHDR depth. Transparent/blended pipelines write no depth (Renderer3D.cpp:3434), and the sea's depth goes into its own WaterSurface target. fog.rvshader:129-133 fogs background to FarClip. A grep finds 'fog' only in fog, tonemap, debug, dither and a water_backdrop comment; there is none in pbr_fragment.glsl, reflection_*.rvshader or the water trace shaders.
- **G-C6** (confirmed). Claim: The sea mirror and refraction are traced per quad inside the water draw on main (waterTraceScale defaults to 1, so the separate pass is off), and the quad lane walks only when TAA jitter is non-zero.
  Evidence: FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:797-802: waterTraceScale = override ? scale : 1, and settled needs scale>1. Renderer3D.cpp:4361 packs 256*scale, so s5Scale=1 and the in-draw path runs (pbr_fragment.glsl:6071-6076). QuadTraceLane (4071-4078): lane 4 (every lane) if RayRates.x<1.5, else it walks (frame&3) only when Jitter is non-zero, otherwise lane 0. Refraction uses the same lane (6378-6398) under RV_RAY_REFRACTION, which is on for SampleProject (RayTracedWaterRefraction: true).
  Correction: 'Per quad' holds only when RtOptimisation is not Off (RayRate 2.0 for Quality/Balanced/Performance, 1.0 for Off: RenderSettings.h:238-244). The preset's ReflectionScale column (2 or 4) is never read by FrameGraphBuilder.
- **G-C7** (confirmed). Claim: Back panes cast 1-8 GGX lobe rays per pixel in the draw, with the lobe and hit-NEE seeds driven by the frame counter in every AA mode.
  Evidence: pbr_fragment.glsl:6115-6124: kGlossySamples = clamp(Indirect.w,1,8) (MirrorRays, 4 at Quality) for RV_TRANSPARENT, cast for every pane without a settled reflection (6079-6095). GlossyReflection seeds from GlobalIllumination.y (3370-3372), which increments every BeginScene whatever the AA mode (Renderer3D.cpp:3940-3945). The hit NEE seed uses RV_TRACE_FRAME (2838-2842), active because HitLightSampling defaults to true (EngineConfig.h:466).
  Correction: Below roughness ~0.01 (alpha<1e-4) GlossyReflection returns the mirror direction, so only the hit NEE is random there.
- **G-C8** (partly true). Claim: The glass layer's histories are full-frame: 6 RGBA16F lanes for reflections (96 B/px for the pair) and 4 lanes for direct light, allocated whatever the glass coverage.
  Evidence: FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:2786-2791: GlassReflections has 6 RGBA16F lanes x 2 = 96 B/px. 2729-2736: GlassDirectLight has 4 RGBA16F lanes plus a 5th R16G16 id lane. The GlassLayer target is created whenever the transparent pass exists (673-688, wantTransparent at 509; DrawTransparent is always set by editor and runtime), and the histories whenever directSignal/traced reflections run.
  Correction: Direct light has 5 lanes (4 RGBA16F + 1 RG16F) = 36 B/px per history, 72 B/px for the pair. The glass total is 168 B/px, which matches the phase-2 gate. The histories are allocated even in a scene with no glass (e.g. the camp).
- **G-C9** (confirmed). Claim: Glows and sorted or additive particles are drawn into the opaque target before the transparent pass, without writing depth.
  Evidence: Renderer3D.cpp:5074-5081 LightGlow::Draw at the end of the opaque pass (additive, depth test on, write off: LightGlow.cpp:80-84). Scene.cpp:5476-5503 draws particles last in the scene draw. ParticleRenderer.cpp:275-281 alpha blend with depth write off. Weighted particles are split off to the transparent pass (ParticleRenderer.cpp:485-497).
- **G-C10** (confirmed). Claim: MegaLights at 1080p on PS5 with 941 lights: translucency plus volumetric fog costs 1.43 ms (sampling 0.48, HW RT 0.11, shading 0.52, visible list 0.10, denoise 0.22) of the 5.51 ms total. This is reconstructed from a garbled table whose rows sum to the stated total.
  Evidence: megalights_2025.txt:1540-1570. The table's columns rebuild to opaque 1.35+0.7+0.47+0.55+0.05+0.96 = 4.08 and translucency+fog 0.48+0.11+0.52(0.1+0.42)+0.10+0.22 = 1.43, which sum to 5.51 ms. PS5, 1080p, 1 spp, async off, 941 lights. The talk's words are '20 to 80 lights per pixel'.
  Correction: These are figures from the MegaLights demo, not from a shipped title.
- **G-C11** (confirmed). Claim: MegaLights rejected reusing the froxel grid for translucency lighting (visible structure and reprojection diffusion when the camera moves). It ships a world-space grid that shares sampling and tracing with the fog froxels (about -25%), and uses the SH dominant direction for glass specular because re-running a front layer can double the lighting cost.
  Evidence: megalights_2025.txt:1383-1402: reusing the froxel grid was rejected because the structure shows (worse when the camera moves) and reprojection causes diffusion errors. 1403-1428: the hybrid shares sampling and tracing at froxel positions and saves about 25% of volume build. 1488-1518: re-running the front layer 'can easily end up doubling' lighting cost, so SH dominant direction is used for now. The talk calls it ongoing R&D, supporting one highlight and jittering if the SH is unstable.
- **G-C12** (confirmed). Claim: Lumen 2022: HWRT surface-cache reflections skip translucent meshes entirely; the front layer is re-run with the denoiser disabled; other layers read the World Radiance Cache, whose probes are marked by rasterising translucent surfaces at low resolution.
  Evidence: lumen_2022.txt:1749-1766: the Surface-Cache pipeline treats the BVH as opaque and skips translucent meshes entirely (alpha-masked are skipped below 50% opacity via MaxTranslucentSkipCount). 2870-2890: the front layer is depth-peeled and the reflection pipeline re-run, forced to mirror with the denoiser disabled; other layers use the World Radiance Cache, with probes marked by rasterising translucent surfaces at low resolution.
- **G-C13** (confirmed). Claim: Hillaire 2020: the aerial-perspective volume is 32x32 over the screen with 32 slices over 32 km, applied per vertex to transparent surfaces; 0.31 ms total at 1280x720 on a GTX 1080; volumetric shadows need a 32-sample march (1.0 ms) reprojected via TAA.
  Evidence: hillaire_egsr2020.txt:244-262: 32x32 by 32 slices over 32 km; 285-289: per-vertex on transparents; 530-535: 0.31 ms total at 1280x720 on a GTX 1080; 588-596: 32 samples bring sky+atmosphere to 1.0 ms, with reprojection via TAA suggested.
- **G-C14** (confirmed). Claim: AVBOIT at 4K on PS5: 4.967 ms with culling against WBOIT 5.908, MBOIT 5.705 and no OIT 4.4 ms (from phase 1, not in this area's source set).
  Evidence: AVBOIT_SIG2025_MDROBOT-final.pdf, PERFORMANCE slide (4k | PS5): Total MBOIT 5.705, WBOIT 5.908, AVBOIT 5.667, AVBOIT+Cull 4.967, No OIT 4.4.
  Correction: In that table WBOIT, the method RageV uses, is the most expensive. AVBOIT+Cull is 0.94 ms cheaper than WBOIT, so the '+0.5-1.3 ms' deltas are against No OIT, not against RageV's method.
- **G-C15** (partly true). Claim: The wave speed is scaled by a fixed 0.32 on deep-water dispersion, and the refraction push is clamped to 0.25-4 m. Neither is in either audit.
  Evidence: water_waves.glsl:165 has the `* 0.32` on the deep-water speed, and it is absent from both docs. pbr_fragment.glsl:6431 has `push = clamp(waterThickness, 0.25, 4.0)`.
  Correction: The push is in audit 2: RT-SERIES-2.md:2587 ('raster refraction push | pbr_fragment.glsl:6431') and RT2-44 at 1896. It is on the raster form only; the RT path (RV_RAY_REFRACTION) does not use it.
- **G-OB1** (refuted). Claim: RageV is BETTER than the top engines at: Many lamps on water (glitter). Ours: The sea's lamps go through the shared DirectTrace on the sea layer (the default). Also present: the retired private choose/shade passes (still compiled), an in-shader sampler, and a private accumulator with fixed 4- and 16-frame memories. The lobe is anisotropic Beckmann widened by Cox-Munk. Theirs: No source shows a shipped many-light water. UE Single Layer Water uses the standard light passes, and the phase-1 docs say MegaLights does not support water. Why: RageV lights about a hundred shadowed lamps per sea pixel with an accumulator. But it has three implementations, and its frame-count memories break rule 2.
  Evidence: The code half holds. WaterDirect defaults to true and routes the sea's lamps through the shared DirectTrace (EngineConfig.h:636-647). The private choose/shade/accumulate passes still exist (FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:3130-3505). The fixed memories are History.y/z = 16 and 4 (water_accumulate.rvshader:396-403; EngineConfig.h:710-722). The MegaLights doc does list Water as unsupported (WebFetch of the Epic page).
  Correction: 'Better than the top engines' is not supported. It rests on no source describing many-light water, which is absence of evidence. UE Single Layer Water takes the standard deferred local lights with their shadows. Path-traced titles such as Cyberpunk Overdrive (ReSTIR DI) light wet surfaces from thousands of shadowed lights, and phase 1 only says no source describing it was read. Per frame, RageV shades a few sampled lamps (RaysPerPixel/4 choices), not about a hundred, and relies on fixed-frame memories, which the claim itself says break rule 2. The evidence is bridge-only. At most: distinctive and unmeasured against a baseline.
- **G-BB1** (plausible). Claim: Could beat both RageV and the shipped baseline: Refraction in screen space first, traced only where that fails: march the refracted ray across the backdrop's depth and read the fully lit, accumulated opaque frame. A traced ray, with its own accumulator, is used only for points where the march leaves the screen, lands in front of the surface, or fails the local depth-gradient test.. Why: UE Single Layer Water uses screen space only, so it misses bottoms that are occluded or off screen. RageV re-shades every sea pixel's bottom with a cheaper shade, per quad, with no accumulator, and throws away the lit seabed. The hybrid gets opaque-quality lighting on the common path, correct geometry where screen space fails, and far fewer rays. It is the screen-space-then-RT pattern that id Tech 8, AC Shadows and Snowdrop ship for reflections, applied to refraction.
  Evidence: Snowdrop does screen space first and then world space for GI and reflections (snowdrop_gdc2024.txt:82-84, 466-479, 536-593). RageV's RT refraction is per quad with ShadeTraced + ProbeIrradiance and no accumulator (pbr_fragment.glsl:6378-6398). The raster guards are at 6440-6447. Refraction toward the normal when looking down is standard Snell.
  Correction: id Tech 8's pattern (phase1) traces in world space for GI and then reads screen lighting at the hit. That is not screen-march-then-trace for reflections, so drop it as a precedent. Snowdrop itself reports seams where screen-space and world-space hits differ, so the seam gate is load-bearing. UE has optional HWRT translucent refraction, so 'screen space only' holds for Single Layer Water specifically. It needs a measurement gate.
- **G-BB2** (plausible). Claim: Could beat both RageV and the shipped baseline: Composite the nearest see-through surface exactly, per pixel: final = its radiance + RGB transmittance x the backdrop. Weighted-blended OIT is kept only for what lies behind it: back panes, particles and smoke.. Why: Today, WBOIT averages the nearest pane with the panes behind it and averages three to five sea crossings (about 10% too bright at Glitter). MBOIT and AVBOIT fix ordering approximately at +0.5-1.3 ms at 4K on PS5. An exact front layer removes the dominant error in this content, one pane or the sea, at close to no cost, because the layer is already rasterised for RT-13.
  Evidence: GlassLayer and WaterSurface single-sample targets exist (FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:626-688). The 3-5 crossings and roughly 10% over-brightness at Glitter are recorded in RT-SERIES-2.md:1583 and FINDINGS:3785. The AVBOIT slide says MBOIT/WBOIT 'fail to occlude glass in the background'.
  Correction: The cost framing is wrong. In the AVBOIT table, WBOIT (5.908) costs more than MBOIT (5.705) and AVBOIT+Cull (4.967), so against RageV's current method those are not '+0.5-1.3 ms'. 'Close to no cost' is unmeasured: the glass layer today holds velocity, normal, albedo and id only, so radiance and RGB-transmittance lanes and a shading step must be added. Particles and glows in front of the layer need their own path; today they draw into the opaque target before the transparent pass (G-C9).
- **G-BB3** (partly true). Claim: Could beat both RageV and the shipped baseline: Shadow rays through glass and water in one query: the candidate loop multiplies the RGB transmittance of each transmissive candidate in any order and never confirms them. Any confirmed opaque hit ends the ray with TerminateOnFirstHit and makes visibility zero. The only requirement is VK_GEOMETRY_NO_DUPLICATE_ANY_HIT_INVOCATION_BIT_KHR on transmissive geometry.. Why: RageV's glass casts no shadow today. Remix and NvRTX need an any-hit stage, which RageV does not have, and phase 1 assumed the product must be bounded by the opaque hit (two queries). For visibility rays that bound is unnecessary: an opaque hit zeroes the result whatever order the candidates came in. That makes it one traversal at the cost of the existing alpha-test loop.
  Evidence: The core logic is correct (see G-C3). But blended materials are excluded from the TLAS (Scene.cpp:2892-2910; Material.h:80-83 TracedAsGeometry = Opaque|Masked), water never enters it (no water in RayShadows.cpp/Scene.cpp AddInstance calls), RayTraverse is shared by every ray type and confirms any candidate that passes the alpha test (ray_shadow_trace.glsl:192-203), and RTAO forces every instance opaque (rtao_compute.rvshader:371).
  Correction: NO_DUPLICATE is not 'the only requirement'. Glass and water must first enter the TLAS with a mask bit of their own. Every ray type that must not see them (AO with gl_RayFlagsOpaqueEXT, reflection, GI, irradiance_fill, water_shade) must drop that bit or branch in RayTraverse. Water needs a ray stand-in because its displacement exists only in the vertex stage. The flag's per-mesh BLAS granularity must be resolved (G-C2).
- **G-BB4** (plausible). Claim: Could beat both RageV and the shipped baseline: One analytic height-fog integral, applied to every path segment and aware of each segment's medium: opaque surfaces from the nearest see-through surface (or the camera), the layer from the camera, each WBOIT fragment by T*C + coverage*S, every reflection and air-side refraction segment, glows from the camera to the lamp, and the sky integrated to infinity. Segments behind a water surface use the water medium, not air.. Why: RageV fogs once after TAA over the opaque depth, so reflections get none and glass is fogged at the depth behind it. AC Shadows uses one simplified step at specular hits and skips near fog. Froxel volumes cover only the camera frustum, so reflection rays need another path anyway. The closed form is exact for height fog, has no noise, costs almost nothing, and removes the sea's dependence on FogFloor.
  Evidence: fog.rvshader:160-250 has the closed-form height integral, floor clip and horizontal branch. I checked the composite algebra: T1*C + a*S1 + (1-a)*[S(0,d2)+T(0,d2)*B] equals the true S1 + T1*[C + (1-a)*(S12+T12*B)] when S adds and T multiplies along the ray. Phase 1 records AC Shadows' single step at specular hits with near fog skipped.
  Correction: 'Algebraically exact' fails with the MaxOpacity cap. The fog is (1-exp(-optical))*MaxOpacity (fog.rvshader:~249), which does not compose over segments, and the bridge profile uses FogMaxOpacity 0.42. Direction-dependent inscatter (SkyAffect/SkyOcclusion) must be taken per segment direction. Also the inscatter is a colour, not lit and not shadowed, so it is exact only for RageV's own fog model.
- **G-BB5** (plausible). Claim: Could beat both RageV and the shipped baseline: The panes behind the nearest, when mirror-smooth, get one fixed mirror ray each: no lobe draw, and a hit shaded only by the deterministic part of the hit contract (stored light plus emissive). Rougher panes read stored light. Nothing then relies on TAA.. Why: UE's deeper layers get only glossy, cache-interpolated reflections. RageV's are sharper but random every frame and averaged only by TAA (the D11 exception). A single mirror ray is exact for smooth glass and holds still in every AA mode, and it costs fewer rays than today's 1-8 lobe rays with NEE.
  Evidence: The lobe and NEE seeds are driven by the frame counter (G-C7). D11 keeps back panes as the TAA exception (RT-SERIES-2.md:30). UE's deeper layers use the World Radiance Cache (lumen_2022.txt:2876-2890). The soft shadow seeds are already gated on jitter (ray_shadow_trace.glsl ~290-320), so the rest of the back-pane path is deterministic.
  Correction: 'Stored light' at a hit has to exist first. The one world-space light store is off in every shipped scene and read by no ray hit (RT-SERIES-2.md:1345), so today the deterministic part is probe/bake plus emissive, and Realtime lamps would drop out of back-pane reflections. A single mirror ray is exact only as roughness goes to 0, so the mirror-tier threshold must come from lobe width against pixel footprint.
- **G-BB6** (plausible). Claim: Could beat both RageV and the shipped baseline: Per-particle light accumulator: light each particle once per frame at its centre from the candidate structure and stored light, and average it in the particle's own GPU state slot. The history moves with the particle, so nothing is reprojected.. Why: It avoids the camera-dependent instability that MegaLights reports for froxel lighting and the cell structure of a world grid, at a cost that scales with the number of particles.
  Evidence: particle_sim.rvshader:25-30 has a persistent per-slot state with Spin.zw unused. megalights_2025.txt:1383-1402 on froxel instability. particle.rvshader:4 is unlit today.
  Correction: Only GPU-simulated emitters have a state slot, and SimulateOnGpu defaults to false (Components.h:1053). CPU emitters need an equivalent. Two floats hold RGB+count only as packed halves. One centre sample cannot vary light across a large sprite, and the history lags a particle crossing a shadow edge. Needs a gate against the world-grid cache.

### G.8 Design changes the verification required

Each was applied to the roadmap's items on 2026-09-25 (section 9 lists them).

- **Transmissive traversal class (glass and water visible to rays).** The design already adds TLAS entry and a mask bit. Add: (1) how NO_DUPLICATE is placed, since the BLAS is per mesh and shared across materials (flag on all BLAS with its cost measured, or a separate transmissive BLAS); (2) the list of ray inits that must exclude the transmissive bit or branch: rtao_compute.rvshader:371 (forces opaque), irradiance_fill.rvshader:360, water_shade.rvshader:383/404, pbr_fragment.glsl:2647, and the shared RayTraverse (ray_shadow_trace.glsl:192-203), which today confirms any candidate passing the alpha test; (3) a gate that glass and water in the TLAS leave AO, GI and reflection images unchanged except where intended. Rests on: G-BB3, G-C2.
- **One transmissive layer and an exact composite.** Restate the cost argument. Against RageV's WBOIT, AVBOIT+cull and MBOIT were cheaper in the Activision table, not +0.5-1.3 ms, so AVBOIT on back layers should stay a considered arm rather than be dismissed on cost. Budget the new radiance and RGB-transmittance lanes and the layer-shading step as real cost with an A,B,B,A gate. Correct the history accounting to 5 direct lanes (72 B/px pair) + 96 B/px reflections. Note that today even glass-free scenes allocate them, which the 'only while a layer is in view' rule fixes. Rests on: G-BB2, G-C8, G-C14.
- **Signals on the layer: direct light and reflections.** The accepted 'white shine' is produced by the absolute cap (8x probe + floor 0.5), and that accepted accumulator exists only on the unmerged wip branch. Replacing the cap with a relative statistical clamp removes the shine. Add an explicit owner look gate (per-pixel diff at the headland towers) and state that the cap also governs glass's in-draw rays today. The 'quarter of pixels' ray rate matches today only for non-Off presets; the dead ReflectionScale preset column should be deleted or wired. Rests on: G-C4, G-C6.
- **Refraction, the medium, and skipping work that is covered.** Drop id Tech 8 as a precedent and cite Snowdrop, including its reported SS/WS seams. The 0.25-4 m push and the occluder tolerance are raster-only and already owned by RT2-44 (RT-SERIES-2.md:1896, 2587-2588), so coordinate rather than double-claim them. Only the 0.32 wave-speed factor is new to the audits. Rests on: G-BB1, G-C15.
- **Fog.** 'Applied the same way everywhere' is impossible for MaxOpacity as written: a cap on total opacity does not compose over segments, and the bridge runs 0.42. Either redefine MaxOpacity (for example as an optical-depth ceiling applied once on the camera-to-pixel path, with segments scaled consistently) with an owner decision, since rule 5 says profile dials stay untouched, or accept an approximation and gate it by diff. Also evaluate SkyAffect inscatter per segment direction. Rests on: G-BB4, G-C5.
- **Back layers and particles.** The mirror-tier back-pane ray shades hits with 'stored light', which does not exist for Realtime lights today. Make it depend on the translucency or world cache, or accept that realtime lamps are missing from back-pane reflections, as an owner-visible trade. Derive the mirror-tier threshold from lobe against footprint. Per-particle accumulation covers only GPU-simulated emitters (default off), so the design must move emitters to GPU or keep the world-grid cache as the particle path. Rests on: G-BB5, G-BB6.

### G.9 New findings from the verification

- **G-N1.** Blended glass and water are not in the ray structure at all (Scene.cpp:2892-2910, Material.h:80-83; water never reaches RayShadows::AddInstance). Glass casts no traced shadow and is invisible to every reflection and GI ray. This is the real prerequisite behind BB3.
- **G-N2.** The RT optimisation preset's ReflectionScale column (Off 1, Quality/Balanced 2, Performance 4; RenderSettings.h:238-244) is dead: FrameGraphBuilder.cpp:797-799 uses 1 unless --water-reflection is given.
- **G-N3.** kTracedBound with the ReflectionFloor (pbr_fragment.glsl:6155-6157) also clamps glass's in-draw lobe rays (RV_TRANSPARENT path at 6096-6159), so ReflectionFloor 0.5 on the bridge affects any glass there too.
- **G-N4.** GlassLayer, GlassDirectLight (72 B/px) and GlassReflections (96 B/px) are allocated whenever the transparent pass and the direct/reflection signals run, including scenes with no glass (DrawTransparent is always set in EditorLayer.cpp:730/872 and RuntimeLayer.cpp:580).
- **G-N5.** In the AVBOIT SIGGRAPH 2025 table, WBOIT is the most expensive OIT (5.908 ms at 4K PS5); AVBOIT+Cull is 4.967. So RageV's own method is not the cheap option the phase-2 cost framing assumes.
- **G-N6.** rtao_compute.rvshader:371 traces with gl_RayFlagsOpaqueEXT, which overrides FORCE_NO_OPAQUE. Any transmissive instance added to the TLAS would fully occlude AO unless AO's cull mask excludes it.
- **G-N7.** Particles are unlit and GPU simulation is off by default (particle.rvshader:4; Components.h:1053 SimulateOnGpu=false).
- **G-N8.** Bridge fog uses FogMaxOpacity 0.42 (bridge_cinematic.rvpostprofile:8), a non-physical cap that stops fog composing exactly per segment.

### G.10 The comparison's corrections to the survey

- MegaLights costs: phase 1 said no volumetric or translucency ms were available. megalights_2025.txt:1540-1625 has them: at 1080p on PS5 with 941 lights, translucency plus volumetric fog costs 1.43 ms of the 5.51 ms total (sampling 0.48, HW RT 0.11, shading 0.52 = 0.1 + 0.42, visible light list 0.10, denoise 0.22). Translucency shading costs almost as much as opaque shading (0.52 against 0.55). The table text is garbled; this reconstruction sums exactly to the stated total.
- MegaLights front layer: phase 1 listed 'plus a per-pixel front-layer pass' as part of the approach. The talk (megalights_2025.txt:1488-1518) says re-running the whole pipeline on a translucency G-buffer is 'usually too expensive', since it can double lighting cost, and that for now they light such surfaces from the translucency volume's SH, extracting the dominant direction for specular.
- MegaLights volumes: phase 1 described the per-voxel sampling but not what the talk adds (megalights_2025.txt:1383-1428). Reusing the froxel grid to light translucency was tried and rejected: the grid's structure shows when the camera moves, and reprojection causes diffusion errors. The shipped hybrid samples and traces at froxel positions and shades into both the world grid and the froxels, saving about 25%.
- Hillaire 2020: phase 1 said the paper was not re-read. Confirmed from hillaire_egsr2020.txt:244-290 and 522-600: aerial perspective is 32x32 over the screen with 32 depth slices over 32 km (a depth-range dial the paper says can be brought closer), applied as a post pass to opaque surfaces and per vertex to transparent ones; 0.31 ms total at 1280x720 on a GTX 1080 (0.14 ms on screen); about 1 ms on an iPhone 6s for Fortnite. Its volumetric shadows use a 32-sample march at 1.0 ms and rely on TAA reprojection, which conflicts with rule 3.
- Lumen 2022: the front-layer claims are confirmed (lumen_2022.txt:2855-2890: depth peeling, forced mirror, denoiser disabled, back layers from the World Radiance Cache with probes marked by a low-resolution raster). One addition phase 1 missed (lumen_2022.txt:1749-1766): HWRT with the surface cache treats the BVH as opaque and skips translucent meshes entirely (MaxTranslucentSkipCount). So shipped Lumen reflections also lose glass, as RageV's do today.
- Tinted shadows: phase 1 said the ray-query loop 'must bound tMax by the opaque hit: either a first opaque-only query, or by committing opaque hits and discarding translucent candidates beyond them'. For shadow and visibility rays that is unnecessary: any opaque hit makes visibility zero, so an unordered product over all transmissive candidates up to tMax is exact. What it does need is VK_GEOMETRY_NO_DUPLICATE_ANY_HIT_INVOCATION_BIT_KHR, which RageV does not set (VulkanResources.cpp:979). Bounding is needed only for closest-hit rays.
- Water grid: phase 1 asked to 'confirm the fixed 3 m spacing'. The code takes spacing per component, clamped to 1024 divisions per side (Water.cpp:126-145); 3 m is the bridge scene's value (GoldenGateDemo.rage:6475), which gives 933^2 quads over 2800 m.
- Light glows: phase 1 framed RageV's glows as legacy billboards to be replaced by fog in-scatter plus bloom. In the code, the glow is an energy-normalised stand-in for lamps smaller than a pixel (light_glow.rvshader:1-35), which solves popping without TAA; in-scatter would not replace that job. Its fixed side-glow and pull values are the actual defects. The bridge profile scales it to 0.02 (bridge_cinematic.rvpostprofile:14), so the energy argument is not what ships.

### G.11 Questions left open

As the survey recorded them. Where the verification later read a primary talk the survey could not fetch, the verification's verdict above supersedes the line here.

- The session's web-search budget was used up before this agent started (200 of 200 calls), so every source here was reached by fetching known URLs or by reading talk PDFs that other agents in the same session had already saved. Anything that would have needed a search is not covered.
- Cyberpunk 2077 RT Overdrive: how glass, particles, water and volumetric fog enter the path tracer (primary source not retrieved).
- Indiana Jones and the Great Circle and Doom: The Dark Ages path-tracing handling of transparency and particles: the Doom path-tracing talk (GPC 2025) PDF exceeded the fetch limit.
- Metro Exodus Enhanced: 4A's article publishes nothing on transparency or fog under RT.
- MegaLights SIGGRAPH 2025 slides (volumetric and translucency ms) and Hillaire's EGSR 2020 sky paper (LUT costs): both PDFs exceeded the fetch limit.
- Ray Tracing Gems II chapter 30 (RT caustics) costs and method details: Springer requires authentication, and the NVIDIA preprint exceeded the fetch limit.
- Lin 2021 volumetric ReSTIR: the resolution behind the 55 ms and 142 ms timings (the repo names an RTX 3090 for paper results).
- Which Call of Duty title shipped AVBOIT; the slides say CoD moved to OIT while supporting live releases but do not name the title in the extracted text.
- Frostbite 2015 unified volumetrics (the EA page returned 404), Sea of Thieves, Atlas and Frostbite water: not re-read, so not used as evidence.
- Whether Portal with RTX used the Remix froxel ReSTIR volumetrics as documented in the current runtime (believed yes, not verified).
- Whether BLAS built from VK_NV_cluster_acceleration_structure can be traversed by inline ray queries (believed yes, not verified), and whether shader execution reordering is available to inline ray queries on Vulkan (believed no, not verified).
- UE5 experimental per-pixel OIT (sorted-pixels cvars) and Substrate's coloured-transmittance blending: not verified from a primary source.
- Unity HDRP: whether water appears in ray-traced effects (not found in the pages fetched).
- Claims the verification could not settle without a measurement or a source: G-BB1, G-BB2, G-BB4, G-BB5, G-BB6 (each is gated in the roadmap item named above).

## Totals

Comparison topics across the seven areas: 94 behind, 41 equal, 20 missing, 20 not comparable, 14 better (claimed) (189 in all). Verification verdicts across 160 claims: 90 confirmed, 37 partly true, 19 plausible, 11 refuted, 3 unsupported. Of the 14 "better" claims, none survived in full: 4 partly true (B-OB1, C-OB2, C-OB4, D-OB1); 8 refuted (A-OB1, A-OB2, B-OB2, C-OB1, C-OB3, F-OB2, F-OB3, G-OB1); 2 unsupported (E-OB1, F-OB1). Of the 30 "could beat both" ideas: 8 partly true (A-BB1, B-BB1, B-BB3, C-BB1, C-BB3, E-BB1, E-BB2, G-BB3); 19 plausible (A-BB2, A-BB3, A-BB4, B-BB2, B-BB5, C-BB2, D-BB1, D-BB2, E-BB3, F-BB1, F-BB2, F-BB3, F-BB4, F-BB5, G-BB1, G-BB2, G-BB4, G-BB5, G-BB6); 3 refuted (B-BB4, D-BB3, D-BB4). The refuted ones are already shipped, so they became baselines; the rest are arms with gates in the items named above.
